# CONFIRMED FINDINGS (35) — corrected claims after independent refutation attempts

Severity shown is the REFUTER-ADJUSTED severity. Read the CORRECTED CLAIM and VERDICT MEASUREMENTS as the truth; the original CLAIM/EVIDENCE is the auditor's first pass.


## F01 [critical] Causal EMA smoother lags the pose behind the keypoints by 4.9 mm median / 25.7 mm max (4.2% / 21.8% of body height) on stop-motion frames

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:183 | category: smoothing | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: critical
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The causal EMA in pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:129-143, invoked at pose3d/pipeline.py:183, lags the displayed and exported pose behind the keypoints by 4.92 mm median / 6.07 mm mean / 25.65 mm max (4.2 % / 5.2 % / 21.8 % of the 0.1178 m body height; max at frame 0014 RIGHT_ANKLE) on the client's 26 stop-motion frames. The displacement is pure backward lag — cos with the motion direction is negative in 95.4 % of 371 samples, median -0.976, and its magnitude matches the closed-form EMA steady-state lag (predicted 6.11 mm vs measured mean 6.07). It is 100 % of what separates the shipped fitted3d from the bone-fitted measurement, since refit(smooth=True) reproduces the shipped file bit-for-bit and refit(smooth=False) differs by up to 23.5 mm. Cost: reprojection onto the source photos degrades from 7.38/4.01 px to 21.65/17.18 px (0.95 %/0.99 % -> 2.8 %/4.2 % of the subject's 776/407 px on-image height); bone-length CV from 0.14 %/0.37 % to 1.04 %/6.35 %, undoing the bone fit that ran one line earlier (L forearm 19 % short in frame 0013); and bone DIRECTIONS — the quantity pose3d/geometry/character.py aims each rig bone along — rotate 4.4 deg median, 11.5 p90, 27.8 max away from the captured direction. Benefit is 17 % less inter-frame jitter (9.16 -> 7.60 mm), on a take that is discrete hand-posed frames where inter-frame jitter is not noise to suppress.

THREE CORRECTIONS to the finding.
(1) The proposed fix is a NO-OP. `smooth=True` is passed EXPLICITLY at pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 and pose3d/ui/model.py:55 (and tests/build_demo_project.py:77); no shipped caller relies on the pipeline.py:153 default, so flipping that default changes nothing in the app. The fix must change those two call sites, or drop the smoothing call at pipeline.py:183, or gate it on a UI opt-in that defaults off.
(2) The severity-per-sample fractions are overstated: I measure 26.3 % of (frame,joint) samples moving >5 % of body height and 5.7 % >10 %, not 41 % / 11.6 %.
(3) Two minor numeric slips: max bone CV is 6.35 %, not 6.16 %; and the alpha-sweep "1.0" row is really smooth=False — true alpha=1.0 gives 0.24 % / 7.40 px, because the NaN branch at bonefit.py:139-140 still runs.

ADDITIONAL DEFECT in the same function, not in the finding: bonefit.py:139-140 substitutes the previous frame's value wherever the current frame is NaN, so unobserved joints are silently invented and shown as measured (2 on this take: frame 0012 RIGHT_KNEE, frame 0021 LEFT_ANKLE). Any fix that keeps a smoother must keep NaN as NaN. The suggested zero-phase replacement (forward-backward EMA or symmetric 3-tap) removes the lag but not the fact that on stop-motion input there is no temporal signal to filter; off-by-default is the right shape, and the regression test proposed (no negative median cos on a synthetic moving sequence) does test the actual mechanism.
- VERDICT REASON: CORE CLAIM CONFIRMED, ONE LOAD-BEARING DETAIL WRONG (the proposed fix is a no-op).

CODE, read by me:
- pose3d/pipeline.py:152-153 `def fit_project(project, bone_lengths=None, smooth: bool = True, alpha: float = 0.6)`; :182-183 `if smooth and len(fitted) > 1: fitted = smooth_temporal(fitted, alpha=alpha)`; :184-185 writes the result to `f.fitted3d`. Confirmed verbatim.
- pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:129-143 `smooth_temporal` is a causal in-place IIR EMA: `out[t] = alpha*poses3d[t] + (1-alpha)*out[t-1]` reading the ALREADY-SMOOTHED `out[t-1]` (line 137 `prev, cur = out[t-1], poses3d[t]`), so the tail is infinite, not "40% of the previous frame" only. Lines 139-140 also substitute `prev` where `cur` is NaN — it INVENTS values for unobserved joints.
- Executed path confirmed: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 and pose3d/ui/model.py:55 both call `fit_project(..., smooth=True)`; the app displays `f.fitted3d` (pose3d/ui/main_window.py:450 `self.view3d.set_pose(f.fitted3d, f.head3d)`) and exports it (main_window.py:355 `poses = np.stack([f.fitted3d for f in frames])` -> blender_export). So the smoothed pose is exactly what the client sees and exports.
- Aggravating, not in the finding: (a) the on-screen accuracy readout uses the RAW pose, not the displayed one — pose3d/ui/model.py:180-184 `reprojection_error(f.pose3d, ...)` — so the app cannot show this error; (b) a manual 2D drag re-fits that one frame with NO smoothing (model.py:157-160 `f.fitted3d = fit_bone_lengths(f.pose3d, ...)`), so an edited frame jumps out of the smoothed sequence, an internal inconsistency.

WHAT IS WRONG: the claim "Every shipped entry point uses that default" is false — both shipped call sites pass `smooth=True` EXPLICITLY (import_dialog.py:214, model.py:55), and so does tests/build_demo_project.py:77. Repo-wide grep finds no caller that relies on the default. Therefore the proposed fix as written ("pipeline.py:153 — flip `smooth: bool = True` to False") changes NOTHING in the running app. The fix must edit the two call sites (or delete the call at pipeline.py:183). The mechanism the fix targets is otherwise correct.

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATIONS CHECKED AND EXCLUDED: both branches share the identical bone fit, triangulation and calibration; the only differing operation is `smooth_temporal`, and `refit(smooth=True)` reproduces the shipped `fitted3d` bit-for-bit (max abs diff 0.0 m, identical NaN masks), so the displacement is entirely attributable to that one line. The displacement is directional lag, not symmetric noise (cos with motion median -0.976), and its magnitude matches the closed-form EMA steady-state lag to within 2%, so it is not a bone-fit or NaN artefact. Detector noise cannot explain it: the same 2D drives both branches.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All from my own script (stdin-piped, nothing written to the repo), cwd=repo root, take = workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, 0 corrections. refit = deepcopy -> triangulate_project -> fit_project(smooth=True|False, alpha=0.6).

SANITY: stored fitted3d vs my refit(smooth=True): max abs diff 0.0 m, NaN masks identical. vs refit(smooth=False): max abs 23.5 mm.

DISPLACEMENT smooth vs nosmooth, n=388 finite (frame,joint): median 4.924 mm, mean 6.071, p90 12.766, max 25.652 mm at frame_id 0014 RIGHT_ANKLE. EXACT match to the finding (4.92/6.07/12.77/25.65, same frame, same joint).

HEIGHT DENOMINATOR: per-frame de-tilted (sequence_up + de_tilt_matrix) z-extent median 0.1178 m (min 0.0915, max 0.1297); nose-to-ankle median 0.1176 m. Confirms the finding's 0.1178 m. => median 4.18 %, p90 10.84 %, max 21.78 % of body height. 26.3 % of samples > 5 % of height, 5.7 % > 10 % (finding said 41 % / 11.6 % — I get materially LOWER fractions; see corrected_claim).

LAG DIRECTIONALITY: cos(disp, inter-frame motion) median -0.976, 95.4 % negative, n=371 — exact match. Backward-lag component median 4.858 mm of the 4.924 mm median displacement (98.7 %). Predicted EMA steady-state lag = median inter-frame motion x (1-a)/a = 9.16 x 0.667 = 6.11 mm vs measured mean 6.07 mm (0.7 % off).

REPROJECTION (pose3d.geometry.triangulate.reprojection_error, fitted3d vs stored kp2d): smoothed L 21.65 px / R 17.18 px; unsmoothed L 7.38 / R 4.01; raw triangulation L 5.11 / R 2.78. 2.9x / 4.3x worse — exact match. SCALE CONTEXT I added: the subject's 2D bounding-box height is 776 px (left) / 407 px (right) median, so smoothing takes reprojection from 0.95 %/0.99 % of subject size to 2.8 %/4.2 % — independently consistent with the 4.2 %-of-height 3D figure.

BONE LENGTHS: CV% median/max — nosmooth 0.14 / 0.37; smoothed 1.04 / 6.35 (finding said 6.16 max; I get 6.35). L forearm frame_id 0013: 0.0173 m smoothed vs 0.0214 m unsmoothed = 19 % short — exact match.

BONE DIRECTIONS (the quantity character.py actually aims at): angle between smoothed and unsmoothed bone vectors, n=361: median 4.44 deg, p90 11.47, max 27.82 — exact match.

BENEFIT: median inter-frame joint motion 9.16 mm (nosmooth) -> 7.60 mm (smoothed) = 17.0 % reduction — exact match. Raw is 9.36 mm.

ALPHA SWEEP (bone CV median / max, reproj L / R px): 1.0 -> 0.24 / 4.46, 7.40 / 4.01; 0.9 -> 0.44 / 3.62, 9.25 / 5.91; 0.8 -> 0.69 / 3.19, 11.89 / 8.96; 0.6 (shipped) -> 1.04 / 6.35, 21.65 / 17.18; 0.4 -> 1.30 / 11.89, 40.54 / 33.78. NOTE the finding's "1.0 -> 0.14 % / 7.38 px" row is actually smooth=False, not alpha=1.0: alpha=1.0 still runs the NaN branch and gives 0.24 % / 7.40 px.

NEW, NOT IN THE FINDING: smooth_temporal RESURRECTS missing joints. NaN joints: 2 with smooth=False (frame 0012 RIGHT_KNEE, frame 0021 LEFT_ANKLE), 0 with smoothing — both are filled with the previous frame's position and displayed as if measured. That hold alone raises max bone CV from 0.37 % to 4.46 % (the alpha=1.0 row).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: fit_project() ends with `fitted = smooth_temporal(fitted, alpha=alpha)` and defaults to smooth=True, alpha=0.6 (pipeline.py:152-153, 182-183). Every shipped entry point uses that default (import_dialog.py:214, model.py:55). The client's take is 26 DISCRETE hand-posed frames, so 40% of the previous, unrelated pose is blended into every frame. The displacement is pure backward lag, not noise reduction: it points opposite the motion direction in 95.4% of samples.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: refit(smooth=True) vs refit(smooth=False), 26 frames, height 0.1178 m: |disp| median 4.92 mm (4.18%), mean 6.07, p90 12.77 (10.84%), max 25.65 mm (21.78%, RIGHT_ANKLE frame 0014). 41% of (frame,joint) samples move >5% of height, 11.6% >10%. cos(disp,motion) median -0.976, 95.4% negative, n=371; backward-lag component 4.86 of the 4.92 mm median. Predicted EMA steady-state lag = median inter-frame motion 9.36 mm x (1-a)/a = 6.24 mm vs measured 6.07 mm mean. Reprojection 21.65 px left / 17.18 px right vs 7.38 / 4.01 px unsmoothed (2.9x / 4.3x worse). Bone CV 1.04% median / 6.16% max vs 0.14% / 0.37%. Bone DIRECTIONS rotate 4.4 deg median, p90 11.5, max 27.8 — and character.py aims every bone at its captured joint. Benefit: inter-frame motion drops only 9.16 -> 7.60 mm (17%). Alpha sweep (bone CV median / reproj left): 1.0 -> 0.14%/7.38 px; 0.9 -> 0.44%/9.25; 0.8 -> 0.69%/11.89; 0.6 (shipped) -> 1.04%/21.65; 0.4 -> 1.30%/40.54. Concrete damage: L forearm 19% short in frame 0013 (0.0173 vs 0.0214 m).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): pipeline.py:153 — flip `smooth: bool = True` to False; a take is stop-motion by construction, so smoothing becomes opt-in (import-dialog checkbox + alpha slider, defaulted off). If kept, replace the causal EMA with a zero-phase filter (forward-backward EMA, or symmetric 3-tap [0.25,0.5,0.25] with NaN-aware renormalisation). Regression test: cos(disp, motion) must have no negative median on a synthetic moving sequence.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/pipeline_probe.py, probe.json (H1_smoothing, H1_motion, H1_alpha_sweep); <SP>/find-pipeline/probe2.json (A_ordering)

## F02 [major] Every bone's roll about its own axis is undefined (_align = minimal rotation): limb twist is 13.6-53.6 deg off the anatomical bend plane and the feet/hands inherit all of it

- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:488 | category: retarget | sources: retarget | auditor severity: critical
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Every bone's roll about its own aim axis is unconstrained (`_bone_fk` uses `R = _align(rest_dir, aim)`, character.py:488, `_align` 131-144 = minimal rotation), on the executed path of both the 3D view (view3d.py:204) and the FBX/BVH export (blender_export.py:87). Limb twist is 13.6-53.5 deg off the anatomical bend plane (max 72.8 on upper_arm.L) and hand.L/R + foot.L/R, which have no `_DIRECT` entry (character.py:69-88, fallback `skin[b] = base` at 667), inherit the parent's matrix verbatim (measured 26/26 frames). The defect is exactly invisible to every positional metric in the repo: the canonical joints move 0.0000 mm when the roll is corrected, because each limb child's head lies on the parent's axis (child head == parent tail to 0.0 rig units).

Corrected magnitudes: the mesh — the only thing that moves — shifts by 0.77 mm frame-median, 7.8 mm median-of-frame-max and 14.5 mm worst on a 117.8 mm subject (12.3% of body height), and that displacement is concentrated in the feet (worst 14.5 mm) and hands (7.3 mm); arms 5.2 mm worst, torso+head 0.15 mm mean. Two of the finding's impact claims are wrong: (a) the "108 deg foot roll" is the foot's TOTAL reorientation from rest, of which at most 45.6 deg is roll — the rest is swing correctly inherited from the captured shin (shin aim is median 31 deg / max 110 deg off rest); a roll fix leaves foot tilt-off-horizontal essentially unchanged (L 38.5 -> 39.6, R 42.7 -> 43.8 deg median) and does not remove the "downward spike feet", which come from the feet having no target and no ground/ankle constraint at all; (b) it is not the most visible artifact — side-by-side renders of shipped vs roll-corrected show near-identical silhouettes, with the difference confined to limb twist and extremity orientation.

The proposed aim+roll fix is aimed at the right mechanism for upper_arm/thigh (the elbow/knee is a hinge, so the bend plane genuinely determines the parent bone's roll), but for forearm/shin it encodes a convention rather than measured truth (no hand or foot keypoints exist), and the 15 deg straightness guard is insufficient: the captured bend-plane normal jumps up to 55.7 deg between consecutive frames on the left arm at a bend angle of 22.7 deg, so the correction itself would jump up to 55.1 deg per frame and trade static twist for visible popping. The fix needs temporal continuity (smooth/hysteresis the roll reference) and a separate decision for the aim-less foot and hand bones.
- VERDICT REASON: Core mechanism and magnitudes independently reproduced; two impact claims (feet, "most visible artifact") do not survive.

CONFIRMED FROM CODE I READ MYSELF:
- pose3d/geometry/character.py:474-492 `_bone_fk` sets `R = _align(rest_d/n_rest, d_want/n_want)` at line 488; `_align` (131-144) is the minimal (geodesic) rotation, and nothing else touches the component about the aim axis. The rotation is absolute (world/rig), not relative to the parent — the parent only supplies the head position (481, 491) — so each bone's roll is a pure function of its own aim direction.
- `_DIRECT` (69-88) has no entry for hand.L/R or foot.L/R; line 667 gives them `skin[b] = base`, i.e. the parent matrix verbatim. Measured: skin[leaf] == skin[parent] in 26/26 frames for all four leaves. The rig has exactly 19 bones, so hands+feet are the only aim-less ones.
- ON THE EXECUTED PATH, twice: live 3D view pose3d/ui/view3d.py:204 `self._character.pose_and_joints(vpose, valid, vhead)` -> `_skin_matrices` (character.py:530-668) -> `_bone_fk`; and the FBX/BVH deliverable, pose3d/export/blender_export.py:87 `ch.pose_bone_matrices(up, valid, hp)` -> same `_skin_matrices`. So the twist is baked into the exported animation, not only the preview.
- Positional invisibility is exact, not approximate: child head == parent tail to 0.0 rig units for all four limb chains, and my roll-corrected variant moves the canonical `posed_joints` by 0.0000 mm max over 26 frames. No positional metric in the repo (retarget %, bend plane, reprojection) can see this.

WHAT I HAD TO CORRECT:
1. Foot/hand attribution is wrong. I built my own roll-corrected `_skin_matrices` (byte-identical to the shipped one when the roll term is off: max |skin diff| = 0.0) and applied it: foot.L tilt-off-horizontal median 38.5 -> 39.6 deg, spread 59.6 -> 60.6; foot.R 42.7 -> 43.8, spread 95.9 -> 85.5. The roll fix does not straighten the feet. Decomposition: foot.R total reorientation from rest maxes at 108.5 deg, of which at most 45.6 deg is roll; the remainder is swing the foot correctly inherits from the captured shin (shin.R aim is median 31.1 / max 110.5 deg off its rest direction). The "downward spike feet" are caused by the foot having NO target at all and no ground/ankle constraint, not by roll. The finding's "108 deg of foot roll" is a total-reorientation number relabelled as roll.
2. "Single most visible artifact" is not supported. Shipped vs roll-corrected mesh (my render.png, 4 frames x front/side): silhouettes are nearly identical; differences are confined to limb twist and extremity orientation. Vertex displacement is 0.77 mm frame-median on a 117.8 mm subject; torso+head mean 0.15 mm.
3. The proposed fix needs more than a 15 deg straightness guard: the captured bend-plane normal moves 11.4 deg median / 55.7 deg max between consecutive frames on the left arm, and the resulting per-frame roll correction jumps up to 55.1 deg (upper_arm.L). The left elbow's minimum captured bend is 22.7 deg, above the proposed guard, so the guard would not suppress that jitter. Without temporal continuity the fix trades static twist for visible frame-to-frame popping.
4. Semantics: the elbow/knee bend plane anatomically determines the UPPER bone's roll (hinge joint), so upper_arm/thigh errors are true errors. The FOREARM's roll (pronation) and the shin's roll are not determined by that plane and no hand/foot keypoints exist, so driving them from it is a convention, not ground truth — forearm.L/R's "20.4/14.4 deg error" is partly definitional, like the HEAD retarget offset.

Not explainable by anything else: I checked the metric is not self-serving by feeding the rig its own rest joints (with the fitted scale neutralised) — twist error is 0.00 deg on all eight bones, so the 13.6-53.5 deg medians are genuine deviation, not a rest-reference artifact. Captured limbs are well bent (elbow bend median 41-55 deg, knee 33-45 deg), so the bend-plane normal is not noise-dominated.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts (mine, all under <SP>/verify-F02/): roll.py, impact.py, decomp.py, checks.py, restcheck.py, mm.py, render.py -> render.png, twist.json, impact.json. Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, fitted3d, de-tilted with sequence_up/de_tilt_matrix exactly as view3d and blender_export do; Character.fit_to_subject -> scale 113.62.

TWIST ERROR (angle between the posed bone's carried rest bend-plane normal and the CAPTURED bend-plane normal, about the aim axis), 26 frames, median / p90 / max deg:
  upper_arm.L 53.5 / 68.1 / 72.8   forearm.L 20.4 / 43.5 / 49.0
  upper_arm.R 13.6 / 36.0 / 41.2   forearm.R 14.4 / 27.3 / 30.1
  thigh.L 21.4 / 31.4 / 37.3       shin.L 13.8 / 20.2 / 23.4
  thigh.R 27.1 / 49.4 / 54.0       shin.R 25.4 / 46.5 / 48.3
(finding claimed 53.6/20.4/13.6/14.4/21.2/13.8/26.7/25.4 medians — agrees within 0.5 deg on all eight)
Captured bend angle, median/min deg: L arm 55.3/22.7, R arm 41.0/36.5, L leg 44.7/13.5, R leg 33.2/16.9.
Metric validity: posing the rig with its OWN rest joints (scale neutralised) gives twist error 0.00 deg on all eight bones; incidental finding, the spine still rotates 7.01 deg and the neck 5.75 deg at rest because `spine` is aimed at _MID rather than its own tail.

MESH IMPACT (shipped vs my roll-corrected skinning; my copy of _skin_matrices reproduces the shipped matrices to max abs diff 0.0):
  all vertices, mm on a 117.8 mm subject: frame-median 0.77, frame-p90 3.87, median-of-frame-max 7.81, worst 14.51 (12.3% of subject height)
  by region (mean / worst mm): feet 3.64 / 14.51 (12.3%), hands 1.33 / 7.25 (6.2%), legs 1.90 / 7.64 (6.5%), arms 1.59 / 5.17 (4.4%), torso+head 0.15 / 4.18 (3.5%)
  excluding hands+feet: per-frame max displacement median 3.88% / worst 6.02% of rig height
  canonical joints moved: 0.0000 mm max over all 26 frames
LEAF BONES: skin[leaf] == skin[parent] in 26/26 frames for hand.L/R, foot.L/R. Posed axis tilt off horizontal (rest 28.2 deg for feet, 68.8 for hands): foot.L median 38.5 max 73.5, foot.R 42.7 / 83.9, hand.L 58.4 / 77.8, hand.R 78.3 / 84.8. Max angular deviation from the take mean: foot.L 59.6, foot.R 95.9, hand.L 125.9, hand.R 27.6 deg.
FIX STABILITY: per-frame roll correction, median / max / max consecutive-frame change (deg): upper_arm.L 53.6 / 72.8 / 55.1; forearm.L 20.4 / 49.0 / 19.8; upper_arm.R 13.6 / 41.2 / 13.8; forearm.R 14.4 / 30.2 / 14.7; thigh.L 21.2 / 37.3 / 7.6; shin.L 13.8 / 23.4 / 9.9; thigh.R 26.7 / 54.0 / 15.9; shin.R 25.4 / 48.3 / 19.3. Captured bend-normal consecutive-frame change: L arm median 11.4 / max 55.7, R arm 7.1 / 14.1, L leg 5.8 / 11.2, R leg 7.3 / 20.4 deg.
RIG FACTS: 19 bones, MakeHuman base mesh; rest elbow bend 52.93 deg, rest knee bend 15.83 deg (so the rest bend reference is well conditioned); rig_h 14.42 (bone heads), mesh z-extent 16.65.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: _bone_fk orients a bone with R = _align(rest_dir, target_dir) (character.py:488, _align at 131-144). _align is the MINIMAL rotation, so the component about the bone's own aim axis is never fitted to anything anatomical. Joint POSITIONS are unaffected (the child's head sits on the parent's axis for every chain), so the bend-plane metric (3.6 deg median) cannot see it; the mesh's twist is what goes wrong. Bones with no _DIRECT entry — foot.L/R and hand.L/R (_DIRECT, character.py:69-88) — carry no aim at all and inherit the parent's arbitrary roll wholesale.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: 26 client frames, angle between the posed bone's carried rest bend-reference and the CAPTURED bend-plane normal, median/max deg: upper_arm.L 53.6/72.8, forearm.L 20.4/49.0, upper_arm.R 13.6/41.2, forearm.R 14.4/30.2, thigh.L 21.2/37.3, shin.L 13.8/23.4, thigh.R 26.7/54.0, shin.R 25.4/48.3. Leaves: foot sole tilt off horizontal median 32.1 deg (L) / 30.4 (R), max 74.0 / 108.0; posed foot-axis direction spread over the take 59.6 deg (foot.L), 95.9 (foot.R), hand.L 125.9. Visually confirmed in contact_front.png / contact_side.png (frames 0006/0011/0016/0021: feet as downward spikes, hands splayed, orange captured skeleton correct). Prototype RollCharacter (aim + explicit roll reference) drives all eight roll errors to 0.0 deg median AND max at 1.51% -> 1.62% of height all-joint cost (0.13 mm on a 0.118 m subject).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Replace the single-axis aim with aim + roll: _bone_fk(self, b, base, end, ref_target=None) — after R = _align(rest_dir, aim), rotate about the aim axis so R @ self._rest_ref[b] matches ref_target projected perpendicular to the aim. Build _rest_ref in __init__ from rest_joints() (limb bones cross(upper_rest, lower_rest); hips/spine the rest hip line; chest the rest shoulder line) and supply ref_target in _skin_matrices from the captured pose, skipping the roll when the limb is straighter than ~15 deg and falling back to the parent's roll. _compute_rest_poles/_pole stay for the IK occlusion path. Working prototype: roll_prototype.py (pure subclass).
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/roll_prototype.py, roll_prototype.json, probe2.json, probe5.json, contact_front.png, contact_side.png

## F04 [major] The app never uses the Halpe-26 model, so HEAD is the nose and NECK/PELVIS are 2D midpoints — worth 10-16% of body height at the head

- file: pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:26 | category: detection | sources: detection | auditor severity: critical
- CORRECTED CLAIM: CONFIRMED IN MECHANISM AND MAGNITUDE, with three corrections.

Corrected claim: RTMPoseDetector defaults `feet=False` (rtmpose.py:26) and neither construction site (import_dialog.py:234, main_window.py:260) overrides it, so the app always takes the COCO-17 branch and HEAD is the nose while NECK/PELVIS are per-view 2D midpoints. Switching to Halpe-26 measurably improves the HEAD and NECK only: HEAD retarget error falls 12.65% -> 3.93% of body height with no face keypoints and 9.09% -> 2.92% with them (the current path), NECK 4.84% -> 1.95%, and — the strongest evidence, which the original finding did not have — the neck-head bone-length CV on the rigid mannequin falls 9.53% -> 3.85%, removing the single worst bone in the baseline. Everything else is a wash or marginally worse: the 12 directly-mapped joints move 0.16-0.93% in 2D and their retarget error goes 2.08 mm -> 2.27 mm, and the neck-Lshoulder bone CV regresses 5.15% -> 8.13% because Halpe's native neck is not the shoulder midpoint.

CORRECTION 1 — SEVERITY: major, not critical. This is a localized head/neck fix, not an answer to "the character does not follow the keypoints". The overall retarget median moves only 1.78% -> 1.65% and the twelve body joints are untouched (slightly worse in absolute terms). It is cheap and worth doing; it is not the root cause of a whole-body tracking complaint.

CORRECTION 2 — THE FIX TARGETS THE WRONG LINE FOR THE RETARGET PART. The finding says to "re-check MIXAMO_BONE[Joint.HEAD] (skeleton.py:123)", but MIXAMO_BONE is only the Blender export bone-NAME map; it does not place the head in the 3D view. What actually consumes Joint.HEAD during retargeting is:
  - character.py:110 `_JOINT_PROBE[Joint.HEAD] = (("head", "mid"),)` — the read-back probe (head-bone midpoint, inside the skull);
  - character.py:38 `_NOSE_PITCH = np.radians(45.0)` and character.py:360 `_head_aim_target` — a 45-degree anatomical fudge applied on the explicit assumption that Joint.HEAD is the nose (see the comment at character.py:33-37). With Halpe's head that assumption is FALSE. Empirically it still improves things only because `want = max(rest_pitch + theta - _NOSE_PITCH, 0.0)` saturates at 0 for a skull-axis point; the constant must be made conditional on the source model rather than left as dead-but-lucky code.
  - character.py:75 `"neck": (Joint.NECK, _EAR_MID)` already routes around the nose when face keypoints exist, which is why COCO's HEAD error is 9.09% and not 12.65% on new captures.

CORRECTION 3 — "PELVIS = 2D midpoint" is not an error source. The pure geometric penalty of triangulating a midpoint-of-projections is 0.25 mm at NECK and 0.08 mm at PELVIS (0.22% and 0.07% of body height). The 3.07% PELVIS shift between models is definitional, and PELVIS's retarget error is exactly 0.0 in both configurations because it is the rig root. Drop PELVIS from the impact statement.

ADDITIONS: (a) rtmpose.py's module docstring (lines 3-6) asserts the opposite of the code — "Uses the Halpe-26 model ... falls back to COCO-17 if requested" — a documentation inversion that should be fixed in the same change and probably explains how the default survived. (b) The repo ships no models/ and no pose3d/assets/models/; feet=True resolves here only out of the developer's ~/.cache/rtmlib, so dropping the tools/fetch_weights.py:69 --feet guard is mandatory, not optional, or the client build silently falls through to rtmlib's downloader — the exact failure models.py exists to prevent. Cost is one file, 55.7 MB (YOLOX is shared). (c) Any regression baseline must be re-derived: reconstructed body height grows 0.1187 -> 0.1303 m (+9.8%) and the character scale fit changes 111.11 -> 110.09, so every "% of body height" threshold shifts.
- VERDICT REASON: I re-derived the code path and reproduced the measurement end-to-end; the core claim holds and is stronger than the finding states, but the severity and the proposed fix's target lines are wrong.

CODE PATH (read myself, confirmed on the executed path):
- pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:26 `feet: bool = False`; :28 `self._map = map_halpe26 if feet else derive_joints`.
- pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:234 `RTMPoseDetector(mode="balanced", device="cpu")`, reached from `_process` (import_dialog.py:141) via `det = self._ensure_detector()` (import_dialog.py:196) -> pipeline.detect_project (pipeline.py:32). pose3d/ui/main_window.py:260 identical, reached from `_on_run_detection` -> model.redetect_all (pose3d/ui/model.py:61) -> pipeline.detect_project. Neither passes `feet`. grep over the whole tree: `feet=` appears only in rtmpose.py:35, tools/fetch_weights.py:68/70 and tests/. So the COCO-17 branch is the only one the app can take. Confirmed.
- skeleton.py:101 `_DIRECT_FROM_COCO[Joint.HEAD] = COCO17_INDEX["nose"]`; skeleton.py:172/175 NECK/PELVIS = 2D midpoints. HALPE26_TO_CANONICAL at skeleton.py:182 maps 17/18/19 natively. Confirmed.
- models.resolve('balanced', feet=True) returns a valid Weights on this machine — but ONLY from ~/.cache/rtmlib/hub/checkpoints; the repo has no models/ and no pose3d/assets/models/ directory, so on a shipped bundle the fix genuinely requires dropping the --feet guard at tools/fetch_weights.py:69. Halpe checkpoint is 55,685,444 B = 55.7 MB; YOLOX det is shared, so it is exactly one extra file. Confirmed.
- Bonus defect the finding missed: the rtmpose.py module docstring (lines 3-6) states "Uses the Halpe-26 model (BodyWithFeet) ... Falls back to the COCO-17 Body model if requested" — the exact inverse of the actual default at line 26. That inversion is plausibly why the shipped default went unnoticed.

WHY NOT REFUTED: I tried three refutation routes and all failed.
(a) "It's a harmless definitional relabel, not error." Refuted by a ground-truth-free metric the finding never used: on the rigid mannequin, the neck-head bone-length CV over 26 frames drops 9.53% -> 3.85%. neck-head is the WORST bone in the baseline (max_cv 9.5%) and the nose is the cause.
(b) "The face-keypoint feature already fixes the head, so this only affects legacy projects." Refuted: character.py:75 aims the neck at _EAR_MID and character.py:~630 orients the head bone from the face basis, which cuts COCO's HEAD retarget error 12.65% -> 9.09%, but Halpe still lands at 2.92%. The gap survives the current code path at 3.1x.
(c) "The 2D-midpoint construction is itself a geometric error." Refuted, and this is an overstatement in the finding: triangulating the midpoint-of-projections instead of projecting the 3D midpoint costs 0.25 mm at NECK (0.22% of height) and 0.08 mm at PELVIS (0.07%). The construction is essentially free; the 3-6% displacements are definitional, not geometric.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All numbers mine, 26-frame client take, both pose models on the IDENTICAL YOLOX box, then the identical downstream pipeline. Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F04/{midpoint_bias.py, ab_models.py, full_take.py, downstream.py, withhead.py}.

VALIDATION OF MY OWN RIG: my COCO run reproduces the stored kp2d to median 0.00 px (max 3.26 left / 2.01 right, n=390/388), so my A/B is on the same detector the project was built with.

1) 2D displacement COCO vs Halpe, median over 8 frame/cam samples, % of figure height:
   HEAD 12.12 (range 10.01-17.28), NECK 4.71 (3.42-6.29), PELVIS 3.07 (0.62-3.77).
   All 12 directly-mapped joints: 0.16-0.93 (L_SHOULDER 0.68, L_HIP 0.93, R_WRIST 0.16).
   Halpe native head/neck/pelvis scores 0.67-1.05; COCO-derived NECK 0.76-0.94, PELVIS 0.70-0.84.
   Reproduces the finding's evidence (it reported HEAD 10.01-15.85, NECK 3.42-6.29, direct joints 0.31-0.87).

2) Where Halpe's head actually sits (% figure height): 10.0-17.3 from the nose, 8.0-11.6 from the ear midpoint. It is the skull vertex, NOT the ear midpoint and not the head-bone centre.

3) RETARGET, full 26 frames, character posed exactly as the app does:
   head_pts=None (legacy / this project):  HEAD 12.65% (15.01 mm) COCO -> 3.93% (5.12 mm) Halpe. NECK 4.84% (5.74 mm) -> 1.95% (2.54 mm).
   head_pts=face kps (current path, 100% coverage): HEAD 9.09% (10.79 mm) -> 2.92% (3.80 mm). NECK unchanged (NECK does not consume face kps).
   Overall median 1.78% -> 1.65%; overall p90 5.50% -> 3.54%; bend-plane median 3.12 deg -> 2.91, max 18.72 -> 15.34.
   12 non-HEAD/NECK/PELVIS joints in METERS (scale-free): 2.08 mm -> 2.27 mm, i.e. marginally WORSE (+9%). PELVIS retarget = 0.000 in both (it is the root, placed exactly).

4) BONE-LENGTH INVARIANCE on the rigid mannequin, raw triangulation, CV% over 26 frames (COCO -> Halpe):
   neck-head 9.53 -> 3.85  (the baseline's worst bone; largest single win)
   spine (pelvis-neck) 4.92 -> 4.17
   neck-Lshoulder 5.15 -> 8.13  (REGRESSION: Halpe's native neck is not the shoulder midpoint)
   neck-Rshoulder 5.01 -> 4.52; pelvis-Lhip 7.13 -> 8.46; pelvis-Rhip 7.12 -> 7.35
   median CV 5.28 -> 5.07; max CV 9.53 -> 8.46.
   L/R symmetry asym%: thigh 4.71 -> 4.64, shin 0.84 -> 2.19, upper arm 0.48 -> 0.11, forearm 6.73 -> 6.94 (a wash).

5) Body epipolar (Sampson), full take: median 4.91 -> 4.80 px, p90 12.85 -> 13.09 px, frac over the 71.5 px gate 0.26% -> 0.00%. No regression.

6) SCALE SIDE EFFECT nobody mentioned: reconstructed body height 0.1187 m -> 0.1303 m (+9.8%), because the head point moves from the nose to above the skull. Character scale fit 111.11 -> 110.09. Every "% of body height" figure in the baseline needs rebaselining after this change.

7) Geometric cost of the 2D-midpoint construction itself (detector-free, 26 frames): |triangulate(mid of 2D) - mid of triangulated endpoints| = 0.254 mm median / 0.396 max at NECK (0.22% / 0.34% of height) and 0.078 / 0.108 mm at PELVIS (0.07% / 0.09%). Reprojection of the true 3D midpoint vs the stored 2D midpoint: NECK 2.07/1.20 px, PELVIS 5.02/2.70 px (left/right medians).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: RTMPoseDetector.__init__ defaults `feet: bool = False` (rtmpose.py:26) and both construction sites pass only mode/device — import_dialog.py:234 and main_window.py:260. So the COCO-17 branch is always taken and derive_joints (skeleton.py:140) sets HEAD = nose, NECK = 2D midpoint(shoulders), PELVIS = 2D midpoint(hips). Halpe-26 has NATIVE head (17), neck (18) and mid-hip (19), HALPE26_TO_CANONICAL at skeleton.py:182 already maps them, and models.resolve('balanced', feet=True) resolves on this machine.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Both pose models run on the identical YOLOX box, 3 frames x 2 cameras: the 12 directly-mapped joints move only 0.31-0.87% of figure height (median), but HEAD moves 10.01, 10.98, 11.43, 12.04, 12.21 and 15.85% of figure height and NECK moves 3.42-6.29%. Halpe's native head/neck/pelvis scores are 0.79-1.05. Those magnitudes match the baseline's unexplained 'definitional' retarget offsets (HEAD 12.6-12.9%, NECK 4.1-4.8%). `feet=True` appears nowhere in pose3d/ (only rtmpose.py:35 forwards it) and tools/fetch_weights.py:69 stages the Halpe checkpoint only behind an opt-in --feet flag.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Pass feet=True at import_dialog.py:234 and main_window.py:260 (or flip the default at rtmpose.py:26), and stage the Halpe-26 checkpoint unconditionally (drop the --feet guard at tools/fetch_weights.py:69, +55 MB). derive_joints (skeleton.py:140) then becomes the COCO fallback only. Re-check MIXAMO_BONE[Joint.HEAD] (skeleton.py:123) against the new head definition and re-measure the HEAD retarget error — it should drop from 12.6% into the 1-4% band. Guard with a test that head-to-neck distance is non-degenerate.

## F06 [major] validate_cross_view deletes detections in place and the loss survives save/reload, so one bad calibration permanently damages the project

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:100 | category: robustness | sources: tests-ui | auditor severity: critical
- CORRECTED CLAIM: validate_cross_view is destructive and the loss is irrecoverable by recalibration — CONFIRMED as a mechanism, on the executed path — but F06's headline number ("f=2833 drops 209 observations, 91/390 3D joints permanently missing") is NOT reproducible on the current tree and is off by ~200x. Corrected: (a) MECHANISM, verified in code I read: pipeline.py:100-101 writes `frame.kp2d[cam][j]=np.nan; frame.scores[cam][j]=0.0`; Frame (core/project.py:48-83) has exactly the fields {frame_id, images, kp2d, scores, pose3d, fitted3d, corrected, head2d, head_scores, head3d} — no untouched copy; io_project.py:118-119 persists the mutated arrays and :146-147 reloads them (I dumped the saved JSON's frame keys: same set, no raw key). Executed path: import_dialog.py:213 triangulate_project → :217 save_project; model.py:53 recompute_all re-runs the same gate over the already-degraded 2D, reached from main_window.py:279-280 _on_recalibrate ← the sidebar's "↻ Recalibrate 3D" (panels.py:224-225). The rig is only loaded at project-open (app.py:75 _load_rig), so a fixed calibration is applied by reopening + Recalibrate 3D — which cannot restore anything. NaN'd joints are hidden in the 2D view (camera_view.py:169-171 setVisible(False)), so the user cannot drag them back either. My round-trip test confirms end-to-end: damage → save_project → load_project → triangulate_project with the good shipped rig recovers 0 observations. (b) MAGNITUDE, corrected: with the EXIF focal applied correctly per camera the gate drops 1/780 observations (0.1%), not 209 — one permanently lost joint, not 91. F06's 209 is an artefact of a mis-specified right camera: the right camera in this take is 1536x2048 with f=2048 (calibration/right_intrinsics.json; all right_*.jpg are 1536x2048), NOT 3072x4080/f=4080 as the task's own context states. Feeding the right camera a 3072x4080 K reproduces 184 drops, close to the claimed 209. The other route to that number is historical: commit f4c92f9's "37.9% rejected" was measured under the OLD flat 30 px gate, which the same commit replaced with 1.4% of the image diagonal (71.5 px here); under 30 px my EXIF rig drops 101/780 = 12.9%, under 71.5 px it drops 1. (c) REAL TRIGGER: the gate needs a genuinely broken extrinsic, not a wrong focal. Perturbing the left extrinsic rotation gives 0 drops up to 5 deg on any axis, 12 at 8 deg about the optical axis, 185 (23.7%) at 12 deg, 386 (49.5%) at 20 deg. The live hazard in this scene is the single-tag IPPE planar-pose ambiguity: taking the second solvePnPGeneric branch for tag 14 in the left view (reprojection 4.36 px vs 0.37 px for the winner, 63.7 deg apart) drops 367/780 = 47.1%, permanently. (d) "Only re-running RTMPose restores them" is right, and that recovery is one in-app click ("▷ Run Detection", panels.py:230 → main_window.py:265 → model.py:63 redetect_all → detect_project overwrites kp2d); I verified that overwriting kp2d with the detector output and re-running the good rig returns the project to the clean 2 NaN. So the loss is undiagnosable and unfixable by recalibration, but not unrecoverable from the images. The proposed fix (kp2d_raw/scores_raw persisted + a `rejected` mask re-derived every recompute) is aimed at the actual mechanism and is correct; it should also fix the adjacent defect that epipolar_threshold (pipeline.py:57-60) derives the tolerance from the LEFT camera's image size alone while epipolar_distance (triangulate.py:79-103) returns a Sampson distance mixing a 3072x4080 and a 1536x2048 view, making the 71.5 px gate ~2x too lenient for right-view error. Severity: major, not critical — real, on-path, irrecoverable-by-recalibration data destruction, currently latent on this take (0 drops shipped, 1 drop under the EXIF focal), armed by any >~8 deg extrinsic error, with a slow undocumented one-click recovery.
- VERDICT REASON: The mechanism reproduces exactly as claimed — I re-derived it from the code and demonstrated the full damage → save → reload → recalibrate → 0 recovered round trip myself — but the finding's headline magnitude does not. Importing the client take with the EXIF focal applied correctly (per-camera: left f=2831.5 on 3072x4080, right f=1421.3 on 1536x2048, extrinsics re-solved by resolve_calibration as the import dialog does) drops 1 of 780 observations, not 209, and loses 1 extra 3D joint, not 91. The claimed number is reproducible only if the right camera is given the LEFT sensor's 3072x4080 K (184 drops) — the right camera is actually 1536x2048 with f=2048 — or under the pre-f4c92f9 flat 30 px gate (101 drops, 12.9%), which the current tree replaced with 71.5 px. So the specific reproducer the finding cites is defused on HEAD. The defect is nonetheless real and latent: it takes a >~8 deg extrinsic error to bite, and the single-tag IPPE ambiguity in this exact scene supplies one — picking the runner-up PnP branch for tag 14 destroys 47.1% of all observations irreversibly. Recovery exists but only via re-detection ("Run Detection"), never via Recalibrate 3D, and the NaN'd joints are hidden in the 2D view so the user cannot drag them back or tell the loss from detector failure. Right in spirit, wrong in its key number and in "critical".
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: <SP>/verify-F06/{f06.py,f06b.py,f06c.py,f06d.py}, results f06c.json/f06d.json. Take = workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, 780 2D obs, 390 3D joints; stored 2D already has 2 NaN obs.
CONTROL (validates my re-solve reproduces the shipped import): resolve_calibration with f_left=4080 (3072x4080) and f_right=2048 (1536x2048) reproduces the shipped extrinsics exactly — dR_left vs shipped 0.00 deg, epipolar median 4.91 px / max 29.38 / 0 over 71.5 px, 0 dropped, identical to the shipped rig.
IMAGE SIZES (contradicts the task context): left_*.jpg 3072x4080 (K f=4080, cx=1536, cy=2040); right_*.jpg 1536x2048 (K f=2048, cx=768, cy=1024). Verified with PIL over the files and by reading calibration/{left,right}_intrinsics.json.
FOCAL SWEEP (extrinsics re-solved by resolve_calibration at each K, i.e. a real re-import); scale x (4080,2048) -> dR_left vs shipped / epipolar median / dropped of 780:
 1.000 (4080,2048)   0.00 deg /  4.91 px /   0 (0.0%)

F06's reproducer, redone with the right camera wrongly given a 3072x4080 K at f=2833: 184 dropped (vs the claimed 209).
OLD vs NEW THRESHOLD on the same EXIF rig: 30.0 px (pre-f4c92f9 flat gate) -> 101 dropped = 12.9%; 71.5 px (today, 1.4% of the left diagonal) -> 1. Shipped rig: 0 under both.
IRRECOVERABILITY ROUND-TRIP (EXIF rig): triangulate_project -> 1 dropped, 3 NaN 2D obs, 3 NaN 3D joints (frames 0004, 0012, 0021; clean run has 2, frames 0012+0021) -> save_project to a scratch folder -> load_project -> still 3 NaN 2D / 3 NaN 3D -> triangulate_project with the good shipped rig -> dropped_more=0, recovered=0, still 3 NaN 2D and 3 NaN 3D. Overwriting kp2d/scores with the detector output first -> 0 dropped, back to 2 NaN 2D / 2 NaN 3D. Saved project.json frame keys are exactly [corrected, fitted3d, frame_id, head2d, head3d, head_scores, images, kp2d, pose3d, scores] — no raw copy.
LEFT-EXTRINSIC ROTATION PERTURBATION -> dropped of 780 (x / y / z axis): 0.5 deg 0/0/0; 1 deg 0/0/0; 2 deg 0/0/0; 3 deg 0/0/0; 5 deg 0/0/0; 8 deg 1/0/12; 12 deg 15/0/185; 20 deg 84/0/386.
IPPE AMBIGUITY, tag 14, left view, frame 0001: cv2.solvePnPGeneric returns 2 solutions with reprojection errors 0.366 px and 4.357 px (ratio 11.9); the runner-up branch is 63.69 deg from the shipped rotation and, if picked, drops 367/780 = 47.1% of all observations, permanently.
CODE READ MYSELF: pipeline.py:63-103 (gate), :100-101 (the NaN/0.0 writes), :57-60 (threshold from LEFT intrinsics only), :126-149 (triangulate_project, validate=True default); core/project.py:48-83 (Frame fields, no raw); io_project.py:102-130 save, :133-161 load; ui/import_dialog.py:200-217; ui/model.py:46-58 recompute_all, :63-70 redetect_all; ui/main_window.py:265-283; ui/panels.py:224-230; ui/camera_view.py:167-172; app.py:68-95 (_load_rig only at open); geometry/triangulate.py:79-103.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: validate_cross_view writes NaN into frame.kp2d[cam][j] and 0.0 into frame.scores[cam][j]. No untouched copy of the detector output is kept; io_project.save_project (io_project.py:118) persists the mutated arrays, and ProjectModel.recompute_all (model.py:53) re-runs the same gate over the already-degraded 2D. Observations rejected under a wrong rig can never be recovered by fixing the calibration and pressing Recalibrate 3D.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Simulated on the client take: importing with f=2833 (the EXIF focal commit f4c92f9 records as having been tried) drops 209 observations, leaving 211 NaN 2D observations. Re-running triangulate_project with the good shipped rig on that project recovers 0 of them: still 211 NaN, versus 2 NaN if the good rig had been used from the start. 91 of 390 3D joints are permanently missing (0 in the clean run). Only re-running RTMPose restores them. Note the gate is inert on the shipped project as it stands (0/388 pairs over the 71.5 px threshold, re-running drops 0 more), so this is a demonstrated mechanism, not a claim that this take is already damaged.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Make the gate non-destructive. Add Frame.kp2d_raw / Frame.scores_raw (persisted by io_project) written once by detect_project, and have validate_cross_view set a Frame.rejected[cam] boolean mask instead of NaN-ing kp2d. triangulate_points takes the mask; the camera views draw rejected joints in a distinct colour. recompute_all re-derives the mask from kp2d_raw every time so fixing the calibration genuinely re-instates observations. Frame.corrected overrides the mask exactly as today.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-tests-ui/ui_signals.json (destructive_gate)

## F07 [major] The Pose Accuracy gauge measures the RAW triangulation, not the pose that is displayed and exported, and is structurally blind to a wrong rig

- file: pose3d/ui/model.py:187 | category: ui-feedback | sources: pipeline, tests-ui | auditor severity: critical
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `ProjectModel._accuracy` (pose3d/ui/model.py:180-191) reports the reprojection error of `f.pose3d` — the raw two-view DLT — while everything the user sees and exports is `f.fitted3d` (model.py:90, model.py:170, main_window.py:450, main_window.py:355). It is the product's only data-driven accuracy measurement (`reprojection_error` has exactly one caller, model.py:186; the sidebar's CALIBRATION text from `calib/quality.py:52` is a static config check that never reads the take), and it feeds both the Pose Accuracy gauge (panels.py:85-125 via main_window.py:234-236) and every timeline chip (main_window.py:479-485).

Three independent consequences, all measured on the client take:
1. WRONG POSE. The gauge reads 52.32 % median from `pose3d` where the displayed/exported `fitted3d` would read 6.24 % (per-joint median 3.96 px vs 19.70 px; per-camera 5.11/2.78 px vs 21.65/17.19 px). The 6 mm mean / 26 mm max that the EMA smoother adds to the displayed pose is invisible to the gauge by construction, since `fit_project` (pipeline.py:184-185) writes only `fitted3d`.
2. GROSSLY UNDER-SENSITIVE TO A WRONG RIG (not literally blind). Rotating the right camera's R and re-triangulating: 15 deg moves every joint 19.13 mm median (16.2 % of the 0.118 m body height) for a 52.32 -> 49.44 % gauge drop; 30 deg moves 35.29 mm for 43.39 %. The band stays "Low" throughout. A DLT point does reproject near its own observations largely by construction, so the metric degrades ~5x slower than the pose error.
3. THE SCALE IS ALSO WRONG, which the finding missed. `accuracy_pct = 100*exp(-err_px/6)` (panels.py:33-37) is absolute pixels with no resolution or scene-scale term. On data/demo_project with GROUND-TRUTH Panoptic calibration the same gauge reads 46.37 % and bands "Low" — worse than the client's suspect rig at 52.32 %. Zero of 26 client frames and zero of 10 Panoptic frames ever reach green. So the readout is uninformative in both directions: a correct rig looks broken, and a 30-deg-broken rig looks the same as a correct one.

CORRECTIONS TO THE PROPOSED FIX. (a) as literally stated — swap `f.pose3d` for `f.fitted3d` at model.py:187 — pins the gauge at 6.24 % median (range 0-50, 25/26 frames red) because the exp(-err/6) mapping is not rescaled; the useful form is emitting BOTH and showing the difference, plus recalibrating the percentage against the image diagonal (or dropping the percentage and showing px). (b) is directionally right but weaker than claimed: bone-length CV is ~7x more responsive than the gauge to an extrinsics error (5.27 -> 7.42 % at 15 deg, -> 10.91 % at 30 deg, vs the gauge's -5.5 %/-17 % relative) yet is BLIND to a shared focal error (f x0.7 lowers median CV to 5.09 %), where epipolar does respond (4.91 -> 6.23 px, and 14.13 px at 2x). Bone CV and epipolar must therefore be shown together, and both are gradual slides rather than alarms — the readout needs an explicit "a rigid subject should give ~0 %" reference to be actionable.
- VERDICT REASON: I re-derived every code claim and reproduced every measurement with my own script; nothing refutes F07, and one extra defect makes it worse.

CODE PATH (read myself):
- pose3d/ui/model.py:180-191 `_accuracy(idx)`: `f = self.project.frames[idx]`; line 186-187 `reprojection_error(f.pose3d, f.kp2d[cam], ...)` for each of CAMERAS, then `np.nanmean` over the two cameras. It is `f.pose3d` — the raw DLT — verbatim.
- What the user sees is `f.fitted3d`: model.py:90 (`set_frame` -> `pose3dChanged.emit(f.fitted3d, f.head3d)`), model.py:170 (drag path), main_window.py:450 (`_on_frame_changed` -> `self.view3d.set_pose(f.fitted3d, f.head3d)`). Export: main_window.py:355 `poses = np.stack([f.fitted3d for f in frames])`.
- The gauge is live and on the executed path: model.py:91/135/171 emit `accuracyChanged`; main_window.py:203 wires it to `_on_accuracy` (main_window.py:234-240), which feeds `JointAccuracyList.update_errors` (panels.py:163-176, returns the mean of the per-joint percentages) -> `PoseAccuracyPanel.set_overall` -> the circular gauge (panels.py:85-125). The same `model._accuracy(i)` also drives every timeline chip (main_window.py:479-485, thresholds 5/12 px on `nanmax`), refreshed at main_window.py:277, 282, 523.
- `grep -rn "reprojection_error"` over pose3d/ returns exactly two hits: the definition (triangulate.py:106) and model.py:186. So this really is the product's only data-driven accuracy measurement. (Small correction to the finding's wording: the sidebar does have a CALIBRATION readout, panels.py:204-212 fed by `calib/quality.py:52 check_rig`, but that is a static config check — "intrinsics were assumed", "nominal up is N deg off" — it never looks at the take's data.)
- Smoother invariance is structural, not just empirical: `pipeline.fit_project` (pipeline.py:152-185) writes only `f.fitted3d` (line 184-185) and never touches `f.pose3d`, so `_accuracy` is bit-identical with `smooth=True/False` by construction.
- One nuance the finding does not mention, in its favour rather than against: the gauge is NOT dead on the drag path — model.py:155 writes the re-triangulated `f.pose3d[joint]` before model.py:171 recomputes it. So it is a working "are my two 2D annotations cross-view consistent" readout; it simply does not describe the pose that is drawn or exported.

MY MEASUREMENTS (script: /tmp/.../verify-F07/gauge.py, fixcheck.py, panoptic.py; I re-implemented `accuracy_pct` and `_accuracy` from panels.py:33-37 and model.py:180-191 rather than importing them, and re-triangulated from the stored kp2d after each rig perturbation, i.e. what `recompute_all` does):
- Shipped gauge (from pose3d): median 52.32 % over the 26 frames; per-joint median error 3.964 px; timeline 0 green / 11 amber / 15 red.
- Same gauge if it read fitted3d: median 6.24 % (mean 9.60, max 50.44); per-joint median 19.70 px; timeline 0/1/25. Per-joint error ratio 4.97x; per-camera medians raw 5.107/2.783 px vs fitted 21.647/17.185 px = 4.24x / 6.17x.
- Smoother: `refit(smooth=False)` moves the displayed pose 6.071 mm mean / 25.652 mm max, and the shipped gauge cannot move at all (see fit_project above).
- Wrong-rig sweep (right camera R rotated about its own y, then everything re-triangulated): 2 deg -> joints move 2.88 mm median, gauge 52.10 %; 5 deg -> 6.93 mm, 51.37 %; 15 deg -> 19.13 mm (16.2 % of the 0.118 m height), gauge 49.44 %, timeline 0/9/17; 30 deg -> 35.29 mm, 43.39 %. A 15 deg extrinsic error costs 2.9 percentage points and never leaves the "Low" band.
Every number in the finding's evidence line reproduced to the digit (19.13/19.128 mm, 52.3->49.4, 11/15 -> 9/17, 6.93 mm, 52.3->51.4, 6.07 mm, 5.11/2.78 vs 21.65/17.18, 52.3 vs 6.2 %, 3.96 vs 19.70 px).

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATIONS CHECKED, none holds: (a) the stored project is not stale — re-triangulating the stored kp2d reproduces pose3d and re-fitting reproduces fitted3d; (b) the fitted-vs-raw reprojection gap is not an artefact of my nanmean — per-camera medians agree with the baseline; (c) the gauge's insensitivity is not because my perturbation was too small — 35 mm of joint displacement on a 118 mm subject still reads 43 % "Low", one band away from where a correct rig reads.

EXTRA DEFECT I FOUND, which strengthens the finding: `accuracy_pct` (panels.py:33-37) is `100*exp(-err_px/6)` — an absolute-pixel scale with no resolution or scene-scale awareness. On data/demo_project, i.e. Panoptic with GROUND-TRUTH calibration, the same gauge reads 46.37 % and bands "Low". So a perfect rig scores WORSE than the client's suspect rig (52.32 %), and 0 of the client's 26 frames ever reach green. The user cannot distinguish good from broken in either direction.

IS THE FIX AIMED AT THE MECHANISM? Partly.
- Fix (a) taken literally (swap `f.pose3d` -> `f.fitted3d`) pins the gauge at 6.24 % median, range 0-50 %, 25/26 frames red — a number just as uninformative, because the exp(-err/6) mapping was never rescaled. The "emit both / show the difference" variant is the one that works, and the mapping must be recalibrated (per-image-diagonal, or a fixed px readout) regardless.
- Fix (b) is directionally right but weaker than implied. Under my sweeps: bone-length CV median goes 5.27 % (correct rig) -> 7.42 % (15 deg) -> 10.91 % (30 deg), i.e. +41 %/+107 % relative where the gauge moves -5.5 %/-17 % — roughly 7x more sensitive at 15 deg, but still a graceful slide, not an alarm. Epipolar median goes 4.91 -> 5.33 -> 7.71 px. Crucially, bone CV is BLIND to a shared focal error (f x0.7 LOWERS median CV to 5.09 %) while epipolar does move there (4.91 -> 6.23 px at 0.7x, 14.13 px at 2.0x), so the two must be shown together; neither alone covers both failure modes.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F07/{gauge.py,gauge.json,fixcheck.py,fixcheck.json,panoptic.py}. Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames.

CODE FACTS I VERIFIED MYSELF: model.py:186-187 uses f.pose3d; model.py:90 and :170 and main_window.py:450 emit/consume f.fitted3d; main_window.py:355 exports f.fitted3d; main_window.py:479-485 bands the timeline from the same model._accuracy; pipeline.py:184-185 writes only f.fitted3d; panels.py:33-37 accuracy_pct = 100*exp(-err_px/6); panels.py:40-48 bands at 85/70; `grep -rn reprojection_error pose3d/` = triangulate.py:106 (def) + model.py:186 (only caller).

Reprojection of a two-view DLT point is near its own observations by construction whatever (K,R,t) are, so the number cannot detect a calibration error either.

As percentages: gauge median 52.3% from pose3d vs 6.2% if it used fitted3d (per-joint median 3.96 px vs 19.70 px). Rotating the right camera's R by 15 deg moves every triangulated joint 19.13 mm median (16.2% of the 0.118 m height) yet the gauge only falls 52.3 -> 49.4% and the timeline goes 11 amber/15 red -> 9/17; 5 deg (6.93 mm) moves it 52.3 -> 51.4%. Turning the EMA smoother off changes the displayed pose by 6.07 mm mean but the gauge is bit-identical (52.3% both ways).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): (a) model.py:187 — measure the pose that is displayed: reprojection_error(f.fitted3d, ...); better, emit both and show two bars ('measured' vs 'after fit'), since their DIFFERENCE is the actionable signal. (b) Add a rig-quality readout reprojection cannot fake: per-joint Sampson epipolar residual from triangulate.epipolar_distance, per-bone length CV across the take from bonefit.measure_bone_lengths (a rigid subject must give ~0), and L/R limb symmetry %. Emit them from a new pipeline.take_quality(project, rig) -> dict called by recompute_all and show them in the CALIBRATION section of panels.Sidebar; the per-joint reprojection gauge becomes a secondary '2D fit' number.

## F10 [major] A manual 2D drag replaces one smoothed frame with an unsmoothed single-frame refit: a ZERO-pixel drag snaps every joint by up to 25.7 mm (21.8% of height)

- file: pose3d/ui/model.py:160 | category: live-edit | sources: pipeline, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: A committed 2D drag replaces the edited frame's smoothed pose with an unsmoothed single-frame fit, so the whole skeleton jumps before the edit contributes anything — median 4.9 mm (4.2% of body height), up to 25.7 mm (21.8%), with all 15 joints moving on a median drag.

`ProjectModel._resolve_joint` writes `f.fitted3d = fit_bone_lengths(f.pose3d, self._bone_lengths, fill_missing=False)` (pose3d/ui/model.py:159-161) — one frame, no temporal smoothing — into a project whose frames were all produced by `fit_project(..., smooth=True)` (import_dialog.py:214, model.py:55; the EMA lives at pipeline.py:181 / bonefit.py:129). I proved the mechanism is exactly and only the lost EMA: the post-drag frame is bit-identical (0.000 mm, all 26 frames) to `fit_project(smooth=False)` on the same 2D, while `f.pose3d` is unchanged (0.000 mm).

Magnitude on the client take, over all 388 valid (frame, joint) zero-pixel drags: dragged joint moves median 4.92 mm / p90 12.77 / max 25.65 mm (4.18% / 10.84% / 21.78% of the 117.8 mm subject); the worst joint in the frame moves median 14.84 mm (12.6% of height); a median of 15 of 15 joints move >1 mm. Frame 0014, LEFT_WRIST at 0 px: dragged joint 21.02 mm, untouched RIGHT_ANKLE 25.65 mm. This dwarfs the correction itself — a real 1 px drag moves the 3D joint 0.139 mm, 20 px moves it 2.78 mm, so the artifact exceeds the intent for any drag under ~35 px.

The edited frame becomes a sequence outlier: editing 0014 grows the 0013->0014 mean per-joint step from 17.83 to 29.71 mm (1.67x) against a median step of 8.44 mm; editing 0020 grows both neighbouring steps (23.37->38.94 and 10.13->22.46 mm). (The original finding's claim that the following step is unchanged does not hold — the neighbour step changes too.) The result is rendered (main_window.py:450), exported (main_window.py:355) and saved (io_project.py:121).

Corrections to the original finding:
- Not "the instant the user touches any joint": camera_view.py:82-84 commits only on mouse-up and only after >0.5 scene units of motion, so a click is inert — but 0.5 units is 0.5 image px on a 3072x4080 photo, so any real drag qualifies.
- Frame index 0 (frame_id 0001) is exempt: the causal EMA leaves t=0 untouched, measured jump 0.00 mm.
- The stale `_bone_lengths` cache (never refreshed except at model.py:54) is a real code smell but not a measurable contributor: `measure_bone_lengths` is a median over frames (bonefit.py:22-31), so one 100 px correction changes every bone target by 0.000 mm and the refit by 0.000 mm; even 26 corrections of 150 px shift one target 12.87 mm for a 0.04 mm median refit change. Treat it as latent, not as part of this defect.
- Aggravating, previously missed: undo/redo also call `_resolve_joint` (model.py:104-115), so Ctrl+Z does not restore the smoothed pose — an undone drag still leaves the frame in the unsmoothed family.
- Mitigating, previously missed: `recompute_all` (model.py:46-60, sidebar Recalculate, main_window.py:280) restores the smoothed pose exactly (0.000 mm from stored) while keeping the correction. The damage is one button press away, but nothing triggers it automatically, including save and export.
- Framing: the jump moves the edited frame TOWARD the 2D measurement (nosmooth reprojection 7.38/4.01 px vs the shipped fitted 21.65/17.18 px). The bug is the inconsistency and the visible discontinuity, not that the edited frame is less accurate than its neighbours.

The proposed fix addresses the right mechanism. One caveat to state in the plan: re-running `fit_project(smooth=True)` after an edit necessarily also moves every LATER frame, since the EMA is causal — correct behaviour, but one drag will visibly restate the tail of the take.
- VERDICT REASON: Core claim independently reproduced, both in code and in numbers; three secondary details are wrong or overstated and one aggravating detail was missed.

CODE (read myself): pose3d/ui/model.py:159-161 `f.fitted3d = fit_bone_lengths(f.pose3d, self._bone_lengths, fill_missing=False)` — single frame, no `smooth_temporal`. The batch paths do smooth: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 `fit_project(project, smooth=True)` and pose3d/ui/model.py:55 `fit_project(self.project, smooth=True)`; pose3d/pipeline.py:181-182 applies `smooth_temporal(fitted, alpha=alpha)` (causal EMA, pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:129-145). `_bone_lengths` is initialised None at model.py:43, lazily filled at model.py:157-158/173-178, and reset ONLY inside `recompute_all` (model.py:54).

EXECUTED PATH (traced): camera_view.py:79-84 `JointItem.mouseReleaseEvent` -> `signals.released` -> camera_view.py:225-227 `jointDragged` -> main_window.py:230-231 `_on_drag` -> `model.set_joint_2d` (model.py:97-103) -> `_resolve_joint` (auto_recalc defaults True, model.py:41). Result is consumed everywhere: `pose3dChanged` -> view3d (main_window.py:201), timeline/character orientation (main_window.py:450, 496-497), export (main_window.py:355 `poses = np.stack([f.fitted3d for f in frames])`), and is written to disk (pose3d/core/io_project.py:121). So the effect is on the live path, is rendered, is exported, and persists.

MECHANISM PROVEN EXACTLY: my post-zero-drag frame is BIT-IDENTICAL to `fit_project(smooth=False)` on the same 2D (max abs diff 0.0 mm over all 26 frames), and `f.pose3d` changes by exactly 0.0 mm. So 100% of the jump is the lost EMA — not the bone-length cache, not least-squares nondeterminism, not the re-triangulation.

WRONG/OVERSTATED DETAILS:
(1) "the instant the user touches any joint" — no. camera_view.py:82-84 commits only on mouse-up and only if the item moved >0.5 scene units, so a plain click does nothing. (It is still effectively any drag: 0.5 units = 0.5 image px on a 3072x4080 image.)
(2) Frame index 0 (frame_id 0001) is exempt — the causal EMA leaves t=0 unchanged; I measure a 0.00 mm jump there. 1 of 26 frames.
(3) The stale-`_bone_lengths` sub-claim is true in code but quantitatively near-nil: `measure_bone_lengths` is a MEDIAN over frames (bonefit.py:22-31) and is therefore robust. After one 100 px correction every bone target changes by 0.000 mm and the refit is bit-identical (0.0 mm). Only after 26 x 150 px corrections (every frame, same joint) does one target move 12.87 mm, changing the refit by 0.04 mm median / 8.09 mm max. Downgrade to latent, not an active contributor.
(4) The neighbour-discontinuity numbers do not reproduce as stated. The claimed "step to 0015 unchanged (6.97 -> 6.93)" is false on my metric: editing 0014 changes the 0014->0015 step 10.13 -> 7.49 mm (-26%), and editing 0020 grows BOTH neighbours (23.37 -> 38.94 and 10.13 -> 22.46). The 1.67x growth of the preceding step does reproduce exactly.

MISSED, AGGRAVATING: undo/redo (model.py:104-115) also route through `_resolve_joint`, so Ctrl+Z does NOT restore the smoothed pose — an undone drag leaves the frame permanently in the unsmoothed family.

MISSED, MITIGATING: `recompute_all` (model.py:46-60, wired at main_window.py:280 to the sidebar Recalculate) re-triangulates and re-runs `fit_project(smooth=True)`; I confirmed it restores the stored pose exactly (max diff 0.0 mm) while keeping the correction. So there is a one-click recovery — just not automatic, and not on save/export.

FRAMING NUANCE the finding glosses: the jump moves the edited frame TOWARD the 2D measurement, not away (baseline: nosmooth reprojection 7.38/4.01 px vs shipped fitted 21.65/17.18 px). The defect is the inconsistency/discontinuity, not that the edited frame is less accurate.

The proposed fix targets the real mechanism. Caveat for the fix: re-running `fit_project(smooth=True)` after an edit also shifts every LATER frame (the EMA is causal) — correct, but it means one drag visibly restates the tail of the take.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F10/f10.py, f10b.py (outputs f10.json, f10b.json). Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, height reference H = 117.8 mm.

A. ZERO-PIXEL DRAG, exhaustive: 388 drags (every frame x every joint valid in both views), each through a Qt-free transcription of `_resolve_joint`. `f.pose3d` change = 0.000 mm in all 388 (the drag itself does nothing). `f.fitted3d` change:
 - dragged joint: median 4.92 mm (4.18% H), p90 12.77 mm (10.84%), max 25.65 mm (21.78%)
 - worst joint in the frame: median 14.84 mm (12.60% H), p90 24.92, max 25.65 mm (21.78%)
 - median joint in the frame: median 4.57 mm, max 14.30 mm
 - joints moving >1 mm: median 15 of 15; min 0 (frame 0001 only), max 15.

B. MECHANISM: post-drag frame vs `fit_project(smooth=False)` on the same 2D = 0.000 mm median AND max (bit-identical). Stored fitted3d vs `fit_project(smooth=True)` = 0.000 mm. Stored vs nosmooth = 4.92 mm median / 25.65 max — identical distribution to the drag jump. The jump IS the EMA, exactly.

C. PER-FRAME (drag LEFT_WRIST 0 px), mm [dragged / worst / worst-joint]: 0001 0.00/0.00; 0008 15.65/15.65 LEFT_WRIST; 0013 25.14/25.14 LEFT_WRIST; 0014 21.02/25.65 RIGHT_ANKLE; 0020 14.15/24.92 RIGHT_ANKLE (median joint 14.30); 0026 2.02/3.15. Frames 0014 and 0020 reproduce the finding's quoted numbers to 0.01 mm.

D. SEQUENCE DISCONTINUITY (mean per-joint inter-frame step of the stored take; baseline median step 8.44 mm): editing 0014 -> step 0013->0014 17.83 -> 29.71 mm (1.67x), step 0014->0015 10.13 -> 7.49 mm (-26%). Editing 0020 -> 23.37 -> 38.94 mm (1.67x) and 10.13 -> 22.46 mm (+122%).

E. SIGNAL vs ARTIFACT: a genuine n-px drag of LEFT_WRIST in the left view moves the triangulated 3D joint by median 0.139 mm (1 px), 0.697 mm (5 px), 2.78 mm (20 px), 13.78 mm (100 px). The un-smoothing jump (4.92 mm median, 25.65 max) therefore exceeds the intended effect of any correction smaller than ~35 px, and a 1 px nudge is swamped 35:1.

F. STALE BONE LENGTHS: one 100 px correction -> all 14 bone targets change 0.000 mm, refit delta 0.000 mm (median and max). 26 x 150 px corrections -> one target (LEFT_SHOULDER->LEFT_ELBOW) moves 12.87 mm, all others 0.000; refit delta 0.043 mm median / 8.09 mm max.

G. RECOVERY: after the drag, `triangulate_project` + `fit_project(smooth=True)` returns frame 0014 to the stored pose within 0.000 mm (vs 25.65 mm immediately after the drag), with the correction retained.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: ProjectModel._resolve_joint writes `f.fitted3d = fit_bone_lengths(f.pose3d, self._bone_lengths, fill_missing=False)` — a single-frame fit with NO temporal smoothing — into a project whose other 25 frames were built by fit_project(..., smooth=True) (model.py:55 / pipeline.py:183). The edited frame jumps to a different pose family the instant the user touches any joint, before their edit has any effect, and the whole skeleton moves, not the dragged joint. Separately self._bone_lengths is computed once (model.py:157-158, 173-178) from the pose3d at the time of the first drag and never refreshed, so late corrections are fitted to stale targets. The difference is saved to disk and persists on reload.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Qt-free reimplementation of _resolve_joint (probe2.py:resolve_joint) with a 0-pixel drag: stored fitted3d moves by median 4.92 mm (4.18% of height), p90 12.77 (10.84%), max 25.65 mm (21.78%); a median of 15 of 15 joints move >1 mm per drag. Frame 0014, dragging LEFT_WRIST by 0 px: dragged joint moves 21.02 mm, untouched RIGHT_ANKLE 25.65 mm. Frame 0020: dragged joint 14.15 mm, other joints median 14.40, max 24.92. The edited frame becomes a sequence outlier: the step from 0013 to 0014 grows 13.62 -> 22.70 mm (1.67x) while the step to 0015 is unchanged (6.97 -> 6.93 mm).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Make the edit path and the batch path produce the same pose. (a) Adopt F01 (smooth off by default) so there is no discrepancy at all; and (b) have _resolve_joint call a new pipeline.refit_frame(project, idx, bone_lengths, alpha) that re-fits frame idx and re-applies smooth_temporal over the affected window (t..end, the EMA being causal) rather than writing a raw single-frame fit. Cheapest correct version: call pipeline.fit_project(self.project, self._bone_lengths, smooth=self.project.smooth) and re-emit — 21.5 ms/frame x 26 = 0.56 s, fine for a drag-release. Refresh _bone_lengths whenever a correction lands instead of caching for the session. Test: fitted3d after a single drag equals fit_project re-run over the whole take on the same 2D, to within the drag's own effect. Redundant afterwards: the bespoke fit_bone_lengths call at model.py:160 and its except-branch.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/probe2.py, probe2.json (B1_zero_px_drag, B2_neighbour_discontinuity_mm), probe.json (H6_zero_px_drag_jump); <SP>/find-tests-ui/ui_signals.json (edit_vs_recompute)

## F13 [major] The Blender export has exactly zero root translation while the subject's pelvis travels 116% of its own body height; the view discards the same motion by a different rule

- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:574 | category: export | sources: retarget | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Both Blender export paths pin the character's root at a constant rig position, so the exported BVH/FBX has exactly zero root translation while the subject's pelvis genuinely travels 0.136 m = 116% of its own 0.118 m body height; the 3D view discards the same translation by a different, per-frame rule, and the resulting preview-vs-export placement gap is up to 25.5% of body height vertically.

Mechanism (verified): `to_rig` (character.py:573-574) is `self.hips_world + (Rz @ (p - pelvis)) * scale` with `hips_world` a load-time constant (character.py:190), so `hips_pos = to_rig(pelvis)` (character.py:613) is the same point every frame; character.py:621 pins the hips bone head there, and `pose_bone_matrices` (character.py:706-717) returns those rig-space matrices without the `_from_rig` undo that `pose_and_joints` applies (character.py:692-694). `blender_job._drive_character_bones_fk` sets `pb.matrix = arm_inv @ Matrix(M)` for every bone including hips (blender_job.py:468). Measured: the hips matrix translation is [0.0, -0.14449994, 0.72685003] in all 26 frames, range [0,0,0].

The FALLBACK export path is equally dead-rooted (the finding omits this): blender_job.py:493 takes `hips_world` from the rest armature, :524 maps every joint as `hips_world + (Rz @ (p - pelvis)) * scale`, and :535 COPY_LOCATION-constrains the hips bone to the resulting always-constant PELVIS empty. No export path in the repo carries root motion.

The discarded motion is real, not drift: the de-tilted pelvis moves monotonically +129.6 mm in x over the 26 frames (median step 6.35 mm), bbox diagonal 0.1362 m, max pairwise 0.1331 m, against a median per-frame body height of 0.1178 m; the same displacement is present in the raw triangulation (raw pelvis max pairwise 0.1357 m), so neither the bone fit nor the EMA smoother creates it.

The view discards it differently: view3d.py:145-146 subtracts the mean of the VALID joints in xy and the min valid z, then :157-159 re-grounds on the posed mesh's lowest vertex. Two corrections to the original evidence: (1) the centroid-vs-pelvis term is NOT 0.0 m — fitted3d is complete in all 26 frames, but mean-of-joints minus pelvis still varies by up to 6.8 mm = 5.8% of body height (0.78 rig units); (2) the larger disagreement is vertical — the export never grounds, and the posed mesh's lowest vertex in rig space swings 3.67 rig units = 25.5% of rig height across the take, whereas the view holds it at 0.

Confirmed as stated: orientation and scale DO agree between view and export (main_window.py:494-504 + view3d.py:106 vs blender_export.py:59-61 + :76; measured scale 113.61997971458045 on both) — only the root translation differs.

Impact, scoped: downstream retargeting of the BVH/FBX gets a character that animates its limbs in place while the real subject slid more than its own height, and the preview's framing is not what the exported clip shows. It does NOT explain the client's "the character does not follow the keypoints" complaint — within-frame bone orientations and the view's character/capture overlay are unaffected.

Fix: the mechanism the proposed fix targets is correct, and offsetting only the hips matrix suffices for `pose_bone_matrices` because children read `base = skin[p]` (character.py:639) and inherit the shift. It must additionally (a) apply the same offset in the fallback path at blender_job.py:524, (b) carry the same `pelvis_ref` into `_from_rig` (character.py:674) so `posed_joints` stays in the capture's space, and (c) not simply delete view3d.py:145-146 — line 146 also seats z, and a take-fixed centre would let the character walk out of the auto-framed camera.
- VERDICT REASON: I re-derived the mechanism from the code myself and reproduced the core measurement with my own script; the claim survives, with two detail corrections and one significant understatement.

CODE PATH (all lines read by me):
- character.py:573-574 `def to_rig(p): return self.hips_world + (Rz @ (p - pelvis)) * scale`. `self.hips_world = self.head[self.hips_idx]` (character.py:190) is a load-time constant, so to_rig(pelvis) is identically hips_world for every frame.
- character.py:613 `hips_pos = to_rig(pelvis)`; :620-621 `skin[hips_idx][:3,:3] = R_hips; skin[hips_idx][:3,3] = hips_pos - R_hips @ hips_head`. The hips bone HEAD therefore lands at hips_world exactly, every frame.
- character.py:706-717 `pose_bone_matrices` returns `skin[b] @ rest[b]` in RIG space, i.e. no `_from_rig` (character.py:674) undo — unlike `pose_and_joints` (character.py:692-694), which is what the view consumes.
- blender_job.py:468 `pb.matrix = arm_inv @ Matrix(M)` for every bone in `_drive_character_bones_fk` (defined :422), reached from blender_job.py:616-620.

EXECUTED PATH CONFIRMED: main_window.py:355 `poses = np.stack([f.fitted3d for f in frames])` -> `_ExportWorker` (main_window.py:19,368) -> blender_export.export_animation -> :132 `_character_bone_frames` -> :87 `ch.pose_bone_matrices(...)` -> :135 `doc["bone_frames"]` -> blender_job.py:616-620. This is the only export the "Export Results" button (main_window.py:219,316) can reach.

WHAT THE FINDING MISSES / GETS WRONG:
(a) Its evidence line "that extra shift is 0.0 m today" is wrong. fitted3d is indeed complete (0 NaN of 26x15 joints, measured), but mean-of-valid-joints is not the pelvis: the xy offset (centroid - pelvis) has range [12.5, 6.7] mm and deviates from its own mean by up to 6.8 mm = 5.8% of the 0.1178 m body height (0.78 rig units, 5.4% of the 14.42 rig height). Non-zero, per-frame varying.
(b) It understates the dominant view/export divergence, which is VERTICAL, not the centroid: view3d.py:157-159 re-grounds on the character's lowest vertex every frame, while the export grounds nothing. I measured the posed mesh's lowest vertex in rig space swinging 3.6735 rig units across the 26 frames = 25.5% of rig height. That, not the 5.8% centroid term, is the preview-vs-export placement gap.
(c) It only indicts the FK path. The fallback `_retarget_character` (blender_job.py:478) pins the root identically: :493 `hips_world = arm.matrix_world @ arm.data.bones["hips"].head_local`, :524 `w = hips_world + (Rz @ (Vector(p) - pv)) * scale`, :535 `pb["hips"].constraints.new("COPY_LOCATION").target = empties["PELVIS"]`. There is no export path in this repo that carries root translation.

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATIONS RULED OUT: the pelvis travel is not fit/smoother drift — it is present in the RAW triangulation (raw pelvis max pairwise 0.1357 m vs fitted 0.1331 m) and the trajectory is smooth and monotone (+0.0 -> +129.6 mm in x, increasing at every one of the 25 steps; median step 6.35 mm, max 21.4 mm). It is genuine subject translation. No comment, docstring or doc in the repo declares dropping it as intent (grep for "root motion"/"in place" found only character.py:491 "pin the head in place", about a bone head, and a dead comment at blender_job.py:116).

SUB-CLAIM I CHECKED AND CONFIRMED: orientation and scale really do agree between view and export. main_window.py:494-504 `R = de_tilt_matrix(sequence_up(...))` with view3d.py:106 `pts @ self._R.T`, versus blender_export.py:59-61 `R = de_tilt_matrix(up).T` with `pose @ R` — algebraically the same map; `fit_to_subject` is called on the same de-tilted stack in both (view3d.py:96-98 vs blender_export.py:76) and I measured scale = 113.61997971458045, matching the baseline's 113.62. So the finding is right that only the root translation differs.

FIX AIMED AT THE RIGHT MECHANISM, with caveats: offsetting the hips matrix alone is sufficient for `pose_bone_matrices` because every child reads `base = skin[p]` (character.py:639), so the offset propagates through the FK chain. But the fix as written is incomplete: it does not touch the fallback `_retarget_character` (which needs the same offset at blender_job.py:524), and `_from_rig` (character.py:674) would need the same pelvis_ref if `posed_joints` is to stay in the capture's space. Its view-side prescription ("delete the per-frame recentring at view3d.py:145-146") is also too blunt: line 146 also performs the z seat that line 157-159 refines, and centring on a fixed take-mean pelvis would let the character walk out of the auto-framed camera (view3d.py `_framed`).

SEVERITY: I keep major. Both export paths lose 110-116% of body height of real, monotone subject translation, and the preview disagrees with the export by up to 25.5% of body height vertically. But it should be recorded that this does NOT explain the client's stated complaint: within-frame bone orientations and the character/capture overlay in the view are unaffected by it.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, fitted3d, de-tilted with sequence_up + de_tilt_matrix(up).T exactly as blender_export.py:59-61.

EXPORTED ROOT (reproduced, my own script):
- Character() bundled rig: rig_h = 14.4228, hips_world = [0.0, -0.14449994, 0.72685003], hips bone name "hips".
- fit_to_subject(poses) -> scale = 113.61997971458045 (== baseline 113.62).
- Hips matrix translation from pose_bone_matrices, over all 26 frames: min = max = [0.0, -0.14449994, 0.72685003]; range = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]; std = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0] rig units. Exactly the claim's [0,0,0], and exactly hips_world (so rest[hips][:3,3] == head[hips] and the matrix translation IS the bone head).
- 26/26 frames produced a non-None matrix dict (no frame fell back).

SUBJECT MOTION (de-tilted, metres):
- PELVIS per-axis range: x 0.129636, y 0.034135, z 0.024232. Bbox diagonal 0.136227 m (matches the finding's 0.1362). Max pairwise distance 0.133063 m.
- Per-frame z-height: median 0.117779 m (== baseline 0.1178).
- Ratio: max-pairwise/height = 113.0%; bbox-diag/height = 115.7% (the finding's figure).
- Frame-to-frame pelvis step: median 6.35 mm, max 21.44 mm, total path 0.1669 m.
- Trajectory is monotone in x (mm, relative to frame 0001): 0.0, 3.9, 7.1, 12.9, 18.4, 23.8, 26.4, 27.8, 28.1, 28.6, 27.8, 32.2, 37.1, 44.6, 51.1, 59.1, 66.9, 74.9, 81.3, 90.2, 94.2, 103.5, 113.5, 117.7, 126.3, 129.6. Genuine slide, not jitter.
