- fitted3d NaN joints: 0 of 390. raw pose3d NaN joints: 2 of 390 (matches baseline).

VIEW-VS-EXPORT PLACEMENT GAP (my own measurement):
- Horizontal: (mean of valid joints - pelvis) in xy has range [0.012471, 0.006682] m and max deviation from its own mean 0.006838 m = 5.8% of the 0.1178 m body height = 0.777 rig units = 5.4% of the 14.4228 rig height. NOT 0.0 m as the finding's evidence states.
- Vertical: posed-mesh lowest vertex in rig space (what the export leaves ungrounded) ranges -8.4699 to -4.7963 rig units, span 3.6735 = 25.5% of rig height. The view forces this to 0 every frame (view3d.py:157-159). This is the dominant disagreement and the finding does not mention it.

CODE LINES I READ MYSELF: character.py:190, 573-574, 613, 620-621, 639, 674, 692-694, 706-717; view3d.py:96-98, 105-106, 138-159; main_window.py:19, 43-49, 219, 316, 355, 368, 494-507; blender_export.py:36-90, 132-135; blender_job.py:422-475, 478-545, 616-620.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: pose_bone_matrices (character.py:706-718) returns matrices in RIG space, where to_rig (character.py:573-574) pins the pelvis at self.hips_world every frame. blender_job._drive_character_bones_fk then sets pb.matrix = arm_inv @ Matrix(M) (blender_job.py:468) for every bone including hips, so the exported hips bone never moves. Meanwhile View3D.set_pose re-centres each frame on the mean of the VALID joints (view3d.py:145-146) and re-grounds on the lowest vertex (view3d.py:156-159) — a different, per-frame, joint-set-dependent translation. Neither preserves the subject's world position and they do not agree with each other.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Measured on the exported matrices: the hips bone's world translation range across all 26 frames is [0.0, 0.0, 0.0] rig units in x, y and z. The captured pelvis travels 0.1362 m = 115.7% of the 0.1178 m reconstructed body height. In the view the discarded translation is the mean of valid joints rather than the pelvis; fitted3d is complete in all 26 frames so that extra shift is 0.0 m today. Orientation maths does agree between view and export (blender_export.py:61 R = de_tilt_matrix(up).T with poses3d @ R equals view3d.py:106; same fit_to_subject at blender_export.py:76) — only the root translation differs.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Expose the root translation as an explicit, opt-in output: Character._skin_matrices already computes to_rig, so expose root_offset = Rz @ (pelvis - pelvis_ref) * scale (pelvis_ref = the take's first or mean pelvis) and have pose_bone_matrices apply it to the hips matrix under a new keep_root_motion flag. blender_export._character_bone_frames passes the flag through; View3D.set_pose uses the SAME rule — centre on the take's mean pelvis once, not per frame on the mean of valid joints — so preview and export coincide. The per-frame recentring at view3d.py:145-146 then becomes redundant and should be deleted.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/probe2.py, probe2.json (H7_export_hips_translation_range_rigunits), probe.json

## F14 [major] Re-ordering the smoother before the fit repairs bone lengths but NOT the lag — re-ordering must not be mistaken for the fix

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:182 | category: smoothing | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: CONFIRMED with three refinements. pose3d/pipeline.py:182-183 applies smooth_temporal AFTER the per-frame fit_bone_lengths loop, so the bone-length constraint the fit just enforced is destroyed unconditionally; both executed call sites pass smooth=True with no user toggle (pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214, pose3d/ui/model.py:55). On the client take this raises bone-length CV from 0.145%/0.374% (median/max) to 1.060%/6.473% and shortens the left forearm 19% in frame 0013 (0.0173 vs 0.0214 m). Moving the smoother ahead of the fit restores rigid bones (0.139%/0.298%) but changes reprojection by under 0.5 px (21.89/17.23 px vs the shipped 21.65/17.18) and leaves the pose 6.09 mm from the fit-only pose vs the shipped 6.07 mm: the pose error is the causal EMA's lag, confirmed by a lag scan showing the smoothed pose sits ~0.5x the 12.4 mm median frame-to-frame motion behind the measurement under BOTH orders. Reordering therefore fixes only the bone metric, and must not be sold as the fix for "the character does not follow the keypoints". Refinements: (1) if the reorder also moves measure_bone_lengths onto the smoothed poses, the whole skeleton shrinks (mean bone ratio 0.982, worst 0.961 on the right shin, 0.967 on the left forearm) — bone lengths must keep being measured on the RAW triangulation at pipeline.py:157-160. (2) The proposed test threshold is too tight: smooth-then-fit reaches 0.599% max CV at alpha=0.2, so assert bone CV < 1%, not < 0.5%. (3) Setting alpha=1.0 does not disable the smoother: smooth_temporal's NaN carry-forward (pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:139) is alpha-independent, fabricates the take's 2 missing joints, and still leaves 4.55% CV on the right shin — only smooth=False (F01) is a real off switch. Related and also unfixed by reordering: the manual-correction re-solve at pose3d/ui/model.py:160 re-fits one frame with no smoothing, leaving a corrected frame unsmoothed among smoothed neighbours.
- VERDICT REASON: Code claim verified by reading: pose3d/pipeline.py:181-185 stacks the per-frame fit_bone_lengths results then applies smooth_temporal last (`fitted = np.stack(per_frame)` :181; `if smooth and len(fitted) > 1: fitted = smooth_temporal(fitted, alpha=alpha)` :182-183; assignment to f.fitted3d :184-185). The smoother is unconditionally last, so no bone-length invariant survives it. Executed path confirmed: both real call sites hardcode it on — pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 `fit_project(project, smooth=True)` (import) and pose3d/ui/model.py:55 `fit_project(self.project, smooth=True)` (Recompute-all / re-detect). smooth and alpha have no UI toggle anywhere (grep over pose3d/ui/ and pose3d/app.py returns only those two lines), so alpha=0.6 fit-then-smooth is the only order a user ever gets. I reproduced all three orderings from the primitives with my own script (not the finding's artifact) and every headline number lands within rounding of the claim; the shipped fitted3d is bit-identical to my fit-then-smooth reconstruction (max abs diff 0.0 m), so I am measuring the shipped object. The core assertion — reordering repairs the bone metric and leaves reprojection/displacement untouched — is confirmed to 3 significant figures. The mechanism attribution ("lag of the causal EMA, not the ordering") is confirmed independently by a lag scan: the smoothed poses sit ~6.2 mm from frame t and ~7.1 mm from frame t-1, roughly half of the 12.4 mm median frame-to-frame motion, exactly the 0.4*motion an alpha=0.6 EMA predicts, and identically so under both orders. Not a warm-up transient either: per-frame median left reprojection peaks at frame index 19 (91.8 px shipped / 89.7 px reordered), near the end of the take. Three corrections/additions the finding misses, none of which overturn it: (a) the reordered variant's exact reprojection depends on where bone lengths are measured — 21.89/17.23 px if measure_bone_lengths still runs on the RAW poses (matches the finding's quoted numbers) vs 21.21/17.26 px if it runs on the smoothed ones; the latter is the naive implementation of the proposed fix and it systematically SHRINKS the skeleton (median bone-length ratio smoothed/raw 0.986, mean 0.982, worst right shin 0.961x, left forearm 0.967x), so the fix must keep bone-length measurement on the raw poses (pipeline.py:157-160) and smooth only the data term. (b) The proposed assertion "bone CV < 0.5% for any alpha" fails as written: smooth-then-fit gives max CV 0.599% at alpha=0.2; use < 1%. (c) alpha=1.0 is not an escape hatch: smooth_temporal (pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:129-143) also fills NaN joints from the previous frame (`blend = np.where(np.isnan(cur), prev, blend)` :139), which is alpha-independent — at alpha=1.0 it changes no finite value (max diff 0.0 m) yet still fabricates the 2 NaN joints (frame 0012 RIGHT_KNEE, frame 0021 LEFT_ANKLE) and leaves fit-then-smooth with 4.55% CV on the right shin and 2.93% on the right thigh. Only smooth=False actually disables it. Also worth folding into the same fix: the manual-correction path re-fits a single frame with fit_bone_lengths and no smoothing at all (pose3d/ui/model.py:160), so a corrected frame is unsmoothed while its neighbours stay smoothed — reordering does not fix that inconsistency either. No alternative explanation survives: the effect is not calibration, not the fit's own residual (fit-only moves the pose 0.83 mm mean from raw vs the smoother's 6.07 mm), and not the NaN fill (worth 2 of 390 joint-observations).
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Client take workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, alpha=0.6, bone CV with ddof=1 (the finding appears to use ddof=0, hence its slightly lower numbers).

fit only (smooth=False)           | 0.145 / 0.374 | 7.38, 4.01   | 0.00 | 2

smooth-then-fit, re-measured BLs  | 0.160 / 0.334 | 21.21, 17.26 | 6.11 | 0

Lag scan (mean 3D distance to raw frame t-k, mm): raw 0.0/12.39/20.38 for k=0/1/2; fit-only 0.83/12.42/20.36; fit-then-smooth 6.22/7.11/14.53; smooth-then-fit 6.22/7.11/14.54 — identical under both orders, ~0.5x frame-to-frame motion, the EMA-lag signature.

Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F14/ordering.py and extra.py; raw numbers in /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F14/ordering.json. Nothing in the repository was modified.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: Because smooth_temporal runs AFTER fit_bone_lengths, the smoother destroys the constraint the fit just enforced. Moving it before the fit (smooth the raw triangulation, then fit) restores rigid bones but leaves reprojection essentially unchanged, because the lag is a property of the causal EMA, not of the ordering. Anyone 'fixing' this by swapping the two lines fixes the bone metric and leaves the client's complaint intact.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Three orderings (bone CV median / bone CV max / reproj median left,right / mean displacement from the fit-only pose): fit only (smooth=False) 0.142% / 0.367% / 7.38, 4.01 px / 0.00 mm. Shipped fit-then-smooth 1.036% / 6.157% / 21.65, 17.18 px / 6.07 mm. Smooth-then-fit 0.137% / 0.293% / 21.89, 17.23 px / 6.09 mm.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Both, in priority order: (1) default smooth off (F01) — that alone gives 0.14% bone CV and 7.38 px; (2) if smoothing is ever enabled, apply it to the RAW poses inside fit_project BEFORE the per-frame loop (pipeline.py:167-181) so fit_bone_lengths always has the last word and 'fitted3d has rigid bones' holds unconditionally. Assertion/test: for any alpha, bone-length CV of fit_project's output must be < 0.5%.
