## F15 [major] The world frame has no gravity reference: up is 89.3 deg off vertical, and orient.sequence_up both leaves a 9-11 deg residual tilt and erases a 10.8 deg median per-frame lean

- file: pose3d/geometry/orient.py:78 | category: orientation | sources: extrinsics, retarget | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The world frame carries no gravity reference, so the whole take renders and exports with a ~15 deg systematic tilt relative to vertical — but per-frame lean is preserved, and the app already discloses the trade-off.

`save_rig` (resolve.py:44-49) persists only {R,t} per camera, and the extrinsics come from one arbitrarily-rotated ArUco tag (resolve.py:147), so the world axes are tag 14's: I measure tag14's Y at 93.9-98.8 deg from every gravity proxy, and `quality.world_up_tilt` = 89.34 deg, which fires check_rig's tilt warning (quality.py:58-68).

With no gravity available, main_window._apply_view_orientation (main_window.py:494-500 — NOT view3d, which never calls sequence_up) levels the take on `orient.sequence_up`: the take's MEAN body axis. blender_export.py:59-61 applies the identical rotation, so the BVH/FBX inherits it.

Magnitude on the client take: sequence_up(fitted3d) sits 9.68 deg from the cross-camera-x proxy, 14.93 deg from a scene-based tag-row proxy computed over all 26 frames, 22.21 deg from the mean-camera-up proxy, and 15.20 deg from the 3-proxy consensus. Best single estimate ~15 deg, dominated by forward/back tilt (14.0 of the 15.2 deg is perpendicular to the tag row; 5.7 deg along it). The three proxies disagree with each other by 8.1-14.2 deg, so gravity itself is only recoverable to about +/-7 deg on this rig — that is the floor any fix can reach.

Two corrections to the original finding. First, sequence_up does NOT erase a 10.8 deg per-frame lean: de_tilt_matrix produces ONE constant rotation, and I measure the per-frame body-axis tilt in the de-tilted view at median 10.83 / max 21.10 deg, identical to the world-frame deviation-from-mean to 1.5e-11. Only the MEAN lean is removed, and that mean lean IS the residual tilt — one effect, not two. Second, it is not silent: panels.py:216-219 shows a standing sidebar note ("A lean held through the whole take reads as upright") whenever levelling is applied, check_rig says the same, and orient.py:88-100 documents the reasoning and already prescribes the fix ("Both become viable once extrinsics are solved from the full tag layout").

The effect is a global rigid rotation: it moves no joint relative to another (retarget/epipolar/bone-length metrics are all invariant to it), but it makes the delivered animation stand ~15 deg off the ground-plane normal, which reads to a client as "the character leans wrong".

Fix: record an explicit world up. The proposed cross(tag_row, mean_tag_normal) is my proxy (c) and is itself 8-9 deg from the other two proxies and depends on tag 13's ambiguous IPPE normal (its two PnP solutions are within a 1.07 error ratio), so it should be one candidate among several rather than the answer — combine the camera-up and tag-layout candidates, surface their disagreement, and expose the manual "level the figure" control, which is the only part that can beat the ~7 deg proxy floor.
- VERDICT REASON: The core mechanism reproduces exactly, but three sub-claims are wrong and one number is understated.

CONFIRMED (re-derived from code I read myself):
- No gravity is recorded. `save_rig` (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:44-49) writes extrinsics.json as {"left":{R,t},"right":{R,t}} and nothing else; I read the shipped workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration/extrinsics.json and it has exactly those keys. The world frame is tag 14's frame (resolve.py:147 builds CalibratedRig from the per-camera single-marker solves; import_dialog.py:175 calls resolve_calibration, :215 calls save_rig). Note the finding's "resolve.py:143" is off — 44-49 and 147 are the lines.
- `world_up_tilt(rig)` = 89.34 deg (I ran pose3d.calib.quality.world_up_tilt on the shipped rig; claim said 89.3) and `check_rig` does fire the tilt warning (_TILT_WARN_DEG=30, quality.py:16,58-68).
- `sequence_up` IS on the executed path, and its result is a single constant rotation for the whole take: main_window._apply_view_orientation (main_window.py:487-507) computes up=sequence_up(all fitted3d), R=de_tilt_matrix(up), pushes it to view3d.set_orientation; blender_export.py:59-61 does the same, so view and export share the tilt. Called from _load_model (main_window.py:518) and after every recompute (main_window.py:281).
- The magnitude of the residual tilt is real.

WRONG IN DETAIL:
1. "view3d falls back to orient.sequence_up" — view3d.py:18 imports only detect_vertical and upright_matrix and never calls sequence_up; the call site is main_window.py:494-500 (and blender_export.py:59). view3d's own detect_vertical path runs only when R is None (view3d.py:139).
2. "erases a 10.8 deg median per-frame lean" — FALSE, and it double-counts the effect. de_tilt_matrix(up) is ONE constant rotation applied identically to every frame, so per-frame deviation from the mean is preserved bit-for-bit. I measured the per-frame body-axis tilt from +Z AFTER de-tilting in view coordinates: median 10.83 deg, max 21.10 deg — identical to the world-frame deviation-from-mean to 1.5e-11 deg. The 10.8/21.1 numbers the finding offers as "lean that sequence_up discards" are exactly the lean it KEEPS. The only thing removed is the MEAN lean, which is the same single quantity as the "residual tilt" — so the finding's two stated harms are one harm counted twice.
3. "silently removed" — FALSE. panels.py:216-219 shows a permanent sidebar note whenever levelling is applied ("Vertical: estimated from the subject... A lean held through the whole take reads as upright"), gated by show_levelling_note (panels.py:280-282, called at main_window.py:504), and check_rig's warning text (quality.py:65-68) says the same thing verbatim. I ran check_rig on the real rig and got that exact string.
4. The finding presents the camera-up disagreement as new evidence; orient.py:96-97 already states it ("disagreed with the body line by 18 degrees") and I measure 18.5 deg (sequence_up(fitted) vs left camera up). orient.py:99-100 already names the proposed fix ("Both become viable once extrinsics are solved from the full tag layout rather than one arbitrary tag"). So this is a documented, disclosed, deliberate trade-off with a known remedy, not an undetected defect.

UNDERSTATED: the finding's "~9-11 deg" leans on the most favourable proxy. Recomputing proxy (c) over ALL 26 frames (the finding appears to have used a subset) gives sequence_up vs c = 14.93 deg, and vs the 3-proxy consensus 15.20 deg. The honest figure is ~15 deg, range 9.7-22.2 across proxies.

Could it be explained otherwise? The residual is dominated by forward/back tilt (14.0 of the 15.2 deg from consensus is perpendicular to the tag row; only 5.7 deg is along it), consistent with a mannequin posed leaning forward on average rather than with proxy noise. But the three gravity proxies disagree with EACH OTHER by 8.1-14.2 deg, so "true" gravity on this rig is only known to about +/-7 deg; proxy (a) is not privileged (cross(x_l,x_r) is vertical only under zero camera roll, and the measured 14.3 deg relative roll proves at least one camera has >=7 deg roll). Any fix therefore has a floor around 7 deg residual until a real gravity source exists.

Fix aim: correct in direction (record an explicit up), but the specific recipe up=cross(tag_row, mean_tag_normal) is proxy (c), which my numbers show is itself 8-9 deg from the other two proxies and depends on tag 13's ambiguous IPPE normal (I measured tag 13's two PnP solutions at reprojection errors 2.13 vs 2.28 px in frame 0003 — a 1.07 ratio, i.e. essentially undecidable). The "let the user nudge" control is the part that actually addresses the real limit.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All run at repo root with .venv/bin/python, read-only (stdin heredocs, no files written to the repo).

EXTRINSICS-ONLY (shipped workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration/extrinsics.json):
- world_up_tilt(rig) = 89.34 deg (finding: 89.3). check_rig fires: "Calibration's nominal up is 89 deg off vertical..." plus the assumed-intrinsics warning and a third one I did not expect: "The two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048)".
- proxy a = normalize(cross(x_left, x_right)) = (-0.9552,-0.1485,0.2559); proxy b = normalize(up_left+up_right) = (-0.9881,-0.1534,0.0115).
- a vs b = 14.17 deg (finding 14.2). Relative camera roll (up_left vs up_right) = 14.30 deg (finding 14.3). x_left vs x_right = 52.29 deg. Baseline 0.559 m.
- tag14 Y vs a = 98.54, vs b = 98.82 (finding 98.5). tag14 -X vs a = 17.21, vs b = 8.85, vs c = 11.49 (finding "gravity ~11 deg from tag-14 -X" is only true for c/b).

TAG-ROW PROXY c (my own ArUco redetection, DICT_6X6_250, IPPE_SQUARE, marker 0.05 m, left camera, ALL 26 frames; 26/26 frames yield >=3 tags):
- ids seen: {13,14,17} in 20 frames, {13,14,15,17} in 6. Row span 0.298-0.410 m, second singular value 0.074-0.099 of the first (finding: 0.093, span 0.298 — matches the 3-tag frames).
- row direction is stable: max 2.63 deg from its own 26-frame mean.
- mean c = normalize(mean over frames of cross(row, mean tag normal)); c vs a = 9.29 deg, c vs b = 8.06 deg (finding claimed 5.9 and 9.8 — I get different values with the full frame set; on the first 8 frames only I get 6.22 and 11.22, closer to theirs, so their numbers come from a subset).
- tag14 Y vs c = 93.9 deg.
- Tag 13's PnP is genuinely ambiguous: solution errors 1.98-2.65 px vs 2.28-4.65 px, ratio down to 1.07 (frame 0003: 2.13 vs 2.28).

SEQUENCE_UP vs GRAVITY (pose3d.geometry.orient.sequence_up on the take's stored poses):
- sequence_up(raw pose3d)    = (-0.9362,-0.0254,0.3505)  [this is the vector the finding quotes]
- sequence_up(fitted3d)      = (-0.9284,-0.0275,0.3706)  [this is what main_window actually uses: main_window.py:496-500 passes fitted3d]
- seq_up(fitted) vs a = 9.68, vs b = 22.21, vs c = 14.93 deg. seq_up(raw) vs a = 8.97, vs b = 21.09, vs c = 13.72.
- 3-proxy consensus = normalize(a+b+c): a is 7.53 deg from it, b 7.03, c 3.36, seq_up(fitted) 15.20.
- Direction decomposition of seq_up(fitted) vs consensus: 5.71 deg along the tag row, 14.03 deg forward/back.
- Angle to the tag row (90 = level): a 94.57, b 95.28, c 90.01 (vacuous, c is built perpendicular), seq_up(raw) 87.38, seq_up(fitted) 87.49.

PER-FRAME LEAN (the finding's key evidence, refuted):
- Per-frame _frame_up vs the 26-frame mean (fitted): median 10.83, mean 10.98, max 21.10 deg (finding: 10.8 / 21.1 — reproduced). Raw: median 11.92, max 27.26.
- Lean components perpendicular to the mean: mean exactly 0 by construction (-8.9e-16, -6.7e-15 deg), std 10.16 and 5.64 deg.
- Per-frame body-axis tilt from +Z measured IN THE DE-TILTED VIEW (fitted3d @ de_tilt_matrix(sequence_up(fitted3d)).T, i.e. exactly main_window.py:500-502 + view3d._to_view): median 10.83, max 21.10 deg. Max |difference| from the world-frame quantity: 1.54e-11 deg. The per-frame lean is preserved exactly; only the mean is removed.
- Per-frame deviations, frame 0001..0026 (deg): 11.3, 8.8, 10.1, 11.4, 16.3, 15.4, 15.0, 12.5, 7.9, 5.5, 2.9, 5.2, 15.2, 10.4, 9.1, 11.5, 12.3, 12.9, 14.9, 21.1, 15.7, 10.1, 7.5, 4.0, 8.4, 10.3.

CODE PATH (lines I read): pose3d/calib/resolve.py:36-49 (save_rig, R/t only), :93, :147; pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:175, :215; pose3d/calib/quality.py:16,19-35,58-68; pose3d/ui/main_window.py:281-288 (check_rig -> sidebar), :487-507 (sequence_up -> de_tilt_matrix -> view3d.set_orientation), :518; pose3d/ui/view3d.py:18 (imports detect_vertical/upright_matrix only), :77-82, :106-109, :139; pose3d/ui/panels.py:216-223, :267-282; pose3d/export/blender_export.py:46-61, :73-77; pose3d/geometry/orient.py:31-60 (_frame_up), :78-112 (sequence_up), :115-134 (de_tilt_matrix).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: resolve_calibration (resolve.py:143) stores only (R,t) per camera; the world axes are whatever tag 14's orientation happened to be, so quality.world_up_tilt = 89.3 deg on the shipped rig and check_rig fires the tilt warning. With no gravity recorded, view3d falls back to orient.sequence_up (orient.py:78-112), which defines 'up' as the take's MEAN body axis — normalising away any lean held for the whole take and forcing a single-frame project upright. Its docstring (orient.py:91-97) justifies this with '3 markers disagreed about up by 6, 92, 89 deg', but that compares the tags' ARBITRARY OWN axes, not gravity.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Three genuine gravity proxies in the tag-14 world frame: (a) cross(left cam x-axis, right cam x-axis); (b) mean of the two cameras' up-vectors; (c) cross(direction of the row of 4 tag centres, mean tag normal). Pairwise: (a)vs(b) 14.2 deg, (c)vs(a) 5.9, (c)vs(b) 9.8. All three put tag-14's Y ~95-99 deg from vertical (tag14 Y to (a) 98.5, to (c) 95.5) and gravity ~11 deg from tag-14's -X. sequence_up returns up=(-0.936,-0.025,0.351), which is 9.0 deg from (a), 11.3 from (c), 21.1 from (b); an independent retarget-side measurement gives sequence_up vs (a) 9.7 deg, vs -(mean camera y) 22.2 deg, and vs a 3-tag row candidate (ids 14,13,17, span 0.298 m, second singular value 0.093 of the first) 18.1 deg. The 14.2 deg (a)-(b) spread equals the two cameras' relative roll (14.3 deg). Individual frames' body axes deviate from the sequence mean by 10.8 deg median / 21.1 deg max, so sequence_up discards real lean, not just noise.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Record an explicit gravity axis. In resolve.py, after solving the per-camera extrinsics, compute up_world = normalize(cross(tag_row_direction, mean_tag_normal)) from the multi-tag layout (available once extrinsics come from a pooled solve, F38), disambiguate the sign with the mean camera up-vector, and store it in extrinsics.json as an 'up' key (save_rig, resolve.py:44-49); add Extrinsics/CalibratedRig-level world_up. view3d.py uses rig.world_up for de-tilting and calls orient.sequence_up only as the single-tag fallback; quality.world_up_tilt becomes a check of the recorded up against the camera proxy. Additionally add orient.up_candidates(rig, poses, tag_observations) returning the named candidates and their pairwise disagreement for the sidebar, and a 'level the figure' control storing a chosen up-vector in project.json that blender_export._character_bone_frames reuses — on a rig whose references disagree by up to 20 deg, letting the user nudge beats any automatic choice.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-extrinsics/sweep_h4_h5_h6.json (H4), perturb_h6.py/.json (up_axis); <SP>/find-retarget/probe.json (H4), probe4.py/.json (H4_tag_row)

## F16 [major] The import dialog runs its own detection loop that silently drops the face keypoints, so imported projects have no head2d/head3d and the head-orientation feature is dead

- file: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:204 | category: head | sources: detection, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: ImportDialog._process runs its own detection loop (pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:199-206) that writes only frame.kp2d[cam] and frame.scores[cam], discarding Detection.head_xy/head_scores; the canonical pipeline.detect_project (pose3d/pipeline.py:38-46) writes all four. Every project created through the Import Images button therefore has head2d all-NaN, and triangulate_project (pipeline.py:145-149) turns that into an all-NaN head3d without erroring. Character._skin_matrices then falls through to the legacy one-nose-point branch (character.py:598-602 → _head_aim_target), so the ear/eye head basis added in 5fdd919 never runs on an imported take. The only path that populates head2d is main_window.py:265-273 Run Detection → model.redetect_all (model.py:61-72) → detect_project; the manual-drag path is explicitly guarded against bootstrapping face points (model.py:144), so there is no migration.

Measured on the client take (26 frames, fresh detection of all 52 images): face points are fully available (130/130 finite in each view, ear score medians 0.820/0.919) and change nothing about the body (fitted3d moves median 0.0000 m, max 0.0068 m), but they rotate the character's head bone by a median 15.5 deg (p90 23.9, max 28.5) and move the head mesh by a median 6.3 % of body height (max 12.2 %). Same images, two different animations, decided by whether the user happened to press Run Detection, with nothing in the UI explaining it.

Two corrections to the finding as filed. (1) The client project.json (2026-07-29) predates the head feature (5fdd919, 2026-08-21), so its missing head keys are explained by vintage and do NOT themselves evidence the import bug — the bug is established by the code plus a stub-detector A/B, and its consequence is that imports made today are still headless. (2) The proposed fix's "loader default of head2d = NaN" already exists (pose3d/core/project.py:65-69, pose3d/core/io_project.py:145-153); only the detect_project call-swap and the non-destructive re-detect command are actually missing. The finding also understates one thing: detect_project overwrites frame.kp2d unconditionally (pipeline.py:42-43) and redetect_all never consults frame.corrected (model.py:61-72), so today's only route to face points silently overwrites every hand-corrected 2D point while leaving its corrected flag True — which is precisely why the "fills head2d without touching corrected kp2d" command in the fix is the load-bearing half.
- VERDICT REASON: Re-derived from the code myself, and the divergence is real, on the executed path, and measurably large.

CODE (read at HEAD, my own line numbers):
- pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:199-206 — the inline loop: `for cam in CAMERAS: d = det.detect(cv2.imread(frame.images[cam])); frame.kp2d[cam] = d.xy; frame.scores[cam] = d.scores` (line 205). Two fields. `Detection.head_xy`/`head_scores` (pose3d/detect/base.py:28, produced at pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:75) are dropped on the floor.
- pose3d/pipeline.py:38-46 — the canonical loop sets all four, guarded by `if det.head_xy is not None`.
- Executed path confirmed: main_window.py:290-305 `_on_import` → `ImportDialog(self)` → `_run_import_dialog` → `dlg.exec()`; import_dialog.py:128 `self.process_btn.clicked.connect(self._process)`. Both paths build the same detector (import_dialog.py:231-233, main_window.py:257-260, `mode="balanced", device="cpu", feet=False`), so it is not a detector-capability difference.
- import_dialog.py:213 then calls `triangulate_project`, which at pipeline.py:145-149 triangulates the all-NaN head2d into an all-NaN head3d. No exception (I ran it: "triangulate with all-NaN head2d ok; head3d all NaN: True").
- Recovery path exists only via main_window.py:265-273 `_on_run_detection` → model.py:61-72 `redetect_all` → `detect_project`. Confirmed no other writer: model.py:129-147 only edits an ALREADY-populated head2d and is explicitly guarded at model.py:144 (`if not np.isnan(f.head2d[cam]).all()`), so dragging cannot bootstrap face points into a legacy/imported project.
- With head_pts=None the character takes the legacy branch at character.py:598-602 (`_EAR_MID` → `_head_aim_target`), i.e. the clamped one-nose-point path.

STUB A/B (no RTMPose, exact copy of import_dialog.py:203-205 vs `detect_project`, same stub Detection with finite head_xy): import-loop head2d all NaN = True; detect_project head2d finite = True. Mechanism proven in isolation.

TESTS: `grep -rn "detect_project\|redetect_all\|_process" tests/` → zero hits. tests/test_ui_smoke.py:188-208 only asserts the dialog constructs and that the button reaches it. Confirmed.

WHAT I WOULD CORRECT IN THE FINDING (details, not the conclusion):
1. Confounded evidence. The client project.json is dated 2026-07-29 22:45; the head feature is commit 5fdd919 dated 2026-08-21 15:53 and import_dialog.py was last touched by ae24d84 (2026-08-21 15:30, i.e. 23 minutes BEFORE the feature). So the client take's missing head keys are fully explained by its vintage and are NOT proof of the import bug — the bug is proven by the code and the stub A/B, and it means every import made TODAY still lands headless. The finding cites the client file as evidence; that link does not carry.
2. "Reproduced the stored body keypoints exactly (median 0.0 px)" overstates slightly. Over all 52 images: median 0.000 px, p90 0.00, but max 3.26 px (left) / 2.01 px (right); re-detection moved fitted3d by median 0.0000 m, max 0.0068 m.
3. The proposed fix's "a pose3d/core/project.py loader default of head2d = NaN" is already implemented — project.py:65-69 defaults and io_project.py:145-153 read the head keys with `.get`, which is why the legacy project loads with NaN heads instead of crashing. That half of the fix is a no-op.
4. Understated, not overstated: `detect_project` (pipeline.py:42-43) overwrites `frame.kp2d[cam]` unconditionally and `redetect_all` (model.py:61-72) never consults or clears `frame.corrected`. So "Run Detection" — the only way to get face points into an imported project — silently destroys every hand-corrected 2D point while leaving the corrected flags True. That strengthens the "no re-detect path that fills head2d without touching corrected kp2d" clause.

Could it be explained by something else? No. The detector is not the limiter (all 260 face points detected, see measurements), the loader is not the limiter (it defaults to NaN correctly and round-trips head keys per tests/test_head_keypoints.py:41-79), and calibration is not involved (head3d uses the same geometry that reconstructs the body fine).
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All runs: cwd=/media/athena/hd3/Projects/pose3d-tool, .venv/bin/python, stdin heredocs (nothing written to the repo).

A. Client project as stored/loaded (workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/project.json): frame key union over all 26 frames = ['corrected','fitted3d','frame_id','images','kp2d','pose3d','scores'] — no head2d/head_scores/head3d. After load_project: head2d all-NaN = True, head3d all-NaN = True.

B. Fresh RTMPose (balanced, cpu, feet=False, bundled COCO-17 weights) on all 26 frames x 2 cams (52 images, ~0.3-0.5 s each):
 - head2d fully populated: 130/130 finite left rows, 130/130 right (26 frames x 5 face points, zero NaN).
 - ear scores over the left camera: median left_ear 0.820, right_ear 0.919 (single-frame samples ranged 0.527-0.928). Nose 0.655-0.825.
 - re-detected kp2d vs stored kp2d: median 0.000 px both cams; p90 0.00; max 3.26 px (left) / 2.01 px (right).
 - after triangulate_project: head3d finite 130/130 points.
 - fitted3d change from re-detection: median 0.0000 m, max 0.0068 m (body pose essentially unchanged; the head data is the only new information).

C. MAGNITUDE of the dead feature — same 26 frames, same de-tilt (de_tilt_matrix(sequence_up(pose))), one Character with fit_to_subject over the take, posed twice per frame: head_pts=None (what an import gives) vs head_pts=head3d (what Run Detection gives). Head bone resolved as ch.bone_names[ch.role["head"]] = "head".
 - head-bone rotation difference: median 15.5 deg, p90 23.9 deg, max 28.5 deg, min 6.4 deg (26/26 frames).
 - canonical HEAD joint displacement: median 0.0061 m = 5.2 % of body height, max 0.0112 m = 9.5 % (body height 0.1178 m, matching the baseline).
 - max skinned-mesh vertex displacement: median 0.0074 m = 6.3 % of body height, max 0.0144 m = 12.2 %.
 So the same images produce two animations whose head differs by ~15 deg median / up to 28.5 deg, purely depending on whether the user pressed Run Detection after importing.

D. Stub-detector A/B isolating the two loops (no model): copy of import_dialog.py:203-205 → head2d all NaN True; pipeline.detect_project → head2d finite True.

E. triangulate_project on the as-loaded (all-NaN head2d) client project: returns dropped=0, head3d all NaN, no exception.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: ImportDialog._process inlines its own per-frame loop (import_dialog.py:200-206) that writes only frame.kp2d[cam] and frame.scores[cam], discarding the Detection.head_xy / head_scores that RTMPoseDetector.detect returns (rtmpose.py:56, 75). The canonical path pipeline.detect_project (pipeline.py:38-46) copies all four. So an imported project has head2d/head3d entirely NaN, triangulate_project (pipeline.py:145) triangulates NaN into head3d, Character.pose_and_joints never receives head points, and the ear/eye head basis is never built — until the user presses Run Detection, which routes through ProjectModel.redetect_all -> detect_project and suddenly populates them. There is no migration or re-detect path that fills head2d without touching corrected kp2d.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Read directly: import_dialog.py:200-206 sets two fields, pipeline.py:38-46 sets four. The client's project.json frame keys are exactly ['frame_id','images','kp2d','scores','pose3d','fitted3d','corrected'] — no head2d, head_scores or head3d. The baseline confirms retarget_error posed the character with head_pts=None throughout. A fresh detection on this take DOES yield usable face points: re-detection reproduced the stored body keypoints exactly (median 0.0 px over 6 image/frames) and skeleton.py:36 records ear scores of 0.91/0.93 on this mannequin. No test exercises ImportDialog._process (tests/test_ui_smoke.py:188-208 only asserts the dialog constructs and opens) and grep over tests/ returns zero hits for detect_project.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Delete the inline loop and call pipeline.detect_project(project, det, cv2.imread); if per-frame progress is needed give detect_project an on_frame(i, n) callback rather than duplicating it. Add a 'Re-detect face points' command that fills missing head2d for an already-imported project without touching corrected kp2d: a pose3d/core/project.py loader default of head2d = NaN plus a main-window action that runs detect per frame and writes ONLY head_xy/head_scores and the triangulated head3d, leaving kp2d, corrected and corrections untouched. Combine with F04 (feet=True) so the same pass upgrades HEAD/NECK/PELVIS. Test: run ImportDialog._process with a stub detector returning non-None head_xy and assert frame.head2d[cam] is populated and frame.head3d is finite.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-detection/detector_probe.py, detector_probe.json; <SP>/find-tests-ui/ (project key dump, see notes)

## F18 [major] _FALLBACK_LENGTHS are adult-human metres (thigh 0.43 m) applied to a 0.037 m limb, and fit_bone_lengths still defaults to fill_missing=True

- file: pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:46 | category: bone-fit | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: _FALLBACK_LENGTHS (bonefit.py:34-50) is an adult-human table in absolute metres — thigh 0.43 m, shin 0.44 m, spine 0.55 m — applied to a subject whose measured bones are 8-41 mm, i.e. the table is 12.0x too large (median measured/fallback = 0.0835). pipeline.py:161 and its duplicate in model.py:177 substitute it for any bone whose median measured length is <= 1e-6, which is exactly a bone unobserved in every frame of the take.

The finding's dismissal of the shipped path is wrong. Both production callers do pass fill_missing=False (pipeline.py:171, model.py:160), but that flag only NaNs the unobserved joint AFTER the solve (bonefit.py:124); the fallback-length bone residuals are in the least-squares either way (bonefit.py:104-106). For a LEAF joint (ankle, wrist) the free endpoint absorbs the residual and the damage really is ~1.2 mm — that is the only case the finding simulated. For an INTERIOR joint the over-long bones over-constrain the OBSERVED neighbours and the solver tears the reconstruction apart on the shipped path: blacking out one hip in one view for a whole take (which also kills the derived PELVIS, skeleton.py:70,173) moves the still-observed joints by 71.6 mm median and 279.6 mm max = 237% of body height; one shoulder gives 110.7/198.6 mm = 168%; PELVIS alone 225.4 mm = 191%. There is no warning: pipeline.py:176-178 prints only on an exception, and nothing reports that a bone fell back.

A control run isolates the cause: with the identical code and blackouts but the fallback table scaled by 0.0835, the worst case drops from 225 mm to 2.3 mm (~100x). The defect is the absolute-metre magnitudes, not the solver or the fill_missing flag.

This is not purely hypothetical for this product: validate_cross_view (pipeline.py:81-102) NaNs observations per (frame, joint) and will blank one joint in every frame when the calibration is systematically bad for it — git f4c92f9 records a configuration with 38% cross-view rejections, and LEFT_ANKLE is already this take's worst joint at 10.4 px median epipolar error.

Impact on the delivered take is still zero (0/14 bones fell back; worst per-joint gap is 1 frame), so this does not explain the client's current complaint. The proposed fix is aimed at the real mechanism and is confirmed effective by control (D): make the table proportions of a measured reference (spine, else the largest measured bone — verified to give scale 0.0865, within 4% of correct even when the spine itself falls back). Flipping bonefit.py:62 to fill_missing=False by default is worth doing for library hygiene but is NOT the fix for the production defect; the user-visible fallback note is.
- VERDICT REASON: All code citations verified by my own reading, and every measurement reproduced. But the finding's impact analysis is wrong in the direction of UNDERSTATEMENT, so I am correcting it upward rather than refuting it.

CODE (read myself):
- pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:34-50 — `_FALLBACK_LENGTHS` in absolute metres: PELVIS-NECK 0.55, thigh 0.43, shin 0.44, upper arm 0.28, forearm 0.25.
- pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:62 — `fill_missing: bool = True` is indeed the library default.
- pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:124-125 — `if not fill_missing: fitted[~observed] = np.nan`. Crucially this NaNs the unobserved joint only AFTER the solve; the fallback-length bone residuals participate in the least-squares regardless of the flag (bonefit.py:95-107). This is the point the finding missed.
- pose3d/pipeline.py:157-161 — `measured = measure_bone_lengths(raw); fb = fallback_bone_lengths(); bone_lengths = {k: (v if v > 1e-6 else fb[k]) ...}`. `measure_bone_lengths` (bonefit.py:22-31) returns 0.0 only when a bone is NaN in every frame, so the fallback fires exactly on a whole-take blackout.
- Identical duplicated logic in pose3d/ui/model.py:173-178 (`_compute_bone_lengths`).
- Executed path confirmed: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:212-214 -> `triangulate_project` then `fit_project(project, smooth=True)` -> pipeline.py:171 `fit_bone_lengths(..., fill_missing=False)`; the drag re-solve is model.py:157-161, also `fill_missing=False`. Those are the only two production call sites (grep over the package); the only `fill_missing=True` call outside tests is tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py:109. So the finding's caller inventory is right.
- pipeline.py:167-179: the only print is on an exception. There is no user-visible note when a bone falls back. Confirmed.

WHY THE FINDING IS UNDERSTATED: it simulated LEFT_ANKLE, a LEAF joint, whose fallback bone has one free endpoint that can absorb the whole 0.44 m residual — hence its "damage is contained, 0.31/1.22 mm". I reproduced that number exactly. But for an INTERIOR joint the fallback bones form an over-constrained chain among OBSERVED neighbours, the residual cannot be absorbed, and the solver drags the observed joints. That happens on the shipped path, with fill_missing=False.

REALISM: a whole-take blackout is not only an occlusion accident. validate_cross_view (pipeline.py:81-102) NaNs observations per (frame, joint); a calibration that is bad for one joint blacks it out in every frame. Git history (f4c92f9) records a configuration with 38% cross-view rejections. On this take LEFT_ANKLE already has the worst epipolar error (10.4 px median). Also, NECK/PELVIS are 2D midpoints of shoulders/hips (skeleton.py:63,70,170-174), so losing one hip in one view also kills PELVIS — my most damaging simulation is that realistic coupled case, not an artificial 3D edit.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: A) FALLBACK TABLE vs MEASURED (client take, raw pose3d, 26 frames) — reproduces the finding exactly, mm:
spine 41.08 (fb 0.55 = 13.4x), neck-head 21.86 (9.2x), neck-shoulder 15.97/16.01 (11.3x), upper arm 22.41/22.52 (12.5x/12.4x), forearm 21.35/19.96 (11.7x/12.5x), pelvis-hip 8.32/8.31 (12.0x), thigh 37.17/35.49 (11.57x/12.12x), shin 37.11/36.84 (11.86x/11.94x). Bones falling back: 0/14. Median measured/fallback ratio 0.0835, i.e. the table is 12.0x too big for this subject. Raw NaN counts: RIGHT_KNEE 1 frame, LEFT_ANKLE 1 frame (right view only); no joint is close to a whole-take blackout on this take.

B) BLACKOUT EVERY JOINT IN TURN, PRODUCTION PATH (fit_project, fill_missing=False, smooth=False), displacement of the OTHER (still observed) joints vs the unmodified fit, median / max mm (body height 0.118 m):
HEAD 0.011/1.99 | NECK 107.28/177.14 | LEFT_SHOULDER 3.51/42.85 | RIGHT_SHOULDER 4.17/41.79 | LEFT_ELBOW 0.002/2.48 | RIGHT_ELBOW 0.002/1.63 | LEFT_WRIST 0.001/1.52 | RIGHT_WRIST 0.001/1.27 | PELVIS 111.92/225.39 | LEFT_HIP 14.43/200.98 | RIGHT_HIP 17.94/190.73 | LEFT_KNEE 0.002/1.71 | RIGHT_KNEE 0.003/34.44 | LEFT_ANKLE 0.000/1.22 | RIGHT_ANKLE 0.000/3.51.
Worst: 225.4 mm = 191% of body height, on the shipped fill_missing=False path.

C) REALISTIC 2D-LEVEL BLACKOUT (NaN the joint in the RIGHT view only, all 26 frames, then triangulate_project + fit_project as production does; the derived midpoint joint dies with it):
- LEFT_HIP + PELVIS: 4 bones fall back -> other joints move median 71.61 mm, max 279.58 mm = 237% of body height.
- LEFT_SHOULDER + NECK: 5 bones fall back -> median 110.65 mm, max 198.56 mm = 168% of height.
- LEFT_ANKLE: 1 bone -> median 0.00, max 1.22 mm = 1%.
- LEFT_WRIST: 1 bone -> median 0.00, max 1.52 mm = 1%.

D) DECISIVE CONTROL — same blackouts, same code, only the fallback table scaled by 0.0835 (shipped med/max mm -> scaled med/max mm):
NECK 107.28/177.14 -> 0.22/2.12 | PELVIS 111.92/225.38 -> 0.08/2.25 | LEFT_HIP 14.43/200.98 -> 0.00/1.74 | RIGHT_HIP 17.94/190.73 -> 0.01/1.97 | LEFT_SHOULDER 3.51/42.85 -> 0.01/1.92 | RIGHT_KNEE 0.00/34.44 -> 0.00/3.38 | LEFT_ANKLE 1.22 -> 1.22 | HEAD 1.98 -> 1.98.
So the corruption is caused by the fallback VALUES, not by structural underdetermination: a correctly-scaled table reduces the worst case from 225 mm to 2.3 mm (~100x). This validates the proposed fix's mechanism.

E) fill_missing=True (library default, tests only): blacking out LEFT_ANKLE, the frame's max joint-pair span goes 0.1391 -> 0.4801 m (3.45x); LEFT_ELBOW 2.66x; LEFT_WRIST 2.22x. The finding said 3.7x using a different baseline span (0.128 = per-frame median max span vs my max-over-frames 0.1391); same order, both correct under their own definition.

F) Scale check for the proposed fix: with NECK blacked out, PELVIS-NECK itself falls back, so the reference must be "largest measured bone" = L thigh 37.17 mm, giving scale 37.17/430 = 0.0865, within 4% of the 0.0835 used in control (D). The fix's stated fallback-reference rule survives its own worst case.

All runs: cd /media/athena/hd3/Projects/pose3d-tool && .venv/bin/python (heredocs, importing scratchpad/baseline/metrics.py). Nothing written to the repo.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: pipeline.py:158-161 substitutes _FALLBACK_LENGTHS for any bone whose measured median is <= 1e-6 — any bone never observed in the whole take. The fallbacks are hardcoded adult-human metres; the client's subject is ~12 cm tall, so the fallback thigh is 11.6x the measured one. Separately fit_bone_lengths' own default is fill_missing=True (bonefit.py:62); only the two production callers pass False, so any future caller gets the invention behaviour by default.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Measured targets used on this take (mm): spine 41.08, neck-head 21.86, neck-shoulder 15.97/16.01, upper arm 22.41/22.52, forearm 21.35/19.96, pelvis-hip 8.32/8.31, thigh 37.17/35.49, shin 37.11/36.84. 0 of 14 bones fell back. Simulation (NaN the LEFT_ANKLE in every frame): the L-shin target becomes 0.44 m for a 0.037 m bone (11.9x). With fill_missing=False the damage is contained (LEFT_KNEE moves 0.31 mm median / 1.22 mm max), but with the library default fill_missing=True the ankle is placed exactly 0.4400 m from the knee and the frame's max joint-pair span goes 0.128 -> 0.4722 m (3.7x).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Replace the absolute-metre table with PROPORTIONS of an observed reference: fallback_bone_lengths(reference_m: float) -> dict, reference being a bone that WAS measured (prefer the pelvis-neck spine, else the largest measured bone), with the table storing ratios (thigh = 1.05 x spine, shin = 1.07 x spine, ...). pipeline.py:160 then calls fallback_bone_lengths(measured[(PELVIS, NECK)]). Flip bonefit.py:62 to fill_missing: bool = False so the safe behaviour is the default, and have fit_project emit a user-visible note naming any bone that fell back (rejection_note is the pattern).
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/probe.json (H4_fallback, H4_sim_missing_left_shin, H4_sim_fill_missing_true)

## F23 [major] resolve_calibration picks the lowest-id common tag in the first frame with no ambiguity check; on this scene tag 13 is ambiguous in 96% of solves and the wrong IPPE branch destroys the reconstruction

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:143 | category: calibration-extrinsics | sources: extrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: resolve_calibration returns the first frame's numerically-lowest common tag and accepts cv2.solvePnP's lower-reprojection-error IPPE branch with no ambiguity check, no cross-frame consistency check, and no report of which tag/frame/branch won (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:134,142,143,153; pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:87-92). On this scene the check is already needed: for tag 13 in the left camera the two IPPE branches are 67-68 deg apart and the shipped solver returns the WRONG one in 9 of 26 frames (35%), ground-truthed against the static-scene requirement that the tag13->tag14 transform be constant (it swings 68.4 deg, while tag15->14 swings 1.2 deg and tag17->14 2.0 deg). The take escaped unharmed only because tag 14 is the sole tag ever common to both views (common == [14] in 10 of the 11 frames that have any common tag, [14,15] in frame 0026) and tag 14's branches are well separated (err1/err0 = 11.92 left, 9.27 right at frame 0001 — the solve that produced the shipped rig, matched by exhaustive search to 0.000000 deg / 0.000000 mm). Impact on the client take is therefore exactly zero and F23 does not explain the reported retargeting complaint. Had the right camera also seen tag 13, sorted(common)[0] would have deterministically preferred it — the worst tag in the scene — and a wrong branch costs 63.7-67.5 deg of extrinsic error, 214-285 px median body epipolar error and 84-94% of observations dropped by validate_cross_view: an empty 3D view with no diagnostic beyond the generic rejection_note.

Two refinements to the original finding. (i) solvePnP is not returning an arbitrary solution — it is bit-identical to solvePnPGeneric solution 0 in 97/97 solves with errors sorted ascending, so it is a maximum-likelihood pick that is right 65% of the time on tag 13: a loaded lottery, not a coin flip. (ii) The proximate cause of tag 13's degeneracy is not viewing angle (obliquity 33.5 deg, unremarkable against the stable tags' 24.9-39.2 deg) but its planar corner-fit residual (err0 2.410 px vs 0.210-0.840 px for every other camera/tag), consistent with the tag being bent over the curved backdrop sweep — so the risk is not confined to face-on tags and any tag on the sweep can go ambiguous.

The proposed fix targets the real mechanism and both its signals are empirically validated here: an err1/err0 >= 2 gate separates tag 13 (median 1.26) from 14/15/17 (5.37-13.80) with zero overlap, and cross-frame consistency separates them 68.4 deg vs 1.2-2.0 deg. One addition the fix needs: because the cameras solve independently, the scoring must range over the JOINT (left-branch, right-branch) pair rather than per camera, and must not assume a shared wrong branch cancels — L1R1 gives the largest relative-pose error of all (103.93 deg) while still recovering a plausible 0.553 m baseline, defeating any baseline-only sanity check.

Severity major, but strictly as a latent per-session robustness defect, not as a cause of the observed client complaint.
- VERDICT REASON: The finding survives every check I threw at it, and my independent numbers are slightly WORSE than the ones it reports.

CODE, RE-READ MYSELF: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:134 `for frame in project.frames:` -> :142 `common = sorted(set(idl) & set(idr))` -> :143 `for tid in common:` -> :153 `return CalibrationResult(...)`. It is literally the first frame yielding a common tag, and within it the numerically lowest tag id; no scoring, no ambiguity test, no cross-frame check. estimate_extrinsics_for_marker (pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:87-92) calls cv2.solvePnP(..., flags=cv2.SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE) and returns the single rvec/tvec. Nothing inspects the second IPPE branch.

EXECUTED PATH, TRACED MYSELF: pose3d/ui/main_window.py:293-294 builds ImportDialog -> pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:141 _process -> :171 uses uploaded extrinsics only if the user supplied a file -> :175-178 otherwise calls resolve_calibration -> :210-215 triangulate/fit and save_rig(rig, folder/"calibration"). pose3d/app.py:75,81 _load_rig then reads that folder for every later session. My exhaustive search proves the shipped Imported_Session/calibration/extrinsics.json was produced by exactly this path.

COULD SOMETHING ELSE EXPLAIN IT? I tested the two obvious alternatives. (a) Detection failure: no - tag 13 is detected in all 26 left frames at 288 px side length. (b) Viewing geometry: no - tag 13's obliquity is 33.5 deg, squarely inside the 24.9-39.2 deg range of the three perfectly stable tags. The real discriminator is the planar corner-fit residual: err0 median 2.410 px for tag 13 vs 0.210-0.840 px for every other (camera, tag). Tag 13's imaged corners do not fit a planar square well - consistent with it being taped to the CURVED backdrop sweep - and that flattens the branch separation. This refines the mechanism rather than refuting it, and makes the risk broader than "face-on tags": any tag on the sweep can go ambiguous.

WHY IT IS NOT MERELY HYPOTHETICAL: I ground-truthed which tag-13 branch is correct (tags 14/17 are stable and the scene is static, so the tag13->tag14 transform must be constant) and found cv2.solvePnP returns the WRONG pose - off by 65-68 deg - in 9 of 26 left frames (35%). The same audit gives 26/26 and 6/6 correct for tags 17 and 15. The solver the app ships is already returning garbage for one of the four tags in this very scene; it escaped only because the right camera never sees tag 13.

TWO CORRECTIONS, neutral or against the finding: (1) "accepts whatever single solution cv2.solvePnP returns" / "coin flip" is imprecise - I verified over all 97 solves that plain solvePnP is bit-identical to solvePnPGeneric solution 0 and that errs[0] <= errs[1] always, so it returns the lower-reprojection-error branch: a maximum-likelihood pick that is right 65% of the time on tag 13, a loaded lottery rather than 50/50. (2) The finding's post-swap bone-CV numbers (12.3%/19.8%) do not reproduce (I get 2.7%/4.2%); that statistic is degenerate anyway once 84-94% of observations are dropped. Every load-bearing number (extrinsic error, epipolar, drop rate, provenance) reproduces.

SEVERITY: I confirm "major" but strictly as a LATENT defect. Impact on this take is exactly zero and F23 cannot explain the client's complaint that the character does not follow the keypoints. What earns major: the trigger is one framing change away (only 11 of 26 frames have any common tag at all), the selection rule is arbitrary and on this scene deterministically prefers the worst tag, the failure is total (94% of observations dropped, essentially empty 3D view), and it is silent - the user sees only the generic rejection_note.

FIX AIMED AT THE MECHANISM? Yes, and I validated both of its signals. The err1/err0 >= 2 gate separates tag 13 (median 1.26) from 14/15/17 (5.37-13.80) with zero overlap. Cross-frame consistency separates them 68.4 deg vs 1.2-2.0 deg. cv2.solvePnPGeneric is the right API and returns solutions already sorted by error. One addition the fix needs: because the two cameras solve independently, the scoring must range over the JOINT (left-branch, right-branch) pair, not per camera, and must not assume a shared wrong branch cancels - it does not (103.93 deg).
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All from my own scripts under <SP>/verify-F23/ (detect.py, analyse.py, mechanism.py, damage.py, errorrate.py) on workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, using the shipped K=[[4080,0,1536],[0,4080,2040],[0,0,1]], zero distortion, marker_length 0.05 m.

DETECTION / VISIBILITY (DICT_6X6_250, 52 images, 97 solves - matches the finding's 97): left tag13 26/26 frames, tag14 26/26, tag15 6/26, tag17 26/26; right tag14 11/26, tag15 2/26, nothing else.

WHAT resolve_calibration ACTUALLY PICKS: only 11 of 26 frames have any tag common to both views. common == [14] in 10 of them; frame 0026 is the only frame with common == [14,15]. So sorted(common)[0] is 14 in every case and the set of tags this take could ever select is exactly {14}. First hit is frame 0001. Confirms the finding's "got lucky".

SHIPPED RIG PROVENANCE (exhaustive match over all 97 solves x both branches vs calibration/extrinsics.json): left -> frame 0001, tag 14, branch 0, dR = 0.000000 deg, dt = 0.000000 mm; right -> frame 0001, tag 14, branch 0, dR = 0.000000 deg, dt = 0.000000 mm. Runner-ups 0.301 / 0.199 deg. Exactly as claimed.

AMBIGUITY, ALL 97 SOLVES: err1/err0 < 2 in 25.8% of solves, min ratio 1.024 (finding: 25.8%, 1.024 - exact match). Per (camera, tag) — median ratio / min / pct<2 / max swing of the CHOSEN pose across frames / camera-centre std:
  left  13: 1.26 / 1.02 / 96.2% / 68.3 deg / 339,158,6 mm
  left  14: 8.72 / 5.87 /  0.0% /  1.3 deg / 3,2,1 mm
  left  15: 5.37 / 4.73 /  0.0% /  0.7 deg / 1,2,2 mm
  left  17: 13.80 / 4.43 / 0.0% /  1.4 deg / 3,3,2 mm
  right 14: 13.41 / 9.27 / 0.0% /  1.3 deg / 2,3,3 mm
  right 15: 9.30 / 9.30 /  0.0% /  0.0 deg / 0,0,0 mm
The finding says tag 13's normal swings 46.0 deg; I measure 68.3 deg max pairwise (branch separation 67-68 deg). The finding UNDERSTATES this.

SOLVER SEMANTICS: plain cv2.solvePnP == solvePnPGeneric solution 0 in 97/97 solves, and errs[0] <= errs[1] in 97/97. The shipped code takes the lower-reprojection-error branch, not an arbitrary one.

GROUND-TRUTHED ERROR RATE (my key new number). Scene is static, so the tag13->tag14 transform in the left camera must be constant; I took the 5-deg consensus cluster (26/52 candidates) as truth. cv2.solvePnP returns the CORRECT tag-13 pose in 17/26 frames (65%) and the WRONG one, off by 65-68 deg, in 9/26 (35%). The correct branch is always within 0.0-3.0 deg and the wrong one always 65.0-67.9 deg - no grey zone. Control: branch 0 self-consistent in 26/26 frames for tag 17 and 6/6 for tag 15.

CROSS-TAG CONSISTENCY IN THE LEFT CAMERA (must be constant for a static rig): tag13->14 max swing 68.4 deg (INCONSISTENT); tag15->14 1.2 deg; tag17->14 2.0 deg.

MECHANISM DISCRIMINATION — mean imaged side length / mean obliquity / median err0 / median ratio:
  left 13: 287.9 px / 33.5 deg / 2.410 px / 1.26
  left 14: 330.0 px / 31.6 deg / 0.522 px / 8.72
  left 15: 384.3 px / 24.9 deg / 0.840 px / 5.37
  left 17: 266.5 px / 33.0 deg / 0.232 px / 13.80
  right 14: 168.2 px / 33.1 deg / 0.210 px / 13.41
  right 15: 152.9 px / 39.2 deg / 0.289 px / 9.30
Ratio is perfectly rank-inverse to err0 and uncorrelated with obliquity or size (tag 17 is the SMALLEST left tag and the most stable).

DAMAGE, all four branch combinations on the actually-chosen solve (tag 14, frame 0001), measured with the baseline metrics.py against a substituted calibration dir. Columns: dR_left / dR_right vs shipped | body epipolar median px | p90 px | observations dropped by validate_cross_view out of 390 | raw bone CV median:
  L0R0 (shipped):   0.00 /  0.00 deg |   4.9 |  12.5 |   0 ( 0.0%) | 5.3%
  L0R1:             0.00 / 67.47 deg | 214.0 | 464.5 | 327 (83.8%) | 4.2%
  L1R0:            63.69 /  0.00 deg | 284.6 | 584.5 | 367 (94.1%) | 2.7%
  L1R1:            63.69 / 67.47 deg |  89.6 | 143.1 | 263 (67.4%) | 35.0%
Finding claimed 63.69/284.6/94.6% and 67.47/214.0/84.3% - my extrinsic errors and epipolar medians match to the decimal, drop rates to 0.5 pp. Its bone-CV figures do NOT reproduce (2.7%/4.2% vs 12.3%/19.8%), but that statistic is degenerate once 84-94% of observations are gone.

JOINT-BRANCH EFFECT ON THE STEREO RELATIVE POSE (frame 0026, the only frame with a second common tag) — dR of R_rel vs shipped and recovered baseline (true 0.5590 m):
  tag14 L0R0  0.95 deg / 0.5520 m   |  tag15 L0R0  0.81 deg / 0.5560 m
  tag14 L0R1 66.23 deg / 0.3905 m   |  tag15 L0R1 79.19 deg / 0.4468 m
  tag14 L1R0 63.74 deg / 0.3904 m   |  tag15 L1R0 49.37 deg / 0.4468 m
  tag14 L1R1 103.93 deg / 0.5528 m  |  tag15 L1R1 101.31 deg / 0.5568 m
Both cameras picking the wrong branch does NOT cancel (103.93 deg), and L1R1 keeps a plausible-looking baseline while being maximally wrong - the most dangerous case for any baseline-based sanity check. Independently, tag 15 at frame 0026 with correct branches agrees with the shipped tag-14 rig to 0.81 deg, confirming the tag-14 choice on this take is sound.

CROSS-FRAME AGREEMENT OF THE SHIPPED CHOICE: across all 11 frames where tag 14 is common, branch-0/branch-0 relative pose differs from shipped by 0.00-1.13 deg and baseline 0.5496-0.5590 m. The chosen rig is stable.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: `for tid in sorted(common): ... return` takes the numerically LOWEST common tag id in the FIRST frame that yields one and accepts whatever single solution cv2.solvePnP returns. Nothing inspects the IPPE_SQUARE planar ambiguity. This take got lucky (only tag 14 was ever common, and its solve is well separated), but the same scene contains a tag whose solve is a coin flip.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: DICT_6X6_250 detected in all 52 images. Over all 97 (frame,tag,camera) solves with cv2.solvePnPGeneric/IPPE_SQUARE at the shipped intrinsics, the second solution's reprojection error is within 2x of the best in 25.8% of solves (min ratio 1.024). Per tag: left:13 ratio<2 in 96.2% of its 26 frames, recovered normal swings 46.0 deg across frames (camera-centre std 339/158/6 mm); left:14 0%, spread 0.81 deg; left:17 0%, 0.71; right:14 0%, 0.67. The shipped rig is exactly tag 14, frame 0001, IPPE solution 0, for BOTH cameras (matched to 0.0000 deg / 0.0000 mm by exhaustive search); at frame 0001 the tag-14 err ratios are 11.92 (left) and 9.27 (right). Substituting the OTHER IPPE branch: left -> 63.69 deg extrinsic error, body epipolar median 284.6 px, 94.6% of observations dropped by validate_cross_view, bone CV 12.3%; right -> 67.47 deg, epipolar 214.0 px, 84.3% dropped, CV 19.8%.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Replace cv2.solvePnP in estimate_extrinsics_for_marker (pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:87) with cv2.solvePnPGeneric(..., SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE) returning BOTH solutions and their reprojection errors. Give resolve_calibration a scoring loop instead of a first-hit return: for every (frame, tag) common to both views solve both branches in both cameras, score each candidate rig by (i) err1/err0 in each camera and (ii) cross-frame consistency of the resulting camera pose, and keep the best. Reject any tag whose ratio is < 2 in either camera unless no alternative exists, and report the chosen tag/frame and its ratio in CalibrationResult.message. sorted(common)[0] becomes redundant.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-extrinsics/h1_h3_h4_v2.json, perturb_h6.json

## F24 [major] The accuracy percentage and timeline bands are absolute-pixel thresholds, so a good reconstruction at phone resolution reads 73% RED / 0 green frames

- file: pose3d/ui/panels.py:33 | category: ui-feedback | sources: detection, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Confirmed as written on the code and the numbers, with three corrections. (1) TIMELINE: it is 0/26 green but 11 amber and 15 red, not 26/26 red — only the gauge/label is 26/26 "Low". (2) The evidence line "the raw two-view DLT residual is ~4 px by construction" is not right: a DLT point does reproject near its own observations, but 3.964 px is real 2D/calibration inconsistency, not a floor (a perfect rig would give ~0). The defect is that the threshold is absolute pixels while the residual scales with image resolution and with how large the subject is in frame — 0.66% of figure height here, identical in both cameras once normalised. (3) The impact line "This alone reproduces 'the 3D character does not follow the keypoints accurately'" is an overreach: the banding is a read-out, it does not move the character or alter any geometry. What it does do is make the only quality signal in the app saturate at "Low" on every frame of every take this rig can produce, so it cannot confirm a fix, cannot flag a regression, and misdirects manual correction. Two additions that strengthen the finding: the gauge scores f.pose3d (raw) while the 3D view renders f.fitted3d (model.py:186-188 vs 90/169) — scoring the displayed pose would read 3.8%; and the per-camera nanmean (model.py:189-191) averages pixel errors from views where the subject is 776 px and 407 px tall, weighting the left camera 1.84x. The proposed fix (normalise by image diagonal, better by detected figure height in px, and give _refresh_timeline_status matching fractional thresholds) is aimed at the actual mechanism; note that error/figure-height is the better normaliser of the two, since it also removes the left/right camera-distance bias, which error/image-diagonal does not.
- VERDICT REASON: I re-derived the code path myself and reproduced every headline number to within rounding, so the core claim survives. Code, read directly: `accuracy_pct(err_px) = clip(100*exp(-err_px/6.0))` at pose3d/ui/panels.py:33-37; `ACC_HIGH, ACC_MED = 85.0, 70.0` at panels.py:21; `acc_band` at panels.py:40-49 is the single banding source, feeding `acc_color`/`acc_label` (panels.py:56-61), `JointAccuracyList.update_errors` (panels.py:164-174, whose return value is the gauge number) and `camera_view._joint_status` (camera_view.py:199-207, dot colour via RAG_COLORS at camera_view.py:25-30). Thresholds invert to 6*ln(100/85)=0.975 px for green and 6*ln(100/70)=2.140 px for amber — bare pixels, no image-size or figure-size term anywhere in panels.py. `MainWindow._refresh_timeline_status` (main_window.py:479-485) bands the per-frame WORST joint at literal `< 5` / `< 12` px. Executed path confirmed: `ProjectModel._accuracy` (model.py:180-192) is emitted from `set_frame` (model.py:91), `recompute_all` (model.py:135) and the drag re-solve (model.py:171); `main_window._wire` connects `accuracyChanged` -> `_on_accuracy` (main_window.py:203, 234-240) which drives the gauge, the JOINT ACCURACY list and BOTH camera views' dots; `_load_model` calls `set_frame(0)` and `_refresh_timeline_status()` on every project open (main_window.py:520-523), and the panels are unconditionally in the right column (main_window.py:185-188). So this fires for the client on load, with no toggle. No alternative explanation fits: the constant 6.0 is a bare literal, and I confirmed the effect is scale-driven, not quality-driven, by rescaling K and kp2d (a similarity that leaves the 3D reconstruction identical): the exact same take rendered at 1080p-equivalent (x0.265) reads gauge median 81.6% with 21/26 timeline frames GREEN and 46.6% of joints green, versus 52.3% and 0/26 green at native 4080. Two details of the finding are wrong or overstated and I corrected them below; two further defects I found make it worse, not better.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: My script (verify-F24/f24.py, f24b.py, f24c.py) reimplements panels.accuracy_pct/acc_band verbatim (PySide6-free) and calls the real pose3d.geometry.triangulate.reprojection_error on the stored raw pose3d, exactly as model.py:186-191 does (nanmean over the two cameras).
CLIENT TAKE, native 3072x4080 — median per-joint err 3.964 px -> median displayed accuracy 51.6%. Bands over 388 finite joint-frames: green 48 (12.4%), amber 58 (14.9%), red 282 (72.7%); +2 NaN joint-frames which acc_band also returns "red" (panels.py:47-48), so 284/390 = 72.8% red. Gauge (mean of finite pcts per frame): median 52.3, min 41.0, max 67.6 -> 26/26 frames band RED "Low". Timeline (worst-joint, 5/12 px): 0 green, 11 amber, 15 red; median per-frame worst joint 13.26 px. Per-joint median displayed accuracy: LEFT_ANKLE 24.0% (8.56 px), RIGHT_HIP 29.5% (7.34), LEFT_WRIST 36.0% (6.14), HEAD 50.6% (4.08), NECK 81.7% (1.21) — NECK is the only joint that even reaches amber. Every one of these matches the finding's evidence exactly.
SCALE COUNTERFACTUAL (same 3D, K and kp2d scaled): x0.471 (1920 long edge) -> median 1.87 px, 73.3%, 26.3% green, 10/26 timeline green; x0.265 (1080 long edge) -> median 1.05 px, 84.0%, 46.6% green joints, 4/26 gauge green, 21/26 timeline green, 0 timeline red. Identical reconstruction, opposite verdict.
PANOPTIC (ground-truth calibration, 1920x1080): median 4.83 px -> 44.7%, 75.0% of finite joint-frames red, 10/10 gauge frames red, 10/10 timeline red. Confirms the gauge cannot register a good rig either.
TWO EXTRA DEFECTS I FOUND. (a) model.py:186-188 scores `f.pose3d` (raw triangulation) while `pose3dChanged` (model.py:90, 169) renders `f.fitted3d`. Scoring the pose actually drawn gives median 19.7 px -> 3.8% displayed accuracy, i.e. the shipped readout is already the optimistic branch. (b) The nanmean over cameras (model.py:189-191) averages two incommensurate pixel scales: the mannequin spans 776 px in the left image but only 407 px in the right, so left median err is 5.11 px vs right 2.78 px — a 1.84x gap. Normalised by figure height those become 0.662% and 0.689%, i.e. the two cameras agree to 4% once scale is divided out. That is direct evidence the proposed normalisation targets the real mechanism.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: accuracy_pct(err_px) = 100*exp(-err_px/6.0) (panels.py:33-37) with ACC_HIGH, ACC_MED = 85.0, 70.0 (panels.py:21) means a joint is green only if its reprojection error is <= 6*ln(100/85) = 0.98 px and amber only below 2.14 px — absolute pixels with no reference to image size, unreachable on a 3072x4080 frame where the raw two-view DLT residual is ~4 px by construction. MainWindow._refresh_timeline_status (main_window.py:479-485) bands at absolute 5 px / 12 px. camera_view._joint_status (camera_view.py:200-219) uses the same band for the dot colour.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Reproducing model._accuracy (model.py:180-192) on the client take: median per-joint error 3.96 px -> median UI accuracy 51.6%. Bands over 390 joint-frames: green 48 (12.3%), amber 58 (14.9%), RED 284 (72.8%). Per-joint medians: LEFT_ANKLE 24.0%, RIGHT_HIP 29.5%, LEFT_WRIST 36.0%, HEAD 50.6%, only NECK 81.7% reaches amber. Frame level: gauge median 52.3% (min 41.0, max 67.6) -> 26/26 frames in the RED 'Low' band, 0/26 timeline-green — on a take whose geometry is good (3.96 px = 0.10% of the left diagonal, 0/388 pairs over the epipolar gate). The ground-truth-calibrated Panoptic reference reads 44.7% — also red. Correlation between the displayed accuracy and the independently measured RTMPose-to-red-dot offset is -0.063, i.e. none.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Normalise before banding. Change accuracy_pct(err_px) to accuracy_pct(err_px, image_diag_px) and score on err_px / image_diag_px — or better, on error as % of the detected figure height, which is what matters for the retarget (green <= 0.15% of figure height, amber <= 0.4%; equivalently express the 6 px decay as a fraction of the 1080p diagonal 2203 = 0.27%). Give _refresh_timeline_status fractional thresholds derived the same way. acc_band/acc_color/acc_label keep their signatures; callers to update: camera_view.py:205, panels.py:136 and the JOINT ACCURACY list. Test: a take with 4 px reprojection on a 4080 px image must NOT be majority-red.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-detection/ui_feedback.py, ui_feedback.json; <SP>/find-tests-ui/scale_blind.py/.json, ui_signals.json (per_joint_gauge_pct_raw)

## F32 [major] No test in CI runs calibration to retarget on realistic data with an accuracy threshold; the synthetic rig is nothing like the client's and the Panoptic validators are not collected

- file: tests/synth.py:47 | category: testing | sources: tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: No collected test exercises the app's reconstruct path on client-like data with an accuracy threshold, and CI cannot run one today.

(a) The app's import path (pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:141 `_process` -> build_project -> resolve_calibration -> RTMPose -> `triangulate_project` -> `fit_project(project, smooth=True)` at lines 212-213) has its final call covered by ZERO collected tests: the only `fit_project` call under tests/ is tests/test_bonefit.py:132 with `smooth=False`, asserting only that a sparse frame does not abort. The default smoothing path — which I measure to contribute 21.8% of body height of max displacement and to make fitted3d reproject 4.24x (left) / 6.17x (right) worse than raw — is guarded solely by tests/test_bonefit.py:53-60 `sm_var < raw_var`.

(b) The camera geometry gap is wider than the finding states. `default_two_cam` (tests/synth.py:47) with `make_intrinsics` (tests/synth.py:13) is the only rig used by the triangulation, cross-view and corrections suites, and every one of them passes a SINGLE Intrinsics object for both cameras (test_triangulate.py:13-14, test_cross_view.py:27-28). The bonefit, orientation and retarget suites use no camera at all (test_bonefit.py:8, test_orient.py:7, test_orient_up.py:14, test_retarget.py:17 import only `sample_skeleton_3d`/`rot_about`). Measured contrast: synth is a 3.857 m baseline at 79.6 deg, 3.01 m away, subject filling 67.2% of a 1280x720 frame at f=900 with identical cameras; the client is a 0.559 m baseline at 63.1 deg, 0.533 m away, subject filling 23.9% of frame, LEFT f=4080@3072x4080 and RIGHT f=2048@1536x2048. tests/test_cross_view.py:26's scale=4 is a geometric no-op (K and image size scale together), changing only `epipolar_threshold` (pose3d/pipeline.py:57-60). tests/test_calib_quality.py:78-81 is the only place the asymmetric 3072x4080 / 1536x2048 pair appears, and it is asserted only as a `check_rig` string.

(c) The Panoptic validators are doubly dead, not just mis-named: pyproject.toml's `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` sets no `python_files`, so `validate_*.py` is never collected (verified: "no tests collected"), AND .github/workflows/windows-test.yml explicitly `--ignore`s both files plus build_demo_project.py, AND `data/hdPose3d/` is gitignored (.gitignore:30) with `data/demo_project/calibration/` untracked. Renaming alone changes nothing in CI.

(d) The suite's one brush with real client data, tests/test_import.py:110-121, is a no-op in CI: `assets/1_10.jpg` is untracked (`git ls-files assets` -> only checkerboard.md), so it skips on the runner, and it asserts only that ArUco detection succeeded.

(e) Correction to the impact statement: the suite does contain accuracy numbers (test_triangulate.py:27 max err < 1e-6; :38 mean < 0.01 m at 0.5 px noise; test_retarget.py:107 < 0.5% of height, :127 < 1%), so a pure math regression in DLT or skinning would be caught. But they run on ideal input — test_retarget's subject is `_subject_from_rig(ch)`, proportions identical to the rig by construction, and 0.5 px noise at f=900/3 m is roughly 24x gentler than the client's ~12 px-equivalent detector noise. Nothing in CI moves for a change to the smoother, the focal assumption, the tag/extrinsics selection, or camera asymmetry.

(f) The proposed fix targets the right mechanism, and the fixture is necessary (workspace/ is gitignored at .gitignore:43; project.json is 199 KB plus 1.5 KB of calibration). Two adjustments: two of its six assertions (reproj ratio <=1.5, max|fitted-nosmooth| <=3% of height) FAIL today at 4.24/6.17 and 21.8%, so they are post-fix regression guards that cannot land before the smoother is changed; and three thresholds sit within 5-15% of today's values (CV median 5.5 vs 5.27, forearm asym 7.0 vs 6.72, epipolar p90 14 vs 12.48), which will flake. Since every geometric threshold already passes, the fixture test as specified would lock in the status quo, not diagnose the client's "character does not follow the keypoints" complaint — only the two smoother ratios have teeth. The Panoptic rename must also remove the two `--ignore` lines from windows-test.yml and commit the calibration, or it stays dead.
- VERDICT REASON: Core claim reproduced in full; two sub-claims are imprecise in ways that UNDERSTATE the gap, and the proposed fix is aimed at the right mechanism but is not landable as written.

CONFIRMED BY MY OWN RUNS/READS:
1. Config: /media/athena/hd3/Projects/pose3d-tool/pyproject.toml `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` sets only `pythonpath=["."]` and `testpaths=["tests"]` — no `python_files`, so the default `test_*.py` applies. `pytest --collect-only -q tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py` -> "no tests collected in 0.31s". Full suite: `183 passed, 6 skipped in 8.33s`, exit 0; `--collect-only -q` -> `189 tests collected in 1.07s`. Matches the artifact exactly.
2. tests/synth.py:13 `make_intrinsics(w=1280,h=720,f=900.0)` and tests/synth.py:47 `default_two_cam(baseline_deg=80.0, radius=3.0, height=1.0)` read as claimed. tests/test_cross_view.py:23-28 calls `default_two_cam()` then `K = geo["K"].copy()*scale` with scale=4 — extrinsics untouched. Scaling K and image size together is a geometric no-op (same normalized rays, same subject fill fraction); only `epipolar_threshold` (pose3d/pipeline.py:57-60, a fraction of the image diagonal) changes. So the "phone size" test does not reproduce any client-like framing.
3. No test file references the client take, demo_project or hdPose3d except tests/build_demo_project.py:27,30 and the two uncollected validators (grep over tests/*.py).

STRONGER THAN THE FINDING CLAIMS (three additions):
4. Renaming the validators would still not run them in CI: .github/workflows/windows-test.yml runs `pytest tests/ -q --ignore=tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py --ignore=tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py --ignore=tests/build_demo_project.py`, and .gitignore:30 ignores `data/hdPose3d/`, so the runner has no Panoptic data. `git ls-files data` tracks only 8 files; `data/demo_project/calibration/` and `data/calibration_171204_pose1_sample.json` are untracked.
5. The one test that touches real client imagery, tests/test_import.py:110-121 `test_resolve_autodetects_6x6_on_client_images`, is dead in CI: `git ls-files assets` returns only `assets/checkerboard.md`; `assets/1_10.jpg` (2.2 MB) is untracked. It passes locally and skips on the runner, and it asserts only `res.ok` / `status == "aruco"` — no accuracy.
6. `fit_project(project, smooth=True)` — the app's actual reconstruct call at pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:212-213, reached from `_process` (line 141) via build_project -> resolve_calibration -> detect -> triangulate_project -> fit_project — is called by ZERO collected tests. The only `fit_project` call under tests/ is tests/test_bonefit.py:132, with `smooth=False`, asserting only that a sparse frame does not abort. `grep -rn smooth tests/*.py` gives exactly two smoothing tests: test_bonefit.py:53 (`sm_var < raw_var`) and :63 (nan-safe). The finding's characterisation is right.
7. Every geometry test passes ONE Intrinsics object for both cameras (tests/test_triangulate.py:11-14 `triangulate_points(pl, pr, intr, intr, ...)`; test_cross_view.py:27-28 `CalibratedRig(intr, intr, ...)`). The client rig is asymmetric — I read it from the shipped calibration: left f=4080 @ 3072x4080, right f=2048 @ 1536x2048. An asymmetric-rig bug is structurally invisible to the suite. tests/test_calib_quality.py:78-81 builds exactly that 3072x4080 / 1536x2048 pair but feeds it only to `check_rig` and asserts `"resolution" in msgs` — confirming the finding's "never exercised through the pipeline".

IMPRECISIONS (both understate the gap, neither refutes):
8. "the only camera geometry the whole suite uses for triangulation, cross-view, bonefit, orientation, retarget and corrections" — triangulation (test_triangulate.py:13), cross-view (test_cross_view.py:23, test_triangulate.py:77) and corrections (test_corrections.py:13) do use `default_two_cam`. But bonefit, orientation and retarget use NO camera at all: tests/test_bonefit.py:8, test_orient.py:7, test_orient_up.py:14, test_retarget.py:17 import only `sample_skeleton_3d`/`rot_about` and operate on 3D arrays. And tests/test_extrinsics.py:38 (`_cam`, f=900, 1280x720) and test_calib_quality.py define separate cameras. So "only" is literally false; the substantive point is worse than stated.
9. Impact "no number in CI that would move if the reconstruction got better or worse" — overstated in general. tests/test_triangulate.py:27 (max err < 1e-6, noiseless) and :38 (mean < 0.01 m at 0.5 px noise), and tests/test_retarget.py:107 (< 0.5% of height) / :127 (< 1%) ARE accuracy numbers. But they are all on ideal synthetic input: test_retarget's subject is `_subject_from_rig(ch)`, i.e. derived from the rig itself (perfect proportions), and 0.5 px noise at f=900/3 m is ~24x gentler than the client's ~12 px-equivalent detector noise. The claim is correct in its intended narrow form: no accuracy number on realistic data, and none of these would move for a change to the smoother, the focal choice, or the calibration selection.

FIX CRITIQUE: aimed at the right mechanism (a committed kp2d+corners+calibration fixture is necessary — `workspace/` is gitignored at .gitignore:43, so the take cannot simply be read in place; project.json is 199 KB + 1.5 KB calibration, so "a few hundred KB, no RTMPose at test time" is accurate). But (a) 2 of the 6 proposed assertions FAIL on today's code, so the test cannot land before the smoother fix; (b) 3 of the geometric thresholds sit within 5-15% of today's values (brittle); (c) all the geometric thresholds pass today, so they lock in the status quo rather than catch the client's complaint — only the two smoother ratios have teeth; (d) renaming the validators is insufficient without also removing the `--ignore` lines in windows-test.yml and committing the Panoptic calibration.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Suite state (my runs, repo root, .venv/bin/python):
- `pytest -q -p no:randomly`: 183 passed, 6 skipped, 8.33 s, exit 0. Skips: 5x tests/test_export_smoke.py (Blender missing), 1x tests/test_retarget.py:533 (Blender/character.blend). No Panoptic/client skips exist.
- `pytest --collect-only -q`: 189 tests, 1.07 s.
- `pytest --collect-only -q tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py`: "no tests collected in 0.31s".

Rig geometry, computed by me (synth vs client), script at <SP>/verify-F32/f32.py:
- SYNTH (default_two_cam): baseline 3.857 m, parallax 79.6 deg, dist 3.012 m, subject height 1.620 m, f=900, size 1280x720, subject 484 px = 67.2% of frame height. Same Intrinsics for both cameras.
- CLIENT (shipped calibration + stored pose3d): baseline 0.559 m, parallax 63.1 deg, dist 0.533 m, subject span 0.1277 m, LEFT f=4080 @ 3072x4080, RIGHT f=2048 @ 1536x2048, subject 977 px = 23.9% of frame height. Asymmetric K and image size.
  -> 6.9x baseline ratio, 5.7x distance ratio, 2.8x fill-fraction ratio, and an asymmetry the suite never constructs.

check_rig on the client rig (pose3d/calib/quality.py, my run): 3 messages fire — world up 89.3 deg off vertical, "intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size", "the two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048)". `looks_assumed` True for both. So the app DOES warn; nothing asserts a consequence.

Client-take metrics I recomputed (26 frames, <SP>/verify-F32/f32b.py, f32c.py; all reproduce the given baseline):

- L/R asymmetry: thigh 4.62%, shin 0.72%, upper arm 0.48%, forearm 6.72%
- body Sampson epipolar: median 4.91 px, p90 12.48 px, n=388, gate threshold 71.5 px
- reprojection medians: left raw 5.11 / nosmooth 7.38 / fitted 21.65 px (fitted/raw = 4.24); right raw 2.78 / nosmooth 4.01 / fitted 17.18 px (fitted/raw = 6.17)
- max |fitted - nosmooth| = 0.0257 m = 21.8% of the 0.118 m de-tilted body height (32.6% of the 0.0788 m raw per-frame z-extent)

Proposed-fix thresholds evaluated against today's code:
  raw CV median <=5.5%   -> 5.27  PASS (4% margin)
  raw CV max <=10%       -> 9.52  PASS (5% margin)
  L/R asym <=7% each     -> 6.72 worst  PASS (4% margin)

  epipolar p90 <=14 px   -> 12.48 PASS (11% margin)

  reproj(fitted)/reproj(raw) <=1.5 -> 4.24 / 6.17  FAIL

Repo/CI facts I checked: .gitignore:43 `workspace/`; .gitignore:30 `data/hdPose3d/`; `git ls-files assets` -> only assets/checkerboard.md; `git ls-files data` -> 8 files, no calibration dir; .github/workflows/windows-test.yml passes `--ignore` for both validators and build_demo_project.py.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: default_two_cam(baseline_deg=80.0, radius=3.0) with make_intrinsics(w=1280, h=720, f=900.0) is the only camera geometry the whole suite uses for triangulation, cross-view, bonefit, orientation, retarget and corrections. The client's rig is a 0.559 m baseline at 62.5 deg parallax, f=4080 on 3072x4080 and f=2048 on 1536x2048, subject 0.12 m across at 0.6-0.8 m. tests/test_cross_view.py:26 scales K by 4 to reach 'phone size' but leaves the 3 m radius and 80 deg baseline untouched. tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py and validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py are named validate_*, so pytest's default python_files = test_*.py never collects them.

No test file references workspace/pose3d_projects, data/demo_project or hdPose3d except build_demo_project.py and the two uncollected validators. Passing CI green today: the EMA smoother contributing 98% of the deviation from the measurement, shortening the L forearm 19% in frame 0013 and making fitted3d reproject 4.2x worse than raw (the only smoothing assertion is sm_var < raw_var, tests/test_bonefit.py:53-60); the assumed f being +14-18% long (test_calib_quality.py:66-73 asserts only that it is reported); single-tag IPPE ambiguity (tests/test_extrinsics.py uses one 0.30 m marker at 1.2 m with f=900 and asserts only that the centre is recovered); absolute metric scale (no assertion anywhere); the 2x resolution mismatch (reported by check_rig, never exercised through the pipeline).

Rename tests/validate_panoptic_geometry.py to test_panoptic_geometry.py so pytest collects it, gated with tests/gates.py when data/hdPose3d is absent. Add close_range_two_cam(baseline=0.56, dist=0.7, f=4080, size=(3072,4080)) to tests/synth.py and parametrise the triangulate/cross-view/bonefit suites over both rigs.
