## F08 [minor] Only a uniform scale is fitted, and the rig's segment ratios differ from the mannequin's by up to 25%; that mismatch accounts for essentially 100% of the measured per-joint retarget error

- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:353 | category: retarget | sources: retarget | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: fit_to_subject (character.py:328-354, scalar assignment at :353) fits ONE uniform scale for the whole take and _bone_fk (character.py:474-492) then rotates each bone while keeping its rest length, so the rig's segment PROPORTIONS are baked into every posed frame: the posed character's canonical bone lengths equal rig_len/113.62 to 5 decimals, while the mannequin's differ by -25.0% (NECK->HEAD, partly definitional), +20.4/+20.7% (PELVIS->HIP), +11.1% (PELVIS->NECK), -8.8/-10.4% (upper arms), +7.3% (R thigh), -5.6% (L shin). This is on the live path (main_window.py:507 -> view3d.py:98 for the view, blender_export.py:76 for the export). It is NOT ~100% of the retarget error: an oracle subject rebuilt with the rig's exact proportions still retargets at an all-joint median of 0.70% of height against the 1.51% measured, so the proportion mismatch explains about half the median overall — ~88-98% at elbows, wrists and neck (NECK 4.11->0.28, R_ELBOW 2.17->0.05), ~45% at the right knee, ~1-22% at the hips, and 0% at the shoulders (which get worse, 1.02->1.96). Nor does the error simply accumulate: a chain-propagation model over-predicts elbow/wrist error by 2.8-4.2x (5.35/5.53% predicted vs 1.90/1.32% measured) because every bone re-aims at its own captured joint and resets most of the parent's error. The residual floor is a different defect: the hip and clavicle roots are rigid rest offsets from the hips/chest heads (hips.tail->thigh.L.head = 1.326 rig units, chest.tail->clavicle.L.head = 0.975) and no _DIRECT bone aims at the hips, so neither proposed fix touches them — my per-bone axial stretch leaves L/R_HIP at 2.10/3.50 bit-unchanged (all-joint median 1.51->1.19), and fix (b) makes them worse (2.10->3.10, 3.50->5.39) for a median of 1.09. Net proportion-attributable effect is ~0.8% of body height median (~0.9 mm on this 118 mm subject), peaking near 3.8% at the neck: a genuine accuracy floor, but an order of magnitude below the EMA smoother's 5.3% mean / 21.7% max displacement, so it does not explain the client's complaint.
- VERDICT REASON: MECHANISM AND PATH: confirmed by my own read. character.py:328-354 `fit_to_subject` accumulates num/den over ALL bones and sets `self._scale = float(num/den)` (line 353) — one scalar, no per-bone term; `self._scale` is the only fit state (230, 356-357). character.py:474-492 `_bone_fk` takes `rest_d = self.tail[b]-self.head[b]` (482), `R = _align(rest_d/n_rest, d_want/n_want)` (488), skin = pure rotation + head pin (489-491) — rig length kept, as claimed. Live path: main_window.py:487 `_apply_view_orientation` -> :507 `self.view3d.fit_subject(np.stack(poses))` (from `_load_model` :519 and on pose change :276/:281) -> view3d.py:85-98 `fit_to_subject`; posing at view3d.py:197-204; export at blender_export.py:76. Both view and export.
PROPORTION MISMATCH: reproduced bone-for-bone (scale 113.62): NECK->HEAD -25.0, PELVIS->L_HIP +20.4, PELVIS->R_HIP +20.7, PELVIS->NECK +11.1, R_SH->R_EL -10.4, L_SH->L_EL -8.8, R_HIP->R_KNEE +7.3, L_KNEE->L_ANK -5.6, R_EL->R_WR +5.3. Per-bone rig/subject ratios span 85.2-137.1 against the single 113.62. Scale itself is not mis-fitted (114.55 without NECK->HEAD, 114.34 also without both PELVIS->HIP, 113.73 unweighted). And it is genuinely baked in: the POSED character's bone lengths equal rig_len/scale to 5 decimals for 12/14 bones.
WHAT I REFUTE: "essentially 100%" and "accumulates down each chain". I built an oracle the finding did not: a subject rebuilt per frame from the CAPTURED directions but the RIG's lengths (zero proportion mismatch by construction). Residual retarget error is not zero — all-joint median 0.70% of height vs 1.51% measured. So proportions explain ~53% of the median, wildly uneven per joint: NECK 4.11->0.28 (93%), R_ELBOW 2.17->0.05 (98%), L_ELBOW 1.90->0.23 (88%), L_WRIST 1.32->0.13 (90%), R_WRIST 0.74->0.03 (96%), R_KNEE 3.44->1.89 (45%), L_KNEE 1.13->1.08 (4%), L_HIP 2.10->1.63 (22%), R_HIP 3.50->3.46 (1%), SHOULDERS get WORSE (1.02/1.05 -> 1.96/1.96, 0%). HEAD 12.58->9.71 is definitional. The floor is structural: hips.tail->thigh.L.head = 1.326 rig units and chest.tail->clavicle.L.head = 0.975 are RIGID rest offsets, and no _DIRECT entry aims anything at the hips, so no length correction can move them.
"Accumulates" is quantitatively wrong: my chain-propagation model predicts 5.35/5.53% at L elbow/wrist where the character delivers 1.90/1.32% — 2.8-4.2x over-prediction, because each bone re-aims at its own captured joint and resets most of the parent's positional error. measured/predicted: NECK 1.07 (the finding's 1.07), but elbows 0.36/0.38, wrists 0.24/0.15, ankles 0.28/0.48.
FIX AIM: (a) is aimed at the right mechanism but incomplete. My StretchCharacter (k = subject_len*scale/rest_len on _DIRECT bones, R @ (I+(k-1)a0a0^T)) gives all-joint median 1.51 -> 1.19 (not 1.41 -> 0.87), elbows 1.90/2.17 -> 0.29/0.28, wrists 1.32/0.74 -> 0.48/0.25, R_KNEE 3.44 -> 1.89, but L/R_HIP 2.10/3.50 bit-unchanged and HEAD 12.58 -> 12.41. Required k range 0.75-1.41 (spine 1.41, chest 0.75), wider than the claimed 0.93-1.12. I also ran fix (b) for real: fit_project(bone_lengths=rig/scale) -> all-joint median 1.09, NECK ->0.29, elbows ->0.45/0.62, but L/R_HIP get WORSE (2.10->3.10, 3.50->5.39) while the exported skeleton moves only 1.4 mm off the measurement. Both fixes leave the largest non-head residual (RIGHT_HIP 3.5%) untouched.
SEVERITY: downgraded to minor. Proportion-attributable error is ~0.8% of body height median (~0.9 mm on a 118 mm subject), peaking ~3.8% at the neck — an order of magnitude below the EMA smoother's already-measured 5.3% mean / 21.7% max displacement, so it is a real accuracy floor but not a plausible explanation of the client's complaint.

- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Rig lengths / fitted scale (113.62) vs subject medians, as % of the subject's bone: NECK->HEAD -25.0, L_SHOULDER->L_ELBOW -8.8, R_SHOULDER->R_ELBOW -10.4, R_ELBOW->R_WRIST +5.3, PELVIS->NECK +11.1, PELVIS->L_HIP +20.4, PELVIS->R_HIP +20.7, R_HIP->R_KNEE +7.3, L_KNEE->L_ANKLE -5.6. Attribution: summing (rig_len/scale - subject_len) along the captured chain reproduces the MEASURED error — measured/predicted = 1.07 (NECK), 1.13 (L elbow), 1.07 (R elbow), 1.20 (R knee); wrists/ankles 0.31-0.80 because successive mismatches partly cancel. Control: a StretchCharacter with one per-bone axial factor cuts the all-joint median from 1.41% to 0.87% of height and end effectors far further — L/R elbow 1.90/2.17 -> 0.50/0.17, L/R wrist 1.32/0.74 -> 0.60/0.13, R knee 3.44 -> 1.89. Required stretch factors 0.93-1.12. Note the uniform scale itself is NOT mis-fitted (scale 113.62; removing the two definitionally-mismatched bones moves it to 114.55/114.34, under 1%).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): (a) Give Character a per-bone length factor: extend fit_to_subject to store self._k[bone] = subject_len * scale / rig_len for each bone in _DIRECT and apply it in _bone_fk as R @ (I + (k-1) a0 a0^T) — pure axial stretch, no shear (prototype scale_prototype.py); or (b) do what commit 02e8a44 claims and pass the RIG's proportions into the skeleton fit — pipeline.fit_project already accepts a bone_lengths dict, so hand it {k: Character().rig_bone_lengths()[k] / scale} after an initial scale estimate. (a) keeps fitted3d an honest measurement and is preferable; (b) makes the exported skeleton the rig's. If (a) is taken, _SCALE_WEIGHTS' leg weighting becomes redundant. See F19: rig_bone_lengths is currently used nowhere except inside the uniform-scale fit.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/probe.json, probe6.py/.json, scale_prototype.py/.json

## F09 [minor] smooth_temporal resurrects joints the bone fit deliberately refused to invent, freezing them at the previous frame's position (20-39 mm = 17-33% of body height off)

- file: pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:140 | category: smoothing | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `smooth_temporal` silently resurrects the joints the bone fit deliberately refused to invent, freezing each at the previous frame's SMOOTHED value and leaking that stale value forward through the EMA.

`fit_project` calls `fit_bone_lengths(..., fill_missing=False)` (pose3d/pipeline.py:171) so an unobserved joint stays NaN and is "not invented" (bonefit.py:71-75, 125; asserted by tests/test_bonefit.py:43-50). Twelve lines later, `smooth_temporal` (pipeline.py:183) undoes it: `blend = np.where(np.isnan(cur), prev, blend)` (bonefit.py:140) copies `out[t-1]` — the previous frame's already-smoothed value — verbatim into the NaN slot.

On the client take this fires on 2 of 390 observations (0.51 %), both single-view (left kp2d present, right absent): frame 0012 RIGHT_KNEE and frame 0021 LEFT_ANKLE. Both are bitwise copies of the previous frame. Because the left camera DID observe both joints, the error is bounded from below without any ground truth: the invented point lies 7.06 mm (6.0 % of the 0.1178 m body height) and 9.16 mm (7.8 %) off the left camera's ray, reprojecting 46.9 px and 67.5 px from the observed keypoint — both worse than the WORST joint in the unsmoothed take (max 44.5 px). Against a linear interpolation of the neighbouring frames the offsets are 9.27 mm (7.9 %) and 19.15 mm (16.3 %); against the next frame's fitted position 20.21 mm (17.2 %) and 38.50 mm (32.7 %). Honest range: 7-38 mm = 6-33 % of body height. The 38.50 mm displacement is the single largest smoother-induced 3D move in the take (all other joints: median 4.92, max 25.65 mm).

Three consequences the original finding did not measure. (a) The invented joint re-breaks the bone-length constraint the fit had just enforced: thigh.R +5.57 %, shin.R -5.58 % at frame 0012, shin.L -6.02 % at frame 0021. (b) The staleness is not confined to one frame — it leaks forward through the EMA; a filter that restarted properly would put frame 0013's RIGHT_KNEE 8.09 mm elsewhere, decaying over ~8 frames (21 joint-frames affected in total, max 15.40 mm). (c) The two code paths already disagree about the policy, showing the fill is accidental: pose3d/ui/model.py:158-161 re-fits a single frame after a manual drag with `fill_missing=False` and NO smoothing, so one drag anywhere in frame 0012 makes RIGHT_KNEE revert to NaN and the limb vanish.

No warning reaches the user: `view3d.py:133` and `view3d.py:203` derive `valid` purely from `np.isnan`, and `main_window.py:323` counts reconstructed joints the same way, so frames 0012/0021 report 15/15. For frame 0012 this also suppresses the occlusion fallback — RIGHT_KNEE is the mid joint of the thigh.R/shin.R chain, and `character.py:653` takes two-bone IK only `if not valid[int(mid_j)]`. (This part of the original impact statement over-generalised: LEFT_ANKLE is that chain's END joint, so no IK path applies to the frame-0021 case.)

Fix as proposed and verified: delete bonefit.py:140, keep line 141. My patched smoother leaves exactly those 2 joints NaN and correctly restarts the filter afterwards; downstream already tolerates NaN. One caveat to state in the fix: the frame after a gap is then emitted unsmoothed (line 141 takes `cur` raw), which moves frame 0022 LEFT_ANKLE by 15.40 mm — correct restart behaviour, but a visible change. Any wanted fill must be an explicit, flagged interpolation step.
- VERDICT REASON: CONFIRMED in mechanism, executed path and (with corrections) magnitude; the headline number and one sub-claim are wrong in detail.

CODE, re-derived by reading it myself:
- `pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:118-126` — `fit_bone_lengths(..., fill_missing=False)` sets `fitted[~observed] = np.nan  # do not invent un-observed joints` (line 125); docstring lines 71-75 state the intent verbatim.
- `pose3d/pipeline.py:171` — `fit_bone_lengths(f.pose3d, bone_lengths, fill_missing=False)`; `pipeline.py:183` — `fitted = smooth_temporal(fitted, alpha=alpha)` on the very next block.
- `pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:136-142` — `prev, cur = out[t-1], poses3d[t]`; line 138 `blend = alpha*cur + (1-alpha)*prev`; **line 140 `blend = np.where(np.isnan(cur), prev, blend)`**; line 141 `blend = np.where(np.isnan(prev), cur, blend)`. Note `prev` is `out[t-1]`, the already-SMOOTHED previous frame, so the resurrected value is the previous frame's smoothed value, not its fitted value.
- `tests/test_bonefit.py:43-50` `test_fit_no_fill_leaves_missing_joint_nan` asserts `np.isnan(fitted[6]).all()` — the contract the smoother then violates. No test covers NaN through `smooth_temporal`.

EXECUTED PATH — confirmed, both entry points:
- `pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:213-214` → `triangulate_project(project, rig); fit_project(project, smooth=True)`.
- `pose3d/ui/model.py:53-55` `recompute_all` → `fit_project(self.project, smooth=True)`.
- Result reaches the UI: `model.py:90/134/170` emit `f.fitted3d`; `main_window.py:202` connects that to `view3d.set_pose`; `main_window.py:450` `self.view3d.set_pose(f.fitted3d, f.head3d)`.
- The "no warning reaches the user" claim holds: `view3d.py:133` `valid = ~np.isnan(pose3d).any(1)` and `view3d.py:203` `valid = ~np.isnan(vpose).any(1)` (fed to `Character.pose_and_joints`), and `main_window.py:323` counts reconstructed joints as `(~np.isnan(f.fitted3d).any(1)).sum()`. With the NaN gone, frames 0012/0021 report 15/15 joints and the character treats the frozen joint as measured.

WHAT I CORRECT:
1. The "20-39 mm" figures are the distance to the NEXT frame's fitted position — that is the full inter-frame displacement, not an error. I bounded it properly instead (see own_measurements): a hard geometric lower bound of 7.06 / 9.16 mm (the left camera DID see both joints; the true 3D point must lie on its ray) and 9.27 / 19.15 mm against a linear interpolation of the neighbouring frames. Honest range 7-38 mm = 6-33 % of the 0.1178 m body height. 2D detector noise is ~0.15 mm/px here (f=4080, d≈0.6 m), so even the 7 mm lower bound is ~50x the noise floor and cannot be explained away.
2. The IK sub-claim is only half right. `character.py:94-99` `_IK_CHAINS` and `character.py:653` `if not valid[int(mid_j)]` — RIGHT_KNEE IS the mid joint of the thigh.R/shin.R chain, so the frame-0012 resurrection does suppress the two-bone IK fallback. LEFT_ANKLE is that chain's END joint, not a mid joint, so no IK path exists for it; the impact statement over-generalises to both cases.
3. Frequency is small on this take: 2 of 390 observations (0.51 %), 2 of 26 frames. It is not a candidate explanation for the client's general "does not follow the keypoints" complaint — within the shipped `fitted3d` the two frozen joints reproject at 46.9 / 67.5 px in the left view against a p90 of 72.5 px for ALL joints, i.e. they are NOT outliers relative to the smoother's ordinary lag. In 3D-displacement terms they are: 38.50 mm exceeds the 25.65 mm maximum `smooth - nosmooth` displacement of every other joint in the take.

THINGS THE FINDING MISSED (support it further):
4. The invented joint breaks the bone-length constraint the fit had just enforced: frame 0012 RIGHT_HIP-RIGHT_KNEE +5.57 %, RIGHT_KNEE-RIGHT_ANKLE -5.58 %; frame 0021 LEFT_KNEE-LEFT_ANKLE -6.02 % (vs each bone's own median over the take).
5. The staleness is not confined to one frame — it leaks forward through the EMA. Patching out line 140 changes 21 joint-frames, up to 15.40 mm; frame 0013 RIGHT_KNEE moves 8.09 mm and the trail decays over ~8 frames.
6. The two code paths already disagree about the policy, which shows the fill is accidental rather than designed: `model.py:158-161` re-fits a single frame after a manual drag with `fill_missing=False` and NO smoothing, so one drag anywhere in frame 0012 makes RIGHT_KNEE revert to NaN and the limb pop out of existence.

COULD SOMETHING ELSE EXPLAIN IT? No. I re-implemented `smooth_temporal` with line 140 deleted; it reproduces the 2 NaNs exactly and nothing else changes structurally. The NaNs originate upstream in triangulation (right-view kp2d absent, left present, for both) — not from `validate_cross_view`, which drops 0 on the stored 2D.

FIX: aimed at the actual mechanism and correct. Deleting line 140 while keeping line 141 leaves both joints NaN and correctly restarts the filter at the next real observation (verified: `fixed_nan` = exactly [0012 RIGHT_KNEE, 0021 LEFT_ANKLE]). Downstream already tolerates NaN — `refit-nosmooth` retargets fine and `view3d`/`Character` derive `valid` from `isnan`. One caveat the fix should state: deleting line 140 also means the frame AFTER a gap is emitted unsmoothed (line 141 takes `cur` raw), which is why frame 0022 LEFT_ANKLE moves 15.40 mm; that is correct filter-restart behaviour, not a regression, but it is a visible change.

SEVERITY: downgraded major -> minor. It is a genuine silent data-invention bug with a one-line fix, but on this take it touches 0.51 % of observations and is not distinguishable from the smoother's ordinary lag in reprojection terms. It would scale to major on a take with realistic occlusion — this take has an unusually good 99.5 % 3D coverage, and the right camera is the weak one.

So the LEFT_ANKLE freeze is the single largest smoother-induced 3D displacement in the take; the RIGHT_KNEE freeze is ~p98.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: fit_project calls fit_bone_lengths(..., fill_missing=False) (pipeline.py:171) precisely so an unobserved joint stays NaN and is 'not invented' (bonefit.py:72-75, tests/test_bonefit.py:44-51). The very next line, smooth_temporal, undoes that: `blend = np.where(np.isnan(cur), prev, blend)` (bonefit.py:140) copies the PREVIOUS frame's value verbatim into the NaN slot. The joint is silently invented, frozen at a stale position, and indistinguishable downstream from a real measurement.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: refit(smooth=False) has 2 NaN joints; refit(smooth=True) has 0. Both resurrected: frame 0012 RIGHT_KNEE and frame 0021 LEFT_ANKLE, equals_prev_frame = True (exact copy). Distance from the invented point to that joint's next real fitted position: 20.21 mm (17.2% of height) for RIGHT_KNEE, 38.50 mm (32.7%) for LEFT_ANKLE. These are the SAME 2 of 390 observations present in only one view (0.51%), so the two defects compound.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): bonefit.py:140 — delete the `np.where(np.isnan(cur), prev, blend)` line so a NaN in the current frame stays NaN, keeping only line 141 (`np.where(np.isnan(prev), cur, blend)`) which correctly restarts the filter after a gap. Test: a sequence with a NaN joint in the middle frame must still have NaN there after smoothing. Any genuinely wanted fill must come from an explicit, labelled interpolation step that sets a 'filled' flag the 3D view draws differently.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/probe.json (EXTRA_nan_resurrection), probe2.json (C_single_view)

## F11 [minor] The ankle has no orientation source and the view grounds on the character's lowest MESH vertex, so the figure bobs by 11.2% of its own height across the take

- file: pose3d/ui/view3d.py:157 | category: retarget | sources: retarget | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: View3D.set_pose grounds on the character's lowest MESH vertex (`dz = float(verts[:, 2].min())`, pose3d/ui/view3d.py:157), and that vertex is a foot vertex in 26/26 frames. Because foot.L/foot.R are absent from `_DIRECT` (character.py:68-88) and from `_IK_CHAINS` (:93-98), each foot inherits its shin's matrix verbatim (verified: max|skin[foot.L]-skin[shin.L]| = 0 over 26 frames), so the ankle-to-sole vertical distance swings with the shin (r = 0.87 against the grounding-side shin's tilt off vertical, which genuinely ranges 16-86 deg). The result is a rigid VERTICAL TRANSLATION OF THE WHOLE SCENE against the fixed grid: |dz| median 7.3% of the character's height, range 3.6-13.2%, peak-to-peak 9.7% (8.7% median / 14.0% ptp if measured against the captured pose's z-extent). It is NOT a divergence between character and capture: view3d.py:159 and :161 subtract the same dz from the captured overlay and the character's joint overlay, and the measured character-vs-capture joint offset is a stable 1.27% of body height (ptp 1.06%). It therefore does not produce the client's "the character does not follow the keypoints" symptom, and it does not affect the Blender export, which applies no grounding at all (blender_export.py:84 "centring is irrelevant here") and whose job deletes the ground plane outright (blender_job.py:601-602). The defect is a cosmetic instability of the ground datum in the live 3D view only. The correct fix is proposed fix (1): ground on the posed rig's own lowest ANKLE minus the constant rest ankle-to-sole height (measured: 0.712 rig units = 4.9% of rig height), which zeroes the bob by construction. Proposed fix (2) — forcing the sole horizontal — should be dropped: on this rigid-footed mannequin, whose shin genuinely tilts up to 86 deg, it would degrade pose fidelity to fix a datum that (1) already fixes.
- VERDICT REASON: MECHANISM CONFIRMED, IMPACT REFUTED, EXPORT CLAIM REFUTED. Re-derived from code I read myself: (a) `View3D.set_pose` computes `dz = float(verts[:, 2].min())` at pose3d/ui/view3d.py:157 after grounding the captured pose on its lowest valid JOINT at :146; (b) it is on the executed path — pose3d/ui/main_window.py:202 `self.model.pose3dChanged.connect(self.view3d.set_pose)` and main_window.py:450 `self.view3d.set_pose(f.fitted3d, f.head3d)`; (c) `foot.L`/`foot.R` resolve to real bones (indices 15/18) but appear in `_ROLE_ALIASES` (character.py:62-63) and `_JOINT_FROM_RIG` (:121-122) only — they are absent from `_DIRECT` (character.py:68-88) and from `_IK_CHAINS` (:93-98), so they fall through `skin[b] = base if end is None else self._bone_fk(...)` (character.py:~669) and inherit the shin matrix verbatim. I verified numerically: max|skin[foot.L] - skin[shin.L]| = 0 exactly over all 26 frames. (d) Feet are excluded by design, skeleton.py:59-60 "feet are intentionally excluded".

WHAT I COULD NOT SUSTAIN:
1. The impact sentence "the whole character rises and sinks ... relative to the grid WHILE THE CAPTURED SKELETON DOES NOT" is FALSE. view3d.py:158-161 subtracts the SAME dz from the mesh (`verts[:, 2] -= dz`), from the captured points (`v[:, 2] -= dz`, :159) and from the character's own joint overlay (`cj[:, 2] -= dz`, :161). The whole scene translates rigidly. I measured the character-vs-capture registration (dz-invariant by construction, and confirmed): median 3D joint offset 1.27% of character height (range 0.79-1.85%, ptp 1.06%); ankle vertical offset median 0.49%, max 1.67%. There is no relative decoupling, so this cannot read as "the character does not follow the keypoints". The capture overlay is also hidden by default (view3d.py:59,63), so the user normally sees only the character translating against the grid — cosmetic.
2. "It is also why the exported turntable shows the figure floating or sinking into the ground plane" is FALSE. `_character_bone_frames` (pose3d/export/blender_export.py:36-88) applies NO grounding whatsoever — it emits bone matrices only, and its own comment at :84 says "upright; centring is irrelevant here". There is no `.min()`/floor code in blender_export.py or blender_job.py. blender_job.py:601-602 additionally DELETES the ground plane from the template ("drop the ground plane + any pre-existing camera"). view3d.py:157 cannot be the cause of anything in the export.
3. ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATION that the finding calls "arbitrary": |dz| correlates r=0.87 with the grounding-side shin's tilt off vertical, which is genuine measured leg motion (median 25.1 deg, range 16.2-86.0 deg). The subject is a rigid mannequin whose foot IS rigidly bolted to the shin, so a foot that swings with the shin is arguably correct subject behaviour. The defect is narrower than stated: not "the foot is wrong", but "the lowest mesh vertex is not a stable ground datum once the foot can leave horizontal".
4. Proposed fix (2) ("sole parallel to the ground plane") is aimed at the wrong thing and would REDUCE fidelity: it would force a horizontal foot onto a leg genuinely tilted up to 86 deg. Fix (1) alone (ground on the ankle minus a fixed rest ankle-to-sole height) fully removes the bob by construction — I measured that constant: rest shin-tail z minus rest lowest vertex z = 0.712 rig units = 4.9% of rig height.
5. One quoted number did not reproduce: R foot sole tilt max 87.1 deg, not 108.0 (L max 74.0 reproduced exactly).
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Independent script (stdin-piped, nothing written to the repo), fitted3d + sequence_up/de_tilt_matrix + Character.fit_to_subject, replicating view3d.set_pose line for line over all 26 frames of workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session:

BOB (dz): median |dz| = 7.31% of the posed character's z-extent, min 3.57%, max 13.23%, ptp 9.66%. In metres: dz median -0.0106 m, ptp 0.0131 m on a 0.1396 m mean character height. Using the CAPTURED pose z-extent (0.1159 m) as the denominator instead: median 8.65%, ptp 14.0%. The finding's "median 9.0%, range 4.0-15.1, ptp 11.2%" sits between my two denominators — same order, core claim reproduced.
Per-frame |dz| (% of char height): 0001-0011: 8.1 7.1 7.5 6.5 6.9 7.1 7.6 9.6 10.4 12.6 11.4; 0012-0026: 4.0 3.6 3.9 3.8 4.5 5.5 7.6 9.9 12.3 13.2 11.9 9.1 6.9 4.9 3.6.

LOWEST VERTEX: a foot-bone vertex in 26/26 frames (foot.L 16, foot.R 10). Lowest captured joint is always an ankle (LEFT_ANKLE 15, RIGHT_ANKLE 11) — so the finding's "sometimes the heel, sometimes the toe-tip" is at least always the foot.

FOOT ORIENTATION: sole normal (rest +Z) tilt off vertical — median 32.1 deg L / 30.4 deg R (finding: 32.1 / 30.4, exact match), max 74.0 L (match) / 87.1 R (finding claims 108.0 — NOT reproduced). Foot bone axis off horizontal: median 38.5 L / 42.7 R, max 73.5 / 83.9; rig REST foot axis is already 28.2 deg off horizontal.
skin[foot.L] == skin[shin.L] bit-for-bit (max abs diff 0) in all 26 frames; eparent(foot.L) = shin.L.

DRIVER: corr(|dz|%, grounding-side shin tilt off vertical) = 0.87. Grounding-side shin tilt median 25.1 deg, range 16.2-86.0 deg.

NO DECOUPLING FROM THE CAPTURE: median character-joint-to-captured-joint distance 1.27% of char height (0.79-1.85%, ptp 1.06%); median vertical component 0.85%; ankle vertical component median 0.49%, max 1.67%. Captured-ankle height above the grid = |dz| exactly (median 7.60%, ptp 9.66%) and character-ankle height above grid median 7.02%, ptp 8.32% — i.e. the two track each other to ~1%.

FIX (1) CONSTANT: rest ankle (shin.L tail z = -7.4478) minus rest lowest vertex z (-8.1600) = 0.7122 rig units = 4.9% of rig_h (14.4228). Grounding on the ankle minus this constant gives a fixed ankle-to-grid height, ptp 0 by construction.

CODE READ: view3d.py:129-166 (set_pose, dz at :157, shared shift at :158-161), :59/:63 capture overlay hidden by default, :180-186 camera framed once; main_window.py:202, :450, :487-507; character.py:62-63, 68-88, 93-98, 121-122, 154-232 (_skin_matrices tail ~660-672), 474-492 (_bone_fk); skeleton.py:55-76; blender_export.py:36-88; blender_job.py:600-602.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: View3D.set_pose grounds the figure with dz = verts[:,2].min() (view3d.py:156-159), i.e. on whatever mesh vertex happens to be lowest. Because foot.L/foot.R get no aim (absent from _DIRECT, character.py:69-88) and the canonical skeleton has no foot keypoints (skeleton.py:58-76, 'feet are intentionally excluded'), the foot's world direction is entirely the shin's arbitrary roll, so the lowest vertex is sometimes the heel, sometimes the toe-tip of a foot pointing 74-108 deg out of horizontal.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Vertical distance from the captured ankle to the character's lowest vertex over 26 frames: median 9.0% of body height, range 4.0-15.1%, peak-to-peak 11.2%. Foot sole tilt off horizontal: median 32.1 deg (L) / 30.4 (R), max 74.0 / 108.0. The roll prototype barely helps (peak-to-peak still 11.2%) because the shin's bend plane is not a foot orientation — the information is simply absent.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): (1) Ground on the ANKLE, not the lowest vertex: in View3D.set_pose use the posed rig's own LEFT/RIGHT_ANKLE joints minus a fixed rest ankle-to-sole height (readable from character.npz once), so grounding no longer depends on foot orientation. (2) Constrain the ankle: give foot.L/foot.R a roll/aim rule — sole parallel to the ground plane, toe direction from the pelvis forward axis — until real foot keypoints exist. (3) The durable fix is a foot-bearing detector layout (Halpe-26 gives big toe / small toe / heel, see F04) plus ankle-to-toe as a real bone, at which point (2) becomes redundant.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/probe2.json, probe5.py/.json, contact_front.png, contact_side.png

## F12 [minor] The character's hip line is 11.8 deg off the captured hip line in every frame because the global yaw is fitted to the SHOULDERS only and the pelvis is aimed with a minimal rotation

- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:564 | category: retarget | sources: retarget | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The retarget has no degree of freedom for a hip-vs-shoulder torso twist: Rz is fitted to the shoulder line alone (character.py:561-571, target rig_right from character.py:191-194) and applied to the whole pose by to_rig (character.py:573-574), while the pelvis is oriented by the minimal rotation R_hips = _align(self._rest_torso, want) (character.py:619), which adds no roll about the torso axis. Because the rig's rest hip line is exactly perpendicular to _rest_torso, the character's hip line always carries the shoulders' yaw: measured |character hip yaw - character shoulder yaw| = 0.30 deg median / 1.39 max over 26/26 frames, with an axial component of exactly 0.000. On the client take the captured hip line is genuinely twisted 10.0 deg median (21.7 max) from the shoulder line - and this is signal, not noise: a Monte-Carlo 2D perturbation at a realistic 2-3 px only moves the hip line 2.3-3.4 deg, and the captured hip yaw is as temporally smooth as the shoulder yaw (3.63 vs 4.00 deg frame-to-frame). The result is a character hip line 11.8 deg median / 21.2 max off the captured one (23/26 frames over 5 deg, not "every frame"), while the shoulder line is right to 0.16 deg. The consequence is a pelvis-mesh twist plus a ~1.7 mm displacement of the two thigh roots - only 1.4% of the 118 mm body height, because the hip line is a 16.3 mm segment. Crucially, _bone_fk (character.py:474-491) derives every bone's rotation from its REST direction, not from its parent's, so the pelvis roll does not propagate into the spine, chest or thigh orientations - only into the child heads' placement and the hips-weighted mesh. Adding a hips roll reference is the right fix for the mechanism, but its payoff is bounded: a perfect roll leaves 4.5 deg median / 14.7 max (the captured hip line is up to 14.7 deg off perpendicular to the captured torso, which no roll can express), and it moves the hip joint position error only from 2.20%/3.57% to about 1.93%/3.31% of body height, because a 19% hip-width proportion mismatch between the rig and the fitted subject (rig rest 2.207 units vs captured 1.85) co-dominates that residual.
- VERDICT REASON: MECHANISM: re-derived independently and confirmed exactly at the cited lines. character.py:561-571 builds Rz from LEFT_SHOULDER/RIGHT_SHOULDER only (target self.rig_right, which is itself the RIG's shoulder line: character.py:191-194 takes it from head[upper_arm.L] -> head[upper_arm.R]); character.py:573-574 applies that single yaw to the WHOLE pose inside to_rig; character.py:612-621 sets the hips skin matrix to R_hips = _align(self._rest_torso, want) (character.py:619), and _align (character.py:131-144) is the minimal rotation, whose axis is perpendicular to both vectors, so it adds no rotation about the torso direction. The rig's rest hip line is exactly perpendicular to _rest_torso (I measured 90.000 deg), so the posed hip line has, by construction, zero component along the torso axis and a yaw fixed by Rz alone. Measured proof, not just algebra: over 26/26 frames the character's hip-line yaw equals the character's shoulder-line yaw to 0.30 deg median / 1.39 deg max, and the character hip line's axial (along-torso) component is 0.000 in every frame while the captured one is 0.075 median / 0.254 max. There is indeed no DOF for a hip-vs-shoulder roll.
EXECUTED PATH: yes. view3d.py:202-204 calls Character.pose_and_joints -> _skin_matrices for the 3D view, and blender_export.py:51/87 calls pose_bone_matrices -> the same _skin_matrices for the export, both after the same sequence_up/de_tilt (view3d.py:85-99, blender_export.py:57-73). So it is baked into both preview and FBX/BVH.
MAGNITUDE: reproduced with my own script on the client take. Character hip line vs captured hip line: 11.76 deg median, 18.46 p90, 21.18 max (raw source: 12.62 / 18.54 / 21.92). Shoulder line, which Rz is fitted to: 0.159 deg median, 0.513 max. Captured hip-minus-shoulder yaw: 10.00 deg median, 16.86 p90, 21.72 max (claim said 11.0/17.3/21.7 - the 11 is the full 3D angle, which I get as 11.91 median).
ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATION TESTED AND REJECTED: the hip line is only 16.3 mm long (13.8% of the 118 mm body height), so I checked whether the twist is 2D-keypoint noise rather than signal. Monte-Carlo (60 trials x 26 frames, Gaussian noise added to the stored hip/shoulder kp2d in both views, re-triangulated with the shipped rig): induced hip-line angular perturbation is 1.15 deg median at sigma=1 px, 2.34 at 2 px, 3.39 at 3 px - an order of magnitude short of 11.8 deg (the take's body epipolar median is 4.9 px, so per-view sigma is ~2-3 px). Temporal check: frame-to-frame |delta yaw| of the captured hip line is 3.63 deg median, essentially the same as the shoulder line's 4.00 deg, i.e. the hip yaw sequence is as temporally coherent as the shoulder one. The twist is signal (real mannequin torso twist and/or a systematic RTMPose hip-placement bias - my data cannot separate those; the sign is positive in 21/26 frames, mean +6.4 deg), not jitter.
WHAT I CORRECT: (1) "every frame" is false - 23/26 frames exceed 5 deg, 15/26 exceed 10 deg, min 0.34 deg. (2) The propagation story needs fixing: _bone_fk (character.py:474-491) computes each bone's rotation as _align(REST direction, want) - absolute, not relative to the parent - so the parent's rotation only sets the child's HEAD position (character.py:481), never its orientation. The pelvis roll therefore does NOT twist the spine, chest or thigh DIRECTIONS; it only displaces the child bone heads (which is where the reported LEFT_HIP/RIGHT_HIP come from, _JOINT_FROM_RIG thigh.L/thigh.R "head") and rotates the hips-weighted mesh. "Both thigh roots face up to 21 deg away" is right about the roots' placement, wrong if read as the thighs being rotated. (3) Impact is overstated in positional terms: 11.8 deg across a 16.3 mm line is a 1.7 mm endpoint displacement = 1.4% of body height. I measure hip joint errors of 2.20% (L) / 3.57% (R) of height, and the claimed prototype only reaches 1.93/3.31 - a 0.2 pp gain. A second, roll-immune cause co-dominates: the rig's rest hip line is 2.207 rig units while the captured hip line scaled by the fitted scale (113.62) is 1.85, a 19% proportion mismatch, and the captured hip line is up to 14.7 deg off perpendicular to the captured torso while the character's is exactly perpendicular - which is exactly why the residual after a perfect roll is still 4.5 deg median / 14.7 max (I reproduce the claim's 4.5/14.7 analytically).
FIX AIM: correct mechanism (an explicit roll reference for the hips bone is the missing DOF), but note its measurable payoff is confined to the hips: for chest/upper_arm/forearm a roll reference changes nothing in joint positions (their joints are heads/tails on the bone axis), only mesh appearance.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: <SP>/verify-F12/f12.py, <SP>/verify-F12/noise.py; data <SP>/verify-F12/f12_fitted.json, f12_raw.json. Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, posed exactly as the app (sequence_up -> de_tilt_matrix -> Character().fit_to_subject -> posed_joints), fitted scale 113.620.
ANGLES (fitted source, n=26): character hip line vs captured hip line 11.76 median / 18.46 p90 / 21.18 max / 0.34 min; raw source 12.62 / 18.54 / 21.92 / 2.20. Character shoulder line vs captured shoulder line 0.159 median / 0.513 max (0.161 / 1.269 raw). Frames over 5/10/15 deg: 23/15/8 of 26.
CAPTURED TWIST: hip-minus-shoulder yaw 10.00 median / 16.86 p90 / 21.72 max / -17.10 min, mean +6.41, std 10.05, positive in 21/26. Full 3D angle between the two lines 11.91 median / 22.55 max.
NO-ROLL-DOF PROOF: |character hip yaw - character shoulder yaw| = 0.303 deg median, 1.386 max over 26 frames. Character hip line axial component along the captured torso = 0.000 in all 26 frames; captured = 0.075 median, 0.254 max. Rig rest: hip line vs _rest_torso 90.000 deg, shoulder line vs _rest_torso 90.000 deg, rig_right = [-1.0, 0.0].
ROLL-FIX CEILING: best rotation about the captured torso axis needs 7.81 deg median (|roll| up to 16.5); residual hip-line error after applying it 4.505 median / 11.87 p90 / 14.705 max - reproducing the claim's prototype figures without running the prototype.
SCALE/PROPORTION: captured hip width 0.0163 m median (13.8% of the 0.118 m height), shoulder width 0.0316 m; rig rest hip width 2.207 units vs captured*scale 1.85 units (19% mismatch). Hip joint position error: LEFT_HIP 2.202% median / 4.094 max of height, RIGHT_HIP 3.570 / 6.507; shoulders 1.007 / 1.022 median.
NOISE MONTE-CARLO (60 trials x 26 frames, Gaussian px noise on stored kp2d hips+shoulders in both views, re-triangulated): hip-line angular perturbation median/p90 = 0.59/1.23 deg at sigma 0.5 px, 1.15/2.46 at 1 px, 2.34/4.79 at 2 px, 3.39/7.28 at 3 px; shoulder line about half that. TEMPORAL: frame-to-frame |delta yaw| hips 3.63 median / 12.41 p90 / 32.38 max vs shoulders 4.00 / 7.49 / 10.28; frame-to-frame hip joint motion 7.68 mm median.
CODE READ MYSELF: character.py:191-194 (rig_right from upper_arm.L/R heads), 561-571 (Rz from shoulders), 573-574 (to_rig applies Rz to everything), 612-621 (R_hips = _align(_rest_torso, want), hips skin), 131-144 (_align minimal rotation), 474-491 (_bone_fk aims from REST, so parent rotation moves only the child's head), 205-214 (_rest_torso from rest joints), 100-124 (_JOINT_FROM_RIG: LEFT_HIP/RIGHT_HIP = thigh.L/thigh.R "head"). Path: view3d.py:202-204 and blender_export.py:51/87.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: Rz is computed from the shoulder line alone (character.py:561-571) and applied to the WHOLE pose by to_rig (character.py:573-574); the pelvis is then oriented by R_hips = _align(self._rest_torso, want) (character.py:619), a minimal rotation onto the torso direction which adds no rotation about that direction. No degree of freedom anywhere can represent a hip-vs-shoulder yaw difference, so the pelvis is forced to carry the shoulders' yaw.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Captured hip-line-vs-shoulder-line yaw: 11.0 deg median, 17.3 p90, 21.7 max over 26/26 frames. Resulting angle between the character's LEFT_HIP->RIGHT_HIP and the captured one: 11.8 deg median, 21.2 max — while the shoulder line, which Rz is fitted to, is correct to 0.2 deg median / 0.5 deg max. Adding a hip-line roll reference to the hips bone (RollCharacter) cuts hip-line error to 4.5 deg median / 14.7 max and hip joint positions from 2.10%/3.50% to 1.93%/3.31% of body height.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Same API change as F02: give the hips bone an explicit roll reference (the captured LEFT_HIP->RIGHT_HIP line matched against the rig's rest hip line) inside _skin_matrices, and give chest the shoulder line. Once every bone carries a roll reference the global Rz from the shoulders (character.py:561-571) is only an initial gauge for to_rig and could be replaced by the pelvis-frame roll. Prototype: roll_prototype.py (_LINE table).
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/probe.json, roll_prototype.py, roll_prototype.json

## F17 [minor] Absolute metric scale is set by an unvalidated spinbox default and no signal in the app or the test suite can detect it being wrong

- file: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:108 | category: calibration-extrinsics | sources: tests-ui, extrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The absolute metric scale of a take is set entirely by an unvalidated, unrecorded spinbox default, and nothing in the app, the exported character, or the test suite can detect it being wrong — but the exported BVH/FBX are NOT affected, so the practical damage is smaller than claimed.

Mechanism (verified): main_window.py:293 -> ImportDialog -> import_dialog.py:108 setValue(0.05) (range 0.005-2.0, no validator) -> import_dialog.py:177 marker_length -> resolve.py:144 -> extrinsics.py:85 marker_object_points (L = marker_length/2, exactly linear) -> solvePnP. Changing 0.05 -> 0.10 leaves both R matrices bit-identical and multiplies both t vectors by exactly 2.0, moving every joint by 274.76 mm median (max 368.96) and doubling the median per-frame span 0.1277 -> 0.2553 m. resolve_calibration at 0.05 reproduces the take's shipped extrinsics bit-for-bit, so the shipped rig was in fact built at the spinbox default.

Invisibility (verified): epipolar median (4.907927 px, identical to full precision), reprojection median (5.107/2.783 px, delta ~1e-4), the pose-accuracy gauge's own input (model.py:180 _accuracy, median 3.9644 px, worst per-joint delta 0.017 px), bone-length CV (5.27%), and validate_cross_view drops (0) are all unchanged. check_rig (quality.py:53-84) warns about tilt, assumed intrinsics and resolution mismatch, never about scale. The tag size, tag id, dictionary and source frame are written nowhere: extrinsics.json holds only R and t, project.json only [name, fps, calibration_ref, frames].

CORRECTION to the impact: the exported character is scale-invariant by the same normalisation as the 3D view. blender_export.py:36-89 calls Character.fit_to_subject (scale 111.10 -> 55.55, exactly 1/2) and then pose_bone_matrices, which returns matrices "in RIG space" (character.py:706); blender_job.py:616-624 drives the armature from those, and the aim-only fallback normalises identically (blender_job.py:487, scale = rig_h/our_h). Measured max difference of every exported bone matrix between marker=0.05 and 0.10: 1.03e-3 rig units. The only scale-carrying deliverable is <name>_poses.json (blender_export.py:93-109), which differs by up to 0.331 m, plus the skeleton-fallback Blender path that only runs when character mode fails. So the wrong tag size never reaches the animation the client retargets; it corrupts the raw-pose JSON and makes the tool's advertised "real-world units" (extrinsics.py:4-5) unverifiable.

CORRECTION to the evidence: the "2.395 tag widths in both 3D and pixels" cross-check does not hold up. 790.6 px is the nose->LEFT-ankle distance in frame 0001 only; the right ankle in the same frame gives 709.4 px and the median over all 52 (frame, ankle) samples is 761.5 px, i.e. ratio 2.307 rather than 2.395. The 3D side of the comparison is a tautology (3D length scales linearly with the assumed tag size), and the pixel side is depth-confounded: the subject sits at 0.577 m from the left camera vs 0.637 m for tag 14, a ~10% bias. The inferred true tag size (~0.078-0.084 m, reproduced: 0.078 gives 0.1868 m nose-to-ankle) still rests on the unverified "~20 cm mannequin" and on treating nose-to-ankle as full height.

ADDITION the finding missed: the pipeline is not actually scale-invariant everywhere. pipeline.py:158-161 and ui/model.py:175-178 substitute fallback_bone_lengths() — hard-coded human metres (bonefit.py:34-50: 0.43 m thigh, 0.55 m spine) — for any bone never observed. On a 0.12 m reconstruction that would inject a 0.43 m target into the fit and wreck the frame. It does not fire on this take (all 14 bones observed), but it means the scale-invariance the finding relies on is accidental.

CORRECTION to the proposed fix: (1) persisting the tag size, id, dictionary and source frame in extrinsics.json and (2) a metric readout (camera baseline 0.559 m, subject height) are both correctly aimed. (3) is not: inter-tag distances solved under an assumed marker_length scale by exactly the same factor as everything else, so a uniformly wrong tag size yields a zero residual in that check — it can only catch differently-sized or inconsistent tags. Only the last resort in the fix ("prompt for one measured distance") actually recovers absolute scale.
- VERDICT REASON: The mechanism reproduces exactly, but the finding's load-bearing impact sentence ("invisible until the client opens the exported BVH/FBX") is wrong: the BVH/FBX character export is scale-invariant too, so I could not confirm the stated harm.

WHAT I CONFIRMED, from code I read myself:
- Executed path is real and unique. pose3d/ui/main_window.py:293-294 is the only construction of ImportDialog. pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:106-108 builds the QDoubleSpinBox with setRange(0.005, 2.0)/setDecimals(3)/setValue(0.05) and attaches no validator or signal; import_dialog.py:175-178 passes self.marker.value() straight into resolve_calibration. resolve.py:96 repeats the same 0.05 as the API default; resolve.py:144-145 forwards it to estimate_extrinsics_for_marker; extrinsics.py:85 -> marker_object_points (extrinsics.py:41-53, L = marker_length/2, so the object points are exactly linear in it) -> extrinsics.py:87-88 solvePnP SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE. Nothing else in the repo sets a scale (grep for marker_length hits only resolve.py, extrinsics.py, import_dialog.py and tests).
- Not persisted: save_rig (resolve.py:44-49) writes only {left:{R,t},right:{R,t}}; the client take's extrinsics.json has keys [left,right] -> [R,t] and project.json has keys [name,fps,calibration_ref,frames]. The string "marker" appears in neither file.
- No detector: pose3d/calib/quality.py check_rig (:53-84) warns about world-up tilt, assumed intrinsics and resolution mismatch only — nothing about metric scale. model._accuracy (ui/model.py:180-191) is reprojection px, which feeds the gauge (panels.py:84-146).
- The 3D view renormalises: view3d.py:85 fit_subject -> character.fit_to_subject (character.py:328-354), whose scale is exactly inversely proportional to subject size, applied in to_rig (character.py:573-574).

WHERE THE FINDING IS WRONG:
1. The character BVH/FBX export is ALSO scale-invariant, by the same mechanism as the view. blender_export.py:36-89 _character_bone_frames calls ch.fit_to_subject(poses3d @ R) and then pose_bone_matrices, documented at character.py:706-708 as returning matrices "in RIG space"; blender_export.py:129-135 puts those in the job JSON and blender_job.py:616-624 drives the armature from them. I measured the max absolute difference of every bone matrix over all 26 frames between marker=0.05 and marker=0.10: 1.03e-3 in rig units (~0.05% of rig height) — numerical noise, not a 2x size change. The fallback aim-only path is normalised too (blender_job.py:483-487, scale = rig_h/our_h). The bundled character asset exists (pose3d/assets/character.blend), so the bone_frames path is the default. So a wrong tag size does NOT put the exported animation at the wrong metric size.
2. The only delivered artifact that actually carries the wrong scale is <name>_poses.json (blender_export.py:93-109, written unconditionally next to the BVH/FBX; data/demo_project/export/demo_poses.json is the shipped example): I measured up to 0.331 m of difference between the two marker sizes. Plus the skeleton-fallback Blender path, which is only reached when character mode fails.
3. The "scale-invariant cross-check" evidence does not survive re-measurement. Tag 14's side is 330.13 px in frame 0001 (their 330.1 reproduces), but 790.6 px is the nose->LEFT-ankle distance in that one frame; the RIGHT ankle in the SAME frame gives 709.4 px, and the median over all 52 (frame, ankle) samples is 761.5 px -> ratio 2.307, not 2.395. The 3D ratio 2.394 is a tautology (3D scales linearly with the assumed tag size by construction), and the pixel comparison is depth-confounded: the subject's pelvis is 0.577 m from the left camera centre vs 0.637 m for tag 14 (ratio 0.907), so the raw pixel ratio overstates the subject by ~10%. The "iff the tags really are 50 mm" statement is therefore not supported to the precision implied.
4. Proposed fix (3) is misaimed. Inter-tag distances solved under an assumed marker_length scale by exactly the same factor as everything else, so a uniformly wrong tag size produces a ZERO residual in that check. It can only catch tags of differing size or per-frame inconsistency, not the failure mode described. Only the fallback ("prompt for one measured distance") actually recovers absolute scale.
5. The finding also misses the one place the pipeline is genuinely NOT scale-invariant: pipeline.py:158-161 (and the same code at ui/model.py:175-178) substitutes fallback_bone_lengths() — hard-coded human metres, bonefit.py:34-50, e.g. 0.43 m thigh, 0.55 m spine — for any bone never observed. On a 0.12 m reconstruction one unobserved bone would inject a 0.43 m target into the fit. All 14 bones are observed on this take so it never fires here, but it means the tool's scale-invariance is accidental, not designed.

SEVERITY: downgraded major -> minor. The mechanism is real, unvalidated and unrecorded, and the tool's own docstring promises the opposite (extrinsics.py:4-5: "The known tag size gives real metric scale, so triangulated bone lengths come out in real-world units"). But the verified client-visible consequence is confined to one auxiliary JSON, it is orthogonal to the reported complaint (the character not following the keypoints), and the deliverable the client retargets is unaffected. It is a provenance/traceability defect, not an output defect.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Script: <SP>/verify-F17/f17.py and f17b.py (outputs f17.json, f17b.json). All on the real take, real images, via the real executed functions.

A. Marker size -> extrinsics (real path, resolve_calibration on the loaded project)
- marker=0.05/0.10/0.078 all return ok=True, status='aruco', approximate=True, message "Calibration estimated from ArUco marker 14 (frame 0001)."
- resolve_calibration(marker_length=0.05) reproduces the SHIPPED calibration BIT-FOR-BIT: max|dR|=0.0, max|dt|=0.0 for both cameras, max|dK|=0.0. => the shipped extrinsics were produced by this path at the spinbox default.
- 0.05 -> 0.10: max|dR| = 0.0 (both cameras); t ratio = [2.0, 2.0, 2.0] exactly, both cameras. t_left(0.05) = [-0.06921, -0.21392, 0.59577]; t_left(0.10) = 2x that.
- Camera-centre baseline: 0.5590 m at 0.05 (scales linearly).

B. Effect on the reconstruction (26 frames, stored kp2d, triangulate_project + fit_project)
- median per-joint 3D displacement 0.05 -> 0.10: 274.76 mm (max 368.96 mm). [matches the finding's 274.76]
- median per-frame joint span: 0.12766 m -> 0.25534 m (ratio 2.00009); at 0.078: 0.19916 m.
- nose-to-ankle median: 0.11972 -> 0.23945 m; at 0.078: 0.18677 m. (0.20 m nose-to-ankle would need ~0.0835 m tags.)

C. Every product signal is invariant (0.05 vs 0.10), measured, not assumed
- epipolar median: 4.90792744631201 px vs 4.90792744631201 px (identical to full precision)
- reprojection median: left 5.10701 vs 5.10688 px; right 2.78338 vs 2.78354 px
- app gauge input (ui/model.py:180 _accuracy, per-joint nanmean of both cameras): median 3.96439 vs 3.96438 px; worst per-(frame,joint) |delta| = 0.0167 px
- bone-length CV median: 5.2717% vs 5.2672%
- validate_cross_view dropped: 0 vs 0
=> "bit-identical" is an overstatement (differences are ~1e-4 px float noise), but the substance holds: nothing in the UI moves.

D. THE EXPORT IS INVARIANT TOO (the finding's key error)
- Character.fit_to_subject scale: 111.10 -> 55.55 (ratio 0.49995 = 1/2, exactly cancelling)
- pose_bone_matrices, i.e. the matrices that drive the exported BVH/FBX armature: max|delta| over 26 frames x all bones = 1.03e-3 rig units (0.05% of rig height). At 0.078: 5.37e-4.
- posed_joints differ by 0.2777 m, but only because _from_rig maps them BACK into subject metric space; the exported rig never leaves rig space.
- Scale-carrying artifact: _poses_to_json output (<name>_poses.json, shipped alongside BVH/FBX) max|delta| = 0.331 m.

E. Persistence
- project.json top keys: [name, fps, calibration_ref, frames]. calibration/extrinsics.json keys: [left, right] -> [R, t]. No marker size, no tag id, no dictionary, no source frame anywhere.

F. Pixel cross-check re-measured (frame 0001, left view, DICT_6X6_250)
- tag side lengths: id14 330.13 px, id13 288.01 px, id17 267.19 px (id14 matches the finding's 330.1)
- nose->LEFT ankle 790.61 px (the finding's 790.6); nose->RIGHT ankle 709.38 px, same frame
- median over all 52 (frame, ankle) samples: 761.50 px (min 371.9, max 1051.1) -> ratio to tag14 = 2.307, not 2.395
- depth confound: left camera centre to tag-14 origin 0.6368 m; to subject pelvis (median) 0.5773 m; ratio 0.9067 (subject ~10% closer, so it looks ~10% bigger per unit length).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: self.marker.setValue(0.05) (range 0.005-2.0, no validation) is the only place the world's scale is decided; it feeds resolve_calibration(marker_length=...) -> marker_object_points -> solvePnP and scales the extrinsic translations linearly. Every quality metric in the product (reprojection, epipolar, bone-length CV, retarget error, the gauge, the timeline) is scale-invariant, and the 3D view rescales the character per take (view3d.fit_subject, view3d.py:85), so a wrong tag size is invisible until the client opens the exported BVH/FBX. The value is not persisted anywhere.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Doubling the marker size (0.05 -> 0.10 m) moves every joint by 274.76 mm median and doubles the subject span 0.1277 -> 0.2553 m, while the gauge (52.3%), timeline (0 green / 11 amber / 15 red), reprojection median (3.96 px), epipolar median (4.91 px) and bone-length CV median (5.27%) are all bit-identical. The reconstruction's median per-frame joint span is 0.1277 m and nose-to-ankle 0.1197 m for a mannequin described as ~20 cm, implying a true tag size of roughly 0.078-0.084 m (INFERENCE — the physical tag was not measured). Scale-invariant cross-check: the mannequin is 2.395 tag widths nose-to-ankle in both 3D and the raw left-image pixel ratio (790.6/330.1 px), so nose-to-ankle = 0.1198 m if and only if the tags really are 50 mm. The focal cannot explain the gap: across f_l = 2500-4600 the reconstructed span only moves 138.2 -> 124.2 mm, the wrong direction.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): (1) resolve_calibration returns the tag size it used and save_rig (resolve.py:36) writes it into extrinsics.json with the tag id, dictionary name and source frame id (see F30). (2) The sidebar shows a metric readout the user can check with a ruler: baseline between camera centres (Extrinsics.camera_center) and reconstructed subject height, both in cm. (3) Add an operator-free scale cross-check: with >=2 tags visible in one view, solve each tag's pose under the assumed marker_length and compare inter-tag distances across frames — inconsistent scale shows up as a residual. Failing that, prompt for one measured distance (tag edge or mannequin height) and solve for the scale factor.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-tests-ui/ui_signals.json (wrong_rig_sweep, entry 'marker size 0.05 -> 0.10 m'); <SP>/find-extrinsics/h1_h3_h4_v2.json, SUMMARY.json

## F20 [minor] The two cameras are DIFFERENT devices at different resolutions (left 3072x4080 Pixel with EXIF, right 1536x2048 with none) and the focal source is decided per camera, which is what produced the 26.6 px epipolar failure reverted in f4c92f9

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:116 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics, extrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The two cameras ARE different devices at different resolutions (left 3072x4080 Pixel 10 Pro with full EXIF, focal_from_exif = 2833 px; right 1536x2048 JFIF-only, focal_from_exif = None on all 26 files), and quality.check_rig (quality.py:74-83) correctly fires the resolution-mismatch warning on this take. resolve_calibration does build the two K's independently from frames[0] only (resolve.py:109-118, per-camera _approx_intrinsics at :116 and :117).

But CURRENT code has no per-camera focal SOURCE to mix: _approx_intrinsics(image) (resolve.py:70,86-90) takes no path and focal_from_exif has zero non-test callers in the package (only tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py). Both cameras get f = max(w,h), and because both images are exactly 3:4 that rule is already FOV-matched: 4080/2048 = 1.9922 vs the diagonal ratio 1.9950, 0.14% apart. The current code therefore already satisfies the finding's own proposed fix on this take. The provenance-mixing defect existed only in e4708bf (`f = focal_from_exif(path) if path is not None else None`, per camera), where it shipped (2833, 2048) = ratio 1.383, and was reverted in f4c92f9.

I reproduce the historical failure exactly: (2833,2048) gives 26.55 px median body epipolar and 37.9% of the 388 pairs over the OLD flat 30 px gate, versus 4.91 px and 0% for (4080,2048). But f4c92f9 also replaced that gate with a diagonal-scaled one (pipeline.py:57, 71.5 px here), and under the current gate the same broken focal drops only 2.1%. The "emptied the 3D view" failure mode is closed by the threshold change independently of the focal, so the residual issue is a latent hazard for a future focal-selection feature, not a live severe fault.

The finding's claim that the data support a focal ratio of 1.74-1.87 and that the shipped 1.99 is therefore wrong does NOT reproduce. The essential-matrix singular-value valley over the same 388 body correspondences is estimator-dependent and brackets the shipped value: 1.87-2.14 with RANSAC-F (at fl=4080 the optimum is fr=2045, ratio 1.995), 1.94-2.46 with 8-point F. With the extrinsics free (E refit per focal pair) the Sampson residual is flat at 3.90-5.49 px over fl 2200-5600 x fr 1100-2900 with no consistent ratio: the body data do not identify the focal. Only the single-tag-PnP grid, which entangles extrinsics with focal, lands on 1.787 — and I do reproduce that grid minimum exactly at (4200,2350) = 4.49 px.

Measured cost of the shipped focal pair on the client take: 0.42 px of median body epipolar (4.91 vs 4.49 at the grid optimum, against a 3.795 px best-fit-F noise floor); 0.227% of subject span median / 0.405% worst in scale-free 3-D shape; NO improvement in the objective rigidity metric — raw bone-length CV is 5.27% median at the shipped focal and 5.79% at the "optimal" one, i.e. worse; and the shipped focal WINS the independent metric holdout, reconstructing tag 15's 50.00 mm square at 50.20 mm (+0.40%) versus 48.91 mm (-2.18%) for the EXIF pair. Nothing here is visible to the client. The frames[0]-only size read is also inert on this take (all 26 pairs uniform in size).

Correct severity: minor — a defensible hardening request against a regression that has already been reverted and whose blast radius has already been reduced, with no measurable present cost on the client take. The proposed fix is directionally right for a future EXIF/BA focal-selection feature but would not have saved e4708bf: its matched-FOV fallback still gives 20.35 px, 4x worse than shipped.
- VERDICT REASON: The finding's facts and its headline measurements all reproduce, but three details that carry its "major" severity do not.

WHAT I CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY.
(a) Code. resolve.py:109-118 reads only project.frames[0]'s two images and calls _approx_intrinsics separately per camera at lines 116 and 117; _approx_intrinsics (resolve.py:70,86-90) is f=max(w,h), pp=centre, dist=0. quality.py:74-83 emits the resolution-mismatch warning; I ran check_rig on the shipped rig and all three warnings fire, including "The two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048)". main_window.py:286-288 surfaces them.
(b) Executed path. import_dialog.py:175-178 calls resolve_calibration with intr_left/intr_right=None unless the user uploaded files, and :213 save_rig's the result. I re-derived the shipped rig from scratch (frame 0001, tag 14, IPPE_SQUARE, K from max(w,h)) and it reproduces workspace/.../calibration/extrinsics.json bit-exactly (dR = 0.0000 deg, dt = 0.000 mm, both cameras). So resolve.py:116-117 did produce this take's calibration.
(c) Image facts. All 26 left = 3072x4080, Model 'Pixel 10 Pro', FocalLengthIn35mmFilm=24, focal_from_exif = 2832.97 px; all 26 right = 1536x2048, header ffd8 ffe0 JFIF, no Exif APP1, no Model, focal_from_exif = None. Exactly as claimed.
(d) History. git show e4708bf:pose3d/calib/resolve.py has `_approx_intrinsics(image, path)` with `f = focal_from_exif(path) if path is not None else None` and calls it per camera with f0.images[CAM_LEFT]/[CAM_RIGHT], so it did ship (2833, 2048), ratio 1.383. Confirmed.

WHAT FAILS.
1. It is not a live defect at the cited line. In current code _approx_intrinsics takes no path, and focal_from_exif has ZERO non-test callers (grep over the whole package hits only tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py). Both cameras get max(w,h). Because BOTH images are exactly 3:4, max(w,h) is FOV-matched by construction: 4080/2048 = 1.9922 vs diagonal ratio 5107.2/2560 = 1.9950, 0.14% apart. The current rule already satisfies the finding's own proposed fix on this take. This is a regression post-mortem plus a latent hazard for a future focal-selection feature, not a defect present at resolve.py:116.
2. "The data support 1.74-1.87" does not survive an independent estimator. On the same 388 body correspondences the essential-matrix singular-value valley gives ratio 1.87 (fl=2200) to 2.14 (fl=5600) with RANSAC-F, and 1.94 to 2.46 with 8-point F; at fl=4080 the RANSAC optimum is fr=2045, i.e. ratio 1.995, essentially the shipped 4080/2048. Only the single-tag-PnP grid (which entangles extrinsics with focal) lands on 1.787. With the extrinsics free (essential matrix refit per focal pair) the residual is FLAT at 3.90-5.49 px over the whole fl 2200-5600 x fr 1100-2900 plane with no consistent ratio. The body data do not identify the focal, so "wrong by +14% for the phone" is unsupported.
3. "Severe when it triggers" is no longer true on current code. f4c92f9 also replaced the flat 30 px gate with a diagonal-scaled one (pipeline.py:57, 71.5 px here). I measured both gates on the same broken rig: (2833,2048) rejects 37.9% at flat-30 (reproducing f4c92f9's 37.9%) but only 2.1% at the current 71.5 px. The "emptied the 3D view" failure mode is already closed by the threshold change, independently of the focal.

Could the effect be explained by something else? Yes: detector noise dominates. The best-fit-F Sampson floor over the 388 pairs is 3.795 px against a shipped median of 4.91 px, and moving to the grid-optimal focal buys 0.42 px while making the rigidity metric WORSE (raw bone-length CV 5.27% median shipped vs 5.79% at (4200,2350)). Nothing about the focal choice moves the client-visible pose.

Proposed fix aimed at the mechanism? Directionally yes (rig-level focal resolution, matched-FOV fallback, per-frame size re-check) — but it is prophylaxis for a feature that does not exist today, and its matched-FOV fallback would only have taken the e4708bf regression from 26.55 to 20.35 px, still 4x worse than shipped. The per-frame size re-check has zero effect here (all 26 pairs are uniform size).

check_rig(shipped rig) output, verbatim, 3 messages: nominal up 89 deg off vertical; "Camera intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size"; "The two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048), so they are different devices or settings. Each needs its own calibration." The resolution warning does fire.

FRAME-SIZE UNIFORMITY: all 26 left images (3072,4080), all 26 right (1536,2048) — the frames[0]-only read has zero effect on this take.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: resolve.py:116-117 calls _approx_intrinsics independently per camera, so any per-camera focal source (EXIF) silently applies only to the camera that has it. The right images are 1536x2048 JFIF-only files with no APP1/Exif segment, so focal_from_exif returns None for them. Commit e4708bf therefore shipped f=(2833 EXIF, 2048 guess): a focal RATIO of 1.38 where the shipped guess pair is 1.99 and the data support 1.74-1.87. resolve_calibration also reads only project.frames[0]'s two images to build both K's (resolve.py:110) and never re-checks that later frames have the same size. check_rig does warn about the resolution mismatch (quality.py:78-83) and it fires on this take.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: PIL/cv2 over all 52 files: left 26x (3072,4080) Pixel 10 Pro with full EXIF, right 26x (1536,2048), first bytes ffd8 ffe0 JFIF, no Exif APP1, Model absent. Single-tag PnP decomposition: f=(2833,2048) -> 26.55 px median body epipolar, (2833,1420) same-FOV -> 20.35, (2833,1200) -> 16.97, (3570,1790) -> 6.43, (4080,2048) -> 4.91. Focal-ratio measurements: BA free-f 3457/2119 = 1.63 (all tags), 3574/1991 = 1.79 (tag 13 dropped); the fundamental matrix over 388 body correspondences puts the essential-matrix singular-value valley at f_l/f_r = 1.76-1.87 across f_l 2200-5600; the single-tag epipolar 2-D grid minimum sits at (4200,2350) = 1.79; shipped 4080/2048 = 1.99. Right-camera focal estimates: BA 1991/2119/1787/1995 by tag subset, frame bootstrap 2056 +/- 144, homography IAC median 1771/1936 — max(w,h)=2048 is accidentally right within ~3% for the right camera and wrong by +14% for the phone. A 7x7 joint focal sweep with the extrinsics re-bundled at every grid point, scored by BODY epipolar, minimises at 5.37 px at (3672,1638); (4080,2048) gives 5.85; the EXIF scale (2833,1423) gives 10.9-11.4; (2448,1229) gives 17.7. The tag data alone does NOT discriminate (BA rms 3.13-3.40 px across the whole grid; tag-15 holdout square 50.49 mm at assumed f vs 49.36 mm at EXIF f against a true 50.00).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Make the focal source a RIG-level decision, not a per-camera one: resolve_calibration gains a step that resolves both cameras' focals together and refuses to mix provenance — if only one camera yields an EXIF/BA focal, either derive the other from the same estimator or fall back to a matched-field-of-view guess f_cam = f_known * hypot(w_cam,h_cam)/hypot(w_known,h_known) rather than max(w,h). Add a rig-level assertion that |log(fov_left/fov_right)| stays inside a tolerance and warn otherwise; extend quality.check_rig's resolution-mismatch message (quality.py:78-83) to say the two cameras also need independent focals. Re-check the image size on every frame, not just frames[0].
- artifacts: <SP>/find-intrinsics/exif_dump.py, exif.json, focal_sweep.py/.json, arbiter.py/.json; <SP>/find-extrinsics/sweep_h4_h5_h6.json, calib_ba_bestfocal/, eval_rigs.json

## F21 [minor] focal_from_exif ignores DigitalZoomRatio, so the 'EXIF focal' it produced (2833 px) is 21% SHORT of the true focal (~3570 px) — f4c92f9 rejected a number that was computed wrong

- file: pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:99 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: focal_from_exif ignores DigitalZoomRatio, so on the client's Pixel captures it returns 2833 px when the geometry says the left focal is ~3350-3570 px. The code defect is real, but it is in DEAD CODE, the correction does not vindicate the EXIF path, and the diagnosis of f4c92f9 is wrong.

WHAT SURVIVES (all reproduced independently):
- Code: intrinsics.py:76 binds only `_F35, _EXIF_IFD = 41989, 0x8769`; :88-90 fetch only 41989; :99 returns f35*hypot(w,h)/43.266615. Neither 41988/DigitalZoomRatio nor 41486/FocalPlaneXResolution appears anywhere in the repo (grep, 0 hits). Confirmed.
- EXIF: all 26 LEFT JPEGs are 3072x4080, Pixel 10 Pro, Software 'HDR+ 1.0.888945374zd', ExifImageWidth/Height == actual size (unedited), FocalLength 6.9, LensModel '6.9mm f/1.68', f35=24, DigitalZoomRatio=1.26 in all 26. focal_from_exif = 2832.96986698; x1.26 = 3569.54. Confirmed to the digit.
- 2833 really is too short. My synthetic control (below) rules it out at ~3 sigma.

WHAT IS WRONG:
1. NOT ON THE EXECUTED PATH. focal_from_exif has ZERO callers in pose3d/ (grep: only its own def at intrinsics.py:66 and a prose mention at resolve.py:81). Production intrinsics come from _approx_intrinsics (resolve.py:70-90), whose signature takes only the image array — no path — and hardcodes `f = float(max(w, h))` at resolve.py:87; called at resolve.py:116-117 from resolve_calibration, called at import_dialog.py:175. The bug cannot change any current import, re-solve, view or export. Measured impact on the client take today: exactly zero.

2. THE FINDING MISSED THAT ONLY ONE CAMERA HAS EXIF. All 26 RIGHT JPEGs are 1536x2048 and carry NO Exif sub-IFD at all (f35 absent -> focal_from_exif returns None; DigitalZoomRatio present in 26 of 52 images, FocalPlaneXResolution in 0 of 52). So focal_from_exif, being per-image, can only ever change the LEFT focal; the right stays at max(w,h)=2048. This makes the proposed fix's FocalPlaneXResolution branch inert on this hardware and leaves the right camera uncalibrated.

3. f4c92f9 DID NOT "REJECT A NUMBER THAT WAS COMPUTED WRONG" — the corrected number loses by the same metric. Rebuilding the rig (tag 14, frame 0001, the same tag/frame resolve.py picks) and measuring body epipolar error on the 388 keypoint pairs:
   shipped 4080/2048 (ratio 1.992): 4.91 px median, p90 12.48, 0/390 dropped
   exif-as-coded 2833/2048 (1.383): 26.55 px, p90 50.70  <- reproduces the commit's 26.6 px exactly
   ZOOM-CORRECTED-as-coded 3570/2048 (1.743): 8.10 px, p90 17.99, 0 dropped
   my self-cal both 3350/1800 (1.861): 10.25 px
   exif both scaled 2833/1422 (1.992): 20.40 px
   zoom both scaled 3570/1792 (1.992): 6.46 px
   The shipped guess is the BEST of all six. The zoom-corrected focal is 1.65x WORSE than shipped. f4c92f9's decision is unchanged by the fix.

4. THE REAL MECHANISM IS THE FOCAL RATIO, NOT THE ZOOM FACTOR. The 2833 blowup happens because EXIF exists for one camera only, so the left/right focal ratio broke from 1.992 to 1.383. The zoom fix only narrows the break to 1.743 — it treats a symptom of an asymmetry it does not address. Note also that the shipped right focal is itself ~12% too long by my self-calibration (recovered 1800 vs assumed 2048), and no EXIF fix can reach it.

5. "~3450-3575" IS OVER-PRECISE AND THE MEASUREMENTS ARE NOT FIVE INDEPENDENT ONES. Evidence (a), (b) and (c) are the same bundle adjustment on the same tag corners. My point estimate is 3350, not 3570. And the finding's own (d) gives two answers 12% apart (3389.6 / 3795.0) — I reproduce both to 0.1 px (3388.9 / 3795.0), which shows the artifacts are honest but the method is not tight. Per-tag IAC medians in the left view span 2325 (tag 13), 3398 (14), 4215 (15), 5642 (17) — a 2.4x spread — and a global least-squares in u=1/f^2 over all 84 observations gives 2760.

CORRECTED SEVERITY: minor. It is a genuine latent defect plus a false comment/test, worth fixing before any future capture actually routes through focal_from_exif, but it changes nothing that runs today, the direct geometric effect is sub-millimetre (0.47 mm median shape shift on a 128 mm subject), and the "correct" value it produces still measures worse than the shipped guess on the only real take. What IS worth keeping from the finding: the assertions at resolve.py:71-84 and tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py:65-86 that "EXIF is physically the right focal" are false — 2833 is not the right focal either — and that wrong premise should be struck.
- VERDICT REASON: The two checkable facts hold exactly — focal_from_exif reads only tag 41989 (intrinsics.py:76, 88-90, 99) and never DigitalZoomRatio, and every one of the 26 left JPEGs carries DigitalZoomRatio=1.26 alongside f35=24, so 2832.97 x 1.26 = 3569.5. My own tag-square self-calibration also agrees that 2833 is too short: the PnP reprojection-rms profile bottoms out at f=3350, and a synthetic control using the take's real 2.73 px corner noise shows that estimator is unbiased (f_true=4080 recovers 4100 [3868-4415]), while distortion (needs k1~+0.25) and principal-point offset (needs ~500 px) cannot explain the result. So refuted=false: there is a genuine defect and 2833 is genuinely wrong. But three parts of the finding do not survive. (1) The cited code is DEAD — grep over pose3d/ finds zero callers of focal_from_exif; production intrinsics come from _approx_intrinsics (resolve.py:70-90), which takes no path and hardcodes f=max(w,h) at resolve.py:87. Today's measured impact is exactly zero. (2) The finding missed that the right camera's 26 JPEGs carry no Exif sub-IFD at all, so focal_from_exif can only ever change the LEFT focal; the proposed FocalPlaneXResolution branch is inert (tag 41486 absent from all 52 images). (3) The headline "f4c92f9 rejected a number that was computed wrong" is false as an operational claim: applied the way resolve.py would apply it, the zoom-corrected 3570/2048 gives 8.10 px median body epipolar error against the shipped 4080/2048's 4.91 px — 1.65x worse by the very metric the commit used — so the revert would have happened anyway. The real mechanism behind the 26.55 px blowup (which I reproduce) is that EXIF exists for one camera only, breaking the focal RATIO from 1.992 to 1.383; the zoom fix only narrows that to 1.743. Severity drops from major to minor: dead code, sub-millimetre geometric effect (0.47 mm on a 128 mm subject), and a "corrected" value that still measures worse than the shipped guess. The one part worth acting on is that resolve.py:71-84 and tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py:65-86 assert "EXIF is physically the right focal", which my numbers show is false for 2833 too.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: CODE (read myself):
- pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:76 `_F35, _EXIF_IFD = 41989, 0x8769`; :88 `f35 = exif.get(_F35)`; :90 `f35 = exif.get_ifd(_EXIF_IFD).get(_F35)`; :99 `return f35 * float(np.hypot(w, h)) / _FRAME35_DIAG_MM`. No DigitalZoomRatio. CONFIRMED.
- grep "focal_from_exif(" over pose3d/ -> NO callers. pose3d/calib/resolve.py:70 `def _approx_intrinsics(image: np.ndarray)` (no path arg); :87 `f = float(max(w, h))`; :116-117 the only intrinsics construction; pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:175 calls resolve_calibration. DEAD CODE.

EXIF (my script, all 52 images):
- LEFT x26: 3072x4080, Google Pixel 10 Pro, Software 'HDR+ 1.0.888945374zd', ExifImageWidth/Height=3072/4080 (== actual, so unedited), Orientation 1, FocalLength 6.9, LensModel 'Pixel 10 Pro back camera 6.9mm f/1.68', f35=24, DigitalZoomRatio=1.26 (all 26), SubjectDistance median 0.470 m (0.439-0.584). focal_from_exif = 2832.969866978549 for all 26; x1.26 = 3569.54.
- RIGHT x26: 1536x2048, NO Exif sub-IFD at all -> f35 None -> focal_from_exif returns None. FocalPlaneXResolution (41486) present in 0 of 52 images; DigitalZoomRatio in 26 of 52.
- Shipped calibration/: left K=diag(4080,4080), pp (1536,2040), size 3072x4080; right K=diag(2048,2048), pp (768,1024), size 1536x2048. Ratio 1.9922.

INDEPENDENT FOCAL ESTIMATE (my selfcal.py; 84 left tag observations = tags 14/13/17 x26 + tag 15 x6; 13 right = tag 14 x11 + 15 x2):
- Zhang IAC, pp centred, unit aspect. LEFT: orthogonality form median 3795.05 (n=28), equal-norm form median 3388.88 (n=82) — reproduces the finding's (d) 3795.0 / 3389.6 exactly. Per-tag medians: t13 2325.5, t14 3398.0, t15 4215.1, t17 5641.8. Global lstsq in u=1/f^2: 2760.0.
- PnP reprojection-rms-vs-f profile (IPPE_SQUARE + ITERATIVE, best of both), LEFT: minimum at f=3350 (rms 1.2727 px); 2750->1.4134, 3000->1.3293, 3250->1.2774, 3500->1.2837, 4000->1.4275, 4250->1.5146. Shallow bowl: 2833 ~= 1.39, 4080 ~= 1.44 — the two rejected candidates are penalised almost equally.
- RIGHT camera, same method: profile minimum f=1800 (rms 0.2198); IAC medians 1936 (orth) / 1771 (norm); tag-14 median 1820.8. So the shipped right f=2048 is ~12% long too.

SYNTHETIC CONTROL (my control.py — the finding has no equivalent):
- Real corner noise measured from the static scene (cameras and tags static, so per-corner std over 26 frames IS detector noise): tag14 2.78 px, tag13 3.30, tag17 2.94, tag15 1.89; mean sigma 2.73 px.
- Injecting that noise into synthetic tags of the SAME geometry at known f_true, 40 realisations, recovered f (median [p10,p90]):
    f_true 2833 -> 2875 [2593, 3248]   (noise-free recovery 2825)
    f_true 3350 -> 3363 [3148, 3535]   (noise-free 3350)
    f_true 3570 -> 3575 [3400, 3778]   (noise-free 3575)
    f_true 4080 -> 4100 [3868, 4415]   (noise-free 4075)
  The estimator is unbiased. My real-data recovery of 3350 is therefore inconsistent with f_true=4080 (whose p10 is 3868) and with f_true=2833, and consistent with 3350 +/- ~200. The finding's 3570 sits at the upper edge of my range, not at its centre.

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATIONS TESTED AND REJECTED (my distortion.py):
- Tag radial positions r/rmax: t13 0.597, t14 0.608, t15 0.764, t17 0.648 (far off-axis). Body keypoints r/rmax median 0.186, p90 0.304 (near-axis).
- Unmodelled radial distortion at f_true=4080: k1=+0.05 -> recovered 3925; +0.10 -> 3775. Reaching 3350 needs k1 ~ +0.25 (pincushion — wrong sign for a phone wide lens, and HDR+ output is distortion-corrected). REJECTED.
- Principal-point offset at f_true=4080: +50 px -> 4025, +100 -> 3975, +200 -> 3925. Reaching 3350 needs ~500 px = 16% of image width. REJECTED.

END-TO-END IMPACT (my impact.py; rig rebuilt from tag 14 in frame 0001 — the same tag/frame resolve.py:134-153 selects; frame 0001 left ids [13,14,17], right ids [14], common [14]):
  name / f_left / f_right / ratio / epipolar median px / p90 / dropped of 390 / raw bone CV % / span m / shape shift vs shipped (Umeyama rms, mm)
