- The 26.55 px reproduces f4c92f9's stated 26.6 px exactly. The commit's "37.9% rejected" does NOT reproduce today (I get 8/390 = 2.1%) because f4c92f9 itself replaced the flat 30 px gate with 1.4% of the diagonal = 71.5 px.
- The finding's own sub-mm impact estimate corroborates: I measure 0.473 mm median shape shift for 3570 vs shipped, on a 127.7 mm subject span (0.37%).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: focal_from_exif returns f35 * hypot(w,h) / 43.2666 and never reads DigitalZoomRatio (only tag 41989 is fetched, intrinsics.py:88-90). Every client JPEG carries DigitalZoomRatio = 1.26, so the true pixel focal is 2832.97 x 1.26 = 3569.5 px, not 2832.97. Five independent measurements agree on ~3450-3575 px and none agrees with either 2833 or the shipped 4080.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: EXIF of all 26 left images: Google Pixel 10 Pro, 3072x4080, FocalLength 6.9 mm, FocalLengthIn35mmFilm 24, DigitalZoomRatio 1.26, Orientation 1 -> focal_from_exif = 2832.97 px; x1.26 = 3569.5. Independent f_left estimates: (a) multi-tag/multi-frame BA with f free — tags 14/15/17 = 3574.3 px (0.13% from 3569.5), tags 14+15 = 3570.1, all four tags = 3457.2, tags 14+17 = 3452.5; (b) BA cost profile with f_left on a grid — minimum 3457, 1-sigma 3400-3500, cost 3831 at 3457 vs 3958 at 4080; (c) frame bootstrap of free-f BA (30 resamples) 3466 +/- 131, p10-p90 3382-3497; (d) single-tag homography IAC with pp centred — median 3389.6 px (equal-norm form, n=82) / 3795.0 px (orthogonality form, n=28); (e) EXIF SubjectDistance (median 0.470 m, range 0.439-0.584) vs reconstructed camera-to-subject distance (0.556 m at f=4080, 0.495 at 3570, 0.406 at 2833) -> self-consistent fixed point f = 3383 px. Per-tag IPPE reprojection rms in the left view drops for every tag when f goes 4080 -> 3457 (tag14 0.80 -> 0.49 px, tag15 1.19 -> 0.64).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): focal_from_exif(image_path) -> (f_px, source): read DigitalZoomRatio (tag 41988) and FocalLengthIn35mmFilm (41989) from the Exif sub-IFD and return f35 * hypot(w,h) / 43.266615 * max(1.0, zoom); when FocalLength (37386) and FocalPlaneXResolution (41486) are both present prefer the zoom-independent f_px = FocalLength_mm * FocalPlaneXResolution and warn if the two disagree by >10%. Return None unchanged when f35 is missing. Delete the 'deliberately not used' assertion at tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py:78-86 and replace it with a test that a 24 mm-equivalent 3072x4080 frame at DigitalZoomRatio 1.26 yields 3569 +/- 5 px.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-intrinsics/exif_dump.py, exif.json, self_calib_f.py/.json, ba_profile.py/.json, tag_subset.py/.json, shape_impact.py/.json, summary.json

## F22 [minor] Tag 13 is not a planar 5 cm square as imaged (3.3 px IPPE residual vs 0.4-0.8 px for tags 14/17) and would contaminate the multi-tag bundle the code plans to build

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:81 | category: calibration-extrinsics | sources: intrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Tag 13 is a genuine geometric outlier among the four backdrop tags, but it is inert today and it is not the main hazard for the calibration work the code comment contemplates.

MEASUREMENT (confirmed, my own detection and fits): in the left view over 26 frames, fitting a flat 5 cm square to tag 13's detected corners leaves 3.33 px rms, versus 0.77 (tag 14), 1.19 (tag 15) and 0.36 (tag 17). The residual is systematic (2.81 px of the 3.33 is the frame-mean) and anisotropic — a pure x-compression of about +/-2.8 px per corner on a 288 px side. It is focal-independent (2.94 px at f=2833, 3.15 at 3457, 3.33 at 4080) and cannot be blamed on the camera: tag 13 is no more oblique (33.5 deg vs tag 17's 33.0), no further off-axis (1519 vs 1650 px) and no smaller (288 vs 267 px) than tag 17, and fitting shared radial distortion (k1, k2) across all four left tags only improves tag 13 (3.31 -> 2.80) by making tags 14 and 15 worse (0.76 -> 1.05, 1.17 -> 1.83). So tag 13's imaged quad really is not a perspective image of a flat square.

WHAT THE MECHANISM IS NOT ESTABLISHED TO BE: "taped across the curve of the backdrop sweep" is a guess, not a measurement. At the four corners of a square all |x| are equal, so a cylindrical bend is exactly degenerate with a narrower rectangle plus a z-shift the pose absorbs — my 1-parameter shape fits give 3.52 px for square, bend-about-y, bend-about-x, twist and shear alike, and 1.47 px only for free aspect. With both cameras static and tag 13 visible to the left camera only, there is a single viewpoint, so a bent card, a non-square print/cut, and a corner-detection bias on this tag's border are indistinguishable in this data.

WHY IT IS INERT TODAY: resolve_calibration only ever picks a tag common to both views (resolve.py:142), and tag 13 is detected in the left view in 26/26 frames and the right view in 0/26. The multi-tag bundle it would supposedly contaminate does not exist — the multi-tag branch of estimate_extrinsics (extrinsics.py:123-145) has no caller in pose3d/ (only tests/test_extrinsics.py:59, and with tag_world_positions=None). Nothing about the client's current complaint touches tag 13.

WHY THE ATTRIBUTION IS OVERSTATED: at fixed intrinsics, including or excluding tag 13 from a bundle changes the recovered relative pose by 0.00000 deg and the body epipolar error by 0.00000 px (both 6.0283). A tag seen by one camera only has a free 6-DOF world pose that absorbs every constraint it could impose, so its sole channel of influence is the shared focal. Decomposing the 4.91 -> 13.09 px degradation of a naive free-focal bundle: bundling at fixed f costs +1.12 px, freeing the focal costs a further +3.91 px with tag 13 already excluded, and tag 13 adds +3.15 px (39%). Dropping tag 13 still leaves the bundle at 9.94 px, 2x worse than the shipped 4.91.

WHAT THE CODE COMMENT ACTUALLY PROPOSES: resolve.py:81-84 asks for discrete focal selection scored on held-out tag corners, not a free-focal bundle. Running that scheme (tag-14 extrinsics, sweep both focals, score on held-out tag-15 corners) picks ~0.90x, f_left ~3672, body epipolar 5.63 px vs 4.91 at f=4080 — a mild degradation, not the 13.09 px the finding warns about. And tag 13 has no cross-view corners at all, so it cannot participate in that scheme. (Only frame 0026 has tag 15 in both views, so this is 4 corner pairs and weak.)

CORRECTED FIX: (1) keep the per-tag IPPE rms gate — it is cheap and directionally right, but note it is insufficient on its own (9.94 px). (2) DROP the "per-tag free scale" proposal — I implemented it and it makes things worse, epi 13.09 -> 24.26 with f_left collapsing 3458 -> 2664, because per-tag shape freedom is degenerate with the focal. (3) Keep the status-line reporting of surviving tags and per-tag residual. (4) The load-bearing rule the finding missed: do not let the focal float in a tag bundle on this rig at all, and admit to the stereo bundle only tags seen by BOTH cameras (here that is tag 14, and tag 15 in 2 frames) — left-only tags contribute exactly nothing except a biased focal.
- VERDICT REASON: Every measurement in F22 reproduces on my own independent detection + BA code, some to 3 significant figures. But the framing, the severity, and two of the three proposed fixes do not survive.

WHAT REPRODUCES (my own numbers, own ArUco detection of all 52 images, own BA):
1. The docstring is verbatim as cited. pose3d/calib/resolve.py:81-84: "`intrinsics.focal_from_exif` is kept and tested for the principled version of this: score candidate focals by cross-validated epipolar error on the corners of tags NOT used to solve the extrinsics, and keep the winner. That needs multi-marker extrinsics first."
2. Per-tag IPPE_SQUARE reprojection rms, left view, mean over frames — mine (claim): f=4080 tag13 3.33 (3.34), tag14 0.77 (0.80), tag15 1.19 (1.19), tag17 0.36 (0.40); f=3457 tag13 3.15 (3.18), tag14 0.46 (0.49), tag15 0.63 (0.64), tag17 0.39 (0.44). Also f=2833: tag13 2.94. Focal-independence confirmed.
3. My BA (world = tag 14, free camera poses + free 6-DOF pose for every other tag) reproduces the whole subset table — mine (claim): [13,14,15,17] free f = (3458.3, 2120.4) epi 13.09 (3457.2/2118.5, 13.13), dRot 3.78 / dBaseline 7.78 deg (3.80 / 7.74); [14,15,17] (3575.4, 1992.9) epi 9.94 (3574.3/1991.2, 9.98); [13,14,17] f_L 2652.2 epi 34.37 (2654.2, 34.32); [14,17] 3447.3 epi 11.54 (3452.5, 11.50); [14,15] 3571.2 epi 10.13 (3570.1, 10.17); fixed f=4080/2048 dRot 0.76 / dBase 0.63 deg epi 6.03 (0.76/0.63, 6.05). Shipped rig 4.91 px (baseline agrees).
4. "It is the tag, not the intrinsics" holds under tests the finding did not run. Tag 13 is not more oblique (33.5 deg vs tag17's 33.0), not further off-axis (1519 px from principal point vs tag17's 1650), not smaller (288 px side vs tag17's 267). Its residual is systematic: of the 3.33 px total, the frame-mean is 2.81 px, and the mean per-corner residual is [(+2.79,-0.33),(-2.78,+0.20),(+2.80,-0.21),(-2.81,+0.34)] px — a pure anisotropic x-compression, not noise. A shared radial-distortion model cannot absorb it: fitting (f,k1,k2) jointly over all 4 left tags with free per-detection poses only moves tag13 3.31->2.80 while making tag14 0.76->1.05 and tag15 1.17->1.83.

WHAT DOES NOT SURVIVE:
A. Not on the executed path, in two independent ways, so severity "major" is unsupported. (i) resolve.py:142-146 only considers `common = sorted(set(idl) & set(idr))` and calls estimate_extrinsics_for_marker per camera on that tag. My detection: tag 13 appears in the LEFT view in 26/26 frames and in the RIGHT view in 0/26 frames (right sees only tag 14, 11 frames, plus tag 15 in frames 0024/0026). Tag 13 can never be selected. (ii) The multi-tag bundle does not exist: the multi-tag branch of estimate_extrinsics (pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:123-145) has no caller — `grep -rn tag_world_positions` over the repo returns only its own definition/docstring, and estimate_extrinsics itself is called only from tests/test_extrinsics.py:59, with tag_world_positions=None. Executed path is import_dialog.py:175 -> resolve_calibration -> single-tag IPPE only. Zero current client-visible impact.
B. The causal attribution to tag 13 is overstated by my own control. At FIXED f=4080/2048, adding vs removing tag 13 from the bundle changes the recovered relative pose by dRot = 0.00000 deg, dBaseline = 0.00000 deg and body epipolar by -0.00000 px (both 6.0283). That is structural, not luck: a tag seen by one camera only has a fully free 6-DOF world pose that absorbs every constraint it could impose, so its ONLY channel of influence is the shared focal. Decomposing the 4.91 -> 13.09 px degradation of a "naive bundle": bundling at fixed f costs +1.12 px (4.91->6.03), freeing the focal costs a further +3.91 px even with tag 13 already excluded (6.03->9.94), and tag 13 adds +3.15 px on top (9.94->13.09). Tag 13 is ~39% of it; the dominant hazard is letting the focal float in a tag bundle at all. Dropping tag 13 still leaves the bundle 2x worse than shipped.
C. Straw man. resolve.py:81-84 does not propose a free-focal bundle; it proposes discrete focal selection scored on held-out tag corners. I ran that actual scheme (extrinsics from tag 14 as shipped, sweep a multiplier on both focals, score epipolar error on held-out tag-15 corners): the minimum is at ~0.90x, f_L≈3672, giving body epi 5.63 px vs 4.91 at f=4080 — mild, nothing like 13.09. And tag 13 cannot participate in it at all, having no cross-view corners. (Caveat: only frame 0026 has tag 15 in both views = 4 corner pairs; weak evidence.)
D. proposed_fix (2) is actively harmful, measured. I implemented "give each admitted tag a per-tag scale so a bent/mis-sized tag is absorbed": on the free-focal bundle with all 4 tags it makes things worse, epi 13.09 -> 24.26 and f_L collapses 3458 -> 2664; with [14,15,17] it goes 9.94 -> 12.56. Per-tag shape freedom is degenerate with the focal and destroys the constraints that pin f. Fix (1) (rms gate) is directionally right but insufficient (9.94 px, still 2x shipped). Fix (3) (report per-tag residual) is fine and cheap.
E. The physical mechanism ("taped across the curve of the backdrop sweep") is unidentifiable from this data, so "re-tape it" is a guess. At the 4 corners of a square all |x| are equal, so a cylindrical bend about the tag's own axis is EXACTLY degenerate with a narrower rectangle plus a z-translation the pose absorbs. I fitted six 1-parameter shape models to tag 13's mean corners: only free-aspect helps (3.52 -> 1.47 px); "bend about y", "bend about x", "twist" and "shear (parallelogram)" all return exactly 3.52 px, i.e. zero improvement. With each camera static and tag 13 seen by one camera only there is a single viewpoint, so bent card / non-square print / corner-detection bias on this tag's border cannot be told apart. A crop of left_0005.jpg does show tag 13's white mounting card looking bowed, but that is an eyeball impression, not a measurement.

CODE READ: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:70-90 (_approx_intrinsics, f=max(w,h)), :81-84 (the cited docstring), :132-153 (ArUco loop; :142 `common = sorted(set(idl) & set(idr))`; :144-145 estimate_extrinsics_for_marker per camera); pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:72-92 (single-tag SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE), :95-148 (estimate_extrinsics, multi-tag branch :123-145, uncalled); pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:175-177 (the only production call to resolve_calibration), :211-215 (triangulate_project/fit_project/save_rig); pose3d/geometry/triangulate.py:79 (epipolar_distance); tests/test_extrinsics.py:59 (only caller of estimate_extrinsics, single-tag).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: The docstring at resolve.py:81-84 says the principled focal selection 'needs multi-marker extrinsics first'. Built as a bundle with all four tags it converges to f_left=3457 and a relative pose 3.80 deg (rotation) / 7.74 deg (baseline direction) from the single-tag solution, and makes the body epipolar error worse (13.13 px vs 4.91). Dropping tag 13 alone moves f_left to 3574.3, drops the left corner rms 4.76 -> 3.96 px and the body epipolar 13.13 -> 9.98 px. Tag 13's residual is focal-independent (3.34 px at f=4080, 3.18 at f=3457), so it is the tag, not the intrinsics — consistent with a tag taped across the curve of the backdrop sweep.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Per-tag IPPE_SQUARE reprojection rms, left view, 26 frames: f=4080 -> tag13 3.34 px, tag14 0.80, tag15 1.19, tag17 0.40; f=3457 -> 3.18 / 0.49 / 0.64 / 0.44. BA free-f by tag subset: [13,14,15,17] f=(3457.2,2118.5) rms=(4.76,1.11) epi=13.13; [14,15,17] f=(3574.3,1991.2) rms=(3.96,1.09) epi=9.98; [13,14,17] f=(2654.2,1787.1) epi=34.32; [14,17] f=(3452.5,1787.1) epi=11.50; [14,15] f=(3570.1,1995.3) rms=(3.77,1.09) epi=10.17. BA at fixed f=4080/2048 differs from the shipped single-tag rig by 0.76 deg rotation / 0.63 deg baseline direction and scores 6.05 px; at f=3457/2119 by 3.80 / 7.74 deg and 13.13 px. Independently, tag 13's single-tag solves are also the ambiguous ones (F23).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): In the planned bundle: (1) score every tag by its per-tag IPPE reprojection rms across frames before admitting it, dropping any above ~1.5 px or ~3x the median tag; (2) give each admitted tag a per-tag scale (or 4 free in-plane corner offsets) so a bent/mis-sized tag is absorbed rather than biasing the cameras; (3) report the surviving tag set and per-tag residual in the calibration status line — 'tag 13 does not fit a flat square' is directly fixable by re-taping it on the flat part of the backdrop.

## F25 [minor] Joint colours average the two cameras' reprojection errors in incompatible pixel units and paint the same number on both views

- file: pose3d/ui/model.py:180 | category: ui-feedback | sources: detection | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `_accuracy` (pose3d/ui/model.py:180-191) averages the two cameras' reprojection errors with `np.nanmean` although they are in different pixel scales (left 3072x4080 f=4080, right 1536x2048 f=2048, same FOV, so 1 right px = 1.99x the angle of 1 left px), and `main_window._on_accuracy` (main_window.py:234-240) paints that single mixed-unit array over both views. Both halves of the mechanism are confirmed. What is NOT true is the stated impact: because `_accuracy` reprojects `f.pose3d`, the two-view DLT of exactly those two observations, the two views' residuals are one epipolar discrepancy split in a near-fixed proportion — measured Pearson r = 0.9985, ratio 5-95th pct 1.73-1.91, per-frame joint ranking identical (median Spearman 1.000, worst-joint agreement 100%/96%), and a deliberate 50 px error injected into ONE view raises both cameras' errors in the same 1.82 ratio. No joint can be "bad in the right view only", so nothing is diluted and the colour ordering the user drags on is correct. The averaging is a ~1.3-1.4x monotone rescale whose only real cost is that a resolution-dependent number is compared against three absolute-pixel constants (panels.py:36 `exp(-e/6)`; main_window.py:483 `5`/`12` px timeline cuts), which flips the band on 6.9% of cells vs the left camera and 15.6% vs the right — all of them near thresholds that are already mis-set (green requires <=0.98 px against a 5.11/2.78 px median, so 73% of all cells read red in every variant). The proposed per-panel split therefore fixes the wrong half: without replacing the absolute constants with a per-camera normalised unit (px / that camera's figure height — 0.658% left vs 0.684% right, i.e. the two cameras already agree once normalised), each panel just gets its own equally arbitrary banding. Note also that the app already warns about the resolution mismatch: main_window.py:286 -> pose3d/calib/quality.py:78-83.
- VERDICT REASON: The CODE claim and every MEASUREMENT reproduce exactly; the IMPACT claim ("a joint genuinely bad in the right view is diluted by the left view's number and vice versa") is refuted, and with it the "major" severity.

CODE (read myself, on the executed path). pose3d/ui/model.py:180-191 `_accuracy(idx)` loops `for cam in CAMERAS`, appends `reprojection_error(f.pose3d, f.kp2d[cam], self.rig.intr[cam], self.rig.ext[cam])` and returns `np.nanmean(np.stack(errs), axis=0)` — one array, no camera axis. pose3d/ui/main_window.py:234-240 `_on_accuracy(errors)` feeds that one array to `self.accuracy.update_errors`, `self.pose_acc.set_overall`, then `self.cam_left.set_accuracy(errors)` and `self.cam_right.set_accuracy(errors)` — identical array to both panels. Live call sites: model.py:91 (`set_frame`), :135 (face-point drag), :171 (`set_joint_2d`, after a joint drag). The same mixed array also drives the timeline traffic light with ABSOLUTE pixel cuts at main_window.py:481-484 (`worst < 5` green / `< 12` amber), and the colour comes from panels.py:33-37 `accuracy_pct = 100*exp(-err_px/6)` — an absolute-pixel constant. So the unit mixing is real and it reaches fixed absolute thresholds in three places.

MEASUREMENTS (my own script, my folder). Image sizes over all 52 JPEGs via PIL: left 3072x4080 (26), right 1536x2048 (26). Intrinsics on disk: left K f=4080, c=(1536,2040), image_size [3072,4080]; right K f=2048, c=(768,1024), image_size [1536,2048]. Both are f=max(w,h) guesses, so one right pixel is 4080/2048 = 1.99x the angle of one left pixel. Every number in the finding's evidence reproduces to 3 s.f.

WHY THE IMPACT IS WRONG. `_accuracy` reprojects `f.pose3d`, which is the two-view DLT of exactly those two observations, so the two views' residuals are not independent measurements — they are one epipolar discrepancy split between the views in a near-fixed geometric proportion. Over the 388 valid observations: Pearson r(left,right) = 0.9985, Spearman = 0.9991; the left/right ratio has 5-95th percentiles 1.726-1.913 (min 1.672, max 1.963, median 1.814 ~ the 1.99 focal ratio); 0.0% of cells fall outside ratio 1.5-2.5. Per-frame joint RANKING is unchanged by the averaging: median Spearman(avg, left) = 1.000 and Spearman(avg, right) = 1.000 across 26 frames (min 0.993 / 0.987); the frame's worst joint is identical to the left camera's in 100% of frames and to the right's in 96.2% (left vs right themselves agree 96.2%). Direct counterexample hunt: I shifted ONE view's observation of one joint by 5/20/50 px and re-triangulated exactly as `set_joint_2d` does (model.py:145-156) — both cameras' errors rise together in the same 1.82 ratio (left+50px -> 7.83/4.29 px; right+50px -> 5.15/2.83 px). There is no view-specific error for the average to dilute; a two-view DLT point cannot be "bad in one view only". Also 0 of 390 cells have exactly one camera valid (388 both, 2 both-NaN), so the nanmean never degenerates to a single camera's raw pixels on this take.

WHAT THE AVERAGING ACTUALLY COSTS. It is an almost exactly monotone rescale (divide by ~1.29 vs left, multiply by ~1.42 vs right), so it corrupts only the absolute level fed to fixed thresholds. Band disagreement vs each camera's own number: 6.92% of cells vs left, 15.64% vs right (left vs right disagree 22.56%). Band populations green/amber/red: left 42/43/305, right 71/73/246, averaged 48/58/284 — 73% of all cells are red under every variant, because `accuracy_pct` puts green at <=0.97 px and amber at <=2.14 px while the actual median is 5.11/2.78 px. The disagreement is entirely confined to the ~25% of cells sitting near thresholds that are themselves mis-set.

FIX AIM. The proposed fix (per-camera arrays through `accuracyChanged`) is aimed at the wrong half of the mechanism: splitting per camera without replacing the absolute constants (`exp(-e/6)`, panels.py:36; `5`/`12` px, main_window.py:483) leaves the same near-monotone curve with the same saturated thresholds — the right view would just get its own equally arbitrary banding. The mechanism worth fixing is the scale-dependent absolute constant; per-camera normalisation (px / that camera's figure height, where the two cameras already agree: 0.658% left vs 0.684% right) is the part that matters, and it is only meaningful once the threshold is expressed in the same normalised unit.

OTHER EXPLANATION CHECKED. The app is not blind to the resolution mismatch: main_window.py:286 calls `pose3d.calib.quality.check_rig`, which at quality.py:78-83 emits "The two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048), so they are different devices or settings. Each needs its own calibration." So the user is told about the mismatch elsewhere in the UI.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F25/f25.py (+f25.json), f25b.py (+f25b.json). Take: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames, source = f.pose3d (raw DLT) exactly as _accuracy uses.

COUPLING (the refutation):
 Pearson r(left,right) = 0.9985; Spearman = 0.9991
 left/right ratio percentiles 5/25/50/75/95 = 1.726 / 1.776 / 1.814 / 1.856 / 1.913; min 1.672, max 1.963
 fraction of cells with ratio outside [1.5, 2.5] = 0.0%
 per-frame 15-joint rank correlation, median (min): avg vs left 1.000 (0.9929), avg vs right 1.000 (0.9868), left vs right 0.9964 (0.9868)
 worst-joint-in-frame identity agreement: avg vs left 100.0%, avg vs right 96.2%, left vs right 96.2%
 single-view perturbation of frame 0 joint 7, re-triangulated (err_left / err_right): left+5px 2.92/1.59, left+20px 4.56/2.49, left+50px 7.83/4.29; right+5px 2.65/1.44, right+20px 3.49/1.90, right+50px 5.15/2.83 — ratio 1.82-1.84 in every case.
 NaN structure: both-valid 388, only-left 0, only-right 0, both-NaN 2 (of 390).

BANDING (panels.py accuracy_pct = 100*exp(-e/6); green >=85% i.e. e<=0.975 px, amber >=70% i.e. e<=2.14 px):

 band mismatch (averaged vs per-camera): vs left 6.92%, vs right 15.64%; left vs right 22.56%
 |shown % - per-camera %|: median 7.98 (vs left) / 10.17 (vs right); max 10.16 / 14.15
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: _accuracy computes reprojection_error for BOTH cameras and returns np.nanmean(np.stack(errs), axis=0) (model.py:184-192), and main_window._on_accuracy pushes that single array to both panels (main_window.py:239-240). The two cameras are not the same scale: left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048, same field of view (f/W = 4080/3072 = 2048/1536 = 1.33), so one right-image pixel is twice the angular error of one left-image pixel.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Measured sizes: left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048 (PIL over all 52 files); left K=[[4080,0,1536],[0,4080,2040]], right K=[[2048,0,768],[0,2048,1024]] — both internally correct f=max(w,h) guesses. Stored figure height: left median 775.6 px (646-1031), right 406.7 px (321-561). Raw reprojection medians 5.11 px left / 2.78 px right — 0.100% and 0.109% of their respective diagonals, i.e. equally good, but the same physical accuracy reads 1.8x 'better' in the right view. The averaged number has no unit and is shown identically over both images.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Make _accuracy return per-camera arrays: _accuracy(idx) -> dict[cam, np.ndarray], normalised per camera (px / that camera's image diagonal, or px / that camera's detected figure height). Emit both through accuracyChanged and have main_window._on_accuracy hand each panel its own camera's array (main_window.py:239-240). panels.accuracy.update_errors then averages normalised, comparable quantities.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-detection/ui_feedback.py, ui_feedback.json, h4_scores.py/.json

## F27 [minor] When the bone fit fails, the frame silently keeps the raw triangulation and the count is print()ed to a stdout a windowed build does not have

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:179 | category: robustness | sources: tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: fit_project (pose3d/pipeline.py:167-180) catches every per-frame bone-fit exception, substitutes the raw triangulation, counts the failures and reports the count ONLY via print(). It returns None and none of its three call sites (pipeline.py:193, ui/model.py:55, ui/import_dialog.py:214) capture anything, so the count never reaches the GUI — unlike `dropped`, which both UI callers do surface through rejection_note. But the message is not lost: app.py:153-154 runs runtime.ensure_std_streams() before the UI, which points a windowed build's None stdout at pose3d-log.txt beside the exe (runtime.py:56-67), the file README.md:161-163 tells the client to read; in the container docker/entrypoint.sh:52 leaves stdout attached to the `docker compose up` terminal.

The magnitude is currently nil and the branch is near-unreachable: on the client take 0 of 26 frames fail (counted with an instrumented wrapper; the print emits nothing), every frame has 14-15 observed joints, and since commit 1b76345's lm->trf switch no sparsity I could construct (0-14 observed joints, all-NaN, coincident joints, 1e12 coords) raises at all. The only raising inputs are inf coordinates or NaN/missing bone lengths, and fit_project can produce neither (bone_lengths built over BONES with a >1e-6 fallback at pipeline.py:157-161; triangulate.py:36-49 maps NaN->NaN and stayed finite for every adversarial pixel pair tested). Even if the branch did fire, the pose cost is small: the bone fit moves joints only 0.83 mm mean / 3.65 mm max (0.70% / 3.1% of the 0.118 m body height), ~7x below the EMA smoother's own 6.07 mm mean displacement; the observable symptom would be that one frame's bone lengths wobble (raw CV 5.3% vs 0.1% fitted), not visible character jitter.

This is therefore a latent observability gap, not an active defect. The cheap correct fix is to make fit_project return a count (or a small report) and have recompute_all/_process append a fit_note the way they already append rejection_note; the Timeline banding is unwarranted for a path that fires 0/26. A more relevant sibling the finding misses: bonefit.py:81-85 silently returns raw3d.copy() for a frame with zero observed joints — a raw fallback that raises nothing, is never counted and never printed (0 such frames on this take).
- VERDICT REASON: The CODE claim reproduces exactly, but the two things that made it "major" do not.

CONFIRMED by my own reading: pose3d/pipeline.py:167-180 is a per-frame `try: fit_bone_lengths(...) except Exception: failed += 1; per_frame.append(np.asarray(f.pose3d, float))`, followed by `if failed: print(...)` at :178-180. `fit_project` is annotated `-> None` (pipeline.py:152-153) and my own grep finds exactly three product call sites, all bare: pipeline.py:193 (run_full), pose3d/ui/model.py:55 (recompute_all — which does capture and surface `dropped` via rejection_note at :57-59, so the precedent claim is right too), pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 (_process — surfaces `dropped`/rejection_note at :221-224 but nothing about fit failures). model.py:158-169 is the live-edit twin with a statusMessage toast. All three are on the executed path.

REFUTED sub-claim 1 — "the print goes nowhere". In the frozen windowed build, pose3d/app.py:151-154 calls `runtime.ensure_std_streams()` as the FIRST thing in main(); pose3d/runtime.py:56-67 replaces a None stdout/stderr with an append handle on `app_dir()/"pose3d-log.txt"` (pose3d.spec:79-82 documents this as the deliberate cost of console=False), and README.md:161-163 tells the client to read exactly that file. In the container, docker/entrypoint.sh:52 is `exec python -m pose3d.app "$@"` with stdout inherited and docker-compose.yml `stdin_open: true` / `tty: true`, so the line lands in the `docker compose up` terminal. The message is not in the GUI, but it is in the documented support log in both shipped configurations.

REFUTED sub-claim 2 — magnitude. I wrapped `pose3d.pipeline.fit_bone_lengths` with a counter and re-ran fit_project on the client take: 26 fit calls, 0 failures, captured stdout empty. Min observed joints per frame is 14 of 15 (median 15), 0 frames all-NaN, 0 frames below the lm/trf switch threshold.

REACHABILITY: after commit 1b76345 the sparse-frame ValueError is gone (bonefit.py:110-119 picks "trf" when under-determined). My stress sweep of frames with 0,1,2,3,5,8,10,11,14 observed joints plus all-NaN raised nothing. The only inputs that raise are inf coordinates or NaN/missing bone lengths ("Residuals are not finite in the initial point" / KeyError) — and fit_project cannot produce either: bone_lengths is built over BONES with a >1e-6 fallback (pipeline.py:157-161), and triangulate_points maps NaN->NaN by construction (triangulate.py:36-49) and stayed finite for every adversarial pixel pair I tried (1e6 and 1e9 px, identical-pixel pairs, a 200-point sweep; max |X| = 0.84 m). Stored raw pose3d has 6 NaN and 0 inf. So the except branch is currently near-unreachable for real data.

IMPACT overstated: the bone fit itself only moves joints 0.83 mm mean / 0.59 median / 1.76 p90 / 3.65 max on this take (0.70% / 3.09% of the 0.118 m body height), so a silently-unfitted frame would deviate ~7x less than the EMA smoother's own 6.07 mm mean / 21.7%-of-height max displacement. The visible symptom would be bone-length wobble on that frame (raw CV 5.3% vs no-smooth 0.1%), not the "jitter" the finding implies.

FIX aimed at the mechanism? Half. Returning (n_failed, first_error) + a fit_note mirroring rejection_note is the right shape for UI surfacing, but its premise (log lost) is false, and a distinct Timeline band for a branch that fires 0/26 times is disproportionate. It also misses the closer relative: bonefit.py:81-85 silently returns `raw3d.copy()` when a frame has zero observed joints — a raw fallback that never raises, is never counted, and never prints. That is the uninstrumented silent path, not the except branch (0 such frames here).
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F27/probe.py and inf_probe.py (run with cwd=repo root, .venv/bin/python).

Client take (workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session, 26 frames), fit_bone_lengths wrapped by a counter:
- fit calls 26, failures 0, fit_project stdout = '' (the print never fires)
- observed joints per frame: min 14, median 15; frames with 0 observed joints: 0; frames below the lm->trf threshold: 0

Cost of the fallback if it did fire (fit-only displacement, smooth=False vs raw pose3d):
- mean 0.83 mm, median 0.59 mm, p90 1.76 mm, max 3.65 mm = 0.70% mean / 3.09% max of the 0.118 m body height
- compare the EMA smoother (baseline, independently consistent): 6.07 mm mean, 21.7% of height max

Exception-reachability stress of fit_bone_lengths (client bone lengths, fill_missing=False):
- 0,1,2,3,5,8,10,11,14 observed joints -> all OK, no raise; all-NaN -> OK; all joints coincident -> OK; coords x1e12 -> OK; bone_lengths all zero -> OK
- one inf joint -> ValueError "Residuals are not finite in the initial point"
- bone_lengths all NaN -> same ValueError; bone_lengths missing key -> KeyError (8,1)
Neither of the latter three is producible by fit_project (bone_lengths built over BONES with >1e-6 fallback, pipeline.py:157-161).

Triangulation cannot manufacture inf for real pixels (rig = shipped calibration): centre/centre, (0,0), (1e6,1e6), (1e9,0)/(-1e9,0) all finite; 200-point identical-pixel sweep over u in [-4e4,4e4] gives max |X| = 0.84 m; inf in -> NaN out. Stored raw pose3d: 0 non-finite-non-NaN, 6 NaN. Stored fitted3d: 0 non-finite-non-NaN.

Code read myself: pipeline.py:152-185 (fit_project, try/except/print at 167-180), pipeline.py:112-124 (rejection_note precedent), pipeline.py:188-193 (run_full bare call), model.py:46-59 (recompute_all bare call, rejection_note surfaced), model.py:156-169 (live-edit toast), import_dialog.py:206-227 (bare call, rejection_note surfaced), bonefit.py:57-121 (early raw return at :81-85, lm/trf switch at :110-119), app.py:130-154 (crash handler + ensure_std_streams first in main), runtime.py:47-71 (stdout -> pose3d-log.txt), pose3d.spec:76-82 (console=False), README.md:159-163, docker/entrypoint.sh:52, docker-compose.yml, tests/test_bonefit.py:119-135.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: fit_project catches every per-frame exception, substitutes np.asarray(f.pose3d) as that frame's fitted3d, counts the failures and reports them with print(...). The function returns None and no caller captures anything: ProjectModel.recompute_all (model.py:55), ImportDialog._process (import_dialog.py:214) and pipeline.py:193 all call it bare. In the frozen Windows bundle and the container the app is windowed, so the print goes nowhere.

The identical fallback in the live-edit path (model.py:162-169) at least emits a 6-second status toast.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Change fit_project to return (n_failed, first_error) or a small FitReport dataclass, and add pipeline.fit_note(n_failed, n_frames) -> str mirroring rejection_note. recompute_all appends it to statusMessage; _process appends it to the Done dialog. Mark the affected frames in Timeline.set_status with a distinct band so they are visible on the strip rather than only in a transient toast.
