## F28 [minor] Nothing in the app can ever improve the intrinsics: calibrate_checkerboard has zero callers, the recalibrate-time upgrade was deleted in f4c92f9, and _approx_intrinsics no longer receives the image path

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:70 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Confirmed with three detail corrections and three strengthening facts.

VERIFIED AS STATED:
- `_approx_intrinsics(image: np.ndarray)` (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:70) receives only the decoded array; its two call sites (resolve.py:116-117) pass one argument. No filename reaches it, so no EXIF/file-derived focal is reachable there.
- `calibrate_checkerboard` (pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:102) has zero callers and zero tests. Repo-wide grep for the identifier outside .venv returns exactly two hits: its own `def` and the prose reference at assets/checkerboard.md:18.
- `git show f4c92f9 -- pose3d/ui/main_window.py` deletes `_upgrade_assumed_intrinsics` (34 lines). The surviving `_on_recalibrate` (main_window.py:279-283) calls `model.recompute_all` (ui/model.py:46-59), which re-triangulates with `self.rig` untouched. "Recalibrate" therefore cannot change K.
- `check_rig` on the actual client rig emits exactly 3 warnings, including the "assumed from the image size … shoot a checkerboard with each camera" one (quality.py:74-76) — an instruction the product has no tool to consume.

DETAIL CORRECTIONS:
1. The finding's `evidence` line mis-describes its own grep: quality.py:76 and tests/test_calib_quality.py:73 contain the WORD "checkerboard", not the identifier `calibrate_checkerboard`. The identifier's only non-definition reference in the repo is assets/checkerboard.md:18.
2. "The only way a user can supply real intrinsics is … the import dialog" omits a second route of the same effort class: hand-placing left/right_intrinsics.json into <project>/calibration, which pose3d/app.py:81-97 `_load_rig` reads on project open. The substance (hand-authored JSON only) stands.
3. "the EXIF path it documents at resolve.py:81-84 is unreachable" overstates. Those lines document that `focal_from_exif` is deliberately NOT used and is retained as the input to a future cross-validated selector; f4c92f9 reverted EXIF on measurements. The one-arg signature is a consequence of a deliberate, measured revert, not an oversight — though structurally the claim is true.

STRENGTHENING FACTS THE FINDING MISSED:
4. The two cameras are different devices: left 3072x4080 with f=4080, right 1536x2048 with f=2048 (both `looks_assumed`=True). `focal_from_exif` returns 2832.97 px on every left_*.jpg but **None on every right_*.jpg** — the right camera records no FocalLengthIn35mmFilm. For that camera the EXIF rung of the proposed ladder does not exist, so checkerboard-or-upload is the only possible remedy and neither exists in the product. The fix's guard ("refuse an EXIF focal for a camera with no EXIF") is required by this very take, not hypothetical.
5. Both persisted intrinsics JSONs contain no "source" key, and `Intrinsics.load` defaults `source="measured"` (intrinsics.py:57-58). The client rig therefore loads reporting `source="measured"` for intrinsics that were guessed; only the K-shape heuristic `looks_assumed` rescues the warning. Any provenance ladder that trusts `.source` would silently treat the guess as measured. This is a small real bug, not just a gap.
6. `calibrate_checkerboard` is functionally correct (my synthetic test: true f=1700, image-size guess 2048, recovered fx=1700.4, rms 0.041 px, pp within 0.2 px, source="measured"). The proposed fix targets working code.

MAGNITUDE — WHY I DOWNGRADE SEVERITY:
On this take the guess costs nothing measurable. Sweeping the focal and re-solving tag-14 IPPE extrinsics per candidate, the body-epipolar median along the ratio-locked diagonal is 20.28 px at the EXIF focal (fL=2833), 11.85 at 3200, 6.16 at 3600, **4.89 at the shipped fL=4080**, 8.67 at 5200, 12.28 at 6000. The full 8x8 grid optimum (fL=3600/fR=1600) is 4.70 px — the shipped f=max(w,h) is within 0.21 px of the best focal the app's own criterion can find. So the guess is near-optimal here and cannot be contributing to the client's reported inaccuracy. Triangulated subject span does move 13.5-16.4 cm across the sweep, so absolute metric scale is focal-dependent, but no in-app metric distinguishes those.

The same sweep is the finding's best argument: the epipolar criterion IS discriminative (4.9 to 20.3 px across candidates, contrary to the concern that a shared-focal error cancels — it does not here, because the two cameras differ in size and the extrinsics are re-solved per f). An automated resolver would have real signal. So F28 is a genuine product-capability gap plus one small provenance bug, aimed at working code, with zero demonstrated cost on the evidence take.
- VERDICT REASON: Every load-bearing structural claim reproduced independently: `_approx_intrinsics` really does take only the array (resolve.py:70, called one-arg at 116-117), `calibrate_checkerboard` really has zero callers and zero tests (identifier grep outside .venv hits only its own def at intrinsics.py:102 and assets/checkerboard.md:18), f4c92f9 really deleted `_upgrade_assumed_intrinsics` (34 lines) leaving `_on_recalibrate` -> `recompute_all` with an unchanged rig, and check_rig on the real client rig really does emit the "shoot a checkerboard" instruction the product cannot act on. I also found two facts that strengthen it: the right camera is a different device (1536x2048, f=2048) with NO EXIF focal at all, so even the EXIF fallback is unavailable there; and both intrinsics JSONs lack a "source" key, so Intrinsics.load's default makes the guessed rig report source="measured". What I could not support is the severity: my focal sweep shows the shipped f=max(w,h) is within 0.21 px of the grid optimum on body epipolar error (4.91 vs 4.70 px) and is the clear minimum along the ratio-locked diagonal (20.28 px at the EXIF focal, 12.28 px at fL=6000). The guess costs nothing measurable on the evidence take, so this is a product-capability gap plus one small provenance bug, not a contributor to the client's reported inaccuracy.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All run by me under /tmp/.../scratchpad/verify-F28/ (chk.py, sweep.py, cb.py); repo untouched.

1. Client rig loaded from workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration:
   left  K = diag(4080,4080), pp (1536,2040), size 3072x4080, dist all 0, rms 0.0, loaded source="measured"
   right K = diag(2048,2048), pp (768,1024),  size 1536x2048, dist all 0, rms 0.0, loaded source="measured"
   looks_assumed(left)=True, looks_assumed(right)=True
   Raw JSON on disk: NO "source" key in either file (so Intrinsics.load's default "measured" is what surfaces).
   Actual image sizes on disk: left_0001.jpg (3072,4080); right_0001.jpg and right_0013.jpg (1536,2048).

2. check_rig(client rig) -> exactly 3 warnings:
   (a) "Calibration's nominal up is 89 deg off vertical …"
   (b) "Camera intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size, not measured. Depth and limb angles will be skewed — shoot a checkerboard with each camera to calibrate them."
   (c) "The two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048) …"

3. focal_from_exif on the take's images:
   left_0001.jpg = 2832.97, left_0002.jpg = 2832.97
   right_0025.jpg = None, right_0026.jpg = None   (right camera records no FocalLengthIn35mmFilm)

4. Focal sweep. For each (fL,fR) I built K=[[f,0,w/2],[0,f,h/2],[0,0,1]] per camera, re-solved extrinsics with estimate_extrinsics_for_marker on tag 14 in frame idx 0 (left sees 13,14,17; right sees only 14), then computed body-epipolar median over all 26 frames of the stored kp2d and re-triangulated for bone CV and span.
   Ratio-locked (fL/wL == fR/wR):
     fL=2400 fR=1200  epi 33.44 px  boneCV 9.26%  span 16.41 cm
     fL=2833 fR=1416  epi 20.28     boneCV 6.61   span 15.71   <- EXIF focal
     fL=3200 fR=1600  epi 11.85     boneCV 5.26   span 15.10
     fL=3600 fR=1800  epi  6.16     boneCV 5.16   span 14.50
     fL=4080 fR=2040  epi  4.89     boneCV 5.26   span 13.93   <- shipped guess, minimum
     fL=4600 fR=2300  epi  5.98     boneCV 6.06   span 13.70
     fL=5200 fR=2600  epi  8.67     boneCV 7.08   span 13.63
     fL=6000 fR=3000  epi 12.28     boneCV 7.93   span 13.60
   Full 8x8 grid best by epipolar: fL=3600/fR=1600 -> 4.70 px (span 14.72 cm); shipped fL=4080/fR=2048 -> 4.91 px. Delta 0.21 px.
   Bone-length CV is a weak discriminator (4.86-9.26% across the whole grid, minimum at an epipolar-terrible point fL=5200/fR=1422 with epi 20.58 px) — consistent with 2D detector noise dominating it.

5. calibrate_checkerboard exercised on 9 synthetic warped-board views, true f=1700 in a 1536x2048 frame (image-size guess would be 2048, i.e. +20.5% error):
   recovered fx=1700.4, fy=1700.4, rms=0.041 px, pp=(767.8,1024.1) vs true (768.0,1024.0), source="measured". Function works.

6. Code, read directly: resolve.py:70 signature; resolve.py:116-117 call sites; intrinsics.py:102 def; intrinsics.py:57-58 source default; quality.py:38-49 looks_assumed, 70-76 the warning; main_window.py:279-283 _on_recalibrate; ui/model.py:46-59 recompute_all; import_dialog.py:166-176 calibration inputs; app.py:81-97 _load_rig; git show f4c92f9 --stat (main_window.py 34 deletions).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: _approx_intrinsics(image) takes only the decoded array, so the EXIF path it documents at resolve.py:81-84 is unreachable without re-threading the filename. calibrate_checkerboard (intrinsics.py:102) is referenced by no code and no test. main_window._upgrade_assumed_intrinsics was removed by f4c92f9. The only way a user can supply real intrinsics is to hand-author a JSON and pick it in the import dialog (import_dialog.py:167-168) — while quality.check_rig tells them to 'shoot a checkerboard with each camera' (quality.py:74-76) with no tool in the product that consumes one.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: grep over pose3d/ and tests/ for calibrate_checkerboard: only its own definition, its docstring, the word inside the quality.py warning string, and one assertion on that string (tests/test_calib_quality.py:73). grep for _approx_intrinsics: defined resolve.py:70, called resolve.py:116-117 with one argument. git show f4c92f9 removes main_window._upgrade_assumed_intrinsics (34 lines). check_rig on the client rig emits exactly three warnings, including the 'assumed from the image size ... shoot a checkerboard' one.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): (1) resolve.resolve_focals(project, load_image, image_paths) -> {cam: (f_px, source)} implementing the ladder uploaded/measured > checkerboard > BA-refined (F38/F22) > EXIF-with-zoom prior (F21) > matched-FOV guess (F20); _approx_intrinsics(image, path) takes the path back and calls it. (2) A checkerboard capture screen that calls the existing calibrate_checkerboard and writes left/right_intrinsics.json with source='measured' and its rms, so the warning becomes actionable. In the UI replace the binary 'assumed' warning with the resolved focal, its provenance and its uncertainty ('left 3570 px from EXIF x 1.26 zoom, refined to 3574 +/- 130 px on 3 tags; right 2048 px assumed from image size, unverified'). Guard: refuse an EXIF focal for a camera with no EXIF.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-intrinsics/tag_subset.json

## F29 [minor] Intrinsics.load defaults source to 'measured', so the client's guessed calibration files load as if they were checkerboard-measured

- file: pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:58 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: CONFIRMED with one addition and a severity downgrade.

pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:58 defaults `source` to "measured" for any file lacking the key, directly contradicting its own comment at :56-57; the dataclass default at :35 is "measured" too. Both of the client's real intrinsics files (calibration/left_intrinsics.json, right_intrinsics.json, mtime 2026-07-29, before the field was added in e4708bf on 2026-08-20) lack the key and load as source='measured' while quality.looks_assumed returns True for both (left f=4080=max(w,h), pp=(1536,2040)=centre, dist=0; right f=2048=max(w,h), pp=(768,1024)=centre, dist=0). The cited line is on two live paths: pose3d/app.py:88-89 (project open) and pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:167-168 (import).

ADDITION F29 MISSED: the mislabel is not merely in-memory, it is PERSISTED and laundered. Re-uploading a legacy intrinsics file at import runs load (import_dialog.py:167) -> passthrough (resolve.py:109-126, which also leaves approximate=False so the "intrinsics are approximate" dialog is suppressed) -> save_rig (import_dialog.py:215) -> Intrinsics.save writing `"source": "measured"` (intrinsics.py:45). I reproduced this: loading and re-saving the client's own left_intrinsics.json emits "source": "measured". A future `if intr.source == 'assumed'` upgrade policy would therefore skip not only legacy projects but NEW projects seeded from legacy files — strictly worse than F29 states.

SEVERITY corrected major -> minor. Zero effect on the client's 3D-accuracy complaint and on every baseline metric: exhaustive grep shows nothing in pose3d/ reads Intrinsics.source (the only `.source` hit outside intrinsics.py is the unrelated rig-asset field at pose3d/geometry/character.py:167), and check_rig (quality.py:70 -> main_window.py:286-288) already warns correctly by inferring from K — I ran it on the real rig and it emits "Camera intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size, not measured". Dormant metadata plus a one-line fix, not a live defect.

The proposed fix is sound and aimed at the real mechanism: default to 'unknown'/'assumed' and let looks_assumed break the tie — looks_assumed (quality.py:47-49) requires zero distortion AND f==max(w,h) AND exact-centre pp, so it will not misclassify a genuine third-party checkerboard file that also lacks the key. Two additions to the fix: (a) change the dataclass default at intrinsics.py:35 as well, not only line 58, or bare constructors such as pose3d/datasets/panoptic.py:59 keep silently claiming 'measured' (harmless there since Panoptic really is ground truth, but the same latent trap); (b) the new test must cover the SAVE side too — assert that loading a key-less file and re-saving it does not stamp 'measured' onto disk, since the existing round-trip test at tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py:88-92 only covers a file that already has the key.
- VERDICT REASON: I could not refute any element of F29; every checkable claim reproduced exactly, and I found one additional mechanism the finding missed that strengthens it.

CODE (read myself). pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py:58 is verbatim `source=str(d.get("source", "measured")),`, directly under the comment at :56-57 ("projects written before this field existed hold whatever the import produced, which was always the image-size guess"). The code does contradict its own stated premise. The dataclass field default at intrinsics.py:35 is ALSO `source: str = "measured"`, so the mislabelling is not confined to `load`. intrinsics.py:32-34 states the field exists so "the focal-selection step has somewhere to put its verdict", confirming F29's reading of intent.

EXECUTED PATH (traced). `Intrinsics.load` is reached two ways in the real app: (a) pose3d/app.py:88-89 in `_load_rig`, called from `build_model` at app.py:75 whenever a project folder is opened; (b) pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:167-168 when the user supplies intrinsics files at import. Both are live GUI paths, so the cited line executes for the client take.

NO CURRENT CONSUMER (exhaustive grep). Nothing in pose3d/ reads `intr.source`. The only `.source` read outside intrinsics.py is pose3d/geometry/character.py:167, the rig asset's unrelated field. So F29's own "no client-visible error today" is correct. check_rig (pose3d/calib/quality.py:70, consumed at pose3d/ui/main_window.py:286-288) infers from K via looks_assumed (quality.py:39-49) and does warn the user correctly on this take.

ADDITIONAL MECHANISM F29 MISSES (strengthens it). The mislabel is PERSISTED, not just in-memory. import_dialog.py:167 loads a legacy JSON as source='measured'; resolve.py:109-126 passes uploaded intrinsics straight through untouched (and leaves approximate=False, so the "intrinsics are approximate" dialog is also suppressed); import_dialog.py:215 calls save_rig, which at resolve.py:42-43 calls Intrinsics.save, writing `"source": self.source` at intrinsics.py:45. I ran this on the client's actual left_intrinsics.json: it comes back out of save() with `"source": "measured"` written to disk. A guessed K is laundered into a permanent false 'measured' stamp, so a future `if intr.source == 'assumed'` policy would skip not only legacy projects but NEW projects seeded from legacy files.

TEST GAP CONFIRMED. tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py:88-92 only exercises a file that HAS the key. No test covers a file lacking it.

COULD IT BE SOMETHING ELSE? The one defensible reading of 'measured' as default is that externally-supplied checkerboard files (from a plain OpenCV script) also lack `source` and genuinely ARE measured. Real, but it does not rescue the code: the comment at :56-57 asserts the opposite rationale, and looks_assumed already separates the two populations (quality.py:47-49 requires zero distortion AND f==max(w,h) AND exact-centre pp, which a real checkerboard result will not satisfy). The proposed fix is aimed at the actual mechanism.

SEVERITY downgraded major -> minor: zero effect on the client's 3D-accuracy complaint, no consumer reads the field, check_rig already warns correctly from K, and no baseline metric can move. Latent trap plus a one-line fix, not a live defect.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: All run with cwd=/media/athena/hd3/Projects/pose3d-tool via .venv/bin/python; my script: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F29/repro.py

1. Client calibration JSON keys (workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration/): left and right both = [K, dist, image_size, rms]. has_source_key = False for BOTH.

2. Intrinsics.load(...).source on both real files = "measured". quality.looks_assumed(...) = True on both. Exactly the contradiction F29 claims.

3. Actual K values I read (F29's evidence is right; the task CONTEXT block is wrong):
   left : K diag (4080.0, 4080.0), pp (1536.0, 2040.0), image_size (3072,4080), dist all zero -> f == max(w,h) == 4080, pp == exact centre.
   right: K diag (2048.0, 2048.0), pp (768.0, 1024.0), image_size (1536,2048), dist all zero -> f == max(w,h) == 2048, pp == exact centre.
   The CONTEXT given to me states K=[[4080,0,1536],[0,4080,2040]] for BOTH cameras; that is incorrect for the right camera. F29's evidence string quotes diag(2048,2048) pp (768,1024) and matches disk.

4. Actual image sizes on disk match recorded image_size: left_0001.jpg = 3072x4080, right_0001.jpg = 1536x2048. So looks_assumed fires for the right reason on both, not from a size mismatch.

5. Intrinsics.__dataclass_fields__['source'].default == "measured" (intrinsics.py:35). load() literal recovered by inspect.getsource: `source=str(d.get("source", "measured")),`.

6. LAUNDERING (my addition): Intrinsics.load(client left file).save(tmp) -> written JSON contains "source": "measured". A guessed calibration is persisted as measured.

7. Fresh-import control: resolve._approx_intrinsics(np.zeros((4080,3072,3))) -> source == "assumed", saves as "assumed", looks_assumed == True. The defect is confined to files lacking the key (legacy or third-party), not today's import path.

8. Consumers: grep over all pose3d/**/*.py for `.source` outside intrinsics.py yields exactly ONE hit, pose3d/geometry/character.py:167 (`self.source = str(d["source"]) ...`), the rig asset. Zero readers of Intrinsics.source.

9. check_rig on the real client rig (built from the actual calibration/ folder) returns 3 warnings, including "Camera intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size, not measured...". The user-facing warning is CORRECT today despite source=='measured'. rig.intr sources = {left:'measured', right:'measured'}.

10. Provenance dates: `git log -S'source: str = "measured"' -- pose3d/calib/intrinsics.py` -> introduced in e4708bf, dated 2026-08-20. Client's calibration files mtime 2026-07-29 22:45. The "predates the field" premise is confirmed by dates, not assumed. (workspace/ is gitignored per .gitignore:43.)

11. tests/test_intrinsics_exif.py passes 6/6; its round-trip test at :88-92 constructs Intrinsics(source="exif") and never exercises a missing key. No test covers the absent-key default.

Nothing in the repository was modified; all outputs are under /tmp/.../scratchpad/verify-F29/.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: source=str(d.get('source', 'measured')) gives the best possible provenance to any file that predates the field. The comment two lines above (intrinsics.py:56-57) says such files 'hold whatever the import produced, which was always the image-size guess' — the code does the opposite of what the comment asserts. Any automatic focal-selection policy keyed on `source` (exactly what intrinsics.py:32-34 says the field is for) would refuse to upgrade precisely the projects that need it.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration/left_intrinsics.json and right_intrinsics.json contain no 'source' key at all (only K, dist, image_size, rms:0.0); Intrinsics.load returns source='measured' for both, while quality.looks_assumed returns True for both (K = diag(4080,4080) with pp (1536,2040), and diag(2048,2048) with pp (768,1024), dist all zero). Verified by running Intrinsics.load + looks_assumed on both files.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Default to 'unknown' (or 'assumed') on load and let quality.looks_assumed be the tie-breaker: source = d.get('source') or ('assumed' if looks_assumed(self) else 'unknown'); treat anything other than 'measured' as upgradable, and have looks_assumed prefer the recorded field when present. Add a round-trip test for a file with no source key.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-intrinsics/tag_subset.py/.json; <SP>/find-tests-ui/ui_signals.json (looks_assumed, intrinsics_source_field)

## F30 [minor] Calibration provenance and quality are never persisted and never shown as a number, so a wrong calibration is unauditable and unrepeatable

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:44 | category: calibration-extrinsics | sources: extrinsics, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Calibration provenance is not persisted, so the world frame, the IPPE branch and — critically — the marker_length that sets the entire metric scale are unrecoverable from a saved project. save_rig (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:44-49) writes only {'left':{R,t},'right':{R,t}}; project.json holds only name/fps/calibration_ref/frames; no logging exists anywhere in pose3d/. resolve_calibration does name the tag and frame in cal.message (resolve.py:148-153) but that string only ever reaches two QMessageBoxes (import_dialog.py:193, 220-225) and is never written down. I recovered the shipped rig's provenance only by brute force — 97 (frame,tag,camera) IPPE solves — finding an exact match at frame 0001, tag 14, solution 0, both cameras (0.0000 deg, 0.0000 mm), with 25.77% of those 97 solves having a runner-up within 2x (min ratio 1.02).

Corrections to the original finding: (a) the sidebar is NOT limited to a tick or triangle — panels.py:209-213 and 276-278 render the full text of all three check_rig warnings, one of which already carries a number (89 deg tilt); the gap is that the PnP residual, ambiguity margin, marker_length, tag/frame id and observation count carry none. (b) The 'a different frame or tag silently produces a different world frame' impact is real but small: the resolver is deterministic for a fixed frame set, and across all 12 (frame, common-tag) options it could pick, the relative pose varies by at most 1.13 deg and the baseline by 1.71%. (c) load_extrinsics_json is resolve.py:52-58, not 36-58.

Additional evidence the finding missed: Intrinsics carries a `source` field (intrinsics.py:28-35) that the pipeline never reads, and Intrinsics.load defaults a missing 'source' to 'measured' (intrinsics.py:58), so the client's assumed f=max(w,h) intrinsics load back labelled 'measured' — provenance is not merely absent but actively mislabelled.

The operative consequence is scale, not geometry: marker_length is the reconstruction's only metric anchor, output scale is linear in it, and reaching a 20 cm mannequin from the measured 0.1448 m median joint span would require 0.0691 m (1.38x the unrecorded 0.05 m default). Nothing on disk lets that be checked, which is why the scale question (F17) cannot be closed. The proposed CalibrationReport / report.json fix targets the right mechanism; it should also persist marker_length and the intrinsics `source`, and put numbers into the existing warnings rather than add a new display surface.
- VERDICT REASON: The core claim reproduces exactly, but three details are wrong or overstated and the impact is smaller than asserted.

CONFIRMED BY MY OWN READING:
- pose3d/calib/resolve.py:44-49 — save_rig writes exactly {'left':{R,t},'right':{R,t}} and nothing else. The shipped workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session/calibration/extrinsics.json contains exactly four arrays (883 bytes).
- resolve.py:148-153 builds cal.message naming tag and frame; it is consumed only at import_dialog.py:193 (approximate branch) and 220-225 (the Done box) and is never written to disk. grep shows resolve_calibration is called only from import_dialog.py:175 and tests/test_import.py.
- marker_length: QDoubleSpinBox default 0.05 (import_dialog.py:106-108) -> resolve_calibration(marker_length=...) (import_dialog.py:177) -> estimate_extrinsics_for_marker -> marker_object_points (extrinsics.py:41-47). It is the only metric anchor and appears in no persisted file.
- project.json top-level keys are exactly ['name','fps','calibration_ref','frames'] — no calibration provenance anywhere. No logging module is used anywhere in pose3d/ (grep for getLogger: zero hits).
- The path is live: import_dialog._process:215 calls save_rig; app.py:81-96 _load_rig reads back only K/dist/image_size/rms and R/t; main_window.py:285-288 _refresh_calibration_status is the only consumer, calling quality.check_rig.

WRONG IN DETAIL (1): 'Sidebar.set_calibrated (panels.py:267) shows only a tick or a warning triangle' is false. panels.py:209-213 creates a word-wrapped calib_warn QLabel below the status, and panels.py:276-278 sets it to the full bulleted text of every check_rig warning and makes it visible. The user sees all three warning paragraphs in the sidebar, not just a triangle. The finding's own evidence section contradicts its claim sentence.

WRONG IN DETAIL (2): 'no number attached'. Warning W1 carries a number (89 deg) and W3 carries both resolutions. What genuinely carries no number: PnP residual, IPPE ambiguity margin, marker_length, world tag/frame id, observation count, and the magnitude of the focal error.

OVERSTATED (3): 'Re-running the import with a different frame or tag silently produces a different world frame with no trace.' I measured this. The resolver is deterministic for a fixed frame set (first frame in order, then lowest common tag id), and I enumerated all 12 (frame, common-tag) options it could ever pick on this take: relative-pose rotation differs from the shipped pick by at most 1.13 deg and the baseline by 1.71% (54.96-55.90 cm). The world frame does move rigidly, but orient.sequence_up removes that. So the non-repeatability is real in principle and negligible in magnitude here.

MINOR LINE-NUMBER ERROR: the fix cites 'load_extrinsics_json (resolve.py:36-58)'; load_extrinsics_json is resolve.py:52-58 (36-49 is save_rig).

NOT EXPLAINED BY SOMETHING ELSE: I checked whether provenance survives anywhere — corrections.sqlite, project.json, the two intrinsics files. It does not. Intrinsics does have a `source` field (intrinsics.py:28-35) written by save (line 45), but it is documented as 'Not read by the pipeline yet' and the shipped client files have no `source` key at all, so Intrinsics.load's default (intrinsics.py:58) makes them report source='measured' for intrinsics that were in fact assumed — an extra provenance defect the finding missed, on the executed path.

THE FIX IS AIMED AT THE RIGHT MECHANISM (a CalibrationReport persisted next to the R/t and surfaced by _refresh_calibration_status), though the sidebar already renders full warning text, so the change needed is numbers-in-the-warnings plus a report.json, not a new display surface.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Provenance brute-force (verify-F30/provenance.py, all 26 frames x 2 cameras, 5 candidate dictionaries, solvePnPGeneric SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE, shipped K, marker_length=0.05):
- 97 (frame, tag, camera) DICT_6X6_250 solves — exactly the finding's 97.
- Dictionary hits: DICT_6X6_250 = 97 corners, DICT_4X4_50 = 0, DICT_5X5_250 = 0, DICT_APRILTAG_36h11 = 0, DICT_ARUCO_ORIGINAL = 1 (spurious, never common to both views).
- The shipped extrinsics.json is reproduced EXACTLY by frame 0001, tag 14, IPPE solution 0, both cameras: dR = 0.0000 deg, dt = 0.0000 mm for left and right. No solve matches solution 1. Six other tag-14 solves land within 0.5 deg / 1.9 mm (frames 0002, 0008, 0009, 0019, 0021); 37 of 97 are within 5 deg. Provenance was recoverable only by this search — nothing on disk states it.
- IPPE ambiguity over the 97 solves: err1/err0 min = 1.02, median = 8.74, 25.77% within 2x (finding said 26%). Best-solution reprojection error median 0.512 px, max 2.786 px (finding said ~0.5 px). Both reproduced.
- Per-tag camera distance stability: left tag 13 0.7180 m +-2.51 mm, tag 14 0.6383 +-0.99 mm, tag 15 0.5946 +-0.90 mm, tag 17 0.7878 +-1.65 mm; right tag 14 0.6315 +-0.77 mm.

Repeatability of the undeclared choice (verify-F30/repeat.py): the only (frame, common-tag) pairs resolve_calibration could ever pick are 11 frames of tag 14 plus ('0026', 15). Over those 12 candidate rigs the left->right relative rotation differs from the shipped pick by 0.34-1.13 deg and the baseline spans 54.96-55.90 cm (1.71% spread).

UI state on the shipped rig (verify-F30/ui_scale.py, via quality.check_rig): world_up_tilt = 89.34 deg; looks_assumed = True for both cameras; check_rig returns exactly the three warnings quoted in the finding, and panels.py:276-278 renders all three as visible bullet text.

Metric-anchor sensitivity: median per-frame max joint span of the RAW triangulation is 0.1448 m. Since output scale is linear in marker_length, a 20 cm mannequin implies marker_length = 0.0691 m (1.38x the unrecorded 0.05 default) and an 18 cm one implies 0.0621 m (1.24x). Nothing on disk records which value was used, so this cannot be checked or corrected after the fact.

Extra defect found: Intrinsics.load('.../left_intrinsics.json') returns source='measured' for the client's assumed f=4080 intrinsics, because the shipped file predates the field and intrinsics.py:58 defaults a missing 'source' to 'measured'.

Artifacts: /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F30/{provenance.py,provenance.json,repeat.py,ui_scale.py}. Nothing in the repository was modified.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: save_rig writes only {'left':{R,t},'right':{R,t}}. Which tag defined the world frame, which frame it came from, which ArUco dictionary won the auto-detect race, which IPPE branch was chosen and how separated it was, the marker_length that set the metric scale, the PnP residual and the world up axis are all discarded. resolve_calibration does name the tag and frame in cal.message (resolve.py:148-153), but that string is shown once in a QMessageBox at import (import_dialog.py:184-186, 225) and never persisted. Sidebar.set_calibrated (panels.py:267) shows only a tick or a warning triangle.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: The shipped calibration/extrinsics.json contains four arrays and nothing else; provenance had to be recovered by brute force — solving all 97 (frame,tag,camera) IPPE pairs and searching for the pose matching the file — to establish tag 14, frame 0001, solution 0, both cameras (0.0000 deg, 0.0000 mm). marker_length is a QDoubleSpinBox defaulting to 0.05 m (import_dialog.py:106-108) and is the reconstruction's ONLY metric anchor; it is stored nowhere. check_rig does fire three warnings on this rig ('nominal up is 89 deg off vertical', 'intrinsics for left and right were assumed from the image size', 'the two cameras differ in resolution (left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048)') — so the user is told the intrinsics are guessed, with no number attached and no way to act.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Have resolve_calibration return a CalibrationReport and save_rig write a 'meta' block / calibration/report.json: {world_tag_id, dictionary_name, world_frame_id, marker_length_m, per-camera PnP RMS px, runner-up IPPE RMS and the angle between the two normals, ippe_err_ratio_per_camera, n_tag_observations_used, up_world, solver:'single-tag'|'bundle', residual_rms_px, camera baseline cm, convergence angle, world-up tilt}. main_window._refresh_calibration_status reads them so the sidebar can say 'world = tag 14, frame 0001, 1 of 97 observations, ambiguity margin 9.3x', with an explicit 'ambiguous tag pose' flag when the runner-up RMS is within 2x. Backwards compatible: load_extrinsics_json (resolve.py:36-58) treats a missing 'meta' as unknown provenance.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-extrinsics/h1_h3_h4_v2.json, SUMMARY.json; <SP>/find-tests-ui/ui_signals.json (check_rig_shipped, world_up_tilt_deg)

## F31 [minor] Blanket excepts in the 3D view and the rig loader make a missing or mis-scaled character look like a bad reconstruction

- file: pose3d/ui/view3d.py:99 | category: robustness | sources: retarget, tests-ui | auditor severity: major
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The three cited blanket excepts are real, are on the executed path, and do collapse distinct failures into an unexplained UI state — but two of the claimed consequences are wrong, and the most consequential instance of the pattern is elsewhere. (1) view3d.py:199-206 is confirmed: a legitimate "no usable pelvis" (character.py:547-551 -> 684-685) and a genuine crash (missing character.npz -> FileNotFoundError; a rig missing the 'hips'/'upper_arm' role -> ValueError at character.py:172, both verified by executing the real _skin) both end in (None,None,None) -> _set_body(None) hides the mesh, the view silently falls back to drawing the captured skeleton (view3d.py:167-172), nothing is logged. (2) view3d.py:91-100 is confirmed as a blanket swallow, but its stated consequence is not reachable: every Character() failure I could construct leaves self._character = None, so the next _skin also fails and the mesh disappears rather than rendering at a stale scale; only an exception injected inside fit_to_subject leaves a stale _scale. The real silent mis-sizing is a different mechanism at character.py:353 — fit_to_subject returns None with NO exception when no bone is measurable, and character.py:554-559 then falls back to per-frame height scaling, which on this take would make the character 7.8% off on median and pulse over a 37.9% range across 26 frames. Fixing only the except would not catch that; the no-fit branch needs its own report. (3) app.py:87-96 is confirmed for missing files, missing keys and truncated JSON (all four verified), all reduced to "Not calibrated" in red with no reason via main_window.py:287 + panels.py:267-275 — but NOT for a bad shape: a 2x2 rotation matrix builds a CalibratedRig and only explodes later inside triangulation, so that site also lacks validation. (4) The finding misses blender_export.py:44-53, which is the same pattern on the deliverable rather than the preview: two blanket excepts around the Character import and construction return (None,None), export_animation (blender_export.py:132-135) then drops "bone_frames" and blender_job.py:616-625 silently substitutes a different aim-only Damped-Track retarget — so an asset failure silently changes the EXPORTED pose and breaks the documented "export is pose-identical to the view" invariant, with no message. (5) The inventory numbers are off: 11 bare `except Exception:` sites, and the remaining ones are silent-but-benign no-ops, not sites that re-raise or report. Impact on the client take remains exactly zero (26/26 frames posed, 0 exceptions, PELVIS never missing), and the asset is explicitly bundled at pose3d.spec:28-29, so this is a diagnostics/robustness finding with no bearing on the reported "character does not follow the keypoints" symptom. The proposed fix is aimed correctly for (1) and (3) but should add: report the silent no-fit at character.py:353, validate R/t shapes in _load_rig instead of only catching, and surface the export's character-unavailable fallback. The suggested "derive a pelvis from NECK and torso length" fallback addresses a condition that occurs in 0/26 frames here and is speculative.
- VERDICT REASON: All three cited code sites exist exactly as described and I re-derived each by reading the file and by executing the real unbound methods. view3d.py:91-100 `fit_subject` wraps Character()+fit_to_subject in `except Exception: pass`; view3d.py:199-206 `_skin` wraps Character()+pose_and_joints in `except Exception: return None, None, None`; character.py:547-551 returns a 4-tuple of None when PELVIS and both hips are absent, character.py:684-685 collapses that to (None,None,None) — identical to the except branch; character.py:170-172 raises ValueError for a rig missing the hips/upper_arm roles; app.py:87-96 `_load_rig` returns None on any exception. All are on the live path: main_window.py:507 calls fit_subject inside _apply_view_orientation, main_window.py:450 calls set_pose (which calls _skin at view3d.py:169), app.py:75 calls _load_rig, and main_window.py:285-288 feeds `rig is not None` into Sidebar.set_calibrated (panels.py:267-275), which prints only "Not calibrated" in red with no reason. I reproduced the evidence measurement exactly and confirmed there is no impact on the client take. It is right in substance, wrong in several details, and it misses a fourth site that matters more than the three it names.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: DROPOUT SWEEP (my own script, 26 fitted3d frames x 5 patterns = 130 poses, Character() with the shipped asset, scale fitted to the take = 113.61998): exceptions 0/130; (None,None,None) returned 52/130 = 26 from dropping PELVIS+LEFT_HIP+RIGHT_HIP + 26 from all-joints-missing; pelvis-only, legs-only and arms-only dropouts all posed 26/26. Real take with unmodified fitted3d: posed 26/26, none 0, exc 0. Reproduces the finding's numbers exactly. Missingness on the take: pose3d has 2 NaN joints total, fitted3d 0; PELVIS is NaN in 0/26 frames and PELVIS+both hips in 0/26, so the legitimate-None path is unreachable on this data. Note the all-missing case never reaches _skin anyway: set_pose returns via _clear() at view3d.py:132-135.
INJECTED-FAILURE TESTS, calling the actual V.View3D.fit_subject / V.View3D._skin on a stub: (A) character.npz path missing -> raw Character() raises FileNotFoundError; fit_subject swallowed it and left self._character=None; _skin returned (None,None,None). (B) npz with the 'hips' role removed and bones renamed -> raw Character() raises ValueError "rig is missing the 'hips' role"; fit_subject swallowed it, _character stayed None; _skin -> (None,None,None). (C) Character() OK but fit_to_subject monkeypatched to raise: _scale stayed at the previous 113.61998 (silently stale) and _skin still returned a mesh (1752,3).
REACHABILITY OF THE 'WRONG SIZE' CONSEQUENCE: fit_to_subject never raised on any degenerate input I could build (all-NaN -> scale None; zeros -> None; single frame -> 111.22; poses*1e6 -> 1.14e-4; short array -> None). So the mis-sizing symptom is NOT produced by the except at view3d.py:99 in practice; it is produced by the silent `den > 1e-12` guard at character.py:353 setting _scale=None with no exception at all, after which character.py:554-559 falls back to a per-frame height scale. I measured that fallback on the de-tilted take: uniform fitted scale 113.62 vs per-frame median 122.46 (min 111.17, max 157.57) = character 7.8% off on median and pulsing over a 37.9% range across the 26 frames.
_LOAD_RIG PROBE (copies of the real calibration folder, mutated): pristine -> rig; missing folder -> None; truncated extrinsics.json -> None (swallowed json.JSONDecodeError); extrinsics.json without the "right" key -> None (swallowed KeyError 'right'); right_intrinsics.json deleted -> None (swallowed FileNotFoundError); left_intrinsics.json = "{}" -> None (swallowed KeyError 'K'). BUT left R replaced by a 2x2 matrix -> _load_rig RETURNS A RIG; the failure surfaces only later, as ValueError inside triangulation. So the "bad shape" example in the claim is wrong: that case is under-validated, not over-caught.
EXCEPT INVENTORY (my grep over pose3d/): 20 `except Exception` lines, of which 11 are bare `except Exception:` — intrinsics.py:91, view3d.py:99, view3d.py:205, app.py:95, app.py:143, blender_export.py:48, blender_export.py:52, blender_export.py:170, blender_job.py:128, blender_job.py:334, main_window.py:33. Not 12, and the claim that "the other nine re-raise, show a QMessageBox, or emit a statusMessage" is false — intrinsics.py:91, blender_export.py:170, blender_job.py:128/334 and main_window.py:33 are silent no-ops (benign ones).
MISSED SITE: blender_export.py:44-53 wraps the character import and Character() in two separate blanket excepts, each returning (None,None). export_animation at blender_export.py:132-135 then simply omits "bone_frames" from the job document, and blender_job.py:616-625 silently switches to a different "aim-only Damped-Track retarget from joint positions". No message anywhere.
PACKAGING: pose3d/assets/character.npz exists (53599 bytes) and is explicitly bundled at pose3d.spec:28-29, so the asset-load failure is a packaging/corruption regression, not a routine condition.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: View3D.fit_subject wraps the whole character-scale fit in `except Exception: pass` (view3d.py:99-100), so if Character() cannot load or fit_to_subject raises, the character keeps whatever uniform scale it had and renders at the wrong size with no message. View3D._skin wraps the skinning call in `except Exception: return None, None, None` (view3d.py:199-206), so both a legitimate None — _skin_matrices returns (None,None,None,None) when PELVIS and both hips are absent (character.py:547-551), propagating through pose_and_joints (character.py:684-685) — and any genuine crash (missing/corrupt character.npz, a rig missing the 'hips'/'upper_arm' role for which Character.__init__ raises ValueError at character.py:172) end in the same place: _set_body(None) hides the mesh and nothing is logged. app._load_rig (app.py:95) returns None for any malformed calibration file — missing key, bad shape, truncated JSON — collapsing every distinct failure into 'Not calibrated' with no reason.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Exhaustive sparse-pose sweep over 26 frames x 5 dropout patterns (130 poses): 0 exceptions raised, 52 poses returned (None, None, None), every one a pattern removing PELVIS + LEFT_HIP + RIGHT_HIP, plus the all-missing case; with the take's real fitted3d the character posed in 26/26 frames, so this does not fire today. Grep over pose3d/ finds 12 blanket `except Exception` sites; these three (view3d.py:99, view3d.py:205, app.py:95) are the ones on the path between the measured pose and what the user sees — the other nine either re-raise, show a QMessageBox, or emit a statusMessage. The character's scale is the quantity the retarget audit measures (111.10 raw / 113.62 fitted), so a silently stale scale is directly a 'character does not match' symptom.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Separate the cases. Let Character.pose_and_joints raise a typed PoseUnavailable for 'no usable pelvis' instead of returning None; in View3D._skin catch that narrowly and catch Exception separately, routing the rest to a new View3D.characterError = Signal(str) that MainWindow shows on the status bar and in the 3D PREVIEW card header ('character could not be posed: <reason>'). Same for fit_subject (view3d.py:91-100). In _load_rig catch FileNotFoundError separately (return None quietly — genuinely uncalibrated) from json.JSONDecodeError/KeyError/ValueError (return a reason string Sidebar.set_calibrated displays). Add a fallback: when PELVIS and both hips are missing but the shoulders are present, derive a pelvis from NECK and the torso length rather than giving up.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-retarget/probe2.py, probe2.json (H6); <SP>/find-tests-ui/ (full except inventory in notes)

## F33 [minor] Manual drags leave the derived NECK/PELVIS stale — the retarget ROOT is never recomputed after an edit

- file: pose3d/ui/model.py:150 | category: live-edit | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Manual drags leave the derived NECK/PELVIS permanently stale, and no recompute repairs them. derive_joints builds NECK/PELVIS as per-view 2D midpoints of the shoulders/hips (skeleton.py:170-174) and runs ONLY inside the detector (rtmpose.py:30). _resolve_joint re-triangulates the single dragged index (model.py:150-155), so after a shoulder/hip correction the derived joint's 2D stays at the old midpoint (exactly half the drag: 30.0 px for a 60 px drag, 208/208 cases) and its RAW 3D does not move at all (0.000 mm); only fit_bone_lengths drags the fitted point 62-66% of the way, leaving a 34-46% residual. On the client take a 60 px one-view drag leaves the derived joint 3.31 mm off (median over 208 cases; p90 5.24, max 10.66) = 2.8% of body height (p90 4.4%, max 9.0%), and it is the worst-placed joint in the pose in every case. The error is SUBLINEAR in drag size, not linear: 0.055 mm/px at 10 px falling to 0.024 mm/px at 224 px, so a full-shoulder-span 224 px correction leaves the root 5.4 mm (4.6% of height) wrong, not 7 mm; the 0.178 mm/px coefficient in the original evidence is a different probe misapplied (a one-view drag moves the dragged joint 0.089 mm/px). Two facts the finding missed make it worse and one makes it milder: (a) recompute_all / "Recalculate 3D" cannot fix it because triangulate_project consumes frame.kp2d verbatim (pipeline.py:138-142) — measured: NECK still 30.0 px stale afterwards — so the staleness is written into project.json by save_project and carried into the Blender export; only a full re-detection rewrites kp2d, and that discards the corrections; (b) it is invisible in the UI, since _accuracy (model.py:180-191) reprojects the stale 3D against the equally stale 2D, leaving the derived joint's accuracy number and RAG colour unchanged; (c) NECK/PELVIS are themselves draggable (camera_view.py:117-123), so a manual workaround exists. Fix: re-derive kp2d[cam][NECK|PELVIS] from the current canonical shoulder/hip 2D after the stack edit and re-run _resolve_joint on the derived index (dependents = {L/R_SHOULDER: NECK, L/R_HIP: PELVIS}), skipping any derived joint whose f.corrected[cam] flag is set so a hand-placed neck/pelvis is not clobbered, and record the derived change on the correction stack so one Ctrl+Z reverses the whole edit (the same pattern already used for the HEAD/nose sync at model.py:137-149). No extra joint2dChanged emit is needed: main_window.py:206 already repaints every joint from f.kp2d via _refresh_overlays (main_window.py:468-473).
- VERDICT REASON: Core mechanism confirmed by code I read myself and reproduced numerically to 3 s.f. with my own script driving the REAL ProjectModel (no reimplementation).

CODE (read by me):
- NECK/PELVIS exist only as per-view 2D midpoints computed at DETECTION time: skeleton.py:170-174 in derive_joints; the only production caller is pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:30 (`self._map = map_halpe26 if feet else derive_joints`). grep shows no other caller outside tests.
- Edit path is live: camera_view.py:117-123 builds a draggable JointItem per joint -> _on_released -> jointDragged -> main_window.py:196-197 wiring -> main_window.py:230-231 `_on_drag` -> model.set_joint_2d (model.py:97-103) -> _resolve_joint (model.py:120-171), which re-triangulates exactly one index (model.py:150-155) and re-fits the frame (model.py:160). Nothing re-derives the midpoints.
- No recovery short of full re-detection: triangulate_project consumes frame.kp2d verbatim (pipeline.py:138-142), so recompute_all / "Recalculate 3D" (model.py:46-59) cannot repair it; only detect_project (pipeline.py:38-46) rewrites kp2d, and that discards corrections. The stale kp2d is what save_project persists and what export consumes.
- PELVIS really is the retarget origin: character.py:566 `to_rig(p) = self.hips_world + (Rz @ (p - pelvis)) * scale`, pelvis read at character.py:547; NECK parents both shoulder bones and the head (skeleton.py:86-88).

WRONG IN THE FINDING (details, not substance):
1. "Error is linear in drag size (0.178 mm of 3D per px of one-view 2D shift)" is wrong on both count and coefficient. It is clearly SUBLINEAR (the bone fit compensates more as the stale midpoint gets more absurd): 0.055 mm/px at a 10 px drag falling monotonically to 0.024 mm/px at 224 px. 0.178 is the artifact's separate B4_mm_per_px probe misapplied; my measured 3D motion per px of ONE-view drag on the dragged joint is 0.089 mm/px (5.32 mm for 60 px).
2. "A gross ~200 px correction would leave the root ~7 mm (6% of height) wrong" overstates: at a 224 px drag (the full left-view shoulder span) I measure 5.40 mm = 4.6% of height.
3. The fix item "Emit joint2dChanged for the derived index so the 2D views redraw it" is unnecessary: main_window.py:206 connects joint2dChanged to `lambda *_: self._refresh_overlays()`, and _refresh_overlays (main_window.py:468-473) repaints ALL joints from f.kp2d[cam]. Fixing kp2d[derived] suffices.
4. The fix must not clobber a hand-corrected derived joint: NECK/PELVIS are themselves draggable (a JointItem is built for every j in range(NUM_JOINTS), camera_view.py:117-123) — that is both the existing workaround and a hazard for a blind auto-re-derive; gate on f.corrected[cam][derived].

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATION? None. In the shipped path RAW pose3d[derived] moves exactly 0.0 mm (measured, all cases) — unambiguous staleness, not fit noise; fitted3d[derived] moves 62-66% of the required distance only because fit_bone_lengths drags it along the PELVIS-hip / NECK-shoulder bones. The bug is also SILENT: _accuracy (model.py:180-191) reprojects f.pose3d against f.kp2d, and after an edit the derived joint's 3D and 2D are both unchanged and mutually consistent, so its reprojection number and RAG colour do not move.

Severity stays minor: 0 corrections on the client take, so none of this touches the shipped numbers; it is a real correctness bug confined to frames the user edits.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts (mine): <SP>/verify-F33/stale_derived.py and fraction.py; outputs stale_derived.json, fraction.json. Method: real ProjectModel(deepcopy(project), rig) per trial; APP path = set_joint_2d(cam, joint, x+dx, y); CORRECT path = same drag, then kp2d[cam][derived] := midpoint of the current canonical shoulder/hip pair in that cam, plus _resolve_joint(derived). Height reference 0.1178 m.

HEADLINE (frame 0001, left cam, 60 px) — matches the finding to 3 s.f.:
- LEFT_HIP drag: PELVIS 2D stale 30.0 px, PELVIS 3D error 2.084 mm = 1.769% of height; whole-pose max error 2.084 mm, argmax IS PELVIS. (finding: 30.0 px / 2.08 mm / 1.77%)
- LEFT_SHOULDER drag: NECK 2D stale 30.0 px, NECK 3D error 1.894 mm = 1.608%; whole-pose max 1.894 mm. (finding: 1.89 mm / 1.61%)

DISTRIBUTION, 208 cases = 26 frames x 2 cams x {L/R shoulder, L/R hip}, 60 px drag:
- derived-2D staleness: median = p90 = max = 30.0 px (exactly half the drag); app-path derived 2D unchanged in 208/208.
- derived 3D error: median 3.31 mm, p90 5.24, max 10.66 mm = 2.81% / 4.45% / 9.05% of body height. PELVIS cases median 3.34 / max 10.66; NECK cases median 2.91 / max 9.66.
- the stale derived joint is the worst joint in the pose in every case (max3d_err == derived3d_err throughout); the dragged joint itself is off by a median 1.43 mm as a side effect.
- scale: the drag itself moves the dragged joint a median 9.37 mm (p90 13.2), so the residual is ~35% of the correction's own magnitude.

SUBLINEARITY SWEEP (frame 0001, left cam, LEFT_HIP / LEFT_SHOULDER):
 10 px -> 0.55 / 0.36 mm; 30 px -> 1.26 / 1.01; 60 px -> 2.08 / 1.89; 120 px -> 3.48 / 3.37; 224 px -> 5.40 / 5.08 mm.
 mm per px of drag: 0.055 -> 0.042 -> 0.035 -> 0.029 -> 0.024. Median left-view shoulder span 224.2 px, hip span 108.8 px (right view 91.6 / 53.0 px).

FRACTION OF NEEDED MOTION MISSED (frame 0001, left cam):
- 30 px: required derived move 2.72 mm (hip) / 2.46 mm (shoulder); app moves 1.62 / 1.54; residual 1.26 / 1.01 mm = 46.3% / 41.0%.
- 60 px: required 5.38 / 4.91; app 3.40 / 3.12; residual 2.08 / 1.89 mm = 38.8% / 38.6%.
- 120 px: required 10.21 / 9.44; app 6.78 / 6.18; residual 3.48 / 3.37 mm = 34.1% / 35.7%.
- RAW pose3d[derived] moves 0.000 mm in the app path in every case, vs 2.68-10.62 mm in the correct path.

NO REPAIR PATH: after a 60 px LEFT_SHOULDER drag followed by model.recompute_all(), kp2d[left][NECK] is bit-identical to its pre-drag value and still 30.0 px from the true shoulder midpoint (recompute_all_repairs_derived_2d = false).
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: NECK and PELVIS have no detector of their own: derive_joints builds them as per-view 2D midpoints of the shoulders / hips (skeleton.py:170-174). _resolve_joint re-triangulates ONLY the dragged joint index (model.py:150-155) and nothing recomputes the midpoints, so after the user corrects a shoulder or a hip the derived joint keeps pointing at the OLD midpoint, in both 2D (still drawn at the stale place) and 3D. PELVIS is the character's root and NECK is the parent of both shoulder bones and the head.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Simulated 60 px drag of LEFT_HIP in the left view, frame 0001, vs the same drag with the midpoint correctly re-derived: PELVIS 2D 30.0 px stale, PELVIS 3D lands 2.08 mm (1.77% of height) off; whole-pose max error 2.08 mm. A 60 px LEFT_SHOULDER drag: NECK 30.0 px stale, 1.89 mm (1.61%) 3D error. Error is linear in drag size (0.178 mm of 3D per px of one-view 2D shift) and the bone fit only partly compensates. The shoulder span in the left view is 224 px, so a gross ~200 px correction would leave the root ~7 mm (6% of height) wrong.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Add skeleton.rederive_derived(xy, scores) (or an in-place variant of derive_joints) recomputing NECK/PELVIS from the current canonical shoulder/hip 2D. Call it from ProjectModel.set_joint_2d / _resolve_joint after the stack edit, then re-triangulate the affected derived index as well as the dragged one: `for j in {joint} | dependents(joint): retriangulate(j)` with dependents = {L/R_SHOULDER: NECK, L/R_HIP: PELVIS}. Emit joint2dChanged for the derived index so the 2D views redraw it, and record the derived-joint change on the correction stack so undo restores it.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/probe2.json (B3_stale_derived_LEFT_HIP, B3_stale_derived_LEFT_SHOULDER, B4_mm_per_px)

## F34 [minor] Fit targets are per-take medians of the raw triangulation, so upstream error is baked in as a permanent skeleton: 6.7% forearm and 4.6% thigh L/R asymmetry on a symmetric mannequin

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:158 | category: bone-fit | sources: pipeline | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Fit targets are per-take medians of the raw triangulation (pose3d/pipeline.py:158 via bonefit.py:22-31), reached from import_dialog.py:213-214, model.py:52-55, and again from model.py:173-177 on the manual-correction path, with no caller supplying bone_lengths. On the client take all 14 targets are medians of raw, and fit_bone_lengths then holds the output to them within 0.019 mm median / 0.142 mm max — roughly 75x tighter than the error the targets themselves contain, so the fit's own residuals can never reveal it. Two of the four limb pairs are measurably asymmetric on a subject that should be symmetric: forearm 21.353 vs 19.965 mm = 6.72% (1.388 mm, 1.18% of the 0.118 m body height) and thigh 37.170 vs 35.492 mm = 4.62% (1.678 mm, 1.42%); upper arm 0.48% and shin 0.72% are clean. This is systematic, not sampling noise: a 300-trial Monte Carlo over a perfectly symmetric synthetic subject shot through the client's own rig, at the pixel-noise level that reproduces the take's own 5.05% bone CV, never once reaches these values (forearm p99 4.24%, max 4.49%; thigh p99 2.61%, max 2.77%; 0/300 in both cases), and the per-frame paired differences are consistent in sign (forearm L>R in 20/26 frames, t=3.47; thigh L>R in 21/25, t=5.18).

CORRECTIONS TO THE FILED FINDING:
(a) The enforcement figure is 0.02 mm median / 0.14 mm max, not 0.01 mm.
(b) The finding's proposed UI copy — "this points at the calibration, not the detector" — is wrong and must not ship. With GROUND-TRUTH calibration on the Panoptic reference, RTMPose alone produces 6.48% thigh asymmetry (n=10 on both sides) where the ground-truth 3D is 1.36% asymmetric: 5.1 pp of purely detector-induced asymmetry, MORE than the client take's 4.62%. And on the client take the two asymmetric bones are precisely the two with a lopsided endpoint 2D residual (LEFT_WRIST 7.91 px vs RIGHT_WRIST 4.99 px; RIGHT_HIP 9.47 px vs LEFT_HIP 6.09 px) while the symmetric pairs have balanced endpoints (elbows 4.20/4.00, knees 5.86/5.05). Per-keypoint localisation is a detector signature; a wrong focal or wrong single-tag extrinsics would bias by depth and limb orientation, not by joint identity.
(c) The proposed 3% warning threshold is below the measured noise ceiling (p99 4.24% at this take's noise level) and would fire on clean takes; ~5% on 26 frames is defensible.

CORRECTED FIX: still do NOT symmetrise (reproduced: symmetrising moves the pose by 0.308 mm median and makes reprojection slightly worse, 7.439/4.193 vs 7.381/4.008 px). Add bonefit.bone_length_report(poses3d) returning per-bone median/std/CV plus the L/R ratios and surface it next to rejection_note — but pair each flagged pair with the per-joint 2D reprojection residual of its four endpoints, and word it as "the two views place <JOINT> less consistently than its mirror — check that keypoint in both images", not as a calibration verdict. The optional user-supplied bone_lengths override remains a good idea (fit_project already accepts it).

SEVERITY: MINOR. The target choice changes the delivered pose by 0.308 mm median (0.26% of body height) against the smoother's 6.07 mm, so this is a diagnostic gap, not a cause of the client's complaint.
- VERDICT REASON: The mechanism and every headline number reproduce independently; one sub-claim (the diagnostic attribution baked into the proposed fix) is refuted, and one detail ("0.01 mm") is wrong.

CODE PATH — CONFIRMED ON THE EXECUTED PATH. pose3d/pipeline.py:152 `def fit_project(project, bone_lengths=None, ...)`; :156-160 builds `raw = np.stack([f.pose3d ...])` then `measured = measure_bone_lengths(raw)` (:158), filling only never-observed bones from `fallback_bone_lengths()`. pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:22-31 confirms it is the per-bone MEDIAN over frames of the raw triangulation. Both live callers pass no `bone_lengths`: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:213-214 (`_process`: `dropped = triangulate_project(project, rig); fit_project(project, smooth=True)`) and pose3d/ui/model.py:52-55 (`recompute_all`). The manual-drag re-fit is a third instance of the same mechanism: pose3d/ui/model.py:173-177 `_compute_bone_lengths` duplicates the identical median-of-raw logic and model.py:157-160 feeds it to `fit_bone_lengths` per corrected frame. On this take zero bones fell back (my B_any_fallback_used = []), so all 14 targets are medians of raw triangulation.

MAGNITUDE — CONFIRMED. All four asymmetry figures reproduce to 2 decimals (forearm 6.72%, thigh 4.62%, shin 0.72%, upper arm 0.48%), as does the symmetrisation negative result (0.308 mm median / 0.590 max pose delta; reprojection 7.439/4.193 px symmetric vs 7.381/4.008 asymmetric — symmetrising is indeed slightly worse).

IS IT REALLY SYSTEMATIC? — CONFIRMED, and strengthened. The finding asserts it with no null model, so I built one: take the client's own 26 poses, refit them to perfectly SYMMETRIC targets to make a synthetic ground truth, project through the client's own K/R/t, add i.i.d. Gaussian 2D noise, re-triangulate, re-measure the median targets — 300 trials per noise level. At sigma=5 px (which reproduces the real take's 5.05% median bone CV as 5.36%), noise alone gives forearm asymmetry median 1.28% / p99 4.24% / max 4.49% and thigh 0.75% / p99 2.61% / max 2.77%; 0 of 300 trials reached the observed 6.72% or 4.62%. Paired per-frame differences agree: forearm +1.165 mm mean, t=3.47, L>R in 20/26 frames; thigh +2.499 mm, t=5.18, L>R in 21/25. Upper arm (0.48%) and shin (0.72%) sit AT or BELOW the noise median (1.15%, 0.99%) — exactly two of four pairs carry real bias.

WHAT I REFUTE — THE ATTRIBUTION, i.e. the sentence the proposed fix wants to put in the UI: "a rigid subject should give ~0 — this points at the calibration, not the detector." Two independent measurements say the detector is at least as likely and probably the actual cause. (1) On the Panoptic reference with GROUND-TRUTH calibration, triangulating the stored RTMPose 2D gives thigh L/R asymmetry of 6.48% (n=10 valid frames on BOTH sides, no missing-data caveat) while the ground-truth 3D poses for the same 10 frames are 1.36% asymmetric — a perfect calibration still leaves 5.1 pp of detector-induced thigh asymmetry, LARGER than the client take's 4.62%. (2) On the client take the two asymmetric bones are precisely the two whose endpoint has a lopsided 2D residual: LEFT_WRIST reprojects at 7.91 px vs RIGHT_WRIST 4.99 px (left view) and RIGHT_HIP at 9.47 px vs LEFT_HIP 6.09 px, while the symmetric pairs have balanced endpoints (elbows 4.20/4.00, knees 5.86/5.05). Per-JOINT localisation is a detector signature; a wrong focal or wrong single-tag extrinsics biases lengths by depth and limb orientation, not by which individual keypoint is placed badly. The finding's `impact` text hedges correctly ("single-tag extrinsics, OR the assumed focal, OR the detector's distal bias F05") but the fix ships the un-hedged version, which would push the user to recalibrate a rig that is not the problem.

DETAIL WRONG: "enforces those numbers to 0.01 mm" — I measure the refit's deviation from its own targets at 0.019 mm median, 0.066 mm p90, 0.142 mm max. The point survives (enforcement is ~75x tighter than the 1.4-1.7 mm asymmetry, so the fit residual genuinely cannot reveal it) but the number should be 0.02/0.14 mm.

SECOND FIX DETAIL WRONG: the proposed "warn when any L/R asymmetry > 3%" threshold is below my measured noise ceiling — pure 2D noise at this take's level reaches p99 4.24% (forearm) and max 4.49% with a perfect calibration and a perfectly symmetric subject. A 3% gate fires on clean takes; ~5% on 26 frames is defensible.

SEVERITY: minor is right and I would not raise it. The choice of target changes the output pose by 0.308 mm median (0.26% of the 0.118 m body height), against the smoother's 6.07 mm, so this is not a candidate cause of the client's complaint. Its value is purely diagnostic — and the diagnosis it actually supports is "look at this specific keypoint in 2D", not "recalibrate".
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: ALL RUN BY ME; scripts in /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F34/ (f34.py -> f34.json, mc.py -> mc.json, panoptic.py -> panoptic.json). Repo untouched.

1) RAW PER-BONE STATS, client take, n=26 frames, ddof=1 (mm):
  PELVIS->NECK med 41.075 std 2.058 CV 4.95% | NECK->HEAD 21.862 std 2.123 CV 9.77% range 8.44
  NECK->L_SH 15.965 CV 5.20% | NECK->R_SH 16.006 CV 5.06%
  L_SH->L_ELB 22.410 std 1.234 CV 5.58% | R_SH->R_ELB 22.518 std 0.902 CV 3.97%
  L_ELB->L_WR 21.353 std 1.085 CV 5.05% | R_ELB->R_WR 19.965 std 1.114 CV 5.49%
  PELV->L_HIP 8.319 CV 7.29% | PELV->R_HIP 8.311 CV 7.28%
  L_HIP->L_KNEE 37.170 std 1.865 CV 4.95% | R_HIP->R_KNEE 35.492 std 2.101 CV 5.96% (n=25)
  L_KNEE->L_ANK 37.110 std 1.176 CV 3.18% (n=25) | R_KNEE->R_ANK 36.845 std 2.133 CV 5.82% range 11.398 (n=25)
  -> CV span 3.18-9.77%, std span 0.61-2.13 mm. (Finding quotes 3.11-9.52% / 0.60-2.09 mm = the same with ddof=0.)

2) TARGETS ACTUALLY USED by fit_project (medians; 0 bones fell back to _FALLBACK_LENGTHS):
  forearm   L 21.353 vs R 19.965 mm -> diff 1.388 mm, asym 6.72%
  thigh     L 37.170 vs R 35.492 mm -> diff 1.678 mm, asym 4.62%

  vs 0.118 m body height: 1.18% and 1.42%. (Finding: 6.72 / 4.62 / 0.72 / 0.48 and 1.2-1.4% — exact match.)

3) IS IT NOISE? paired per-frame (L-R), mm:
  forearm   n=26 mean +1.165 sd 1.711 t=+3.47 L>R in 20/26 (sign test p=0.0094)
  thigh     n=25 mean +2.499 sd 2.414 t=+5.18 L>R in 21/25 (p=0.0009)
  shin      n=24 mean +0.402 sd 2.123 t=+0.93 12/24 (null)
  upper arm n=26 mean -0.606 sd 1.529 t=-2.02 12/26 (weak)

4) MONTE CARLO NULL (new; 300 trials/level). Synthetic subject = the client's poses refit to exactly symmetric targets (residual asymmetry <=0.15%), projected through the client's own rig, i.i.d. Gaussian pixel noise, re-triangulated, medians re-measured:
  sigma=3px -> take-CV 3.25%; forearm asym med 0.76 p99 2.65; thigh med 0.50 p99 1.70
  sigma=5px -> take-CV 5.36% (matches the real 5.05%); forearm med 1.28 p90 3.12 p99 4.24 max 4.49, 0/300 >= 6.72; thigh med 0.75 p90 1.94 p99 2.61 max 2.77, 0/300 >= 4.62
  sigma=7px -> take-CV 7.49%; forearm 3/300 >= 6.72; thigh 1/300 >= 4.62
  Observed upper arm 0.48% and shin 0.72% are BELOW the sigma=5 noise medians (1.15%, 0.99%).
  => forearm and thigh asymmetry is systematic at p < 1/300 at the noise level that reproduces the take's own bone CV.

5) HOW TIGHTLY ARE TARGETS ENFORCED? |fitted bone length - target| over 26 frames x 14 bones: median 0.0188 mm, p90 0.0663 mm, max 0.142 mm (worst PELVIS->NECK 0.142, NECK->HEAD 0.136). Not 0.01 mm, but ~75x tighter than the 1.4 mm asymmetry it bakes in.

6) SYMMETRISATION EXPERIMENT (reproduced exactly): forcing L/R-mean targets moves the fitted pose by 0.308 mm median / 0.458 p90 / 0.590 mm max, and reprojection goes 7.381/4.008 px (asymmetric targets) -> 7.439/4.193 px (symmetric), vs 5.107/2.783 px for the raw triangulation. Symmetrising is a no-op at best.

7) ATTRIBUTION TEST — Panoptic, GROUND-TRUTH calibration (data/demo_project + data/hdPose3d, 10 frames, cm):
  RTMPose 2D triangulated with GT calibration: thigh L 38.517 / R 41.098 = 6.48% asym (n_L=10, n_R=10); upper arm 13.29% and forearm 17.31% but n_L=3, unusable.
  GROUND-TRUTH 3D, same 10 frames: thigh L 44.731 / R 44.126 = 1.36%; forearm 0.86%; upper arm 3.16%; shin 2.14% (all n=10).
  => a perfect calibration leaves 6.48-1.36 = 5.1 pp of thigh asymmetry attributable to the detector alone — larger than the client take's 4.62%.

8) PER-JOINT 2D RESIDUAL LOCALISATION, client take, raw, median px (left view / right view):
  LEFT_WRIST 7.91/4.36 vs RIGHT_WRIST 4.99/2.63  (the 6.72% forearm pair)
  RIGHT_HIP  9.47/5.21 vs LEFT_HIP   6.09/3.41  (the 4.62% thigh pair)
  LEFT_ELBOW 4.20/2.38 vs RIGHT_ELBOW 4.00/2.13 (the 0.48% upper-arm pair — balanced)
  LEFT_KNEE  5.86/3.24 vs RIGHT_KNEE  5.05/2.77 (the 0.72% shin pair — balanced)
  The asymmetric bones are exactly the ones with a lopsided endpoint residual, and the lopsided endpoint is a single named keypoint each time.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: `measured = measure_bone_lengths(raw)` takes the median over frames of the RAW triangulation and fit_bone_lengths then enforces those numbers to 0.01 mm. Whatever systematic error the calibration and 2D detector contribute becomes the definition of the subject's skeleton for the whole take, and the fit's own residuals can no longer reveal it. The subject is a rigid, bilaterally symmetric mannequin, so the true L/R difference is ~0.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Raw per-bone spread across 26 frames (a rigid subject implies 0): CV 3.11-9.52%, std 0.60-2.09 mm; worst neck-head 9.52%, R shin 5.67% (11.40 mm range), R thigh 5.80%. L/R asymmetry of the targets used: forearm 21.35 vs 19.96 mm = 6.72% (1.39 mm), thigh 37.17 vs 35.49 mm = 4.62% (1.68 mm), shin 0.72%, upper arm 0.48%. So 1.4-1.7 mm (1.2-1.4% of body height) of the reconstruction is systematic. IMPORTANT NEGATIVE: forcing symmetric targets (L/R mean) changes the fitted pose by only 0.308 mm median / 0.590 mm max and makes reprojection slightly WORSE (7.439/4.194 px vs 7.381/4.008 px) — symmetrising hides the evidence rather than recovering the error.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Do NOT symmetrise the targets. Add bonefit.bone_length_report(poses3d) -> dict returning per-bone median/std/CV and the four L/R asymmetry ratios, call it once in fit_project, and show it in the import dialog next to rejection_note with thresholds (warn when any L/R asymmetry > 3% or any CV > 6%) and the sentence 'a rigid subject should give ~0 — this points at the calibration, not the detector'. Optionally let the user supply true measured limb lengths as a bone_lengths override, which fit_project already accepts.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-pipeline/probe.json (H3_raw_bones, H3_symmetry_raw, H3_targets_used), probe2.json (D_symmetric_targets)

## F36 [minor] The epipolar gate is 14.6x the observed median and is scaled from the LEFT image only, so it is inert for detector hallucinations and lets 7 deg of calibration error pass silently

- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:53 | category: cross-view-validation | sources: extrinsics, intrinsics | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: The epipolar gate is ~15x wider than the data it judges, and the quantity it judges is not in either camera's pixels — so it misses the exact hallucination its docstring names and stays silent through ~7 deg of extrinsic error. (a) `_EPI_THR_FRAC = 0.014` (pipeline.py:53) sizes the tolerance from the IMAGE, not the data: 71.50 px against an observed body-keypoint Sampson distribution of median 4.91 / p99 23.97 / max 29.38 px over 388 pairs — 14.6x the median, 3.0x p99, 0/388 rejected, so the gate is provably inert on this take. (b) `epipolar_threshold` (pipeline.py:57-60) reads only `rig.intr[CAM_LEFT].image_size`, and this rig really is mixed-resolution — left 3072x4080 f=4080, right 1536x2048 f=2048, exactly 2:1 — so the gate is 1.400% of the left diagonal (5107 px) but 2.793% of the right's (2560 px). (c) The Sampson distance it is compared against (triangulate.py:99-103) sums squared line gradients from both images, giving (d_L^-2 + d_R^-2)^-1/2 — dominated by the SMALLER, low-resolution residual. Measured: d_L median 10.21 px, d_R median 5.57 px, Sampson 4.91 px, i.e. BELOW both, 0.483x the left-image distance. The real gate is therefore ~148 px of left-image disagreement = 2.9% of the left diagonal, twice what the comment at pipeline.py:49-52 advertises. On the executed path via import_dialog.py:213 and model.py:53. IMPACT, both sides measured. (1) The gate cannot do its stated job. Injecting the hallucination its own docstring names (pipeline.py:66-67, "an ankle collapsed onto the knee") into the client take: ankle-on-knee is caught in only 15/26 frames (58%), wrist-on-elbow in 3-5/26 (12-19%). Only long-range collapses — ankle-on-hip 24/26, wrist-on-shoulder 21/26 — clear 71.5 px. So the 0/388 result is the gate's width, not merely a clean take. (2) It gives false assurance. Perturbing the right camera's orientation about the tag origin — the physically admissible model, costing only 2.2-6.0 px of tag-14 reprojection at 2-5 deg, versus 65-164 px for a rotation about the camera centre, which solvePnP could never return — 5 deg passes with 0.0% rejections (epipolar median 12.3-16.0 px) and 7 deg with 0.4-1.2%, by which point the reconstruction carries 0.84-0.87% of body height of shape error. The 15% `_REJECT_NOTE_FRAC` note (pipeline.py:109) does not fire until ~12-20 deg. NARROWER THAN ORIGINALLY CLAIMED: the left-camera-only scaling changes no outcome here — with the resolutions swapped thr would be 35.8 px, still 7.3x the median and still 0/388 — so (b) is a latent defect, not a realised one; and the consequence is small in absolute terms, 0.87% of height being ~1.1 mm on a 0.128 m subject against a smoother displacement of 5.27% mean / 21.7% max. FIX, with two corrections to the original proposal: make `epipolar_threshold` data-driven, thr = max(FLOOR, k * robust_scale) — but k~5 on the median gives 24.6 px, which clips this take's own tail (max 29.38), so k must land at >=30 px (k~6-7 on the median, or a p99-based scale); and evaluate a symmetric point-to-line distance in each image against 1.4% of THAT image's own diagonal, rejecting when EITHER exceeds, not both — since d_R is systematically the smaller, an "only if both exceed" rule requires d_R > 35.8 px (measured max 33.8) and is strictly more permissive than the status quo, the opposite of the intent. Separately surface the median epipolar distance from `triangulate_project` in `main_window._refresh_calibration_status` so a 5 deg error becomes visible. tests/test_cross_view.py:51-57 (`test_the_tolerance_scales_with_the_image`) asserts on the single-arg signature and needs rewriting.
- VERDICT REASON: Every load-bearing element reproduces from my own read and my own scripts. CODE, read directly: pose3d/pipeline.py:53 `_EPI_THR_FRAC = 0.014`; pipeline.py:57-60 `epipolar_threshold` does `w, h = rig.intr[CAM_LEFT].image_size` and returns `0.014*hypot(w,h)` — LEFT only, no right-camera term; pipeline.py:79 `epi_thr = epipolar_threshold(rig)`; triangulate.py:99-103 builds the Sampson denominator as `Fxl[0]**2+Fxl[1]**2+Ftxr[0]**2+Ftxr[1]**2`, i.e. it sums squared line gradients from the RIGHT image (Fxl) and the LEFT image (Ftxr) — genuinely mixed units. ON THE EXECUTED PATH: import_dialog.py:213 `dropped = triangulate_project(project, rig)` -> pipeline.py:137 `validate_cross_view` -> :79; and model.py:53 in `recompute_all` (the post-correction / post-redetect path) hits the same. Not dead code. THE MIXED-RESOLUTION PREMISE IS REAL and is stronger than the briefing implied: I read both calibration files — left_intrinsics.json is 3072x4080 with f=4080, right_intrinsics.json is 1536x2048 with f=2048. Exactly 2:1. So thr = 71.5009 px = 1.400% of the left diagonal (5107.21) and 2.793% of the right's (2560.0), matching the finding to 3 significant figures. WHERE I TRIED HARDEST TO REFUTE AND FAILED: the perturbation evidence. My first model — rotating the right camera about its own optical centre (R'=dR@R0, t'=dR@t0) — gave a 6x stronger reaction (5 deg -> 102 px median, 67.7% dropped) and would have refuted impact-(2). But that model is physically inadmissible: I measured its tag-14 corner reprojection cost at 65.4/163.7/329.7 px for 2/5/10 deg, and solvePnP minimises exactly that quantity, so it can never be a returned calibration. The admissible models (scene rotated about the tag origin, which is what a single-tag PnP orientation error / IPPE branch confusion is) cost only 2.2-6.0 px at 2-5 deg, and they reproduce the finding's column within a few percent. So the finding's model was right and mine was wrong. I also independently confirmed impact-(1) with a test the finding only asserted: injecting the exact hallucination named in the gate's own docstring (pipeline.py:66-67, "an ankle collapsed onto the knee") escapes the gate ~40% of the time. CORRECTIONS, all sharpening or narrowing rather than overturning: (i) the unit-mixing has a definite sign the finding did not state — Sampson = (d_L^-2 + d_R^-2)^-1/2, dominated by the SMALLER residual, so it is below BOTH per-image distances and the effective gate in left-image pixels is ~148 px = 2.9% of the left diagonal, twice what the comment at pipeline.py:49-52 advertises; the mixing compounds (a) rather than being an independent defect; (ii) the mixed-resolution scaling changes NO outcome on this take — with the resolutions swapped thr would be 35.8 px, still 7.3x the observed median and still 0/388 drops — so "actively wrong the moment the left camera is the low-resolution one" is latent, not realised, and the claim should say so; (iii) the proposed fix's "reject only if BOTH exceed" variant is internally inconsistent with its own goal: since d_R is systematically the smaller (median 5.57 vs 10.21 px), requiring both to exceed their own 1.4% is strictly MORE permissive than the status quo; "either exceeds" is the tightening variant; (iv) k~5 on the median gives 24.6 px, which clips this take's own tail (measured max 29.38 px) — k must land at >=30 px, so k~6-7 on the median or a p99-based scale; (v) I could not reproduce the "bone CV 5.27 -> 5.96%" sub-number (my harness call to bone_length_stats returned NaN medians from a key-structure mismatch); it is not load-bearing and the shape-error figure it accompanies did reproduce. SEVERITY stays minor, and my numbers justify that: 7 deg of extrinsic error — the point where the gate is still silent — costs only 0.87% of body height of shape error, ~1.1 mm on a 0.128 m subject, against a baseline smoother displacement of 5.27% mean / 21.7% max. The gate's width is a real latent defect, not a driver of the client's complaint. The named test tests/test_cross_view.py:51-57 does assert on the single-arg `epipolar_threshold` signature, so the finding is right that it needs rewriting.
