FIX ARITHMETIC CHECK: a 30 px gate drops 0/388 on this take (measured max 29.38) and would drop >50% at the 10 deg model-B case (median 32.19); a 25 px gate (k=5 x median 4.91 = 24.6) clips this take's own tail. So the fix works at thr >= 30 px, i.e. k ~ 6-7 on the median, not 5-6.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: Two defects in one function. (a) _EPI_THR_FRAC = 0.014 sizes the tolerance from the IMAGE, not from the data: 71.5 px against an observed body-keypoint distribution whose median is 4.91 px. (b) epipolar_threshold (pipeline.py:57-60) reads only rig.intr[CAM_LEFT].image_size, so on this mixed-resolution rig the gate is 1.4% of the left diagonal (5107 px) but 2.79% of the right's (2560 px) — and the Sampson distance it is compared against (triangulate.epipolar_distance) mixes both cameras' pixel units in its denominator, so with a 2:1 resolution ratio the thresholded quantity is not in either image's pixels.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Shipped rig, 388 valid cross-view pairs: median 4.91, p90 12.48, p99 23.97, max 29.38 px (0.096% of the 5107 px diagonal); 0/388 over threshold, so the gate is provably inert on this take and re-running it drops 0 more. Threshold is 14.6x the median and 3.0x p99. Perturbing the right camera's rotation by theta (mean over 5 random axes) gives epipolar median / % dropped: 0.5 deg -> 5.1 px / 0%; 1 -> 5.6 / 0%; 2 -> 7.8 / 0%; 5 -> 16.1 / 0%; 10 -> 33.1 / 11.8%; 20 -> 79.0 / 46.5%; 45 -> 254 / 78.2%; 63.7 (wrong IPPE branch) -> 413 / 90.2%. So the gate first reacts at ~7-10 deg, by which point the reconstruction has 0.9-1.35% of height of shape error and bone CV has risen 5.27 -> 5.96%. Every rig built justifies the same data-driven threshold (BA p99 25.66 px, best-focal BA 22.11 px).
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Make epipolar_threshold(rig, project) data-driven: compute the epipolar distances of all valid pairs once, take a robust scale (median or 1.4826*MAD) and use thr = max(FLOOR_PX, k * scale) with k ~ 5-6 — 25-30 px here, still 0/388 drops while catching the 10 deg case; keep 1.4% of the diagonal only as an upper clamp, and compute that clamp from BOTH cameras (hypot of each, return the smaller allowance) or normalise the Sampson distance by the geometric mean of the two diagonals — better, evaluate a symmetric point-to-line distance in each image against 1.4% of that image's own diagonal and reject only if both exceed it. Separately return the median epipolar distance from triangulate_project and surface it in main_window._refresh_calibration_status (main_window.py:285-288) so a 5 deg error becomes visible. tests/test_cross_view.py::test_the_tolerance_scales_with_the_image needs rewriting against the new signature.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-extrinsics/perturb_h6.json, sweep_h4_h5_h6.json (H6); <SP>/find-intrinsics/tag_subset.py/.json

## F39 [minor] Zero distortion is nearly free for the subject but not for the tags: k1 = 0.03 moves the tags 10.9 px and the subject 0.27 px, so the missing distortion model biases the extrinsics, not the pose

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:89 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: Zero distortion is nearly free for the subject but not for the tags, and the mechanism is confirmed by direct experiment: 96% of k1's effect enters through the extrinsics solve, not through the subject's own undistortion.

`_approx_intrinsics` (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:89) sets dist = zeros((1,5)); this is the live path for any import without uploaded intrinsics (import_dialog.py:167-177 -> resolve.py:113-118 -> save_rig at import_dialog.py:215 -> reloaded at app.py:88-89), and the shipped client calibration carries all-zero dist. The subject sits at 18.6% (left) / 25.4% (right) of the half-diagonal while the ArUco tags sit at 59.7-76.4% (left) / 67.0-77.0% (right), so a residual radial term is ~41x larger at the tags than at the subject in the LEFT camera and ~22x in the RIGHT — NOT "~40x" uniformly, because the right camera is a different, lower-resolution sensor (1536x2048, f=2048, not 3072x4080).

Corrected magnitudes (cv2.undistortPoints on the actual stored keypoints; the finding's LEFT figures are ~1.4x too large, its RIGHT figures are correct to within 8%): at |k1| = 0.03 the left subject moves 0.19 px median / 2.25 px max (finding said 0.27 / 3.15) and the left tag corners 7.81 px (finding said 10.87); the right subject 0.25 px median / 2.94 max and the right tags 5.35 px (finding said 4.99). At |k1| = 0.1 the left subject moves 0.65 px and the left tags 21-45 px.

The k1 sweep reproduces exactly: with extrinsics re-solved under the same k1, median body epipolar is 6.39 / 6.03 / 4.91 / 4.28 / 4.47 / 5.07 px at k1 = -0.05 / -0.03 / 0 / +0.03 / +0.05 / +0.08 (shallow minimum at +0.03) and bone CV is flat at 5.2-5.6%. My k1=0 rig reproduces the shipped extrinsics bit-for-bit, so the sweep is anchored on the real calibration.

Direct mechanism test (new): applying k1 ONLY in the PnP solve gives 4.303 px and ONLY in the subject undistortion gives 4.871 px, against a 4.908 px baseline — 0.605 of the 0.628 px improvement (96%) is extrinsic, 0.037 px is the pose.

Impact, corrected and quantified in 3D: the best k1 buys at most 0.63 px of the 4.91 px median epipolar error; the reconstructed pose SHAPE changes by only 0.20 mm median / 0.42 mm p90 = 0.17% / 0.35% of the 118 mm body height after similarity alignment, with no bone-length CV change. The 2.4 mm of raw joint motion is a rigid+scale shift of the whole take in an arbitrary tag frame that view3d.py de-tilts anyway. One secondary effect the finding omits: absolute metric scale moves about +1.0% per +0.03 of k1 (tag distance 0.6368 -> 0.6434 m), i.e. k1 cannot account for the open ~0.7x scale question. Not a client-visible cause.

The proposed fix is aimed at the real mechanism and should stand, with one amendment: because 96% of the effect is extrinsic, validating a future distortion model by "subject-radius displacement under ~1 px" is necessary but NOT sufficient — a model that leaves the subject at 0.2 px can still shift the extrinsics enough to move the epipolar residual 0.6 px and absolute scale 1%. Validate on the extrinsic consequences (cross-validated epipolar on held-out tags, and metric-scale stability) as well as on subject displacement.

Unverified: the finding's bundle-adjustment claim that k1 runs to +0.052 (left) / +0.296 (right) for a 4.76 -> 4.74 px corner-rms gain. I did not re-run the BA. It is consistent with my sweep's shallow left minimum near +0.03 and with the right camera seeing one tag in only 11 of 26 frames.
- VERDICT REASON: The core claim survives independent re-derivation on both the code and the numbers, and I added an experiment the finding did not run that CONFIRMS its stated mechanism rather than merely being consistent with it.

CODE (read myself). pose3d/calib/resolve.py:89 is `return Intrinsics(K=K, dist=np.zeros((1, 5), dtype=float), image_size=(w, h), source="assumed")` inside `_approx_intrinsics` (resolve.py:70-91). It IS on the executed path: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:167-168 loads uploaded intrinsics only if the user picked files, :175-178 calls `resolve_calibration`, which at resolve.py:113-118 substitutes `_approx_intrinsics` per camera when either is None; the resulting rig is written to <project>/calibration at import_dialog.py:215 (`save_rig`, resolve.py:36-51) and reloaded on every subsequent open at pose3d/app.py:88-89. The zeros are consumed at pose3d/geometry/triangulate.py:19 (`cv2.undistortPoints(pts, intr.K, intr.dist)`) and :92-94 (epipolar gate). The shipped left/right *_intrinsics.json on the client take both carry dist = all zeros, rms 0.0.

GEOMETRY (my radii.py, over all 390 left / 388 right stored keypoints and all 336 left / 52 right detected tag corners). Left (3072x4080, f=4080, half-diag 2553.6): subject radius median 476.2 px = 18.6%, max 1078.9 = 42.2%; per-tag median radii 1525.4 (id13, 59.7%), 1553.4 (id14, 60.8%), 1656.2 (id17, 64.9%), 1950.8 (id15, 76.4%). Right (NOT 3072x4080 — it is 1536x2048 with f=2048, half-diag 1280): subject median 325.0 px = 25.4%, max 746.7 = 58.3%; tags 857.0 (id14, 67.0%) and 986.2 (id15, 77.0%). Every radius figure in the finding's evidence reproduces to within 2%.

SWEEP (my sweep.py, extrinsics re-solved from the cached ArUco corners under each k1, same marker-selection logic as resolve.py:134-152). k1 = -0.08/-0.05/-0.03/-0.01/0/+0.01/+0.03/+0.05/+0.08/+0.12 -> median body epipolar 7.575/6.390/6.030/5.277/4.908/4.765/4.280/4.465/5.074/6.283 px; median bone CV 5.75/5.62/5.53/5.37/5.27/5.21/5.25/5.31/5.42/5.58%. This matches the finding's quoted 6.39/6.03/4.91/4.28/4.47/5.07 to three digits. Anchoring check: my k1=0 rig reproduces the SHIPPED extrinsics bit-for-bit (R and t maxdiff exactly 0.0, both cameras; marker 14, frame 0001), so the sweep is on the real calibration.

MECHANISM (my mech.py — the experiment the finding did not run). I split k1 between the PnP solve and the subject undistortion. At k1=+0.03: both = 4.280 px, extrinsics-only = 4.303 px, subject-only = 4.871 px, against a 4.908 px baseline. So 0.605 of the 0.628 px change (96%) enters through the extrinsics and 0.037 px (6%) through the subject's own undistortion. At k1=-0.03: both 6.030, extr-only 5.915, pose-only 4.931 — same asymmetry. The finding's headline ("biases the extrinsics, not the pose") is therefore directly demonstrated, not inferred.

IMPACT (my delta.py). At |k1| = 0.03 the triangulated joints move 2.37-2.48 mm median in the tag world frame, but after per-frame similarity (Procrustes) alignment the SHAPE change is only 0.197 mm median / 0.415 mm p90 = 0.17% / 0.35% of the 118 mm body height. Bone CV is unchanged (5.27% -> 5.25%). So the residual is a near-pure rigid+scale shift of the whole reconstruction relative to an arbitrary tag frame that pose3d/ui/view3d.py de-tilts anyway. Not client-visible. Severity minor is correct.

The proposed fix targets the right mechanism: keep dist = 0 for the guessed model, forbid the tag bundle from fitting k1, source distortion only from the checkerboard path. I would add one amendment from my split experiment (see corrected_claim).

WHAT I COULD NOT VERIFY: the finding's BA result (k1 running to +0.052 left / +0.296 right with corner rms 4.76 -> 4.74 px) — I did not re-run the bundle. It is consistent with my sweep's shallow left minimum near +0.03 and with the right camera seeing one tag in only 11 of 26 frames, so I have no reason to doubt it, but it is unreproduced by me.

MECHANISM SPLIT (k1 in the PnP solve vs in the subject undistortion), baseline k1=0 epipolar 4.908 px
 k1=+0.03  both 4.280 | extrinsics-only 4.303 | subject-only 4.871
 k1=+0.05  both 4.465 | extrinsics-only 4.499 | subject-only 4.753
 k1=-0.03  both 6.030 | extrinsics-only 5.915 | subject-only 4.931
=> 96% of the epipolar response at +0.03 comes through the extrinsics; the subject's own undistortion accounts for 0.037 of 0.628 px.

3D IMPACT (triangulation vs the k1=0 reconstruction)
 |k1|=0.03: absolute joint motion median 2.37-2.48 mm, p90 3.47-3.68, max 4.04-4.33 mm
 |k1|=0.03: SHAPE-only after per-frame similarity alignment: median 0.197 mm, p90 0.415 mm = 0.17% / 0.35% of the 118 mm body height
 k1=+0.10: absolute 7.72 mm median; shape-only 0.635 mm median / 1.332 mm p90 (1.13% of height)
METRIC SCALE (secondary effect the finding does not mention)
 tag->camera distance (left/right) and median per-frame max joint span:
  k1=-0.03: 0.6300 / 0.6262 m, span 0.1264 m
  k1= 0.00: 0.6368 / 0.6317 m, span 0.1277 m
  k1=+0.03: 0.6434 / 0.6369 m, span 0.1285 m
 => about +1.0% of absolute scale per +0.03 of k1; ~10% across the full -0.08..+0.12 sweep. Far too small to explain the open ~0.7x scale question.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: _approx_intrinsics sets dist = zeros((1,5)). On this geometry the subject sits at 19% (left) / 25% (right) of the half-diagonal while the ArUco tags sit at 59-77%, so any residual radial term is ~40x larger where the calibration is solved than where the subject is.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Image radii: left subject median 476 px (19% of half-diagonal), max 1079; left tags 1519-1943 px (59-76%); right subject median 325 px (25%), tags 853-983 px (67-77%). Displacement at |k1| = 0.03: subject median 0.27 px / max 3.15 px, tags median 10.87 px (left); right subject 0.23 px / max 2.78, tags 4.99 px. At |k1| = 0.1: subject 0.90 px, tags 36.2 px. k1 sweep with single-tag extrinsics re-solved under the same k1: epipolar 6.39 / 6.03 / 4.91 / 4.28 / 4.47 / 5.07 px at k1 = -0.05 / -0.03 / 0 / +0.03 / +0.05 / +0.08 (shallow minimum at +0.03), bone CV flat at 5.2-5.4%. Freeing k1 inside the tag bundle is not identifiable — it runs to +0.052 (left) and +0.296 (right) while corner rms improves only 4.76 -> 4.74 px, because the tags occupy a narrow band at the top of the frame.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Keep dist = 0 for the guessed model but stop treating it as exact: the checkerboard path (calibrate_checkerboard, F28) is the only legitimate source of a distortion model, and the tag bundle must NOT be allowed to fit k1 from tags confined to one image band. If a distortion model is ever added, validate it by the subject-radius displacement (must stay under ~1 px) rather than by tag-corner rms.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-intrinsics/ba_profile.py/.json, arbiter.py/.json

## F40 [minor] The principal point is the one intrinsic the data cannot recover and the one the geometry is most sensitive to: +/-50 px costs 1.4 px of epipolar error, +/-200 px costs 19 px

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:88 | category: calibration-intrinsics | sources: intrinsics | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: `_approx_intrinsics` (pose3d/calib/resolve.py:88) fixes the principal point at (w/2, h/2) for both cameras, and that assumption reaches the shipped client calibration unmodified (left pp (1536,2040) on 3072x4080; right pp (768,1024) on 1536x2048). Single-tag IPPE_SQUARE PnP (extrinsics.py:71-92) cannot constrain pp, so the TAG geometry cannot verify it — a full bundle that frees pp wanders to absurd values (right pp offset -915 px = 60 % of that image's width) at unchanged corner rms.

But the assumption is both harmless and, contrary to the finding, in-session checkable:
- Cost of a realistic offset: at ~1 % of image width (30 px left / 15 px right, applied to both cameras and the extrinsics re-solved) the reconstruction changes by 0.128 mm = 0.111 % of the 114.8 mm body height, bone-length CV is unchanged (5.155 % vs 5.272 %), and body epipolar error IMPROVES to 4.471 px from 4.908. Even a pessimistic 100/50 px offset costs 0.43 mm (0.377 %H) and still improves epipolar.
- The dramatic epipolar numbers (23.4 px at a ±200 px diagonal shift) are an artefact of the measurement currency, not of reconstruction damage: perturbing K and re-solving the extrinsics makes epipolar error report how badly the perturbation resists being absorbed into the camera pose. A focal error is absorbed almost perfectly (it is nearly a pure baseline rescale, 0.516 -> 0.603 m over ±200 px), a pp error is not. Measured on the reconstruction instead (Umeyama similarity residual vs the unperturbed triangulation), the same ±200 px diagonal pp shift costs only 0.705-0.788 %H (0.81-0.90 mm) against 0.150 %H for the same-magnitude focal shift.
- Therefore the sensitivity ratio is ~3.4x on reconstruction shape (2.4x per px of displacement) and 25-50x on epipolar px — the finding's "roughly 4x" is not the ratio its own quoted evidence supports; it is recoverable only under an unstated per-1 %-of-parameter-scale normalisation with an x-only shift. (The quoted "±200 px" sweep is also a diagonal, i.e. 283 px of pp displacement, and "±50 px costs 1.4 px" is the worse of the two signs; +50 costs 0.92 px.)
- "No in-session estimator can check the assumption" is false as stated. The body keypoints are non-planar, seen in two views, and unused by the calibration, and the body-epipolar objective is NOT flat in pp: it has a clean minimum at dcy = 0 exactly (4.908 px, rising to 5.23/5.45 at ∓50 and 15.58 at -200) and at dcx = +75 (4.817, only 0.09 px below centre). Its per-px curvature is ~0.009 px/px in cy against ~0.0013 px/px in focal — pp is the intrinsic body epipolar checks ~7x BETTER, and the check confirms the centred assumption rather than being unable to test it.

Fix: drop the "never let the in-session bundle free pp" clause — grep shows no bundle adjustment exists anywhere in pose3d/, so it constrains code that was never written. The check_rig half is already shipped: quality.py:39-49 already tests that pp sits at the exact centre and quality.py:71-76 already tells the user the intrinsics were assumed from the image size and to shoot a checkerboard (surfaced at main_window.py:286-288). What is genuinely actionable is one line of wording, and one line of documentation in resolve.py's docstring recording the measured price (~0.13 mm at a realistic offset, ~0.4 mm at a pessimistic one), so nobody spends effort on a pp estimator. This is an informational note about a correct design decision, not a defect: at ~0.1 % of body height it is 1-2 orders below the shipped pipeline's dominant errors (EMA smoother 5.27 %H mean / 21.73 % max, retarget 1.5 %H, raw bone CV 5.3 %) and cannot contribute to the client's "character does not follow the keypoints" complaint.
- VERDICT REASON: CODE CLAIM: verified exactly, read myself. `pose3d/calib/resolve.py:86-90` — `h, w = image.shape[:2]; f = float(max(w, h)); K = np.array([[f,0,w/2.0],[0,f,h/2.0],[0,0,1.0]])`, pp hard-fixed at the exact image centre, dist all-zero, source="assumed". ON THE EXECUTED PATH: `pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:161-162` passes `il/ir = None` when no intrinsics file is picked; `resolve.py:109-118` then calls `_approx_intrinsics` for both cameras and sets `approximate=True`; `resolve.py:144-145` feeds that K straight into `estimate_extrinsics_for_marker` (`pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:71-92`, single tag, SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE). The client take's shipped calibration is bit-exactly that output: left K f=4080 pp=(1536,2040) for 3072x4080, right K f=2048 pp=(768,1024) for 1536x2048 (NOTE: the shared context is wrong about the right camera — it is a 1536x2048 image with f=2048, not 4080).

MEASUREMENT: reproduced independently (own script, own ArUco detection, own PnP). My re-solve of the extrinsics from frame 0001 / tag 14 with the unperturbed K reproduces the shipped extrinsics.json to max|dR| = 0.0, max|dt| = 0.0 — so my perturbation harness is the app's own path. The diagonal left-pp sweep gives body-epipolar 23.373 / 11.767 / 6.306 / 4.908 / 5.824 / 9.887 / 19.838 px at -200/-100/-50/0/+50/+100/+200, matching the finding's 23.37 / 11.77 / 6.31 / 4.91 / 5.82 / 9.89 / 19.84 to 3 decimals. Bone CV 5.158-5.613 % vs its 5.16-5.61 %. The evidence is real and exactly repeatable.

WHAT IS WRONG IN DETAIL — three things:

(1) The "4x more sensitive than the same-magnitude focal error" ratio is not what the cited evidence shows. Running the SAME magnitudes on the left focal with the extrinsics re-solved: epipolar 5.215 / 4.936 / 5.024 / 4.908 / 5.023 / 5.034 / 5.515 px at -200..+200 — a swing of 0.6 px against pp's 18.5 px. On the finding's own currency the ratio is 25-50x, not 4x. (4x is recoverable only under an unstated normalisation — per 1 % of each parameter's own scale, using an x-only pp shift — which is not the sweep the finding quotes. The diagonal sweep it does quote is also ±283 px of displacement, not ±200.)

(2) Epipolar px is the wrong currency for "the geometry is most sensitive to". Perturbing K and re-solving the extrinsics from the tag makes epipolar error measure how badly the perturbation FAILS to be absorbed into the camera pose — a focal error is absorbed almost perfectly (it is nearly a pure baseline rescale: 0.516 → 0.603 m over ±200 px) while a pp error is not. On the reconstruction itself the picture is far tamer. Umeyama (scale+R+t) residual of the perturbed triangulation against the baseline triangulation, as % of the 114.8 mm nose-to-ankle height: pp diagonal ±200 px → 0.705 / 0.788 %H (0.81 / 0.90 mm); left focal ±200 px → 0.150 / 0.139 %H; both-camera focal ±200 px → 0.233 / 0.210 %H. So the reconstruction-shape ratio is ~3.4x for the diagonal, ~2.4x per px — the "4x" number is coincidentally near the shape-error ratio but the finding never measures shape error. Bone-length CV is essentially inert across the whole pp sweep (5.13-5.61 % vs 5.27 % baseline), i.e. the perturbation is nearly a similarity transform.

(3) "No in-session estimator can check the assumption" is over-broad and, as stated, false. It is true of the TAG bundle (single-tag IPPE_SQUARE fits 4 coplanar points with 6 pose DOF; nothing constrains pp), and the finding's BA result — right pp offset (-914.9, 517.0) px, i.e. 60 % of the right image width, at equal corner rms — is credible in direction, though I did not reproduce the BA. But the BODY keypoints are a non-planar structure seen in two views and they DO constrain pp: my fine scan of the body-epipolar objective (25 px steps) is not flat — cy has a minimum at exactly 0 (4.908 px), rising to 5.23 at -50 and 5.45 at +50 and 15.58 at -200; cx has a shallow minimum at +75 (4.817 vs 4.908 at 0). Per-px curvature near the optimum is ~0.009 px/px in cy vs ~0.0013 px/px in focal — pp is ~7x sharper, i.e. the one intrinsic body epipolar is BEST at checking, not worst. The scan vindicates the centred assumption rather than being unable to test it.

MECHANISM / ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATION: none needed — the effect is exactly what a pp shift does (it rotates the single-tag PnP solution, which is not a similarity of the stereo pair). But it is nowhere near the client complaint: at the realistic phone offset the finding itself names (~1 % of image width, ~30 px), I measure shape error 0.111-0.113 %H = 0.13 mm, bone CV 5.16-5.40 %, and epipolar 4.47-5.23 px — applying it to BOTH cameras actually IMPROVES epipolar (4.471 vs 4.908). Even a wildly pessimistic 100 px (left) / 50 px (right) diagonal offset costs 0.377 %H = 0.43 mm and still improves epipolar (4.334 px). Against the baseline's dominant errors — EMA smoother displacement 5.27 %H mean / 21.7 % max, retarget 1.5 %H, raw bone CV 5.3 % — the pp assumption is 1-2 orders of magnitude down.

PROPOSED FIX: half of it targets code that does not exist. `grep -rn "bundle" --include=*.py pose3d/` finds only PyInstaller/asset uses; there is no bundle adjustment anywhere in the shipped package, so "never let the in-session bundle free the principal point" is advice for a hypothetical future estimator, not a change. The other half is already shipped: `pose3d/calib/quality.py:39-49` `looks_assumed` explicitly tests `abs(K[0,2] - w/2) < 1 and abs(K[1,2] - h/2) < 1`, and `check_rig` (`quality.py:52`, called from `pose3d/ui/main_window.py:286-288`) already emits "Camera intrinsics for {who} were assumed from the image size, not measured. Depth and limb angles will be skewed — shoot a checkerboard with each camera to calibrate them." The fix reduces to a wording refinement of an existing warning.

VERDICT: not refuted — the code claim is exact, the measurement is bit-reproducible, and severity "minor" is correct and if anything generous. But the headline is wrong in currency ("the geometry is most sensitive to pp" is an artefact of measuring in epipolar px rather than reconstruction error), wrong in the 4x ratio as evidenced, and wrong in asserting unverifiability in-session.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Reproduction of the app's calibration (own script, /tmp/.../verify-F40/sweep.py): frame 0001 is the first with a tag common to both views (left ids [13,14,17], right ids [14]) -> tag 14; re-solved extrinsics match shipped extrinsics.json with max|dR| = 0.000e+00, max|dt| = 0.000e+00 m for both cameras.

## F41 [minor] The multi-tag extrinsics path assumes every tag shares one rotation; on this curved backdrop that is wrong by up to 179.4 deg, and the path has zero callers

- file: pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:132 | category: calibration-extrinsics | sources: extrinsics | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: CONFIRMED with two detail fixes and one addition.

`pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:132` builds each tag's world corners as `obj_all.append(half + center)` — a pure translation of the canonical corner set — so the `tag_world_positions: dict[int, np.ndarray]` API (extrinsics.py:99) can only express a layout in which all tags share the world frame's identity orientation. On the client's curved backdrop that is wrong: measured in the left camera at left_0005.jpg, tag 15 is rotated 90.8 deg, tag 13 ~98 deg (median over 26 frames; the 114.7 deg at frame 0005 is an ambiguous solve, 2nd IPPE solution within 1.67x) and tag 17 a stable 179.4 deg (179.0-179.7 over all 26 frames) relative to tag 14, with the four tag normals spread up to 52.2 deg about their mean. The docstring at extrinsics.py:105-107 nevertheless calls the branch "robust, recommended for the 4-tag backdrop".

The branch is dead: a repo-wide grep finds `tag_world_positions` only inside extrinsics.py, and `estimate_extrinsics` is called only at tests/test_extrinsics.py:59 in single-tag mode; the app path (pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:175 -> pose3d/calib/resolve.py:144-145) uses `estimate_extrinsics_for_marker`. No runtime impact today.

Quantified consequence if it were reached (my simulation, projecting the true 6-DoF layout through the real left-camera pose): the branch returns ok=True with a 676 mm camera-centre error against a true 0.64 m camera distance (106%), 98.6 deg rotation error and a 1132 px mean reprojection residual that the code never inspects. Two-tag subsets are equally bad (641-1082 mm).

ADDITION the finding misses: the same branch is broken independently of the rotation assumption. When exactly one known tag is visible it uses `cv2.SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE` (extrinsics.py:137-138) on corners already translated off the object-frame origin by `center`, violating IPPE_SQUARE's centred-square precondition — measured camera-centre errors of 1019.6 mm (tag 17's offset) and 527.3 mm (tag 13's). The proposed fix as written (build `(R_tag @ local).T + t_tag`) fixes the rotation assumption but leaves this flag bug intact; the one-tag case must fall back to a tag-local IPPE_SQUARE solve composed with the tag's world pose, or use a non-IPPE flag.

Preferred remedy is unchanged and stronger for it: delete the multi-tag branch and let a bundle adjustment estimate the layout rather than requiring it as input, keeping `estimate_extrinsics_for_marker` as the single-tag path. tests/test_extrinsics.py exercises only the single-tag branch, so nothing breaks.

Severity: minor — zero callers, no effect on the client take or any shipped output; the cost is a mislabelled trap in the API surface.
- VERDICT REASON: All three legs of F41 reproduce independently.

(1) CODE, read myself. pose3d/calib/extrinsics.py:126-132: `half = marker_object_points(marker_length)` then per tag `center = np.asarray(tag_world_positions[mid], ...)`; `obj_all.append(half + center)`. That is a pure translation of the canonical corner set, so the parameter (typed `dict[int, np.ndarray]` at extrinsics.py:99) can only encode a layout in which every tag has the SAME orientation, and that orientation is the world frame's identity. The docstring at extrinsics.py:105-107 does say "assemble object points for all seen tags in the shared world frame and solvePnP once (robust, recommended for the 4-tag backdrop)" — it recommends the branch for precisely the layout it cannot represent.

(2) ZERO CALLERS, re-derived. A repo-wide grep (all file types, excluding .venv/.git) for `estimate_extrinsics` minus `_for_marker` returns only its own definition (extrinsics.py:95) and tests/test_extrinsics.py:4,10,59; line 59 is `estimate_extrinsics(img, intr, marker_length=marker_len)` — no `tag_world_positions`, i.e. the SINGLE-tag branch (extrinsics.py:115). `tag_world_positions` appears in no file but extrinsics.py itself. App path traced: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:175 calls `resolve_calibration`; pose3d/calib/resolve.py:144-145 calls `estimate_extrinsics_for_marker` per camera on a common tag id. The multi-tag branch is unreachable from the GUI and from tests.

(3) MAGNITUDE, remeasured from the images. My own PnP (cv2.solvePnPGeneric, IPPE_SQUARE, K=[[4080,0,1536],[0,4080,2040],[0,0,1]], L=0.05) on left_0005.jpg — which I independently confirmed is the first of 26 left frames showing all four tags — reproduces the finding's numbers essentially exactly. Tag 17 sits at a 179.4 deg full rotation relative to tag 14 and that figure is stable across all 26 frames (179.0-179.7), so it is not an artefact of the planar-pose ambiguity.

I also went further than the finding and simulated the branch (projecting the TRUE 6-DoF layout with the real left-camera pose, then feeding the code's translation-only object points to the same solvePnP+RefineLM sequence): it returns ok=True with a 676 mm camera-centre error on a 0.64 m camera distance and 98.6 deg rotation error. "Silently produce garbage" is literal — the branch never checks the residual it would need to notice.

Two corrections to detail, neither undermining the finding: (a) the finding's 114.7 deg for tag 13 comes from an AMBIGUOUS solve — I measure the 2nd IPPE solution within 1.67x of the best for tag 13 in that frame, and tag 13's relative rotation swings 97.9-115.9 deg across frames with median 98.3; the trustworthy headline is tag 17's stable 179.4 deg. (b) I find the max normal spread about the mean to be 52.2 deg, not 53.4 (immaterial; likely a different ambiguity resolution for tag 13).

One thing the finding MISSES: the same branch has a second, independent defect. When exactly one known tag is visible it takes `flags = cv2.SOLVEPNP_IPPE_SQUARE` (extrinsics.py:137-138) but passes corners already TRANSLATED off the origin by `center`. IPPE_SQUARE assumes a square centred on the object-frame origin, so this is wrong even when the same-rotation assumption happens to hold. Measured: 1019.6 mm centre error for tag 17's offset, 527.3 mm for tag 13's. The proposed fix (build corners as R_tag @ local + t_tag) does not by itself repair this — the single-tag flag path must also change.

Severity stays minor: zero runtime impact on the app or on the client take, since resolve.py never reaches this code.

## F45 [minor] No test anywhere measures 2D keypoint ACCURACY — the detector suite only checks shapes and file plumbing

- file: tests/test_detect.py:43 | category: testing | sources: detection | auditor severity: minor
- CORRECTED CLAIM: No collected test measures 2D keypoint ACCURACY. tests/test_detect.py:41-49 asserts only output SHAPES on np.zeros — it passes even when the detector returns the all-NaN `_empty_detection()` (pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:78-83). tests/test_detect_models.py:96-121 asserts our two-stage path equals rtmlib's on rng noise: an equivalence guard, not accuracy; its other 8 tests are checkpoint naming/search-order plumbing. Across the 189 collected tests, no test runs a detector on a real subject. Stronger than the finding states: (i) the repo DID write a real-image accuracy measurement, tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py, but it contains no assert, is not collected (pyproject sets no `python_files`), and is explicitly `--ignore`d in .github/workflows/windows-test.yml; (ii) `map_halpe26` (pose3d/core/skeleton.py:202), the mapping selected by `feet=True` at pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:30, has no test at all — tests/test_skeleton.py covers only the COCO `derive_joints`; (iii) pose3d/selftest.py:71 checks weight-file presence only. The untested configuration is the shipped one: import_dialog.py:233-234 and main_window.py:257-260 both build RTMPoseDetector(mode="balanced") with feet=False.

CORRECTIONS TO THE FINDING'S EVIDENCE AND FIX: (a) the client take is NOT "in the repo" — `.gitignore` line `workspace/`, `git ls-files workspace` = 0 files, and the repo tracks zero JPEGs; the 64 MB of images are local-only, so the proposed fixture must ship data (or a fetch step) plus a `needs_*` gate like tests/gates.py:64-68, or it will silently skip on any fresh checkout. (b) The nearest-red-dot oracle is usable only on the LEFT view: I measure a stable 2.8-5.0 % median joint-to-marker distance per frame there, but the right view yields only 5-11 blobs for 15 joints and degenerates to 17-72 % on frame 0005. (c) The metric is threshold-sensitive (all-joint p90 moves 28.6 % -> 17.8 % on an HSV/area retune), so a CI gate needs marker-to-joint ASSOCIATION, not nearest-neighbour, and generous bounds. (d) Proposed assertion (b) about HEAD/feet=True is not testable with a nearest-dot oracle at all (nose and head-centre share the same nearest marker); my HEAD median is 6.6-7.2 % of figure height, not the quoted 10-16 %, which is a 3D retarget quantity. A defensible first gate would be: left view only, 3 frames, per-joint distance to the ASSOCIATED marker, with knees (11-14 %, the reproducible worst joints) recorded as the loosest bound.
- VERDICT REASON: CORE CLAIM REPRODUCES EXACTLY, and is if anything understated; two supporting details are wrong.

VERIFIED VERBATIM (files read in full by me):
- tests/test_detect.py:41-49 — `test_rtmpose_shapes` builds RTMPoseDetector(mode="lightweight") and calls `det.detect(np.zeros((256,192,3), np.uint8))`, asserting only `out.xy.shape == (NUM_JOINTS,2)` and `out.scores.shape == (NUM_JOINTS,)`. On a blank image `_empty_detection()` (pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:78-83) returns all-NaN and the test still passes, so it is vacuous w.r.t. keypoint values.
- tests/test_detect_models.py:96-121 — `test_bundled_path_gives_the_same_numbers_as_rtmlib` runs on `rng.integers(0,255,(480,640,3))` noise with a hardcoded bbox and asserts only `np.allclose(ours, rtmlib)`. That is an equivalence/refactor guard, not accuracy. The other 8 tests in the file are checkpoint naming, search order and fallback (lines 23-93). Both cited line ranges are correct.
- No other collected test invokes a detector on a real image: `grep -rn "RTMPose|detect(" tests/*.py` hits only test_detect.py (blank/10x10 arrays), test_detect_models.py (noise + a FakeTwoStage monkeypatch, line 81-92) and tests/build_demo_project.py (a data-generator script, not collected).
- `pose3d/selftest.py:71 check_pose_weights_offline` only checks that weight FILES exist; no numeric check.
- `.venv/bin/python -m pytest --collect-only -q` -> 189 tests from 28 test_*.py files; zero assert a keypoint position on a real subject.

THE FINDING MISSES THREE FACTS THAT STRENGTHEN IT:
1. An accuracy oracle already exists but is doubly disabled: `tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py` runs the real detector on real images and computes `sync_2d_px` vs projected GT plus 3D error — but it has no `assert`, it is a `main()` printer, pyproject.toml `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` sets only `pythonpath`/`testpaths` (no `python_files` override) so `validate_*.py` is never collected, AND `.github/workflows/windows-test.yml` explicitly passes `--ignore=tests/validate_panoptic_rtmpose.py`. The repo built the measurement and then excluded it from CI.
2. `map_halpe26` (pose3d/core/skeleton.py:202), the mapping used whenever `feet=True` (pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:30), has NO test at all — not even plumbing. tests/test_skeleton.py:16-51 covers only `derive_joints` (COCO-17). So the feet path is untested at every level, not just accuracy.
3. The gate mechanism the fix wants already works: `tests/gates.py:64-68 needs_weights` + `tests/conftest.py:11-16` turn a skip into a hard failure when `POSE3D_REQUIRE_WEIGHTS=1`, which windows-test.yml sets. A needs_weights-gated accuracy test would genuinely run in CI.

TWO DETAILS IN THE FINDING ARE WRONG:
A. "52 images already in the repo workspace" — false as a CI premise. `.gitignore` contains `workspace/` (and `data/demo_project/images/`); `git ls-files workspace` returns 0 files; `git ls-files | grep -Ei '\.(jpg|jpeg)$'` returns 0 — the repository tracks no JPEG at all. The 52 images (64 MB) exist only on this machine. The proposed fixture therefore needs new committed/downloaded data plus its own gate, or it silently skips on a fresh checkout — exactly the failure mode gates.py was written to prevent.
B. Proposed assertion (b) "HEAD lands within X% ... fails today with feet=False, passes with feet=True" is not supported by a nearest-red-dot oracle: nearest-dot cannot distinguish "nose" from "head-centre" because the nearest marker to either is the same head/neck dot. My measured HEAD→nearest-dot median is 6.6-7.2% of 2D figure height, not the 10-16% the evidence cites (that figure is a 3D retarget offset, a different quantity). Assertion (b) needs an explicitly labelled head marker, not nearest-neighbour.

MECHANISM CHECK: the untested code IS on the app path — `pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:233-234` and `pose3d/ui/main_window.py:257-260` both construct `RTMPoseDetector(mode="balanced", device="cpu")`, i.e. feet=False / `derive_joints`, exactly the configuration whose output values nothing asserts.

ALTERNATIVE EXPLANATION CONSIDERED AND REJECTED: geometry accuracy IS numerically tested (tests/synth.py projects known 3D->2D; test_triangulate.py, test_bonefit.py, test_retarget.py assert numbers), so this is not a repo-wide "no numeric tests" situation — the gap is specific to the detector, as the finding says.
- VERDICT MEASUREMENTS: Scripts I wrote and ran (read-only, outputs under my folder): /tmp/claude-1000/-media-athena-hd3-Projects-pose3d-tool/0fcb8c9e-52c1-483d-9e0a-80896b078fa5/scratchpad/verify-F45/reddot.py, reddot2.py, reddot.json, reddot2.json.

FEASIBILITY TEST OF THE PROPOSED ORACLE (HSV red mask -> connected components -> centroid; distance from each stored kp2d to the NEAREST red blob, normalised by 2D figure height = ymax-ymin of the keypoints; 6 frames x 2 cams = 12 images of workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session):

LEFT view (usable): blobs found 25-37 per frame (15 joints => full marker coverage). Median joint->nearest-dot distance by frame 0001-0006: 3.2, 2.8, 3.7, 5.0, 4.1, 4.6 % of figure height (16-39 px at figH 665-787 px). Max per frame 7.1-13.7 %.

RIGHT view (NOT usable as-is): only 5-11 blobs found for 15 joints (figure is smaller, figH 354-463 px). Medians 4.4, 6.6, 8.0, 9.2, 17.0, 4.9 %; frame 0005 right degenerates (2-5 blobs, median 60-254 px = 17-72 %). The metric there is aliasing onto wrong dots, not measuring detector error.

THRESHOLD SENSITIVITY (why a naive gate would be flaky): changing only the HSV bounds (S>=90,V>=60 -> S>=70,V>=45) and scaling the min-area with figure size moved the all-joint p90 from 28.62 % to 17.80 % and the median from 4.39 % to 4.78 %. A CI threshold on this oracle would be measuring the oracle's tuning as much as the detector.

PER-JOINT medians (% of 2D figure height, n=12 images, second/looser thresholding): HEAD 7.22, NECK 4.49, L_SHOULDER 3.55, R_SHOULDER 5.20, L_ELBOW 3.75, R_ELBOW 6.88, L_WRIST 4.41, R_WRIST 2.95, PELVIS 8.01, L_HIP 3.88, R_HIP 5.12, L_KNEE 14.36, R_KNEE 11.21, L_ANKLE 5.42, R_ANKLE 10.77. All-joint median 4.78 %, p90 17.80 %. First thresholding: HEAD 6.60, L_KNEE 14.38, R_KNEE 11.64, all-median 4.39 %, p90 28.62 %. KNEES are the consistently worst joint under both settings (11-14 %) — a real, reproducible signal an accuracy test could gate on.

REPO/CI FACTS I RAN: `pytest --collect-only -q` = 189 tests, 28 files, validate_panoptic_*.py and build_demo_project.py not collected. `git ls-files workspace | wc -l` = 0. `git ls-files | grep -Ei '\.(jpg|jpeg)$' | wc -l` = 0 (only 2 tracked PNGs, both screenshots). `models.resolve()` returns non-None for all four (lightweight/balanced x body/feet) on this machine, so a weights-gated test is runnable locally; CI stages them via tools/fetch_weights.py and sets POSE3D_REQUIRE_WEIGHTS=1.
- ORIGINAL CLAIM: test_rtmpose_shapes runs the detector on np.zeros((256,192,3)) and asserts only out.xy.shape == (NUM_JOINTS,2) (test_detect.py:43-49). test_detect_models.py tests checkpoint naming, search order and that the bundled two-stage path equals rtmlib's — all plumbing, on rng.integers noise (test_detect_models.py:107-121). Nothing asserts that a keypoint lands anywhere in particular on a real subject, so every bias in F05, F04 and F26 is invisible to CI.
- ORIGINAL EVIDENCE: Both files read in full. grep -rn 'feet=True' over the repo returns only tools/fetch_weights.py:70 and tests/test_detect_models.py:43 — the accuracy consequence of the COCO/Halpe choice (10-16% of body height at the head) has no test. The client take is a ready-made fixture with a ground-truth-free oracle: 15 red dots per frame, a rigid mannequin, 52 images already in the repo workspace.
- ORIGINAL IMPACT: None
- PROPOSED FIX (auditor): Add tests/test_detect_accuracy.py, gated like needs_weights, running the detector on 2-3 frames of workspace/pose3d_projects/Imported_Session and asserting: (a) median RTMPose-to-nearest-red-dot distance <= a recorded threshold as % of figure height; (b) HEAD lands within X% of figure height of the head marker (fails today with feet=False, passes with feet=True); (c) L/R limb-length asymmetry of the raw triangulation stays under the recorded value, since the mannequin is symmetric. Reuse the blob detector from F05's pose3d/detect/reddot.py so test and product share one implementation. Complements F32's geometric regression fixture.
- artifacts: <SP>/find-detection/red_dots.py, offsets.md, halpe_vs_coco.json


# UNVERIFIED MINOR FINDINGS (5) — the refuter never ran; treat as auditor-measured only


## F37 [minor, unverified] The right camera contributes half the angular resolution and less than half the marker evidence, and the unweighted DLT does not know it

- file: pose3d/geometry/triangulate.py:44 | category: detection
- CLAIM: triangulate_points calls cv2.triangulatePoints(P1, P2, nl.T, nr.T) on NORMALIZED coordinates (triangulate.py:41-47) — correct in that it weights both views equally in angular units — but the two views are not equally good and no stage accounts for it. The right images are 1536x2048 against the left's 3072x4080 at the same field of view, and the subject is smaller in them.
- EVIDENCE: Sizes over all 52 files: left 3072x4080, right 1536x2048; f/W identical at 1.33. Depth error per pixel from the rig geometry: left 0.556/4080 = 0.136 mm/px, right 0.510/2048 = 0.249 mm/px — 1.8x. Stored figure height: left median 775.6 px vs right 406.7 px. Red-dot yield: median 16 blobs per left frame vs 7 per right (n=26 each), and only 153 of a possible 364 joint associations in the right view vs 285 in the left. The effective pose-model resolution is nevertheless identical in both (119-159 of 256 model rows), so this is purely an optics/sensor asymmetry, not a preprocessing one.
- IMPACT: About half the triangulation evidence is 1.8x noisier per pixel than an operator would assume from 'both cameras are 4K phones', and the app never says so. It also means any accuracy number quoted in raw pixels (F24, F25) is not comparable between the two panels.
- PROPOSED FIX: (a) In the import path, warn when the two cameras' image_size differ by more than ~20% or their f/W differ, and record the ratio in the project so the UI can normalise. (b) When a per-joint 2D uncertainty exists (F26's residual, or a marker blob's centroid covariance), replace the unweighted DLT with an inverse-variance-weighted solve in triangulate_points; epipolar_threshold should likewise be per-view (F36).

## F38 [minor, unverified] The rig is solved from 1 of 97 available tag observations; pooling them all is cheap and well-conditioned but changes the reconstruction by only 0.158 mm of shape

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:134 | category: calibration-extrinsics
- CLAIM: The `for frame in project.frames:` loop returns on the first success, discarding 96 of the 97 tag solves the take contains (left sees 13/14/17 in all 26 frames plus 15 in 6; right sees 14 in 11 frames and 15 in 2). The pooled alternative (calib_ba.py: 30 unknowns = 2 camera poses + 3 tag poses relative to tag 14, residual = every detected tag corner, Huber f_scale=2, scipy least_squares) buys almost nothing, because the shipped single-frame rig is already at the noise floor.
- EVIDENCE: BA at the shipped intrinsics: 776 residuals, rms 6.81 -> 3.16 px, 21 evaluations (<2 s). vs shipped: relative rotation 0.696 deg, baseline 0.5588 vs 0.5590 m, camera centres move 5.9 / 12.6 mm. Re-triangulating the stored 2D: median 3D displacement 2.38 mm (max 4.40) but only 0.158 mm of SHAPE change after per-frame similarity Procrustes (max 0.51 mm) = 0.13% of the 118 mm height. Metrics barely move: raw bone CV median 5.27 -> 5.36%, L/R asymmetry mean 3.13 -> 2.90%, retarget 1.78 -> 1.76% of height, bend plane 3.15 -> 3.18 deg; body epipolar gets slightly WORSE 4.91 -> 5.85 px. Independent holdout (BA never seeing the right camera's tag-15 observations, then triangulating tag 15's corners cross-view in frame 0026): 50.49 mm vs the true 50.00; the SHIPPED rig scores 50.29 mm. Sensitivity: 0.13% of body height of shape error per degree of relative-rotation error (0.06/0.13/0.26/0.65/1.35% at 0.5/1/2/5/10 deg). Cross-frame spread of the per-frame single-tag relative pose over the 11 both-see-tag-14 frames: 1.13 deg rotation, baseline 0.5496-0.5590 m (9.5 mm, 1.7%).
- IMPACT: Bounds the whole extrinsics dimension: the rig is uncertain at <=1.1 deg, costing <=0.15% of body height in reconstructed shape — against 5.3% of height (mean) / 21.7% (max) attributed to the EMA smoother alone. Extrinsic calibration cannot be the cause of the client's complaint on this take; the value of pooling is robustness (F23) and provenance (F30), not accuracy.
- PROPOSED FIX: Keep the single-tag path as the initialiser and add pose3d/calib/bundle.py::bundle_adjust_rig(observations, intrinsics, marker_length) -> (Extrinsics, Extrinsics, tag_layout, quality) implementing calib_ba.py, with the per-tag admission gate from F22. resolve_calibration detects markers in ALL frames (it already loads every image), feeds them to the bundle and returns the pooled rig plus the tag layout; the first-hit `return` inside the frame loop is removed. The layout it produces is what the up-axis fix (F15) and a cross-validated focal search (F28) both need. Justify it as robustness + provenance, not as an accuracy gain.

## F42 [minor, unverified] A camera that never sees a tag has no recovery path, and the body-keypoint fallback is measurably not accurate enough to be one — keep the failure, fix the message

- file: pose3d/calib/resolve.py:155 | category: robustness
- CLAIM: When no frame yields a tag common to both views, resolve_calibration returns ok=False and the import dialog offers 2D-only review (import_dialog.py:180-190). This take came within a hair of that: the right camera detected NO tag in 15 of 26 frames and never saw more than two tags. The obvious fallback — recovering the relative pose from the RTMPose body correspondences — is not viable at the accuracy the pipeline needs.
- EVIDENCE: Tag visibility: left sees 13,14,17 in 26/26 frames and 15 in 6; right sees 14 in 11/26, 15 in 2/26, nothing in 15/26; frame 0001 happened to be one of the 11. Essential matrix from all 388 valid body correspondences at the shipped intrinsics: cv2.findEssentialMat RANSAC (thr 3 px) keeps 115/388 inliers, 5.72 deg rotation error and 5.62 deg translation-direction error vs the tag rig; LMEDS keeps 368/388 and gives 13.81 / 8.13 deg. The 8-point design matrix's singular values end 0.0430, 0.0336 — s8/s9 = 1.28, i.e. the null vector is barely separated for a 0.128 m subject with static cameras. At 0.13% of height per degree that is 0.7-1.8% of body height of shape error, and scale is not recoverable at all without a tag.
- IMPACT: Zero on this take. The finding is that 'fail and offer 2D-only' is the correct behaviour and should be kept, with the reason recorded so nobody implements the body-keypoint fallback later. What IS missing is diagnosis: the user is told 'no ArUco markers common to both cameras were detected' with no indication that one camera saw tags in 26 frames and the other in 11, i.e. that the right camera's framing is the problem.
- PROPOSED FIX: Keep the failure path. Count per-camera tag detections while scanning and report them in the CalibrationResult.message construction at resolve.py:155-159: 'left camera saw tags 13,14,17 in 26/26 frames; right camera saw tag 14 in 11/26 and nothing in 15 — move the right camera so at least two tags are in frame'. Two lines plus a per-camera counter in the detection loop. Do NOT add an essential-matrix fallback.

## F43 [minor, unverified] The bundled rig's near-180 deg aim singularity is real but is not reached on this take

- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:136 | category: retarget
- CLAIM: _align's antipodal branch (character.py:136-139) picks an arbitrary perpendicular axis, so as a bone's target approaches the exact opposite of its rest direction the roll becomes numerically unstable and flips — the classic failure of aim-only retargeting, which will bite as soon as a subject raises an arm fully overhead against a rest pose that points down.
- EVIDENCE: Synthetic sweep on upper_arm.L: a 1 deg perturbation of the aim direction changes the bone's roll by 0.7 deg at 150 deg from rest, 1.1 at 160, 4.9 at 176, 9.7 at 178, 163.8 at exactly 180, then 9.4 / 4.6 / 3.0 at 182/184/186. On the client take the largest swing any bone makes from its rest direction is 110.5 deg (shin.R), medians 12-52 deg, so the singular region is never entered.
- IMPACT: Zero on this take. Latent: any pose with a fully raised arm or a fully extended leg opposite to rest produces a 180 deg mesh twist that appears as a one-frame explosion of the character.
- PROPOSED FIX: Subsumed by F02: once every limb bone's roll is set from an explicit reference rather than left to _align's arbitrary choice, the antipodal branch stops mattering (the aim axis is still ill-conditioned there, but the roll no longer depends on which perpendicular _align picked). If F02 is not taken, at minimum make the antipodal branch deterministic in the bone's own rest frame and add a regression test at 179/180/181 deg.

## F44 [minor, unverified] The trf fallback for sparse frames is 34.8x slower than lm (746.78 ms vs 21.47 ms per frame), so exactly the takes that are already degraded also freeze the UI

- file: pose3d/geometry/bonefit.py:121 | category: bone-fit
- CLAIM: `method = 'lm' if n_res >= NUM_JOINTS*3 else 'trf'` (bonefit.py:120-122) switches solver whenever fewer than ~11 of 15 joints are observed. The problem is under-determined in that branch (45 free variables, 3 residuals per observed joint + 14 bone residuals) and trf spends its whole max_nfev=200 budget on a null space nothing constrains.
- EVIDENCE: Timed on the client take with its own measured bone lengths: the shipped path (all frames, mostly lm) averages 21.47 ms/frame; the same fit on a frame with 5 joints NaN'd (forcing trf) averages 746.78 ms/frame over 10 runs — 34.8x. On a 26-frame take that is 19.4 s instead of 0.56 s, and it is per interactive drag on the live-edit path (model.py:160), which runs in the Qt main thread.
- IMPACT: Not an accuracy defect, but it makes a degraded take feel broken and it blocks F10's fix (re-running fit_project on every drag), which is only affordable at 21 ms/frame.
- PROPOSED FIX: Make the problem well-posed instead of switching solvers: solve only for the OBSERVED joints plus any unobserved joint lying on a bone chain between two observed ones, and freeze the rest at their x0 (they are returned as NaN anyway when fill_missing=False). That drops the variable count below the residual count in every realistic case and keeps 'lm' everywhere. fit_bone_lengths gains an internal index map; the signature and the fill_missing contract are unchanged. Also lower max_nfev for the trf branch and add a test asserting a 5-joint frame fits in under 100 ms.


# REFUTED FINDINGS (5) — do not build on these


## F03 [refuted] The legacy no-face-keypoint head path clamps in 46% of frames; the character's head is 39.4 deg off the captured nose direction in EVERY frame
- file: pose3d/geometry/character.py:384
- WHY REFUTED: The code path and the raw numbers reproduce exactly, but the causal attribution and the impact do not. (1) PATH CONFIRMED: io_project.py:153 fills a missing head3d key with NaN (project.py:45), so main_window.py:450 -> view3d.py:154 -> character.py:580-587 gets a (5,3) all-NaN array, ear_mid stays None, and character.py:602 calls _head_aim_target every frame; _head_basis returns None at character.py:412 so head_R is None. I verified head3d is present-but-all-NaN in the loaded take. (2) NUMBERS REPRODUCE: _rest_head_pitch=5.7330 deg (character.py:219-229), _NOSE_PITCH=45.0 (character.py:38); captured nose off torso 39.58 deg median (29.72-55.13); want 0.31 deg median, clamped at exactly 0 in 12/26 = 46.2% of frames; character-vs-captured NECK->HEAD angle 39.44 deg median (31.90-41.35); HEAD 12.58% of body height (9.83-12.72). All match the finding to 2 decimals. (3) THE CAUSAL CLAIM FAILS: with the clamp removed (want = _rest_head_pitch + theta - _NOSE_PITCH, signed) the HEAD error does NOT improve — 12.66% median / 41.3 deg, slightly WORSE than shipped. Clamp-only HEAD displacement is 0.00% of height median, 2.97% max, nonzero in 12/26 frames. So the 12.58%/39.4 deg is not produced by the max(...,0) at character.py:384, and the decomposition "only 4.6 pp is the definitional length difference" is wrong. (4) WHAT ACTUALLY PRODUCES IT: _JOINT_FROM_RIG[Joint.HEAD] = ('head','mid') (character.py:110) is a point on the skull AXIS, 3.73 deg off the torso line in the rig's rest pose, while the canonical HEAD is the NOSE, 39.6 deg off the torso here and 41.8 deg off in Panoptic ground truth (101 GT frames, range 37.6-47.7). That ~36-40 deg gap is the intended nose-to-skull-axis rotation plus an incomparable readout point — the finding's own fix (1) — but it is the whole effect, not 8 of 12.6 points. Identity control: feeding the rig its OWN rest joints back gives HEAD error 2.97% of rig height (all-joint median 1.52%). (5) THE IMPACT CLAIM IS INVERTED: the captured nose marker lands 1.56 mm from the nearest character mesh vertex (0.63-2.37 mm; 1.33% of body height) in the shipped build — visually ON the head. The finding's own NoFudge control, which scores 1.72% on the head-mid metric, TRIPLES the visible error to 4.34 mm (3.68%). The client cannot see a 12.6% head offset; the readout point is inside the skull by construction. (6) _NOSE_PITCH=45 deg is defensible: Panoptic GT median nose pitch is 41.8 deg. The real residual is that the neutral target (rest_head_pitch 5.73 deg) sits ~7 deg below the GT ear-mid direction (12.6 deg median) and that the clamp discards the negative half of the nod range.

## F05 [refuted] RTMPose puts the distal joints ~10% of a bone-length past the mannequin's physical (red-dot) joints — 4.0 mm / 3.4% of body height in 3D
- file: pose3d/core/skeleton.py:102
- WHY REFUTED: The code claim is correct and the raw magnitudes reproduce almost exactly, but the mechanism, the impact and the proposed fix are all wrong, and the finding reached "12 of 12, p=2^-12" by omitting the four measurements that contradict it.

VERIFIED (code): pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:26 `feet: bool = False`, :30 `self._map = map_halpe26 if feet else derive_joints`; both call sites construct `RTMPoseDetector(mode="balanced", device="cpu")` with no `feet` argument (pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:233-234 `_ensure_detector`, pose3d/ui/main_window.py:257-260), so `derive_joints` (pose3d/core/skeleton.py:140-176) and `_DIRECT_FROM_COCO` (skeleton.py:101-116) are genuinely on the executed path. COCO-17 indices are taken verbatim. That part stands.

REFUTED #1 - the knee control was omitted. Re-running the bone-frame projection myself over all 26 frames and both views, the four KNEE measurements are +0.2 %, +4.0 % (left view) and +5.3 %, -0.1 % (right view): essentially zero, and two of them positive. Including them gives 13 of 16 negative, not 12 of 12; the finding's set is elbow/wrist/ankle only. The 12 are also not independent — they are 2 views x 2 sides of 3 marker types on a rigid figure whose marker geometry never changes, so the effective n is ~3, not 12, and the quoted p=2^-12 is meaningless.

REFUTED #2 - the dots are not where the finding assumes. Full-resolution crops (my tiles.png / tiles2.png, frames 0001/0008/0015/0022) show the "wrist" dot moulded on the distal FOREARM roughly one dot-diameter proximal of the wrist/hand junction, and the "ankle" dot on the lower SHIN above the malleolus with a SEPARATE dot at the toe tip; RTMPose's keypoint sits at the actual mechanical pivot in both cases. The knee dot is on the kneecap, i.e. at the pivot — and that is exactly the joint with ~0 offset. The sign of the along-bone offset therefore tracks where the moulding is, not a detector bias: where the marker is at the pivot the detector agrees. The overlay also shows dots at the crown, chin, sternal notch, sternum, navel and both toes — this is a decorative moulded marker set, not a mocap marker set, so "the mannequin's mechanical joints the client physically marked" over-reads it. PROJECT_CONTEXT.md:90-91 itself lists "hands" and "feet", not wrists and ankles.

REFUTED #3 - the per-joint 3D pattern contradicts "distal joints". My 3D distances are flat, not distal-weighted: WRISTS 2.93 mm, SHOULDERS 3.82 mm, ELBOWS 4.05 mm, KNEES 4.51 mm, ANKLES 5.20 mm, HIPS 5.23 mm. The knee — the one joint whose marker is verifiably at the pivot — is 4.51 mm, HIGHER than the wrist. A uniform ~4 mm across every joint including the controls is a definition-plus-noise floor, not a distal bias.

REFUTED #4 - the impact statement is false. Comparing 3D bone lengths from RTMPose vs dot-to-dot on the same frames: L upper arm 1.000x, R upper arm 1.135x, L forearm 1.021x, R forearm 0.948x, R thigh 0.903x — scattered either side of 1.0, median ~1.0. Limbs do not "read ~10 % too long"; there is no systematic length distortion. This is also internally inconsistent with the finding's own sign convention (a proximal-shifted elbow shortens the upper arm).

REFUTED #5 - the fix targets the wrong thing and my numbers say it degrades accuracy. On the frames where both references exist, bone-length CV across the rigid mannequin is RTMPose 5.07 % median vs dot-to-dot 9.18 % (per-bone std 0.82-1.43 mm vs 1.63-2.68 mm) — the proposed "ground truth" is 1.8x noisier than the thing it would replace. The finding's own merge_notes already record 5.27 % -> 8.85 %, and it then proposes rescuing this with a per-joint radius offset fitted once per take, which cannot fix a reference whose per-frame scatter is the problem, and which would have to model a wrist marker sitting on a different SEGMENT rather than a surface radius.

What is actually true is much smaller: RTMPose keypoints and the moulded red markers differ by a mostly-systematic ~4 mm (3.4 % of the 118 mm reconstruction), roughly uniformly at every joint, with no bone-length distortion. That is a definitional offset against a noisier reference, not a critical detection defect, and it is not "the second-largest error source" — the EMA smoother's 6.07 mm (baseline) is both larger and demonstrably an error rather than a definition.

## F19 [refuted] rig_bone_lengths() is dead on the executed path except inside the uniform-scale fit: the skeleton is fitted to its OWN measured lengths, never to the character's, contrary to commit 02e8a44
- file: pose3d/pipeline.py:157
- WHY REFUTED: I re-derived every code claim myself and reproduced the measurement with my own script. The mechanical code observation is TRUE but carries no defect; the three assertions that make it a "major finding" are each false.

WHAT I CONFIRMED (read myself):
- pose3d/pipeline.py:152-161 — `def fit_project(project, bone_lengths=None, smooth=True, alpha=0.6)`; when `bone_lengths is None` it builds them from `measure_bone_lengths(raw)` plus `fallback_bone_lengths()` for never-observed bones.
- Every call site passes no lengths: pose3d/ui/import_dialog.py:214 `fit_project(project, smooth=True)`; pose3d/ui/model.py:55 `fit_project(self.project, smooth=True)`; pose3d/pipeline.py:193 (run_full); tests/build_demo_project.py:77; tests/test_bonefit.py:132. Full `grep -rn 'fit_project'` output — no other hit.
- pose3d/ui/model.py:157-160 re-fits a dragged frame with `self._bone_lengths`, built at model.py:173-178 from `measure_bone_lengths(raw)` + fallback.
- `rig_bone_lengths` is defined at pose3d/geometry/character.py:317 and referenced only at character.py:344 and tests/test_retarget.py:563.

REFUTATION 1 — "dead on the executed path" is false, and the title refutes itself. character.py:344 sits inside `fit_to_subject`, which IS on the executed path twice: pose3d/ui/view3d.py:98 (`self._character.fit_to_subject(poses)` in `fit_subject`) and pose3d/export/blender_export.py:76 (`ch.fit_to_subject(poses3d @ R)`). The function runs once per take in both the preview and the export. "Dead except where it is used" is not a defect.

REFUTATION 2 — "contrary to commit 02e8a44" is a misreading; I read the full commit body. It says the opposite of what the finding attributes to it: "The character used to be deformed to match each subject: every bone carried a fitted length multiplier... Bone lengths are now inviolable and motion transfers as rotation alone" and "A single UNIFORM scale, fitted once per take by least squares over the canonical bones, sizes the character to the subject: it changes size, never shape." The title "fit the skeleton to the character" names the RETARGET stage (captured directions imposed on fixed-length rig bones), never fit_project. The commit even measures and names the exact residual the finding claims it contradicts: "On the client's capture the wrists reach exactly (0.0%) and the ankles to 3.0%, while the knees sit 11.9% out — that residual is the rig's anatomy error and is what the rig swap addresses next." There is no false premise in the history to correct.

REFUTATION 3 — "producing the entire measured retarget error" is measurably false. My experiment (f19.py): de-tilt fitted3d with sequence_up/de_tilt_matrix, `Character().fit_to_subject` -> scale 113.62, then rebuild each frame keeping every bone DIRECTION but forcing every bone length to rig_len/scale, and re-run the retarget. Body retarget median (excl. definitional HEAD) falls 1.415% -> 0.52% of height; all-joint median 1.507% -> 0.700%. So proportion mismatch explains ~63% of the retarget error, not all of it. The residual is structural: LEFT/RIGHT_SHOULDER get WORSE (1.02/1.05% -> 1.96/1.96%), RIGHT_HIP is unchanged (3.50% -> 3.46%), and HEAD stays at 9.71% because HEAD=nose is aim-corrected by `_head_aim_target` (character.py:~370) and never aims at the nose.

REFUTATION 4 — the proposed fix moves the error and amplifies it 2.5x. Forcing the skeleton onto rig proportions displaces the skeleton itself off the measurement by median 3.61% / p90 5.57% / max 7.7% of body height — versus the 1.4% retarget error it buys back. Cost against the client's actual complaint ("the character does not follow the keypoints"): re-fitting the raw triangulation with rig lengths reprojects at 10.26 px (left) / 6.67 px (right) median unsmoothed, vs 7.38/4.01 px for the shipped measured-length fit — 39%/66% worse against the 2D keypoints (raw = 5.11/2.78 px; with the smoother 23.53/18.08 vs 21.65/17.18). The character would agree with fitted3d while fitted3d agrees less with the pixels the client is looking at.

MAGNITUDE — 1.4% of a 0.118 m subject is 1.7 mm. The EMA smoother alone displaces joints by 5.3% of height mean / 21.7% max (baseline stage_deltas, which I did not need to re-derive for this). This effect is ~4x smaller than the already-identified dominant one.

What survives is a documentation/ownership nit, not a bone-fit defect.

## F26 [refuted] Detector confidence carries almost no information about the real error, so kpt_thr, the RAG bands and the correction workflow are all blind on this subject
- file: pose3d/detect/rtmpose.py:27
- WHY REFUTED: The score-distribution half of the evidence reproduces exactly, but every load-bearing part of the finding — the impact, the executed path, the attribution of the two NaNs, the correlation magnitude, and the fix — fails.

(1) THE UI DOES NOT BAND ON CONFIDENCE. `camera_view._joint_status` (pose3d/ui/camera_view.py:199-217) reads `err = self._accuracy[j]` first and uses `rag_status` ONLY in the `else` branch when that error is non-finite (camera_view.py:202-211). `_accuracy` is a per-joint reprojection residual pushed from `model._accuracy` (pose3d/ui/model.py:184-190) via `accuracyChanged` -> `main_window._on_accuracy` -> `cam_left/right.set_accuracy` (pose3d/ui/main_window.py:234-240); it is NaN only when `self.rig is None` (model.py:186-187). On the client take the rig exists, so I measured the fallback firing for 0 of 390 joint-frames — the string "detection confidence 88%" in the finding's impact is unreachable for this project. Worse for the finding's premise "no gate ever fires": the band the app actually shows (accuracy_pct = 100*exp(-err/6), ACC_HIGH=85 / ACC_MED=70, pose3d/ui/panels.py:21,33-49) comes out 284 RED / 58 amber / 48 green out of 390. Confidence bands would have given 386 green / 4 amber (left) and 382/6/2 (right). The user-facing signal is already the independent residual the finding asks for, and it is already almost all red.

(2) THE TWO NaNs ARE NOT kpt_thr REJECTIONS. Their stored scores are exactly 0.0 while every other score is a full-precision float (0012/right RIGHT_KNEE, 0021/right LEFT_ANKLE). `reject()` at rtmpose.py:63-70 writes `xy[bad] = np.nan` and never touches `scores`; the only code that zeroes a score is `validate_cross_view` at pose3d/pipeline.py:100-101. So kpt_thr fired 0/780 times (0.00%, not the claimed 0.51%) and the cross-view gate fired twice. The finding attributes to a blind gate two drops made by a different, working gate.

(3) THE CORRELATION DOES NOT REPRODUCE, AND ITS SIGN FAVOURS CONFIDENCE. With my own HSV red-blob detector (n=778 nearest-blob matches) I get pearson -0.082 / spearman -0.149 pooled, and -0.141 / -0.148 after z-scoring within joint — 2-3.5x weaker than the claimed -0.288. My terciles (cuts 0.804 / 0.884) are 7.41% / 4.67% / 4.29%, MONOTONE, not the claimed "top tercile no better than middle" (3.52/2.48/2.55). Bottom vs top decile: 7.72% vs 4.72%, ratio 1.64, Mann-Whitney p=0.029. Confidence carries weak but real, correctly-signed information.

(4) THE PROXY IS CONFOUNDED, SO NEITHER SIDE'S NUMBER MEANS "REAL ERROR". Looking at the overlays I rendered, the markers sit on limb surfaces (deltoid, patella, upper thigh, forehead) and there are decoy dots with no canonical joint (sternum, navel, toe caps), so PELVIS necessarily matches the navel dot and NECK the sternum dot. 30% of the red-dot distance variance is a fixed per-(joint x camera) offset. Decisively: the median nearest-dot distance is ~34 px in BOTH cameras (33.8 left, 34.7 right) even though the right image is half resolution and the figure spans 425 px there vs 804 px left — 8.17% vs 3.91% of figure height for the same physical rig. A proportional localisation error cannot do that, and a 34 px RANDOM localisation error is flatly incompatible with the 4.9 px median epipolar residual I re-measured. The offset is systematic/proxy artifact, not the per-observation noise any confidence could flag.

(5) THE FIX IS AIMED AT THE WRONG MECHANISM AND ITS SIGNAL IS WORSE. Fix (a) proposes banding on the Sampson epipolar residual "instead of rag_status in camera_view._joint_status when available" — that override already exists (with reprojection instead of Sampson). And measured against the same red-dot proxy the finding uses as truth, the epipolar residual scores spearman +0.042 and the displayed reprojection accuracy +0.043 (n=776) — i.e. zero, and worse than confidence's -0.149. Replacing a weak signal with a null one is not a fix.

What survives: confidence is nearly INERT (not misleading) — its only three live consumers are kpt_thr (0 firings), the drop-the-worse-view tie-break in pipeline.py:92-95 (2 firings), and a tooltip fallback that never executes here; `Detection.rag()` (pose3d/detect/base.py:30-32) is dead in the app, its sole caller being tests/test_detect.py:18. That is a minor dead-code/tuning observation, not a major detection defect.

## F35 [refuted] (NECK, HEAD) is treated as a rigid bone although it is the least rigid measurement in the skeleton: 9.52% CV, 38.6% range
- file: pose3d/core/skeleton.py:86
- WHY REFUTED: The raw statistic reproduces exactly, but every load-bearing interpretation around it fails.

(1) CODE — verified myself. `(Joint.NECK, Joint.HEAD)` is `pose3d/core/skeleton.py:86`, `HEAD = 0  # nose` is `skeleton.py:62`, and `fit_bone_lengths` does enforce it (`bonefit.py:95` builds `bones` from BONES, `bonefit.py:105-107` adds `bone_weight*(d-L)` per bone, default `bone_weight=5.0` at `bonefit.py:61`). It is on the executed path: `import_dialog.py:212-214` calls `triangulate_project` then `fit_project`, which measures lengths at `pipeline.py:158` and fits at `pipeline.py:171`, smooths at `pipeline.py:183`; the drag path re-fits at `model.py:160`. So far the finding is right.

(2) "LEAST RIGID MEASUREMENT / WORST OF ALL 14 BONES" — a normalization artifact, refuted. My own per-bone table over the 26 raw frames: NECK->HEAD median 21.86 mm, std 2.082 mm, CV 9.52%, min 17.71 / max 26.15, range 38.6% — identical to the finding. But ranked by ABSOLUTE std it is statistically tied for the middle of the pack: R_KNEE->R_ANKLE 2.090 mm, NECK->HEAD 2.082, R_HIP->R_KNEE 2.059, PELVIS->NECK 2.018, L_HIP->L_KNEE 1.829. Every bone on this rigid mannequin carries the same ~1-2 mm of triangulation noise; NECK->HEAD only "wins" the CV ranking because it is the second-shortest segment (21.9 mm). Nor is there evidence of head articulation: the lag-1 autocorrelation of its length residual is 0.41, mid-pack among the 14 bones (-0.24 to +0.92), i.e. it looks like the same white-ish noise as the genuinely rigid limbs, not like a smoothly pitching head.

(3) IMPACT MECHANISM — backwards, refuted. The finding says the stiffened neck-nose length "will systematically mis-aim the head" via the ear/eye/nose head feature. The opposite holds. When face keypoints exist, the neck bone aims at `_EAR_MID` (`character.py:73-75`, `590-604`) and the head bone takes a full measured rotation `head_R = _head_basis(head_rig) @ _rest_head.T` (`character.py:622-629`, applied at `character.py:661-662`), both from `head3d`, which `pipeline.py:145-148` triangulates raw and which `fit_project` never touches — no bone fit, no smoothing. The canonical HEAD joint is used for the head only in the LEGACY `_head_aim_target` fallback (`character.py:360-390`, reached at `character.py:600-603` when `ear_mid is None`). I measured it: perturbing the canonical HEAD joint by 3.4 mm (the largest displacement the fit ever applies) changes the posed head-bone and neck-bone axes by 0.0000 deg median AND max WITH face keypoints, vs 5.42 deg median / 8.46 max without. The head feature is precisely what makes this bone irrelevant to head aim.

(4) MAGNITUDE — an order of magnitude smaller than implied. I ran the exact counterfactual (monkeypatched `bonefit.BONES` in memory; no repo edit): dropping (NECK,HEAD) moves HEAD 0.84 mm median / 3.39 max, moves no other joint more than 0.97 mm, rotates the raw neck->nose direction only 0.61 deg median / 1.72 max (because the length term displaces the point RADIALLY, and `_bone_fk` normalizes its aim vector, `character.py:484-486`), and end-to-end changes the POSED head/neck bone axis by just 0.52 deg median / 1.17 p90 / 2.42 max, plus a 0.82% shift in the character's uniform scale. The smoother changes the same posed axis by 4.13 deg median / 13.00 max — 8x more. Nose reprojection: raw 5.23/2.94 px (L/R), fit-with-bone 7.65/4.05, fit-without-bone 5.23/2.94, shipped(smoothed) 30.68/20.98, shipped-without-bone 26.72/18.38 — so the bone constraint accounts for ~13% of the shipped nose reprojection error and the smoother for the rest. On top, the legacy head aim clamps at `want = max(rest_pitch + theta - 45 deg, 0)` and theta is below the clamp in 12 of 26 frames (median theta 39.6 deg vs the 39.27 deg clamp point), so half this take's head aim is saturated at neutral and cannot respond to the bone at all.

(5) PROPOSED FIX — option (a) is not the "simpler" one. BONES is not a fit-only constant: it also draws the 2D overlay bones (`camera_view.py:113`), the 3D skeleton segments (`view3d.py:192`), the exported bone topology (`blender_export.py:104`), and the character's rig/subject length tables for the uniform scale (`character.py:321`, `character.py:343`). Removing the pair would silently delete the head segment from both camera views, the 3D view and the export payload. Only option (b) (per-bone weights) or a separate FIT_BONES list targets the fitting mechanism without collateral.

Net: reproducible statistic, wrong diagnosis, wrong impact path, and a fix aimed at a shared constant. Residual real effect is 0.5 deg on the posed character on legacy projects only.
